madam president, the israeli attack on gaza is a war crime and a crime against humanity. it is tragic what is happening: 900 palestinians have already died, including many children, and thousands have been injured. we must demand an end to the attack on gaza. we must demand an end to the israeli blockade which has turned gaza into a concentration camp. so we join with all the people in all the countries and on all the continents who are protesting against the massacres, with the people in all the countries and on all the continents who are outraged, who are going out into the streets and saying 'enough with the crimes'! the european parliament and other responsible bodies of the european union must demand an immediate end to the attack on gaza and demand an immediate end to the israeli blockade.
the debate is closed.

the report by barbara weiler, on behalf of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, on the transposition, application and enforcement of directive 2005/29/ec on unfair business-to-consumer commercial practices in the internal market and directive 2006/114/ec on misleading and comparative advertising (2008/2114 (ini)) (a6-0514/2008) follows.
member of the commission. − mr president, first of all i would like to thank the rapporteur, mrs weiler, for her report, the contents of which of course will be carefully considered by the commission, but also to thank her for the comments she made regarding this procedure. the commission fully agrees that it is now very important that member states adequately transpose the new concepts introduced by the directive on unfair commercial practices and that national authorities also contribute to uniform implementation of the directive right across the eu. as far as transpositions are concerned, two member states are still late: luxembourg and spain; the commission referred these cases to the court of justice in june last year. the commission also coordinated cooperation on transposition in order to avoid incorrect transpositions. nevertheless, some problems remain in a limited number of countries mainly because of their reluctance to comply with full harmonisation. for those cases the commission will not hesitate to launch infringement proceedings. the report mentions the need to protect not only consumers but also smes against unfair commercial practices. on this point the commission reminds the european parliament that a full harmonisation directive on unfair business-to-consumer practices was already a very ambitious proposal which would have failed if its scope had been extended to business-to-business unfair competition practices. it has been concluded from the consultation leading up to the proposal and from the deliberations in council that there was little support for extending the scope of the directive to cover business-to-business unfair commercial practices. as regards aggressive practices which were regulated for the first time at eu level through the directive on unfair commercial practices, it was considered that such practices occur almost exclusively in business-to-consumer relationships. misleading business-to-business practices are already covered by the directive concerning misleading and comparative advertising. such practices should continue to be regulated solely by this directive. as regards enforcement of consumer protection legislation, the commission will continue to coordinate enforcement actions through the consumer protection cooperation network. in this context the commission notes that parliament’s support for the ‘sweeps’ as an enforcement tool. the commission intends to further develop this mechanism and has planned a further sweep for later this year. further to the request from parliament, the commission is also pleased to add that the forthcoming second version of the consumer scoreboard will contain data gathered during the sweeps carried out so far. as the report mentions the need for information campaigns to raise consumer awareness of their rights, the commission would like to inform the members of its recent ‘is it fair?’ website which includes for example educational material on the blacklist of banned practices. to conclude, the commission would like to assure this house that it will continue to work closely with member states to ensure adequate and effective enforcement of the directives on unfair commercial practices and misleading comparative advertisement. the database containing national transposition measures and case-law will be set up this year and will serve as a useful tool in this respect.
this point is considered closed. the vote will take place on tuesday at 12 noon. (the president read out the rules of procedure, rule 45 (2)) (the president read out the rules of procedure, rule 45 (2)) (the president read out the rules of procedure, rule 45 (2)) (the president read out the rules of procedure, rule 45 (2)) (the president read out the rules of procedure, rule 45 (2)), which is the second part of rule 45 (2) which is the second part of rule 45 (2) which is the third part of rule 45 (2) which is the fourth part of rule 45 (2) which is the fourth part of rule 45 (2)) the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
the report by mr pedro guerreiro, on behalf of the committee on fisheries, on the cfp and the ecosystem approach to fisheries management (2008/2178 (ini)) (a6-0485/2008) follows.
the rapporteur. − the commission's communication, with its wide range of issues, is part of the debate on the possible reform of the common fisheries policy by 2012. this report, which was approved by the committee on fisheries of the european parliament, makes a number of advances which we consider important in this debate. fisheries are a vital activity for ensuring food and survival for humans, and this is the primary objective of any fisheries policy. in this connection, it is important to stress the importance of fishing in the eez waters of each member state for its sovereignty and independence, particularly at the food level. the common fisheries policy (cfp) should promote the modernisation and sustainable development of the fisheries sector, ensuring its socioeconomic viability and the sustainability of fisheries resources, and guaranteeing the public supply of fish and food sovereignty and security, the maintenance of jobs and the improvement of the living conditions of fishermen. in view of its own objectives, a cfp should not be subordinated to other community policies which have been defined in the meantime. that is to say, a fisheries policy is not and cannot be a policy for the oceans or the marine environment. the first and foremost task of fisheries management, as an activity exploiting a self-renewable resource, is to control the total fishing effort in order to ensure maximum sustainable catch. a fisheries policy must start from the assumption of the interdependence between the well-being of fishing communities and the sustainability of the ecosystems of which they are an integral part, in particular by recognising the specificity and importance of small-scale coastal fishing and artisanal fishing. the implementation of an ecosystem-based approach to marine management necessarily requires multidisciplinary and cross-sectoral action across a range of measures, which go far beyond and are ancillary to those adopted in the fisheries sector, which have an impact on marine ecosystems. rather than guesses based on preconceived ideas, the proposal for an ecosystem analysis of fisheries resource assessment should be based on validated scientific data. on the other hand, it is necessary to recognise that there are important differences between the various marine areas and resources that occur in each, the various fleets and gears used and their impacts on ecosystems, which requires diversified, specific and appropriate fisheries management measures, compensating fishermen, where necessary, for their socio-economic consequences. in order to ensure the sustainability of resources, fishing activity and local communities, we consider it essential that member states exercise their sovereignty over the 12 miles of the territorial sea and that the area corresponding to the eez of the outermost regions be considered an exclusive access zone. the proposals concerning access to resources, which aim to promote a system of individual transferable quotas, with the consequence of concentrating fishing activity and individual appropriation of fishing rights, are worrying in this respect. it should also be noted that a policy which encourages the indiscriminate slaughter of vessels, which does not take into account the specificities of the fleets, the resources, the consumption needs of each member state and their socioeconomic impact, is inappropriate and unjustified. finally, i would like to stress that the sharp decline in incomes in the sector is due not only to restrictions on fishing activity but above all to the stagnation/reduction of prices at first sale, accompanied by the increase in production costs (gasoline and petrol).
this point is considered closed. the vote will take place on tuesday at 12 noon. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
the report by mrs teresa riera madurell, on behalf of the committee on women's rights and gender equality, on the transposition and application of directive 2002/73/ec of the european parliament and of the council of 23 september 2002 amending council directive 76/207/eec on the implementation of the principle of equal treatment for men and women as regards access to employment, vocational training and promotion, and working conditions (2008/2039 (ini)) (a6-0491/2008) follows.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, it is regrettable that this is not a time for a deep reflection on the consequences of the implementation, under the pretext of the euro, of neoliberal and monetarist policies which have contributed to the serious social situation we are experiencing, to the increase in inequalities, unemployment, precarious and low-paid work and poverty. it is unacceptable that ideological dogmas such as price stability and the irrational criteria of the stability pact are maintained as a pretext for resorting to privatisation and the disempowerment of the social functions of the state, which also includes the thesis of the minimum state and the greater efficiency of the private sector, seeking to impose the acceptance of the so-called wage moderation which translates into low nominal wage increases and even a decrease in real wages, as the portuguese case unfortunately demonstrates. we cannot accept the european central bank's status as a false autonomy, which drags down decisions to cut interest rates, which keeps the euro overvalued only to protect the countries with the most developed economies and the most powerful economic and financial groups, thus aggravating the tragedies of the weakest economies and the populations with the weakest economic resources. this policy of the european central bank has led to the contraction of the european economy, making it clear that further cuts in reference interest rates are needed. while the purchasing power of workers, pensioners and pensioners has declined over the last ten years, the profits of economic and financial groups have become the highest in the last thirty years. and that's why the joy and the satisfaction we hear here. even now, in the crisis situation they have created, what they are doing is nationalising the losses and then privatising the profits, while the workers, the micro and small entrepreneurs, the retired and the unemployed suffer the consequences of the crisis, entitled at best to a few crumbs. look at what is happening in the financial sector, where some banks have already increased spreads more than once since the financial crisis began and where the margin charged has doubled in the space of a year, further penalizing new lending. we therefore insist on the urgency of a real break with these neoliberal and monetarist policies, putting an end to the stability pact, the tax havens and the false autonomy of the european central bank. we therefore insist on the need to increase the community budget significantly on the basis of a fair distribution of income and wealth, so that there is a policy of economic and social cohesion and not insistence on measures that are more of the same, namely 'save who can', which allows the richest to become richer and the poorest poorer, as the worsening inequalities and divergences in the economies of the euro zone clearly demonstrate. we therefore insist on the need to support production, micro, small and mediumsized enterprises, to promote quality public services, to facilitate credit and to improve the purchasing power of households, not only of the most disadvantaged but also of the middle classes, in order to create more jobs and to reduce poverty and misery for millions of people in our countries.
in writing. the air agreements between the european community and the united states of america (usa) originally relate to and are based on the liberalisation of air transport. such agreements at eu level (or rather, by the european community the only legal entity and its single community market, which is intended to be fully liberalised) seek to supersede bilateral agreements between different member states and the us. as in other resolutions previously adopted by the european parliament, we stress that we will obviously be the first to take steps to ensure a high level of civil aviation safety and to minimise the economic burden resulting from the duplication of regulatory supervision of aircraft. however, we have two important safeguards: (1) that these processes are not intended and expected to create and facilitate the conditions for further liberalization of air transport by means of harmonization of standards; (2) that these processes do not promote harmonization under safety standards and rules, particularly where safety, burden minimization and liberalization are combined, where profit and concentration are the main considerations. we believe that air transport must be defended as a public service, provided by public companies in each country, which guarantee the quality and safety of the services provided to citizens.
in writing. the future of foreign transport policy requires good relations between the european community and the united states of america. the commission has also proposed that the european union and the united states agree to establish a joint aviation safety regulatory cooperation agreement. the aim of this agreement is to facilitate the exchange of goods and services in the aeronautical sector by limiting as far as possible the duplication of assessment, testing and control tasks to the major regulatory differences between the two parties. we also hope to be creating a flexible daily operating framework which will allow technical problems arising from its application to be solved through a system of continuous cooperation and consultation. this agreement is another fundamental step towards the external dimension of european transport policy, which is why i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - we agree with several aspects of the report, namely: - the change in policy has resulted in a loss of market opportunities for european union producers and has led to an increased dependence on imported food products from third countries, produced using very different production standards, which has placed community agricultural products in a situation of inequality; - concern about the escalating costs of agricultural production factors, which can lead to a lower use and reduction of agricultural production, contributing to the food crisis in europe and the world; - the need for policy instruments to prevent these sharp and harmful price fluctuations; - concern about the increasing concentration of the food retail market, which has created monopoly situations, and the need for alternative solutions in negotiations with retailers, in favour of small producers. there are, however, aspects with which we cannot agree: the reinforced market orientation of the cap and the devaluation of food sovereignty, placing the emphasis solely on food security, forgetting that it is difficult without it. that is why we abstained.
in writing. the mcguinness report addresses a subject which i consider to be strategic: food security and the importance of a strong and competitive european agriculture in a globalised world. food safety, following the recent food price crisis, must be an eu priority. although a new food shortage crisis is not likely any time soon, it is likely to occur in the future, given the negative effects of climate change on agricultural production and the continuing increase in demand. since developing countries are unlikely to be able to produce enough food to feed their growing populations, industrialized countries will continue to have the important task of producing and exporting food. the cap must therefore once again be a european priority and the basis of european food security policy, and more than ever essential in a time of financial and economic crisis.
in writing. - the recent extraordinary rise in food prices has rightly sparked a debate on agricultural policies, food security and development. unfortunately, the issue of international trade is often absent from these debates, contributing to the search for solutions that ignore the positive potential that increased global consumption can generate. if in the first stage this food inflation threatens to cause famine in countries and populations without resources and requires increased humanitarian aid, in the second stage it stimulates the overall increase in production capacity and the increase in global trade. this is an opportunity for the world's agricultural populations that must be seized. as regards europe and the cap, adaptation to this new world context - with a potential for slower growth than expected - must not be at the expense of protectionism and new barriers or market distortion. the medium and long-term profitability of european agriculture and rural development should be the criteria for the cap and its reform.
in writing. the report is another step towards the ep's majority in its effort to sell the lisbon treaty at any cost, a task which has not proved easy, judging by the results of the referendums already held. this multiplication of efforts has at least one merit: it shows clearly how difficult, even painful, it is for its advocates to find arguments to promote it. there is no propaganda, and that is what this report is about, to disguise the undemocratic nature of the insistence of the european union's officials to force a new referendum in ireland in order to impose this treaty proposal. we do not share the narrow view of those who think that the establishment of a civil dialogue or a citizens' initiative is sufficient to counteract the nature of a proposal which, taken as a whole, excludes the citizens of each member state from the possibility of defining their collective future, which insists on measures leading to precarious employment, increased working hours, facilitation of dismissals, privatisation of public services. no amount of propaganda can hide the neoliberal, federalist and militarist content of this draft treaty. that is why we are voting against it.
in writing. - in a very brief way, this own-initiative report of the european parliament aims to integrate the community's requirements for the preservation of the marine environment with the common fisheries policy, which has as one of its operational objectives the progressive implementation of an ecosystem approach to fisheries management. as a key point in this report, i would like to stress that the current system of tacs and quotas is considered to be unsuitable for the purposes of the reformed cfp and has proved inadequate both for the community fisheries sector and for the conservation of resources. alternative management systems must be rapidly equalised and in this respect i think the ec should be more rapid in discussing alternative forms, some of which, such as management based on fishing rights (for example) are the mainstay in countries such as the united states, new zealand, norway, iceland, all of which have a strong tradition and great potential in the fisheries sector. the reformulation of the recovery plan for fisheries and lobster is another key point to be considered. this report deserves my vote in favour.
in writing. - although it does not share the full content of the adopted resolution, it does contain a set of important objectives and principles which should guide a fisheries policy. it is especially important to reaffirm and defend these objectives and principles (as the cfp has consistently done), many of which are not respected by the common fisheries policy (even if some of them are enshrined in it), when the european commission announces the presentation next april of a green paper on the future of the common fisheries policy, pointing to a possible reform of this policy by 2012. in view of the objectives and intentions that have been expressed by the european commission and other eu institutions regarding the future of fisheries, the sector in portugal - which is facing a long crisis, the causes of which lie in the heavy policies followed for decades, at national and community level - should be alert and mobilized against new and more heavy measures, which, if adopted and implemented, would represent the destruction of a large part of this strategic sector, with negative consequences for the country. such a policy is not a fatality! there are alternative policies for fisheries in portugal! policies which the pcp has been proposing and defending both at national level and in the european parliament!
in writing. − again, the final compromise ended up taking into account many of the criticisms we had made of the initial proposal, particularly as regards reduction rates, targets, measures and timetables for reducing the risks and hazards associated with pesticides and dependence on pesticides. it seems to us more reasonable not to quantify these targets at the outset, so as not to create ever more obstacles to small-scale farming. we also welcome the fact that the exemption from the inspection requirement for equipment and accessories covered by the european commission's initial proposal has been maintained and that the requirement for all equipment and accessories has been dropped, including those used on small family farms. we believe that this practical and principled differentiation between family farms and intensive agro-industry should be present in every decision. by the way, it is always worth remembering that it was not family farms and the non-intensive production methods that caused mad cows, dioxins, nitrofurans and other food disasters... that is why we voted for the compromise.
in writing. - i agree with the rapporteur and congratulate her on the final outcome of the document. the entry into force of this directive will be of the utmost importance in increasing pressure for urgent amendment of the pesticide risk reduction policy, which in the eu has been characterised by a certain lack of information and supervision of practices and products. in the interests of human health and the environment, it is essential to contribute to changing the problem of agricultural pesticides. this document is essential because it lays down rules for informing and training those who use pesticides and it requires monitoring of equipment. it also prohibits aerial spraying (permitted in cases of absolute necessity and when there are no alternatives). another positive aspect is the possibility for each member state to define protection and risk zones.
in writing. - the compromise which has been reached in the european parliament represents a step back from the maximalist proposals which existed on the elimination of active substances, particularly in the negative implications they would have for insecticides and pesticides, especially in countries such as portugal, which is being heavily affected by certain pests (insects) on fruit and vegetable crops, potatoes and olives and by certain diseases such as pine nematode and chestnut cancer, where, also due to the lack of effective phytosanitary controls, great damage is being caused, particularly to family farms. although we have many doubts about some aspects of the compromise, such as the question of non-chemical methods of control or phytopharmaceutical prevention and pest and crop management, we think it is right to apply the principle of mutual recognition of authorizations for plant protection products and to create zones that aggregate regions with similar ecological and climatic characteristics. but we insist on studies which give us the real reality of the consequences of these measures on productivity and consequently on farmers' incomes, so that this bill can be distributed throughout society, since we are talking about environmental and food safety requirements.
in writing. the aim of this document is to help harmonise legislation on pesticides. i agree with the report adopted, particularly because the application of the principle of mutual recognition of authorizations for plant protection products will put an end to the competitive imbalances that exist between different member states (with different market sizes) and will above all reinforce environmental and food safety concerns. the creation of three zones that aggregate regions with similar soil and climate characteristics is very positive. it would be risky to put completely different realities in the same bag. the question of endocrine disruptors has a fundamental basis for me: the proposed text is based on scientific opinions. the problem with endocrine disruptors is that, unlike carcinogens or mutagens, they have no toxicological parameters, but produce a variety of effects ranging from mild hormonal imbalances to genital malformations and/or cancer. it is important to regulate substances that have a proven negative effect on human health. the regulation has a triple legal basis (agriculture, internal market and public health), which i think is very positive.
in writing. − it is interesting that the report acknowledges that the analysis of the state of public finances in 2007 and the first part of 2008 shows a change in the economic trend and the threat of a downturn in the economy and growth prospects, accompanied by a persistently low inflation rate and rising income inequality. but then, in the face of the crisis, it is basically the same recipes that have led to the present situation, instead of taking this moment to propose changes to the neoliberal and monetarist policies that have contributed to the serious social situation that we are experiencing: increased inequalities, unemployment, precarious and low-paid work, poverty. it is therefore insisting on price stability and the stability pact, albeit with some flexibility, and on the lisbon strategy, which, as we know, has served as a pretext for resorting to privatisation and the discharge of the social functions of the state, which also includes the thesis of the minimum state and the highest efficiency of the private sector, and also seeks to impose the acceptance of the so-called wage moderation, which translates into the loss of purchasing power of wages. that is why we are voting against it.
the rapporteur. - mr president, mr vice-president jacques barrot, ladies and gentlemen, the proposal we are discussing today aims to amend the regulation adopted in 2004, which improved and harmonised the security rules on the protection of passports and travel documents of citizens of the european union against their fraudulent use, while introducing biometric identification elements. unlike in 2004, we are now in co-decision. i would like to thank the french presidency and commissioner barrot for the enormous effort they have put into this dossier with a view to an agreement at first reading. i would also like to thank the shadow rapporteurs for their work and cooperation, which was indispensable to the achievement of this result. this was a necessary solution, given that this regulation entered into force in 2004 and that, from june this year at the latest, all member states would have to collect fingerprints from children from birth. however, existing studies, resulting from pilot projects carried out in several member states, show that it is very difficult to collect, or even to rely on, fingerprints from children under the age of six. it is true that the national legislature could create exceptions to that obligation. however, this would mean that up to the age limit at which such exemption was granted, only temporary passports could be made. it would be an unreasonable burden for parents to have to obtain a passport for each of their children whenever they wanted to travel outside the schengen area. we have thus been able to reach an agreement stipulating a four-year period, in which the age limit will be fixed at 12 years with a derogating clause which should allow those states which have already adopted legislation setting a lower limit to do so, provided that this limit is not less than six years. we also awaited a review clause stipulating that after four years, and taking into account the results of the study we have asked the commission to draw up on the credibility of children's fingerprints, the age limit will be fixed definitively and in a harmonised way for all member states. a second derogation has been introduced for persons who, for various reasons, are physically unable to provide fingerprints. the icao recommendation of 'one person-one passport' was also welcomed. as the european data protection supervisor has said, this is an added benefit in the fight against child trafficking. in the field of child protection, we also obtained an interinstitutional agreement between the three institutions to establish a common position for the adoption of the rules necessary to protect children against abduction and trafficking. the commission should take the relevant initiatives in the respective area of civil law. i must confess that we are struggling with the question of the limited competence of the union in this area: the european union can only intervene in the reinforcement of biometric data in passports and travel documents with the aim of improving the security of these documents in the context of border control. i should say that we have established rules to safeguard the exercise of community competence by determining what types of data - fingerprints and photographs - and also the limitation of the use that will be made of them. they may only be used for the purposes of this regulation - border control - and to verify the authenticity of the document and to verify whether or not the person carrying it is the rightful holder. we have also reached agreement on two studies: one on so-called breeder documents, to ensure that the documents which allow the issuing of passports deserve the same reliability as the passport we want to safeguard, and another on matches at border controls, so that we can study false rejection rates. as a result of these studies, and taking into account the four-year review clause, the necessary amendments should be introduced in due course in a codecision procedure, without forgetting the importance of consulting the european data protection supervisor, which was unfortunately not taken into account in the preparation of this proposal.
mr president, mr barrot, ladies and gentlemen, i congratulate carlos coelho, martine roure and the other members on their work. in december 2004, the council adopted the regulation laying down standards for the security features and biometric data of passports and travel documents issued by the member states. now we need to take further steps to combat child abduction and trafficking. the use of passports by children in accordance with the principle of 'one person, one passport' can be a key means of winning this difficult and important battle. in my country, portugal, fingerprinting children from the age of six is a longstanding practice, which is perhaps why i have no objection to it. as a supporter of the european cause, i believe that harmonization is important in this area. i am reassured that the member states which, like portugal, have long since set the minimum age for fingerprinting at six years old will not have to change their national legislation. i should like to stress that the security of passports which is being strengthened today is not limited to the existence of a passport. the passport itself corresponds to an increase in the level of security, ranging from the presentation of the documents necessary for the issuance of passports, followed by the collection of biometric data, to the verification and matching at cross-border control posts. this report is another step towards affirming the rights of the individual and ensuring his safety.
the rapporteur. - mr president, if there is one thing that emerges from the debate, it is that the concern of the vast majority in this house is to combat effectively trafficking in human beings and, in particular, trafficking in children. this is the major utility of this instrument which we are going to approve and i am glad that almost all the speakers have spoken about this. i would like to thank once again all the shadow rapporteurs for their cooperation and i do not wish to return the compliment. it is fair to say that mrs martine roure was crucial in getting the agreement, to which commissioner barrot contributed a great deal and also the french presidency. i would particularly like to thank commissioner barrot for the commission's willingness to provide institutional support to strengthen the fight against the trafficking of children, but also for the commission's willingness to cooperate in the three studies we have requested: the reliability of fingerprints of minors, breeder documents and false rejection rates, which are some of the concerns we have with the implementation of these rules. and finally, mr president, mr brejc has raised a question: he asked if we could say that the issuing of passports is safe. to be completely honest, i have to say that this varies from country to country. some countries have systems which are more stringent than others and that is also why the study of breeder documents is very important. i know that the european institution has no competence in this area, the issue of passports is a national competence, and so i was very pleased when commissioner barrot accepted that the european commission should collaborate in this study on breeder documents. there's no point in having very secure passports if that security is sabotaged in the issuing process. it is not a question of imposing measures on member states, but of sharing best practice, so that we can be sure that the european passport is a secure reality at our external borders. thank you all for your cooperation.
mr president, vice-president wallström, ladies and gentlemen, the principle of transparency is a fundamental principle of the european union: it strengthens the democratic nature of our institutions, facilitates the participation of citizens in the decision-making process and thus ensures greater legitimacy for public administrations by making them more effective and accountable to the citizens. i support the principle that any democratic institution has a duty to ensure that its documents, activities and decisions are made public. access to documents is an essential element in the necessary loyal cooperation between the institutions. during the investigation into the cia's activities in europe, i directly criticised the council for violating this loyal cooperation by restricting access to all or part of key documents. it is for this reason that i also welcomed the court of justice's decision in the turkish case, concluding that there is in principle a duty to disseminate the opinions of the council's legal service on a legislative procedure, which may be of crucial importance in order to understand the direction taken in the decisionmaking process. i agree that the 2001 regulation should be revised not only to address the shortcomings that have been identified in the meantime but also to incorporate the latest case law, in particular the turkish judgment. however, we must of course respect the legal basis that has been given to us. some of the rapporteur's proposals go far beyond what is permitted by the legal basis, as has already been mentioned by mr herrero and mr cashman. there are, for example, rules addressed to national parliaments without any legal basis. the court of justice itself, in the turkish judgment, recognises that the regulation must be applied by balancing the risk associated with disclosure with the general interest in making such a document accessible. in some cases, marco cappato makes proposals which, in my opinion, call that balance into question.
mr president, first of all i would like to welcome rapporteur alexander lambsdorff and the shadow rapporteurs, with particular reference to barbara weiler. i believe that we must make progress towards the integration of national defence markets and even the strategic coordination of their production. we must indeed move forward with the specifics and precautions needed to apply the essential rules of the internal market to the defence industries, by increasing transparency in the tenders that are organised and in the contracts that are signed involving member states of the european union. but at the same time we must help to develop the conditions which will make european equipment and products more competitive on world markets. commissioner mccreevy, i think it is important that this directive should ensure that the legal framework for public procurement in the fields of defence and security is advanced, while making progress in the construction of the internal market and, of course, respecting the interests and rights of the member states, especially in the field of security, and in particular those manufacturers of arms, ammunition and defence and security equipment, such as my own country. mr president, i would also like to stress the provisions which will make it easier for small and mediumsized enterprises to access this market, in particular by improving the rules on subcontracting and the prospect of making further progress with greater impact and depth in the industrial fabric, towards the creation of a genuine european market in defence industries, which could also help to strengthen research and development, not only in defence industries, but in a whole range of european industries which benefit from the development of defence industries. finally, i think it is worth mentioning the obvious link between defence industries and the union's external policies. but i would like to quote, even in connection with a recent intervention, a classic roman saying: 'if you want peace, prepare for war'. in the european union, which is almost a peaceful superpower, we call these industries 'defence industries' and not 'war industries', because we want peace and not war, and therefore we also need defence industries.
commissioner, i appreciate your clarification. i regret that i cannot share your optimism about how these increases in production authorization will benefit the production of the azores. what i can assure the commissioner is that these effects, which are already estimated, according to a review this week, to have been a drop of over 60% in world market prices for milk powder since august 2007, are having a profound effect on farmers' incomes in the autonomous region of the azores and, surely, in many other parts of europe as well, and that these measures, which i hope will have some effect, have not yet had any effect. i would therefore ask the commissioner to pay the utmost attention to this situation.
mr barrot, i agree with you, i agree with you about the dissatisfaction. we must not be satisfied with the vast body of measures. the truth is that irregular immigration continues into europe; human trafficking continues; there are areas where there is a lot of immigration, namely the mediterranean and towards italy; france has made progress with a number of important bilateral cooperation projects, but other member states have not. the commission must continue its policy of cooperation and assistance to the member states with unwavering enthusiasm. (the president cut the speaker off)
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work is a very good one. this convention, adopted in june 2007 at the 96th session of the international labour conference, aims to establish minimum international labour standards for the fisheries sector, promoting decent living and working conditions for fishermen, and covers, among others, related conditions on such important issues as health and safety at work, the number of actual workers and hours of rest, the crew list, repatriation in case of problems, recruitment, the placement of workers and social security. the adoption of this ilo convention on work in the fisheries sector has long been anticipated by fishermen, who consider it to be a step forward in the dignification of work in this important strategic sector, which employs some 30 million men and women worldwide. it was in 2003 that the ilo management and its tripartite members began work on the development of comprehensive and up-to-date international labour standards for the fisheries sector, with the aim of providing fishermen with adequate protection on a global scale due to the particular nature of the sector and the specific living and working conditions, which imply the need for special protection, also taking into account that this is a sector where there are many fatal accidents to which serious exploitation and unprotected situations contribute. the convention also revises other existing conventions in the sector, in particular on the minimum age, the medical examination of fishermen, their employment contract and the accommodation of crew, and is accompanied by a recommendation (no 199). it is noted that this convention became necessary following the adoption of the ilo consolidated convention on maritime labour in february 2006, which excluded the fisheries sector from its scope. we therefore call for every effort to be made to ensure that the various member states proceed to its rapid ratification, preferably before 2012, as the convention will take effect after it has been ratified by 10 of the 180 member states of the ilo, of which 8 are expected to be coastal countries. but it is equally important that they apply its provisions urgently where they do not already exist. finally, i would like to thank all those who have contributed to the preparation of this report, including my colleague mr willy meyer, rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on fisheries, and i hope that all members will encourage their national parliaments and governments to ratify this convention as soon as possible.
i too would like to join the voice of the rapporteur, the commissioner and all our colleagues who have rejoiced at this initiative by the international labour organisation and who call on all member states to ratify convention no 188. the point is that deregulation of fisheries, the unregulated market without borders, principles and limits, is not only a victim of the sustainability of the resource fish but also of the fishermen themselves. and the protection of fishermen must become an essential part, if not the very heart of the common fisheries policy. this is my fundamental appeal: that we do not stop at ratifying this international convention, but that we put the protection of fishermen at the heart of the common fisheries policy.
the rapporteur. - i thank the president and the commissioner and all the members who spoke and who supported the ratification of this convention. the report will certainly be adopted by the european parliament tomorrow, thus making the necessary contribution to the rapid ratification by the member states of convention 188 and its accompanying recommendation 199 on work in the fisheries sector. as i have said, our aim is that it should come into force soon, preferably before 2012, and we therefore call for its ratification by all member states. it will make an important contribution to the establishment of minimum international standards at world level, which will guarantee better working conditions, greater safety and fewer fatal accidents in a sector which is very dangerous but also strategic. it will also help to recognise the dignity of fishermen, whose work is so hard and who should be at the centre of our concerns.
mr president-in-office, you spoke of a europe with rules. you've talked a lot about rules, by the way. one of the rules of democracy is to keep your promises. you had committed yourself to ratifying the lisbon treaty by the end of 2008. you didn't, and you did wrong. so i ask: when will the czech republic ratify the treaty of lisbon? you also spoke of self-confidence. it sounded arrogant to me. the czech government, including its president, has every right to say and do what it wants, but it cannot forget that it belongs to the european union and that it now speaks for some 500 million citizens and 27 member states. it cannot therefore ignore the fact that 25 member states have already ratified the treaty of lisbon and that ireland has already called a second referendum. and fortunately, the polls are a yes majority. the irish have realised that in this particularly difficult international environment, being part of the european union and the eurozone has protected them from greater evils. the czech republic has yet to say when it will ratify the treaty of lisbon. as my leader, martin schulz, has said, at a time when europe is facing unprecedented challenges, it is necessary for europe to speak with one voice. that will only be possible with the treaty of lisbon.
in writing. the czech presidency has put forward three priorities: the economy, energy and external relations. the presentation of the programme of the czech presidency thus followed the lines already known, but it had some particularities, particularly as regards the ratification of the draft treaty of lisbon. the czech prime minister stressed the need to respect the sovereignty of the irish people. he also said that if a referendum had been held in the czech republic, the result might have been the same as in ireland, with the draft treaty rejected. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competition' in the context of the directive on the protection of consumers. as regards socioeconomic issues, nothing different has been achieved, which means that the neoliberal positions, the proposals rejected by the european parliament on the working time directive, the stability pact and the neoliberal lisbon strategy have been maintained, while the economic crisis and its serious social consequences have been ignored. we regret that the commission has not intervened in the war crimes that israel continues to commit against the palestinians.
in writing. − this regulation aims to amend the standards for biometric security devices in passports and travel documents issued by the member states. a regulation we have disagreed with since its inception in 2004 for introducing the harmonisation of security devices and the integration of biometric identifiers as part of the security policies promoted at european union level. the main purpose of this amendment is to introduce exemptions for children under 12 years of age a derogation for 4 years, which countries with legislation setting a lower age may maintain provided they respect the minimum limit of 6 years (cases of portugal, france and estonia) , in addition to other aspects relating to the protection and security of biometric data. although the proposal lays down exemption rules for children under 12 (a decision based on purely technical issues), we consider that it does not call into question the substantive issue, i.e. the use of biometric data, in particular of children, and its harmonisation at eu level (especially since the issuing of passports is a competence of each member state), within the framework of its security policy. that is why we abstained.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the carl schlyter report on the restriction of the placing on the market and use of certain dangerous substances and preparations (dichloromethane). i believe that this proposal to amend directive 76/769/eec will contribute to an effective reduction of the risk of environmental and human exposure to substances with dangerous properties such as dichloromethane (dcm), which has a unique profile of adverse effects on human health. the protection of human health must take precedence over industrial interests.
in writing. - we voted in favour of the compromise text, as this measure can have a positive impact on workers in various industries, including automotive and shipbuilding, and minimises their exposure to highly harmful toxic agents. it is dichloromethane (dcm) which is a colourless chemical compound with a sweet, pleasant and penetrating odour similar to that of ether. its main marketing activities are the production of pharmaceuticals, solvents and auxiliary products, paint strippers and adhesives. dcm has a unique profile of negative effects on human health and is included in the list of 33 priority substances established under the water directive. this substance is classified as a category 3 carcinogen. it has a narcotic effect and, at high exposure levels, causes central nervous system depression, loss of consciousness and cardiotoxic effects, with a direct risk of death from inappropriate use. according to the scientific committee on health and environmental risks, one of the main concerns related to the toxicity of dcm is the risk to particularly vulnerable populations. several alternatives to dcm-based picklers are already available on the market.
in writing. - i agree fundamentally with the aim of the proposal: to reduce the risks of using dichloromethane (dcm) by the general public and professionals. we know that dcm has a unique profile of adverse effects on human health: is a carcinogen, has a narcotic effect and, at high exposure levels, causes central nervous system depression, loss of consciousness and cardiotoxic effects, with a direct risk of death from inappropriate use. according to the commission, 18 deaths due to the use of mcas were recorded in the eu between 1989 and 2007. i think it is imperative to introduce european measures to ban or replace this substance. following the vote in the envi committee, i support a ban on dcm for general users, but allowing its use by professionals, under safety conditions.
in writing. - the vote today on this report, which i tabled on behalf of the committee on employment and social affairs, on the ilo fisheries labour convention 2007, is an important contribution towards minimum international standards at world level, which guarantee better working conditions, more safety and fewer fatal accidents in a sector which is very dangerous but which is also strategic, putting the defence of the dignity and the hard working life of fishermen at the centre of our concerns, knowing that this is where the highest percentage of fatal accidents occur. the report was approved by 671 votes to 16. convention no 188 will take effect after it has been ratified by 10 of the 180 member states of the ilo, of which 8 are expected to be coastal countries. it is recalled that the convention revises the conventions on the minimum age of fishermen, on medical examination, on the employment contract of fishermen and on the accommodation of crew, also covering issues such as health and safety at work, recruitment, placement and social security.
in writing. − the union is pursuing, through the common fisheries policy, the effectiveness of fishing activities so that the sector, including aquaculture, is economically viable and competitive, ensures a fair standard of living for the populations that depend on fishing activities and meets the interests of consumers. the ilo convention 188, adopted in june 2007, is a document that aims to allow fair competition between fishing vessel owners and to give workers in the sector dignity at work. the convention pursues these objectives by establishing a set of international minimum standards for the fisheries sector which, in certain areas, are the exclusive competence of the community. it therefore seems necessary to propose that the member states ratify this convention in the interests of the community and the coherence of the common fisheries policy itself.
in writing. − for several years the council has submitted an annual report to the european parliament on the situation of fundamental rights in the union, to which parliament has responded by drawing up an own-initiative report. this report was drawn up for the first time on the basis of the findings of the fundamental rights agency and is of particular importance given that no report on this subject has been adopted since 2003. i think that the rapporteur has developed too much on certain matters which have already been discussed in his own reports, and there are a number of points which are very controversial and on some of which i voted against, as they are clearly against my principles. however, there are many other points with which i fully agree and for this reason i voted against, not only because i believe that the text has been substantially improved by the excellent work done by my colleague, mrs kinga gál, but also because i cannot in good conscience vote against a report in defence of fundamental rights.
in writing. − to think that fundamental rights are fully guaranteed and protected in the european union and the member states would be an error of analysis and a detriment to political action. fundamental rights, even in free and democratic societies, lack protection and promotion. however, there are separate questions about this report and this debate. first, the monitoring of the protection of these rights at national level. in societies such as ours, this function is exercised primarily within the national framework. of course, without prejudice to the possibilities of appeal at european level which we have. between the institutions' duty to guarantee the treaties and national sovereignty, there is room for each party to exercise its function without prejudice to the institutional nature of the eu. on the other hand, in this particular report there is an undesirable confusion between what are fundamental rights and what are ideological choices about the organization of society. such a debate is interesting. but the attempt to impose these positions on the member states, against their democratically formed will and against the principle of subsidiarity, proves precisely the risks of communitizing what is national. that is why i voted against this report, because i disagree with much of its content.
in writing. - if i were in favour of the no campaign in the irish referendum, i would applaud the giusto catania report and its supporters enthusiastically. it is such a gross disregard for the principle of subsidiarity and such a violation of the institutional rules of the european union and the powers of the member states that it justifies all those who are suspicious of the political voracity of brussels. to attack safeguard clauses, which are a direct expression of the treaties and a fundamental guarantee of the democracy of states, as a codification of discriminatory practices is pathetic, grossly offensive to the fundamental rights of citizens. to argue that the signing of international conventions by a majority of member states imposes an obligation on the whole eu to be bound by them is a complete legal absurdity, a plunge into darkness that goes beyond the most extreme federalism. i also reject the absolutely perverse assertion that europe lacks credibility, which is designed to create a tactical inferiority for us: the european union is not the sudan, the people's republic of china, cuba, somalia or north korea. finally, the report is spreading itself over areas of political struggle which have nothing to do with fundamental rights, and it is also lacking in credibility, consistency and effectiveness. i voted against it.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this report on the proposal for a council directive implementing the agreement concluded by the european community shipowners' association and the european transport workers' federation on the maritime labour convention, 2006, and amending directive 1999/63/ec, as it consolidates international minimum labour rights, which is important to ensure better working conditions and greater safety, while respecting the dignity of these professionals. but we regret that the proposals put forward by our group, in particular those aimed at removing any legal uncertainty and undermining the work done by the social partners to reach agreement, were not adopted. the convention itself admits that countries do not use flexibility, and this is what the proposal for a directive has accepted, with the agreement of the social partners, so we disagree that the majority of the european parliament has included the question of flexibility in paragraph 6.

in writing. - if the united nations and the various agencies and organisations associated with it are by their nature to be a mirror of the world, it is natural that the image thus reflected is not the one we would wish or would like to construct. these considerations are necessary in the context of the debate on the united nations human rights council. debating and voting on human rights in the world with countries that do not pay any respect to them will, as expected, have a bizarre outcome. it is not just a question of legitimacy. it is first and foremost a question of language. what human rights standards can we expect the governments of libya or zimbabwe to share with democratic states and to be accountable to their people? none, that's for sure. and yet, it is precisely for the dialogue between those who speak different languages that diplomacy is useful. between equals, mediation is not necessary. it follows that the existence of dialogue places should be a policy to be encouraged and promoted. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.

the question of transport and animal welfare is one which, as our colleague neil parish and others have very well said, must be seen essentially from a civilizational perspective. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to improve the quality of the environment and to improve the quality of life of the animals. but it should also be borne in mind that the price to be paid for the application of the strict and costly rules in force is the distortion of competition which has a strong impact on rural development in some regions of the european union. the regions and member states which do not have the capacity to supply their markets with certain species and which are further away from the centres of production, as is the case in my own country, now have greater difficulties in being competitive in their slaughter and processing industries, while the surplus regions and member states have gained added advantages because it has become easier for them to sell the products already processed, with the inherent advantages in terms of employment and added value. at the end of two years of application of this legislation, it is fully justified for the commission to provide as extensive an assessment as possible, not only of the concrete issues of strict application of the transport regulation but also of the economic and social consequences for regions and member states with deficits in the production of certain animal species relevant for human food. i therefore think that the commission should respond to these questions as quickly, objectively and broadly as possible.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, initiatives to promote and safeguard the cultures of peoples, particularly in the field of food, such as the application of the mediterranean diet as an intangible cultural heritage of humanity, which will be considered by the responsible unesco committee in 2009, should be welcomed. the achievement of this objective can help to preserve, promote and make known a diet which is predominant in the mediterranean countries and which is considered healthy by a number of nutritionists and other experts. as has already been said, this diet is based on the traditional products of the mediterranean region, such as fruit and vegetables, olive oil, fish, cereals, dried fruit, aromatic herbs, dairy products, sheepmeat and goatmeat, and wine. however, there has been a decline in the population maintaining these healthy eating habits. we therefore believe that, among other consequential and necessary political initiatives, effective measures should be taken to support traditional mediterranean production, particularly in the framework of the common agricultural policy and the common fisheries policy. measures for small and family farmers and fishermen in these regions, which guarantee their income. measures to promote traditional and artisanal products and to encourage the development of local markets. in the end, measures to counter the increasing abandonment of agricultural activity and the decline in the population that is responsible for and safeguards the production of these traditional quality products by means of its knowledge. all issues and measures within the european commission.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is not easy for the same subject to bring together, at the same time and in a positive way, three aspects as important as health, culture and the economy. the mediterranean diet can do that. from the health point of view, there seems to be no scientific doubt about the benefits to human health of a diet based on fresh and natural foods such as already mentioned here cereals, rice, legumes, nuts, fruit and vegetables, frequent consumption of fish, olive oil as the main dietary fat and moderate consumption of wine. from a cultural point of view, the production systems and the processing and consumption of the products mentioned are associated with ancestral techniques and traditions that go beyond greco-roman culture and that, in many respects, continue to be present in the uses, customs and techniques of the communities of the mediterranean basin. from an economic point of view, activities linked in particular to agriculture, fisheries and rural tourism, which are so important for the occupation of the territory and the vitality of rural and coastal areas, continue to have a very considerable socioeconomic impact, while at the same time being, and interestingly, in the case of agriculture, the most competitive productions in the european union and those which have always benefited from the least financial support under the common agricultural policy. in view of all that i have just said, the initiative of the spanish authorities to have the mediterranean diet recognised by unesco as a cultural heritage of humanity deserves to be welcomed, and i think it is the duty and obligation of the european union to do everything it can to influence this decision, because this is not in the specific interest of one country or group of countries, it is in the interest of the whole union.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the madurell report on the transposition and application of directive 2002/73/ec on the implementation of the principle of equal treatment for men and women as regards access to employment, vocational training and promotion, and working conditions, as i considered it important to apply the principle of equal treatment for men and women in the field covered by this directive. among the many shortcomings in the transposition of the directive, i would highlight the fact that the legislation of several member states does not specifically refer to gender discrimination. as the rapporteur points out, the pay gap remains high, with women earning on average 15% less than men, and between 2000 and 2006 the gap narrowed by only 1%. in the context of the lisbon strategy, it is essential that this status quo should change, and i agree with the rapporteur on the importance of recommending that the european commission actively monitor the transposition of the directive and the compliance of national legislation with it.
