the number of workers affected by unemployment in portugal is growing exponentially. every day, companies announce production cuts, layoffs or even complete closures. the most serious cases include the threat to some 2,000 workers at qimonda in vila do conde following the declaration of insolvency of the parent company in germany. on wednesday a delegation of workers from both countries will be here in parliament, hoping for solidarity and also our support in the fight they are waging to defend their jobs. but the scandal of late payment of wages and other remuneration due to workers is also increasing, as is the case in cork companies, in some textiles, ceramics and metalworking, among others. this situation is creating serious social problems, increasing poverty and even reaching dramatic levels when it comes to members of the same family working in the company. as i was able to verify just a few days ago, at subercor, a company in santa maria da feira (a suberus, in the shoe area), where workers are struggling because they have not received wages and there are couples who are hungry, who no longer have food for their children. these are dramatic situations of crisis which are plaguing us, but which have consequences on the human face and on people's lives, and therefore not only solidarity but also the call to resolve these serious problems.
in this context of very serious financial and economic crisis it is absolutely essential to move towards the completion of the doha negotiations. in a time of crisis, there is always a tendency to give in to the temptation to be protectionist. protectionism is indeed a kind of neurosis which marks societies and states a little at a time when they are confronted with serious crises, such as the one we are experiencing. we must therefore clearly combat the possibility of the emergence of this protectionist temptation, because we know historically where this leads. it leads to the general impoverishment of the world community and does nothing to solve the serious problems we face. but protectionism, which is totally objectionable and must be combated, is one thing, and the need to ensure the protection of the legitimate interests of the various areas of the world in which we are divided is quite another. the european union also has an obligation to defend the interests of europeans. the interests of europeans as consumers, but also the interests of europeans as producers. that is why it is important to move forward with the multilateral negotiations in the doha framework. if we know that protectionism is indeed a mistake, we also know that the wild liberalization of international trade inevitably leads to very serious disasters from the economic and social point of view. the only way to avoid this unbridled liberalization is to establish an agreement at the headquarters, the world trade organization, a multilateral agreement which establishes rules to safeguard the legitimate interests of all parties involved. the role of the european commission and the role of the european union is precisely this, to restore confidence to europeans here too. there is also a crisis of confidence in europe today in the defence capacity and political control of those who represent it. whether at the level of the member states, the european commission or the european union as a whole. so the challenge we have is precisely this: to help end this crisis of representation and trust by ensuring that we move forward in the best possible way. and the best way forward in this case is to secure a multilateral agreement that safeguards all our legitimate interests.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, in the continuation of other initiatives we have proposed to the committee on international trade of the european parliament that an oral question with debate in plenary be prepared on production and employment in the textile and clothing sector in different states of the european union because we consider it urgent and essential. we also proposed that this debate should include the council and be concluded by a resolution of this parliament, but these proposals were not supported by other parliamentary groups. it has been more than a year since the debate in this parliament on 12 december 2007. as we pointed out then, unless measures are taken to protect production and employment in textiles and clothing, we will continue to be faced with the slow agony and destruction of much of this strategic sector. since then, as has been the case up to now, thousands of jobs have been destroyed and many businesses have closed down, with 350,000 jobs and 5% of businesses reported to have disappeared in the last two years alone. this is what the european commission claims to be competing through restructuring, we ask. since then, and as has happened hitherto, workers have continued to be confronted with unemployment, often with the absence of payment of compensation due or with wages not paid, with the intensification of exploitation, with more precariousness, with wages in arrears, with the deregulation of working hours. such a reality has its own causes and responsibilities. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the textile and clothing industry is not left to the mercy of the market. in the face of this reality, the european union has either been "ears" or has moved forward with mitigating measures that are far from responding to the problems and needs of the sector. for the european commission, unlike other sectors, textiles and clothing are not considered special as it claims. the urgent measures that must be implemented at the level of each member state must also be addressed at the level of the european union in order to deal with the serious problems facing the sector. commissioner, when will binding rules on the affixing of origin labels be implemented, for example by adopting the regulation on the indication 'manufacture in ...'? when will the same safety and consumer protection requirements be applied to imported products as are required for products produced in the european union? how will the european union continue to monitor in real time the development of imports and customs supervision and control, keeping the sector fully informed by claiming safeguard clauses where necessary? how will you use the 2007-13 financial framework, including the so-called globalisation fund, to defend production and employment in textiles and clothing, in particular of small and medium-sized enterprises affected by liberalisation? when will we have a monetary and exchange rate policy which does not penalise the exports of some member states? when will the community programme be established, as proposed by this parliament, and when will the financial resources be released for the modernisation and promotion of the sector and the diversification of industrial activity, particularly for the less-favoured regions which depend on it?
madam president, commissioner, this is a very specific case in which the distinction we made a little while ago in the previous debate between protection and protectionism is well applied. the european parliament has voted against protectionism which impoverishes, but protection is absolutely necessary to safeguard the fundamental rights of europeans. this is a very important sector in several regions, in several european countries, such as the region where i come from, the region of northern portugal. it is of crucial importance to the regional economy. it has been a sector particularly exposed to the process of globalisation. and in the context of a serious financial crisis, such as the one we are experiencing, this sector is living through absolutely tragic times. the european union and the member states must pay more attention to the textile industry, opting for defensive measures and offensive measures. the defence measures require the use of all the trade defence mechanisms and instruments at our disposal. the commission has also been asked to take a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are not undermined by the failure of the member states to implement the common agricultural policy. to defend european producers, entrepreneurs and workers is to defend european citizens and it is also to defend european consumers. that is what we must be aware of once and for all. at the same time, we must use these defensive measures in accordance with principles as simple as those already set out here: the principle of reciprocity and the principle of the permanent fight against unfair competition. we are not asking for the european union, and for the most affected regions of the european union, to be given any special treatment. we are simply asking for rules and for those rules to be based on the fundamental principle of reciprocity. but while the european union and its member states must strive to ensure that the instruments of trade defence which are most appropriate at any given moment are applied without any hesitation, we must also develop policies and offensive measures, as has already been done, to modernise the sector, which is also happening in terms of the development of human resources, the emphasis on vocational training, technological modernisation and the development of territories. there are territories today, and i know one of them well, which is, as i said a moment ago, the northern region of portugal, which are faced with truly tragic situations and must be faced with total frankness.
madam president, commissioner, i will try to put the matter very succinctly. firstly, the question of the european union's special monitoring mechanism for textiles, which, as has been said, expired on 31 december 2008. we need timely and regular publication of statistics on imports, exports and prices, as is the case in the united states. without such data, the european union is unable to react against possible unfair practices and the commission itself is unable to define its strategy. i would ask you to pay attention to that, as other colleagues have done. secondly, production within the european union increasingly requires compliance with safety standards, social standards and environmental standards. reach, for example, is a recent initiative that puts even more constraints on us. it is important that the commission has a clear, discernible and traceable strategy so that imported products have identical requirements. how are these aspects being properly addressed in the free trade agreements currently in progress? and as for 'made in', can 'made in' actually help solve this issue? thirdly, the european union has launched an economic recovery plan to combat the current crisis. what role will trade policy play in this context? what is the commission preparing to do at this moment when a number of other countries, such as china, are beginning to proliferate and increasingly introduce non-tariff barriers to prevent our european imports from accessing chinese markets? what is your proposal for updating or adjusting the globalisation fund and the support available under the structural funds to improve the immediate situation of the european textile industry? the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval, and the council will then be able to take a decision on the matter. how can the commissioner make her colleagues and the bodies dealing with european monetary policy aware of the need to rebalance... (the president cut off the speaker)
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, developing a coherent and comprehensive community energy policy is a logical step and a necessary type of intervention. the commission proposal and mrs laperrouze's report are in that direction. the european union will bring added value to the initiatives developed by the member states. oil is finite and the european union and norway's 2007 exploration contributes only 30% to domestic demand. the european union's dependence on oil imports increases indirectly our dependence on countries which are politically unstable or with which any energy partnerships create major geostrategic tensions, as we have recently seen with russia. for these reasons, it is important to reorient the search for alternative energy sources to oil in a strategic way, but also to look geographically with greater commitment at the south american and african markets, which are currently expanding and to which the development of the eu-brazil and eu-africa partnerships can contribute greatly by using the iberian area, spain and portugal where i come from, as a key platform for logistics and distribution in the european area. as regards the fundamental need to enhance energy efficiency, it is important to ensure appropriate synergies between sectors that can contribute to greater energy efficiency. only by adopting a comprehensive and coordinated approach between community and national policies, particularly in the fields of cohesion, agriculture and transport, will we be able to devise a strategy with a long-term vision. the link between energy and territorial cohesion is undeniable as regards possible long-term solutions for all regions of the european union, including the most isolated and outermost regions.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, the general and business aviation sector has not been specifically addressed at european level until now. the political significance of this report is therefore due to the fact that it is the first time that the commission and parliament have dealt with this activity, which is growing rapidly both in volume and in economic importance. the numbers speak for themselves: in business aviation alone, the number of small and medium-sized enterprises engaged in this activity is increasing, the number of aircraft is expected to double to 3,500 in the next decade and the annual turnover amounts to more than eur 25 billion. directly or indirectly, it generates 154,000 jobs at european level. together with general aviation, where there are an estimated 30,000 to 50,000 aircraft, the sector accounts for about 9% of all recorded air traffic and is the fastest growing in europe, twice as fast as the rest of traffic. the business aviation segment provides significant social and economic benefits: by providing flexible, point-to-point transport, it increases the mobility of citizens, the productivity of businesses and regional cohesion. general aviation provides essential services in a wide range of areas, from search and rescue, firefighting, traffic control, mapping and recreational and sport aviation. it is also an important source of professional skills for the entire aviation sector. i would also like to say a word to the european industry in this sector, which has been showing a steady growth in its share of the world market, which is currently around 16%, and which therefore needs to be supported. as regards the report, we, as rapporteur, have followed the commission's concern in its communication to address the specific features of the sector and identify its main themes. the first concerns the collection of data and is based on the need to provide policy-makers with sufficient data and statistical information to enable them to know the sector better and thus be able to regulate it properly. the second point concerns the application of the principle of proportionality in its regulation. the central question is whether the rules designed to govern the operation of commercial aircraft will be suitable for the operation of simpler and smaller aircraft, often single-engine. we support the commission's intention to apply the principle of proportionality both in the drafting of standards and in their implementation, always bearing in mind the need not to compromise safety in general. this is the case, for example, with the adaptation of certain airworthiness rules already adopted by the asa to non-commercial aircraft operations, or the possible application of specific rules with regard to simplified security and passenger screening procedures in business aviation. the third point concerns the problem of access to airports and airspace. the diagnosis is made: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's air transport system is not affected by the crisis. the solutions range from optimising the use of existing capacity to possibly revising the slot regulations. it is also necessary to stimulate investment in small and medium-sized airports by promoting increasing interconnection between the various european regions and cities. on the issue of airspace capacity, the importance of the single european sky and cesar reforms is underlined and the need to avoid imposing disproportionate requirements on small aircraft in terms of air navigation equipment, while respecting safety limits, is reiterated. the fourth and final point relates to the environmental sustainability of this sector of activity. although co2 emissions from small aircraft are lower, there is still a need to promote research, development and innovation not only in the context of the clean sky and cesar initiatives, but also in the use of less polluting engines and cleaner fuels. i would like to conclude, mr president, commissioner, by expressing the hope that this report will provide a real framework for future legislative and regulatory interventions in this sector. this is the intention of the committee on transport when it asks the commission to give its opinion again by the end of 2009 on the progress made on the issues identified in the report. with tomorrow's vote, i expect that will be extended to a large majority of members of this house.
mr president, mr vice-president barrot, ladies and gentlemen, the 2003 framework decision was intended to approximate the laws of the member states in the fight against the sexual exploitation of children and child pornography. to this end, it has adopted a common framework of provisions to regulate criminalisation, applicable sanctions, protection and assistance to victims, among other aspects. i regret that not all member states have taken the necessary measures to implement this framework decision, and the deadline for doing so has already passed. it is essential that all member states criminalise all forms of sexual abuse of children, that all european citizens who commit sexual offences against children, inside or outside the european union, are covered by a criminal law of an extraterritorial nature, common to the whole union. it is important to ensure that the perpetrators of such crimes cannot escape the clutches of justice. i also support the revision of the framework decision to ensure at least the same level of protection as the 2007 council of europe convention. it is regrettable that not all member states have signed this convention. it is also important to strengthen this framework decision in order to respond to the latest developments in the field of communication technologies. the internet is increasingly being used by children and has become one of the preferred tools of actual or potential offenders, in particular through the solicitation and fraudulent solicitation of children for illicit purposes, grooming, which has already been mentioned by my colleague, mr angelilli, whom i would like to congratulate on the excellent work he has done and on the report he has presented to us. i am not unaware of the complexity and difficulty of this fight, but i believe that it is possible to tackle the sexual exploitation of children with integrated actions and a joint effort where the emphasis is on prevention, by promoting campaigns to raise awareness among parents and children of the risks of child pornography, particularly on the internet and in particular the risk of sexual exploitation in chat rooms and cyber forums.
madam president, mr vice-president, ladies and gentlemen, we have been adopting in this house a number of initiatives to create a coherent and integrated policy on immigration. this common policy should, on the one hand, involve the opening up of legal channels for immigration and the integration of these immigrants into the host societies, and to this end we adopted two months ago the directive on the blue card and the directive on the single procedure for granting a single residence and work permit. at the same time, we must effectively combat illegal immigration and all forms of crime associated with it. this initiative seeks to combat the factors that attract illegal immigration to the european area and to put an end to situations of exploitation of illegal workers. it is important that those who are trying to enter the european area at any cost, sometimes at the cost of their own lives, understand that there is only one possible way, and that is legal immigration, with all the rights and possibilities that go with it. it is estimated that there are between 5 and 8 million irregular migrants in the european union: many of them are in low-skilled, very low-paid work and sometimes in situations of deep exploitation. i congratulate the rapporteur, mr claudio fava, and especially my colleague, mrs edit bauer, on the work they have done and the commitment they have shown. it's the mark of the quality, rigorous work that we've both grown accustomed to. i therefore support the fight against illegal work throughout the european union. the directive aims to ensure that all member states can introduce similar sanctions for employers of illegal immigrants and enforce them effectively. three types of sanctions may be imposed: financial, administrative and criminal, depending on the seriousness of the situation. it also requires employers to take preventive measures and carry out checks to verify the regularity of the situation of such individuals, in order to prevent them from employing workers who are in an irregular situation in the union.
the fava report is a moment of progress and humanization in immigration law. he leaves us with a moral comfort we owed ourselves since the return directive. the general ban on the employment of illegal immigrants is not only preventing a state of endemic illegality in immigration matters. it is above all to prevent the potential for exploitation and exploitation of the human misery which is generally linked to this kind of employment. the first key point of the fava report is that it rejects the prospect of illegal immigration based on an easy but unacceptable condemnation of the immigrant and opposes a systemic response that makes the state and the employer jointly responsible. because to date, the biggest failure of immigration policies has been the absence of a just response to the dramatic plight of the irregular immigrant. the criminal vertigo that falls on the immigrant, his status as a culprit instead of a victim. the second key point of the report is that it brings to the european public sphere an ethic of shared responsibility between the state and business. the employer's duty of prior supervision, of controlling the worker's residence, has the value of a competence attributed to private individuals which european republicanism has experienced very little. we welcome this competence because the defence of legality and public ethics is not the responsibility of the state alone, but of everyone. the report therefore falls within the bounds of a new political method which other reports will have to follow. the third and perhaps most fundamental point is this extraordinary abstraction which separates the obligation to pay wages from the problem of the legality of residence. it is a simple dictum of universal morality that says that humanity exists before the rules of the legal system and is above them. congratulations on that, mr fava.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the gyula hegyi report because i believe that the protection and promotion of wilderness areas in europe must be improved. taking into account the environmental pressures of human activities over the centuries, wilderness areas now represent only 46% of the earth's surface. i believe that it is up to the european commission to make recommendations to the member states, which should include the drawing up of a map and a strategy for europe's wilderness areas.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this report, bearing in mind that we must protect nature, but through human use. forests now cover 33% of the territory of the countries in the eea region, or 185 million hectares. only about 9 million hectares of forest (5% of the total forest area) are considered selvagens. these areas, together with the native plant and animal communities and ecosystems of which they form part, are in a state of essentially naturalness. these wilderness areas must enjoy effective and specific protection conditions as genetic reserves and as a refuge for many species which cannot survive even in slightly altered conditions, in particular large mammals such as the brown bear, the wolf or the lynx. we have an obligation to ensure that future generations can enjoy and benefit from wilderness areas in europe, but the development of sustainable tourism can be used as a means to add economic value to wilderness areas and to foster conservation support, encouraging the ordinary citizen to discover the hidden values of nature without damaging it, and increasing acceptance of conservation policy, as citizens understand the need for protection through their personal experience, as they help to maintain wilderness areas economically, which can generate employment opportunities for the people in the area.
in writing. − europe's wilderness areas are now reduced to small fractions of what they were in the past and therefore it is a priority to protect them. they should therefore be central to european biodiversity policy and the natura 2000 network should take these spaces into account in order to make the best use of their ecosystem services. i therefore approve the hegyi report, hoping that europe's wilderness areas will be better preserved for the benefit of future generations.
in writing. - long gone are the days when human history was a story of survival in the face of nature's adversity. in our part of the world, while we still have to protect ourselves from nature and its aggressions, it has also become necessary to protect nature from human presence and domination. and we must do it for ourselves, for our interest in preserving the richness of biodiversity, for the need to preserve the planet on which we have been offered to live. it is on these premises that we must look at efforts to preserve wildlife in europe and, in particular, in the outermost regions where such diversity is so important. the same assumptions call for balance and balance in intervention and regulation. if we want to stimulate a new use of rural space, we cannot make the human activities taking place there unbearably difficult. the protection of wild nature, especially where it coexists with human activities, must mean the promotion of balance, conservation and sustainability. we cannot impose an unbearable burden on rural life or the human desertification of an already impoverished territory.
in writing. - we voted against this report because we disagree with many of the proposals which are advocated here, always based on competition and the liberalisation of markets in a strategic area where public policies and public ownership of the main means of energy production were essential. we have nevertheless voted in favour of several proposals. for example, we are also concerned about the safety of fossil fuels such as oil and gas, and the rapporteur's statement that world production will be unlikely to exceed 100 million barrels a day (currently 87) when the needs are estimated at 120 million barrels a day in 2030, and the risk of a deep crisis over the next decade. we also agree that there should be more research in the field of energy, particularly in the field of nuclear waste transmutation and nuclear fusion. however, we disagree with the attempt to put the economic groups of the european union in a position of strength vis-à-vis public undertakings in third countries and the apology it uses to make the lisbon treaty and the call for its ratification.
in writing. - the context in which a strategy is considered, which is desired in the long term, has a decisive influence on the outcome of the analysis and the content of the proposals. the debate on the strategic analysis of energy policy is not without its mark. however, there are repeated indicators in this context which make it more structural than transitory. energy dependence (whether from russia or from the major oil-producing countries) and its consequences; the rising cost of energy, whether due to the increase in its price due to the global increase in demand, or due to the decrease in the purchasing power of states impoverished by a severe economic crisis; the environmental consequences, at various levels, of a steady increase in global energy consumption, which the economic crisis should not reverse, all these factors converge on the need for a strategic approach based on less dependence and therefore greater diversity of both suppliers and energy consumed, greater efficiency, sustained development of research into alternative energies, greater integration and, at the same time, development of local production capacities, namely through alternative energies. it is a huge challenge, but it is a strategic issue that we cannot ignore.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this alternative position put forward by the green group, even though we do not entirely agree with it on one or other point, because it improves the rapporteur's proposal. this is an area where public policies are fundamental and intrinsically linked to the achievement of equality between women and men. the existence of public services and the guarantee of access to quality services for all, regardless of their economic status and gender, without any discrimination, is essential, which presupposes free or semi-free national public health services and free quality public education for all. it is also essential to create and maintain quality and affordable public facilities with opening hours that meet the needs of parents and children, as well as good quality and affordable care facilities for the elderly and dependants. all this is essential to guarantee better living conditions for the general population and to facilitate women's access to the labour market and to paid employment, so as to enable them to become economically independent, which is a basic issue for the emancipation of women.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the roberta angelilli report on the fight against the sexual exploitation of children and child pornography, as i consider it essential to update the means of combating all forms of exploitation of children in order to ensure a high level of protection for minors in the european union. i therefore support the recommendations in this report, in particular the proposal to criminalise all sexual offences against children in all member states, to increase the surveillance and control of new forms of solicitation of minors, in particular via the internet, and to set up a missing children alert system to promote better cooperation at european level.
in writing. - as mentioned in the present ep recommendation to the council, the council of europe convention on the protection of children against sexual exploitation and sexual abuse (ec convention), which has already been signed by 20 eu countries, is the first international legal instrument to criminalise the different forms of sexual abuse of children, including abuse committed, inter alia, by force, coercion or threat, even within the family. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. the report also calls for the eu to take action to ensure that the rights of children are respected and that the eu's legal framework is strengthened. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of children and young people, and to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of children and young people in the context of the european union.
mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, madam vice-president of the commission, ladies and gentlemen, this parliament has repeatedly called for the closure of the guantanamo prison. we were joined by the other two institutions: the commission and the council. now that the decision has been made, there can be no misunderstanding of our position. we warmly congratulate president obama on this decision, a decision which he justified by the need to restore to the united states of america the moral authority it enjoyed before the world. let us be clear: the bush administration's decision to open guantanamo as well as its extraordinary rendition program have shaken that moral authority of the united states. the ends do not justify the means. it is unacceptable that international law is violated, that torture is used, that secret prisons are opened and that prisoners are disappeared. in a state governed by the rule of law, prisoners are brought to justice and have the right to defend themselves. it was wrong for the bush administration to commit these abuses, as it was wrong for those who, by conniving applause or shameful silence, were complicit. if the united states of america asks the european union for help, we should not refuse it, as commissioner barrot has said. not by sacrificing the safety of our citizens, but by not using it as an excuse not to cooperate. we also need the cooperation of the united states to help shed light on the atrocities that have been committed in europe, to hold accountable and to ensure that the same crimes are not repeated in the future. how should we ask the member states and the european institutions what they have done to implement the recommendations that this parliament adopted in february 2007 and what explanation they have for not doing so, if any. and since my friend claudio fava has quoted president barroso here, i think he deserves a word of thanks because, unlike the council, which resisted, lied and withheld information from this parliament, the european commission has behaved in a loyal manner and has given full cooperation to our investigations.
mr president, portugal urged an eu agreement on resettling persons from guantánamo, and this is strategic for transatlantic solidarity as well as being a humanitarian gesture towards people cleared of suspicion who endured detention, torture and deprivation of justice. but it is also a duty for 14 eu states which colluded with the bush administration in subcontracting torture to guantánamo and secret prisons, as this parliament pointed out. european responsibility for violations of the rule of law and human rights cannot be erased. president barroso has denied knowledge of the cooperation provided by his government in the transfer of prisoners to guantánamo and secret prisons, yet no one believes that his military, police, intelligence and administration would be so incompetent as to allow portuguese air, sea and land to be systematically abused by the usa. in order to clarify this, will mr barroso make public the notes of the meetings between his diplomatic advisers and ms condoleezza rice while he was prime minister? will mr barroso make public the legal opinion he then asked from his legal adviser mr carlos blanco de morais in order to impose special navigation rules for ships approaching us military vessels carrying prisoners through portuguese waters?
for us, of course, we must applaud the decision of obama, the decision to close guantanamo, to ban torture, illegal interrogations and the suspension of military trials. however, mr president, portugal and the portuguese socialist government do not feel any guilt. the portuguese socialist government did not cooperate with the bush administration, quite the contrary. it is precisely now, with the portuguese administration, with the portuguese government, with the initiative of minister luís amado, that the possibility and the duty of the european union and the democratic countries to welcome and receive the prisoners without guilt formed in guantánamo has been unleashed. that is what is really important, that is what should be highlighted as an example for the other member states of the european union to follow, how important it is that tomorrow the resolution is adopted by all the parties that make up this house so that, in unity, the decision can have more force.
in writing. - the recent decisions of the us administration only confirm - if necessary - what has long been denounced: the existence of a network of kidnapping, abduction, illegal detention and torture promoted by the us. such decisions should not serve to whitewash the responsibilities of the us and eu governments for the systematic violation of international law and the most basic human rights. as before, we must demand a total end to these despicable practices and the establishment of the truth, including the responsibility of eu governments for the use of their airspace and territory for the arrest and transport of illegally detained prisoners, for example to the us military base at guantanamo bay. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are consistently opposed to any agreement between states or between the us and the eu on the 'transfer of prisoners' held at guantanamo. this does not mean that decisions and requests freely expressed by each citizen, in particular requests for asylum in portugal, cannot be considered, in the context of respect for national sovereignty, the portuguese constitution and international law, including the right to asylum.
