in writing. - i voted in favour of the karl-heinz florenz report on '2050: the future starts today - recommendations for the future integrated eu policy on climate change', as it presents a series of proposals to the european union, the member states and its citizens with a view to ambitious targets for the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions in the european union. i would like to stress that the problem of climate change requires a transversal approach at all levels of public policy-making and that investment in green technologies is also a requirement of the current economic crisis which will create more jobs. i consider the outcome document of the clim committee, of which i was a member, to be a very positive contribution to the fight against climate change and to clearly demonstrate the need to reach an international agreement at the copenhagen conference at the end of the year.
in writing. - the report fails to address the essential issue of the causes of environmental abuse: the predatory nature of capitalism. it is simply seeking to spread responsibility to all in order to justify proposals which are fundamentally based on the liberalisation of markets and the burden of costs on users and workers. although the final text adopted in plenary is more restrained than the initial proposal, and there are some positive aspects, we do not agree with other points, in particular when they use the cloak of environmental protection as another opportunity to intensify the ideological offensive, to make people and workers responsible, to commercialise and make all environmental activity profitable. we have therefore followed the vote in favour of some of the proposals put forward by our group and others which aim to give the report a more correct content, but we could not help but express our disagreement with attempts to commercialise everything essential to human life, including air itself.
in writing. - this report brings together the positions of various political parties and sectoral sensitivities, compiling the most recent and reliable scientific data. it is therefore a document of undeniable rigour, comprehensiveness, timeliness and relevance. i agree with the report as a whole, but i voted against the more direct references to the impact of livestock on climate change, because i think they are too much. agriculture should not be ostracized, but rather the production and consumption of local products with less greenhouse gas emissions from their transport should be promoted. i also voted in favour of references to the problems of the agricultural sector resulting from the impacts of climate change, considering that the most adversely affected regions should be adequately compensated. as regards adaptation to climate change, i agree with the urgency of the implementation of the new framework directive on soil protection and with the need to adapt cohesion policy, water protection and the natura 2000 network to the expected impacts. finally, i voted in favour of references to the need to avoid excessive use of the kyoto protocol's flexibility mechanisms, since europe must indeed reduce its emissions if it is to continue to lead the international negotiations and to achieve a global agreement in copenhagen.
in writing. − the range of issues included in the debate on the future of an integrated european climate change policy is enormous and should lead us to seek reasonable, feasible and scientifically sound solutions. a polluted debate, which rejects science beyond the official, which ignores the need for research, which abandons the perplexity of scientific research, turns science into dogma, and dogmas are of little use to policy makers. our priority must therefore be to focus on energy production and consumption that is diversified, efficient, capable of reducing dependencies and capable of ensuring the quality of life we want for all (europeans and non-europeans). we are therefore faced with a major scientific challenge, in which it is for the public authorities to signal the priority of investment in research and development, but also, in particular, in so far as they are market players, to encourage the creation of profitable markets for the most energy-efficient products. climate change calls for a step forward in development, not a step back. let's make that effort.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the challenge of energy efficiency and information and communication technologies (ict) because i believe that ict plays an essential role in improving energy efficiency, with its use estimated to save more than 50 million tonnes of co2 annually. the potential provided by icts should be fully exploited by member states in order to achieve the objectives set out in the climate and energy package of a reduction of greenhouse gas emissions by at least 20%, an increase to 20% of the share of renewable energy and a 20% improvement in energy efficiency in the european union by 2020.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this report by a czech member of our political group because we consider that it deals with a subject of the utmost importance: the challenge of energy efficiency through information and communication technologies. these technologies can be drivers of higher productivity, growth and cost reductions, without compromising competitiveness, sustainable development and the quality of life of eu citizens. we therefore support the proposal to ask future council presidencies to choose information and communication technologies as one of their priority topics and their significance in stopping climate change and adapting to it. we also believe it is important to redouble efforts at all levels of the decision-making process to use the financial instruments available for the development and deployment of new ict-based technological solutions that increase energy efficiency. similarly, given the backlog in the systematic approach to smart ict solutions, it is important to raise awareness with a particular focus on reducing emissions in the development of urban centres, including through the development of smart buildings and lighting, transmission and distribution networks, as well as through the organisation of public transport.
in writing. - we voted in favour of the european parliament resolution on the possibility of receiving guantanamo detainees without guilt, considering that european union cooperation is essential to reinforce international law and respect for human rights and to ensure fair and impartial treatment of guantanamo detainees. we therefore consider that the initiative and willingness of the portuguese government to cooperate with the us administration in the process of closing the guantanamo prison is an example for other member states to follow in supporting the united states in solving this complex problem, within a framework of respect for human rights and the rules of international law.
in writing. - i voted against this resolution because i think it is inadmissible, in view of recitals d (third paragraph) and f, that the eu should advocate that its member states be prepared to take in prisoners released from guantanamo following an unfortunate and demagogic suggestion by the portuguese foreign minister. in fact, it is unacceptable for eu member states to take in detainees who are considered to be 'potential threats' (recital d), let alone the 61 ex-prisoners who, after being released, engaged in terrorist activities (recital f). since it is not possible to distinguish safely between those which constitute and those which do not constitute potential threats, it is clear that the so-called 'precautionary principle' should not apply only within the framework of reach.
in writing. - although the resolution includes some aspects which we consider positive, in particular that 'the united states bears the main responsibility for the entire process of closing the guantanamo bay detention centre and for the future of its prisoners', it does not clarify the terms in which, in our view, the very serious humanitarian situation in question should be considered. as we have said, we are opposed to any agreement between states or between the us and the eu on the 'transfer of prisoners' held at guantanamo. this does not mean that decisions and requests freely expressed by each citizen, in particular requests for asylum in portugal, are not considered in the context of respect for national sovereignty, the constitution of the portuguese republic and international law. the resolution also calls for the eu to take action to combat terrorism and to ensure that the eu's security forces are able to effectively combat terrorism and to ensure that the eu is able to take effective action to combat terrorism.
in writing. - the difficulty europe is having in dealing with the decision to close guantanamo is a clear indication of the distance between intentions based on sound principles and the reality, which is fraught with difficulties. the closure of guantanamo is good news, both in itself and as a symbol. but this shutdown doesn't solve the problem that it was created to solve, and it didn't solve it either: how do we deal with a threat to national and international security that presents itself with characteristics entirely different from those of traditional enemy combatants, for whom international law was designed and prepared? more than cooperating in the eventual reception of former guantanamo detainees - which may be necessary but must take into account various limitations - europe, the united states and the international community must cooperate in seeking a stable and lasting legal solution to this challenge of international terrorist fighters. without it, guantanamo will be replaced by another bad solution. as regards the reception of former prisoners, in addition to the duty of coordination at european level, it would be advisable not to receive those who would not otherwise be granted visas for security reasons. goodwill and caution should be the criteria.
the financial crisis has brought credit paralysis; it has affected businesses and households; in the case of the united kingdom, the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the restructuring of the steel industry. in this context, exceptional measures are justified, particularly in the case of strategic sectors such as the automobile sector, which accounts for 6% of employment. however, almost the whole of the programme for the relaunch of the european economy, for which i have the honour of being rapporteur on behalf of this parliament, is based entirely on national initiatives. how does the committee ensure that there is coordination on the ground and that countries do not start a war of support? what mechanisms are in place to safeguard employment in countries that do not have the financial backing to secure the jobs that are vital to them? for some countries, textiles or electronics are as important as cars! what intervention can be expected? does the commission think that it will be more aware of the role of european industry in europe's survival? there are limits to what can be expected from the european investment bank - will we have the budget to meet europe's challenges?
the debate is closed. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
the question period is now over (b6-0006/2009). the following questions were put to the council.
since we are already very late, i propose that the council should answer all the supplementary questions together. as you know, i can only accept two supplementary questions in addition to the original author's. i have therefore chosen two of the five questions based on the criterion of differentiation by political group and nationality. i have chosen in particular mr philip bushill-matthews and mrs silvia-adriana ţicăue.
question no 2 by mr medina ortega (h-1035/08) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: basis for a new world trade agreement following the breakdown of the multilateral trade negotiations in the doha round last summer and the agreements reached by the group of 20 at the washington summit last november, does the council consider that there are grounds for the european union to put forward new trade proposals that are acceptable to the developing countries?
question no 3 by silvia-adriana ticau (h-1038/08) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: measures to improve the energy efficiency of buildings the council proposed a 20% reduction in greenhouse gas emissions by 2020 and an improvement in energy efficiency by the same proportion. it also proposed that 20% of the energy consumed in the european union should come from renewable sources. now, 40% of all greenhouse gas emissions come from buildings. improving the energy efficiency of buildings can therefore make a significant contribution to reducing these emissions. in november, the commission proposed a revision of the energy efficiency directive in this sector. given the importance of this issue to citizens for its potential contribution to reducing electricity and heating bills, can the council indicate the priority it intends to give to the issue of energy efficiency in buildings during the period january to april 2009?
question no 4 by mr burke (h-1040/08) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: energy, external relations and the economy during the czech presidency in the light of the priorities announced by the presidency for its term of office, can it indicate how it intends to integrate the three priorities in terms of concrete initiatives? the author of the question refers specifically to the discussions with the eastern partners on strategic energy corridors that can ensure the eu's energy security and economic competitiveness in the future.
question no 5 by avril doyle (h-1044/08) subject: the use of the european parliament in the european parliament subject: tobacco control and smoking cessation the who framework convention on tobacco control is the first international health convention signed and ratified by the european union and all member states except the czech republic. since the czech republic will take the lead in the discussions on the who framework convention on tobacco control, what are the czech presidency's plans for ratifying the convention?
questions which were not answered in due time will be answered in writing at a later date (see annex).
question time to the council is closed. (the sitting was suspended at 7.15 p.m. and resumed at 9.00 p.m.)
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in this debate it is important to take account of the need to improve the conditions and support for information and promotion measures for agricultural products, both in the european union and in third countries, by extending the scope of the proposals put forward by the european commission. we therefore support the proposals of the rapporteur and the committee on agriculture, in particular those aimed at greater involvement and strengthening the role of the professional associations and organisations operating in the sector, in accordance with their competences and importance in terms of quality assurance and control, including the associations and organisations of the member state drawing up the programme, as well as the increase in the percentage of the community's financial contribution. it is important that there should be additional support, especially for projects selected by the member states. we also believe that promotion and publicity measures in third countries should be able to benefit other important productions besides olive oil and olives, particularly wines with a protected designation of origin or geographical indication. i would like to remind you of the important role played by farmers' associations, particularly cooperative wineries and other associations of small and mediumsized farmers, whose existence is essential to ensure the disposal of the produce of farmers who cannot survive on their own. and once again i call for increased support for these farmers' organizations to play their full part in defending family farming and promoting the good quality agricultural products they produce, which are indispensable to ensuring a healthy diet for the people of our countries. we shall therefore vote in favour of this report.
in writing. − member states need to promote the image of their agricultural products both to community consumers and to consumers in third countries, in particular with regard to quality and nutritional information, food safety and safe production methods. i agree that in the absence of programmes proposed by agri-food organisations, member states should be able to establish programmes and select, by means of an award procedure, a body to implement the programme. the commission proposal, in so far as it allows member states to draw up national programmes, will improve existing legislation. i welcome the dumitriu report and welcome the inclusion of the wine sector in this proposal.
in writing. - the majority of the ep has already become accustomed to passing resolutions without legislative consequences, with content contrary to the legislative resolutions it adopts. the closer the ep elections are to the european elections, the more likely they are to be. we are faced with an example of these "two faces", the real one and its mask, in this case the latter. there is no doubt that - and we have long and consistently advocated this - it is necessary to guarantee the rights of asylum seekers in respect of reception, access to information and the right to interpretation, free legal aid, health and employment. it is certainly important to point out that the number of people detained under the dublin system is increasing, with the almost systematic use of custodial measures and restrictions on access to reception facilities. however, it is also necessary to demand the end of detention centres and to reject a community policy which sets, at the lowest common denominator, reception standards and the procedure for granting asylum. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment from harmful substances.
in writing. − the granting of asylum is the ultimate gesture of a state, and of an entire community, assuming the inability to defend human rights globally and the willingness to act, nevertheless, in accordance with this scale of values. its regime must therefore be clearly distinct from that of immigration. the more one tries to extend the concept to include what does not belong to it, the less its value will be, and the confusion can only harm legitimate asylum seekers. it is therefore important that the rules are clear, the procedures are swift and the treatment is dignified in all circumstances. while there is a need to coordinate actions and options, asylum, in numbers, in scale and conceptually, does not raise the same issues as immigration, even in a space without borders. the member states have their own traditions in the field of asylum and this difference should not be ignored in the context of the coordination referred to. as regards the applicants to whom asylum is or should be refused, this conception, which is based on a generous but restrictive idea of asylum, cannot produce any less humanity in the reception and treatment of people who will always be vulnerable because of their condition.
in writing. - it is no coincidence that, as the european parliament elections approach, resolutions of intent are being issued in an attempt to avoid the responsibility of the policies adopted by the eu (in particular, in the last 5 years) for the very serious situation in which most micro, small and medium-sized enterprises find themselves, particularly in portugal. smes, not large transnational corporations, are the victims of the eu's market liberalisation (as if a framework of 'survival of the fittest' could be in their favour...). many smes 'participate' in 'international trade' because of their dependence on large transnational companies, for whom they produce at prices that often do not correspond to production costs. it is clear that trade defence instruments, intellectual property rights, designations of origin and geographical indications for agricultural products must be safeguarded (and applied) and that the internationalisation of smes must be supported. so why is it that the majority of political forces represented in the ep, which corresponds to the majority represented in the commission and the eu council: do not adopt the 'made in' regulation; do not apply to imported products the same safety and security requirements as those required for products produced in the eu; do not use the financial framework 2007-2013 to defend production and employment by supporting smes?
in writing. - at a time when the world is looking for a response to the global economic crisis and protectionist tendencies are on the rise, as was the case recently with the discussion on the 'buy american' clause in the united states congress, it is the duty of governments and community institutions to safeguard the interests and ensure that the rules applicable to smes and international trade are respected. the value of accessibility to international markets for european smes is clear. as the studies show, when these companies operate in the non-eu market they tend to acquire good practice, to innovate and to be more competitive. it is not a question of the commission's being too rigid. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are protected. at the same time, this claim must be valid with regard to third countries. international trade is only fair if it is fair to both sides.
in writing. − e-commerce is a great business window for smes and young entrepreneurs. it allows for the overcoming of non-traditional technical barriers by enabling access to otherwise inaccessible markets. for the same reason, this type of trade ensures even greater participation of ldcs in international trade circuits. however, the inclusion of these partners depends on the creation of basic infrastructure to which we should contribute unconditionally. it should also be noted that piracy, counterfeiting or data breaches are not intrinsic to this type of trade, but are rather adaptations of old practices. with the necessary adaptations, we must ensure all the certainties of traditional trade. the legal web around electronic commerce is a dispersed one, prohibiting a reductive view: the governance of the internet, which is not yet subject to an adequate structure that is internationally respected, as well as questions of private international law or taxation. at the wto level, everything is confused for electronic commerce, and despite various requests and insistence, negotiations on this type of trade continue to be drawn up in dangerous bilateral compartments.
in writing. − the internet has assumed an increasingly important role in business relations and international trade. however, serious shortcomings remain in the protection of users and consumers, with regard to the protection of their personal data and the quality assurance of the service provided or the product purchased. the report, although it mentions these facts, does not make any proposals to improve the protection of users and the quality of the service provided by the use of an imminently public service such as communications. the main objective of the programme, which incorporates a number of aspects which we welcome, is to promote the development and use of electronic commerce as a facilitator of international trade and as a means of overcoming the current difficulties in opening up markets. in other words, its primary concern is to facilitate and promote electronic commerce, i.e. the production, promotion, sale and distribution of products via telecommunications networks, in the interests of the liberalization of world trade. that is why we abstained.
in writing. - i am in favour of the compromise reached and i consider the new regulation to be very positive, as it will make it possible to harmonise the conditions for the placing on the market and use of feed and ensure that adequate information is provided to both livestock producers and meat consumers, thus ensuring the proper functioning of the internal market. i would stress the importance of the 'open declaration', a list of substances used in the food mixture in descending order of their relative weight, which will help to maintain a high level of confidence among farmers and consumers. on the other hand, the production companies will now have clearer rules for placing animal feed on the market, and it will be easier to prevent possible criminal practices.
in writing. - once again, due to the resistance of a number of african, caribbean and pacific (acp) countries, despite being obliged to use 'politically correct' language, the ep majority cannot hide the real matrix and intentions of the economic partnership agreements (epas) between the eu and the acp countries. while article 36 (1) of the cotonou agreement enshrines the conclusion of 'trade agreements compatible with wto rules, progressively eliminating obstacles to trade and strengthening cooperation in all trade-related fields', the eu's aim is to go beyond what is currently established and achieve what has not yet been achieved in the wto, even if the 10th european development fund is used for this purpose while at the same time reducing official development assistance. i mean, trying to get in through the window after you couldn't get in through the door. the eu is seeking trade liberalisation, from which its major financial and economic groups seek to secure open markets, sell goods and services, exploit raw materials and impose an export-oriented production model in their own interests. we need a different policy which promotes effective independence, sovereignty, cooperation, solidarity, development and social justice.
in writing. − the aim of this resolution is to whitewash the eu's support for the illegal unilateral declaration of independence of the serbian province of kosovo. the european parliament has called for the eu to be given a role in the negotiations on the future of the eu, and for the eu to be given a role in the negotiations on the future of the eu. the existence of a 'pseudo-state' under 'supervised sovereignty', protected by the eu/nato through, inter alia, its 'eulex' mission and its 'vice-kings', the 'civilian international representative' and the 'eu special representative', who hold judicial, police and customs power and 'responsibilities of an executive nature' and oversight, constitute unacceptable acts of a neo-colonial nature. the resolution states that 'the most important task of the european security and defence policy (eu) to date' is a flagrant violation of the un charter and a dangerous precedent in international law, with unforeseeable consequences for the stability of borders, particularly on the european continent. some of those who called for respect for international law, for the territorial integrity, sovereignty and independence of georgia are, after all, the same people who promoted and supported the aggression against yugoslavia. this resolution is just another example of the hypocrisy and cynicism of the majority in the ep.
in writing. − the eu has been china's largest trading partner since 2006 and china has been europe's second largest trading partner since 2007. china currently holds 6% of world trade. the prc has come a long way since the report i had the honour of presenting to this house in 2002. however, it seems that many of the issues that then needed to be fixed still persist, although somehow they have been offset by significant progress. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft directive on the protection of the environment. china and the eu have been negotiating a partnership and cooperation agreement since october 2007, with results to be seen. given the european support in many aspects of international trade, china should not be breaching wto commitments. barriers have been introduced in the form of rules and regulations restricting access for european companies in strategic sectors. in november, the prc declared its intention to abandon the 2007 double-checking system for imports of textiles and footwear. the statistics available do not facilitate discussion, but we may be faced with a commercial controversy.
in writing. - since it is impossible to assess the many issues raised by this resolution, we consider it essential to stress that we advocate the deepening of real and effective cooperation relations between the eu and china, based on responding to the needs of different peoples, mutually beneficial and contributing to mutual development, respecting the principle of non-interference and respect for national sovereignty. on the basis of these principles, although the resolution contains some points which we share, we clearly reject it because it takes on the neoliberal matrix, in particular by pushing for the continuation of trade liberalization, in this case with china. the resolution, while ignoring the very serious consequences of the liberalisation of world trade, encourages the continuation of the opening of markets between the eu and china, insists on efforts to speed up wto negotiations and 'stresses that the new eu-china partnership and cooperation agreement should aim at the establishment of free and fair trade'. as is evident from other similar ep resolutions, the aim is to meet the needs of the eu's large economic and financial groups for expansion, which is in contradiction with meeting the needs of workers and smes in the different eu countries, particularly portugal.
in writing. - the debate on trade relations with china always refers to the question of human rights in that country. it's understandable, you know. the objections raised by the relationship with china are understandable in the light of various assessments, in most cases acceptable. the perception that china's economic growth is unparalleled in respect for human rights and democracy in that country, and in its international performance, the exploitation of labour, an incomparably less demanding labour regulatory framework, disregard for environmental issues and for rules on intellectual property and patents, all these factors are hindering an open trade relationship marked by respect for good international rules. however, this trade is there, it is growing. china's role in the contemporary world economy is undeniable and its participation in overcoming the current crisis is indispensable. it is therefore necessary to press for respect for the rules and principles of international trade, for equal access to markets and for the defence of democracy and human rights, without denying the reality of growing interdependence. on the contrary, we must make the best of this circumstance, even to better influence this great country.
in writing. what should (and is not) the 'eu role in the middle east'? what principles should be guided by? demand an end to the aggression and the inhumane blockade imposed on the people of the gaza strip and ensure urgent humanitarian aid to the palestinian population. condemnation of the brutal aggression, the crimes, the violations of the most basic human rights, the state terrorism perpetrated by israel against the palestinian people, which nothing can justify. it is an unequivocal denunciation that in palestine there is a colonizer and a colonized, an aggressor and a victim, an oppressor and an oppressed, an exploiter and an exploited. suspension of the association agreement and of any strengthening of bilateral relations with israel, as decided at the eu foreign affairs council on 8 and 9 december. we must demand that israel respect international law and comply with un resolutions, and that the occupation, the settlements, the separation wall, the killings, the arrests and the numerous humiliations inflicted on the palestinian people be brought to an end. demand and strive for respect for the inalienable right of the palestinian people to an independent and sovereign state, with the 1967 borders and its capital in east jerusalem! in essence, to cease being an accomplice to the impunity of israeli colonialism.
in writing. - after more than 18 months of an inhumane blockade, the 22 days of brutal israeli aggression against the palestinian people in the gaza strip have left at least 1 324 dead and more than 5 000 wounded, many of them children. more than 100,000 people have been displaced and more than 15,000 homes destroyed. basic infrastructure and essential public services have been destroyed or dismantled, putting in question the ability to respond to the most basic needs of the palestinian population. in the face of this heinous crime, the european parliament has not a word of condemnation for israel. there is no doubt that aid to the palestinian people is urgent. there is no doubt that the suffering of the palestinian people must be acknowledged. however, it is imperative to denounce and hold the aggressor accountable. the resolution insists on the whitewashing of israeli aggression in the gaza strip, hiding it in what it calls a 'conflict'. the european union is not prepared to accept the palestinian people's right to selfdetermination, but to accept the right of the palestinian people to selfdetermination. the eu, always so quick to invoke human rights, soon "forgets" them in relation to israel, which has been colonizing the palestinian territories of the west bank, gaza strip and east jerusalem for more than 40 years.
portugal shall be guided in its international relations by the principles of national independence, respect for human rights and the rights of peoples, equality between states, the peaceful settlement of international conflicts, non-interference in the internal affairs of other states and cooperation with all other peoples for the emancipation and progress of mankind. portugal advocates the abolition of imperialism, colonialism and all other forms of aggression, domination and exploitation in relations between peoples, as well as simultaneous and controlled general disarmament, the dissolution of political-military blocs and the establishment of a system of collective security with a view to creating an international order capable of ensuring peace and justice in relations between peoples. i refer to article 7 of the constitution of the portuguese republic to highlight how far and deeply contradictory the european union is to these principles - the european union which is taking on itself as the european pillar of nato, in partnership with the usa, to better promote the militarization of international relations, the arms race, interference and aggression with the aim of ensuring domination and the sharing of markets and natural resources by the great powers.
i thank the rapporteurs ari vatanen and von wogau for their work and consensus-building efforts, particularly on the difficult subject of nuclear doctrines which the european union and nato must urgently review, at a time when president obama is reviving the objective of freeing the world of nuclear weapons and two european nuclear submarines are on the verge of catastrophe. the vatanen and von wogau reports point to the need for a european union which is politically, strategically and operationally autonomous, through an ambitious esdp. we need the institutional, financial and operational instruments to achieve these objectives. we therefore call for close cooperation between nato and the eu, based on respect for the political autonomy of each of the organizations, which are complementary. we therefore call for the establishment of a permanent eu headquarters in brussels, capable of planning and conducting military operations of the esdp autonomously. we therefore call on the member states of the union to intensify their efforts to spend their national defence budgets, which are not very good on their own, in a better, more efficient and more european way. the message from this parliament is unequivocal and serves as a warning. without a europe of defence, defence in europe is in question. our defence industries could be at risk. the capabilities that europe needs to exercise its responsibility to protect civilian populations and prevent massacres and genocide could be at stake. europe as a global actor in crisis management may be at stake. the extension of european political integration to security and defence, as envisaged in the lisbon treaty, is urgent and needs to be accelerated. it is not only in the interest of the european union but also in the interest of nato, as both organisations will reap the rewards of a europe better equipped to deal with the growing challenges to the security of europeans and to global security.
i thank mrs napoletano for her excellent report. fourteen years after barcelona and five years after the first euro-mediterranean parliamentary assembly, we have the union for the mediterranean with economic power, with parliamentary power, with civil society, with heads of government deciding together. all of them wanting to do more and better in a territory where 720 million citizens represent a potential for development and peace for the world. it is a clear mistake to confine ourselves to the economic and business side. responding to growth crises naturally requires a political response, but responding to humanitarian crises, emergencies and military crises such as the one experienced in late 2008 and early 2009 is crucial. politicians and citizens have to give answers to the world's problems. the world has asked for answers and months later we, citizens and politicians, especially on a platform such as the union for the mediterranean, are still delaying in reacting. i hope that at the very least this moment has been one of learning and moving forward, because only those who make mistakes learn, but moving forward in speeding up the reaction is absolutely crucial.
in her speech, commissioner ferrero-waldner summarised the objectives of the new union. in fact, this union was born under the french presidency under the sign of mistakes. first mistake: he has removed from his agenda the conflicts in the region, in particular the israeli-palestinian conflict and the conflict in western sahara. second misconception: it wants to assert itself by sharing economic and environmental projects, without questioning the free trade context in which it operates. third mistake: it suggests the possibility of transferring turkey's application for membership of the european union to the union. mrs pasqualina's report does not address the first misconception, and that is its limit. no economic and regional integration will come to fruition unless politics has the courage to address existing conflicts and to do so on the basis of international law. the report does, however, contain clear recommendations on the second and third misunderstandings, and these are all in the right direction. the union for the mediterranean cannot be the waiting room or the back door for turkey, provided that it meets the accession criteria, and the union for the mediterranean cannot be an addition to the european free trade strategy without any social integration policies. the report's recommendations are important: a union with a citizen and representative dimension involving the arab league; regional projects with a social dimension; the extension of projects to areas essential to people's lives: water, agriculture, education. there are also clear rules in the programme agreements and a euro-mediterranean bank. the union was born equivocal, but it is there. i support this report because i believe that this union will eventually be what we are able to make it. i give you the benefit of the doubt.
a large part of the issues of major political importance to europe pass through the mediterranean basin. the union for the mediterranean initiative is therefore worthy of our support, since it can give new impetus to a process which is necessary and useful, but which has not had the necessary success or visibility. the question is how we can pursue this strategy. the model followed in barcelona did not have the expected results. can this new partnership work? the report by mrs napoletano is in line with this view. but we hope that the union will not suffer the same damage as the barcelona process. there are many projects, many areas of intervention, but a poor hierarchy of priorities. to relegate social and cultural understanding between the peoples of the two sides of the river to paragraph 26 of the report, to leave the subject of democratization and the promotion of human rights for paragraph 27 and migration flows for paragraph 28, after much talk about other sectors and other subjects, is to give the wrong signal as to what the priorities of this partnership are, or should be. it's important to have a clear strategy. we believe that this strategy must involve offering our neighbours more benefits, more cooperation, but also asking them to deliver more in the economic, social and democratic fields, and concentrating on well-defined sectors, perhaps in smaller numbers, in order to avoid all areas being given equal priority at the same time and at the risk of none being given priority in the end. and, of course, doing so with the proper financial support and framework. the recognition of the need for an investment bank for the mediterranean must finally be met with a favourable response. you have to be ambitious, which means doing well within your means. finally, mr president, a word on the conflict in the middle east. this process, which is not an alternative to peace negotiations, can and should contribute to better understanding, interdependence and mutual respect between the belligerents. these are essential factors - we all know this - for the pacification of the region.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment. − the opinion of the committee on women's rights stressed the need for greater attention by all member states to ratification of the convention on the elimination of all forms of discrimination against women, as well as all other un and international labour organisation instruments in the field of human rights. the situation of women was also addressed, and it was regretted that the position of the commission communication did not give it special attention, particularly in the 'projects' dimension, where it should include the promotion of geographical, economic and social cohesion and should always take into account the issue of equal opportunities for men and women and the gender perspective. finally, i would like to stress the concern about poverty and social exclusion, which mainly affect women. i would like to leave a final word on the serious cases of palestine and western sahara, where women and children are the main victims of war, exploitation and, in short, of the whole process of discrimination which affects these peoples and which affects women and children in particular.
i would like to bring up today the scandalous situation of corticeira amorim in portugal, whose profits over the last two years have exceeded eur 30 million, with over eur 6 million in 2008. however, this month the amorim corticoid plant announced the dismissal of about 200 workers, citing the effect of the crisis, forgetting that it was they who helped build the group that is worth many millions and that received public support, including community funds, for the millions of profits it still has. so here i leave my protest and indignation against this announcement of dismissals, which is a scandal, and i hope that everyone will join me in repudiating this exploitation of the crisis to fire workers even when companies continue to maintain high profits.
i would like to apologize to mr alexandru nazare because, by mistake of the bureau, we did not give him the floor when he was registered for the normal part-session. i'll give you the floor after the "catch the eye" period.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday, 19 february 2009. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
the report by mr thijs berman, on behalf of the committee on development, on the financing of actions not related to official development assistance in the countries covered by regulation (ec) no 1905/2006 (2008/2117 (ini)) (a6-0036/2009) follows.
on behalf of the pse group. – mr president, it is crucial to solve the current legislative gap concerning the financing of non-oda actions in countries covered by the dci. this proposal for an instrument to address this gap must preserve the dci unequivocally as an instrument for oda and it must allow for a clear separation between financial sources allocated to pure oda development cooperation and those allocated to other types of non-oda development cooperation with developing countries. this separation is a very relevant political message in itself and it would give proper visibility to the eu’s development cooperation policy. the new or revised instrument should also be sufficiently broad to cover a wide range of actions that do not comply with the oecd dac guidelines but which are crucial for the eu’s cooperation with developing countries, for instance the development of the akkas gas fields in iraq or cooperation on aviation security with india. this is why i am not in full agreement with the restrictive legal basis proposed. i fully back commissioner ferrero-waldner in finding article 181a, possibly, a more adequate legal basis, which would provide for these kinds of concerns that i am highlighting. however, i am not convinced either by the alternative presented by the ppe-de group amendment that we are supposed to vote on tomorrow. therefore, i hope that, under the guidance of our rapporteur, thijs berman, we may find more time to have a thorough discussion on this matter and consider what is the best legal basis, namely, the proposal made by commissioner ferrero-waldner.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday, 19 february 2009. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
the following is the report by mrs teresa riera madurell, member of the committee on industry, research and energy, on the proposal for a council regulation on the community legal framework for european research infrastructures (eris) (com (2008) 0467 - c6-0306/2008 - 2008/0148 (cns)) (a6-0007/2009).
the interest in adopting this proposal for a directive lies mainly in the way it can help to stimulate economic activity at local level and support actions at the social level. this application of a reduced rate is of great importance in the housing sector, both in construction and renovation, allowing member states that adopt it to reduce the cost to the end user. the provision of local services, particularly by small businesses, can also benefit from this reduction in the vat rate, securing and generating more and better jobs by encouraging the consumption of goods and services, thus stimulating the economy itself. the restoration industry, which is essential for the proper functioning of local economies, and the increase in tourism itself can thus benefit greatly at local level from this reduction in vat. these services, ladies and gentlemen, must not be allowed to circulate on the european internal market, which will help to reduce the reservations of some member states which have already been noted in the council's plan. i cannot therefore agree to extend this reduced rate to equipment, whatever the pretext. the prudent way in which this proposal is to be dealt with in the european parliament should not give the governments, which have some reservations about the european commission's proposal, the pretext to use in practice the veto right which european legislation still confers on them in tax matters. so here we must prudently reject amendments which make little sense and which only make it more difficult to accept the text. as has been said here, not only is the perfect the enemy of the good, but the all-winner loses. this proposal for a directive is important not only for the strengthening of local economies but also reflects the ambitions repeatedly expressed by several member states. we therefore support the commission's proposal as it has been put forward, and that was the vote of the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, with my agreement. i welcome the work of the rapporteurs and i call for this proposal to be adopted in the exact terms in which it was submitted by the commission and voted on in the parliament's committee on the internal market and consumer protection.
in writing. today's debate and vote on reduced vat rates will send a clear signal to the council, in line with what the intergroup on the family and child protection has been insisting on for years. among the various amendments i support, i would like to highlight those which directly affect products for children. it is an important measure to support families with children, reducing unfair tax burdens on them. i welcome the commission's proposal to settle once and for all the absurdity of the diaper dispute and the question of baby seats for younger children. but it is also important, as the ppe group has advocated, that the reduced vat rate can be applied to footwear and children's clothing throughout the eu and not just in the member states which had such a scheme in 1991. we call on the council not to fall behind parliament and to go as far as it is necessary to go in understanding the legitimate needs and aspirations of families. i hope that we are finally at the successful conclusion of a long struggle and insistence by civil society and members of this parliament in defence of the family's tax rights, which should not be penalized for the children it gives birth to, supports and educates.
madam president, i would like to second the rapporteur. following the debate we had here yesterday, and in view of the commission’s contribution, i very much agree that we need more discussion and that we need to find a proper legal basis for actions that are not pure oda actions – but are still very important for development cooperation with developing countries – and therefore do not fall under the other instruments regarding cooperation with industrialised countries, nor under the current dci. it is also very important to freeze the money foreseen for the dci for cooperation in accordance with oda criteria, so i support our colleague mr berman in his request for more time for consideration.
in writing. − the report takes up the objectives of the 2007 green paper on the european research area: 'develop world-class research infrastructures'. the european strategy forum for research infrastructures (esfri), mandated by the council, started preparing a coordinated strategy for research infrastructure activities in april 2002. although the aim of the proposed legal framework is announced to be to facilitate the establishment of such infrastructures, practice has shown that, in general, the most beneficiary countries are the most developed. so we are not disputing the importance of simplifying the creation of world-class research infrastructures in europe and the added value they can bring in terms of the quality of research and teaching, the links between public and private researchers and their overall positive socio-economic impact. but we cannot accept that the creation of large infrastructures will only create more opportunities for achieving excellence in research in various fields, which will lead to certain countries being the main beneficiaries of the process. we want everyone to benefit, even those who cannot set up centres of excellence, to enable the democratisation of research in the most diverse fields. that is why we abstained.
in writing. - during the debate on this report we sought to improve its defence of the rights of workers in all sectors. we therefore welcome the fact that our proposal, which now appears in the european parliament's resolution, is as follows: 'grant the representatives of workers in the public administration and in public and financial sector undertakings the same rights to information and consultation as other workers'. we hope that the member states will take this into account by ensuring that all workers have the same rights to information and consultation. the report is generally positive, stressing the need to strengthen the rights of information and consultation in the various member states. we also expect the european commission to present an evaluation report on the results achieved through the implementation of directive 2002/14/ec on the strengthening of dialogue. as regards mergers and acquisitions, they could have gone further in the aftermath than they have done at other times, in particular when they approved our proposal to guarantee workers' representatives throughout the restructuring process of companies, particularly industrial companies, access to information and the possibility of decisive intervention, including the right of veto, in the event of companies not complying with their contractual obligations.
in writing. - we made a number of contributions by participating in the debate in the committee on employment and social affairs and we put forward a number of proposals to improve the rapporteur's initial position. we have not always succeeded in getting our views accepted, and the final decision falls short of what would be desirable. but we recognize the important role of the various forms of the social economy, the richness of the diversity of their institutions and the specific characteristics they have with different needs, and we advocate their support, but we consider that this cannot call into question the quality of public services, particularly in areas where the social economy also plays a significant role. we recognise that the social economy can contribute to strengthening economic and social development and greater democratic participation, especially if it is supportive and does not serve to undermine universal public services accessible to all. it must be borne in mind that the social economy cannot replace the public ownership and management of public services, but can be an important complement.
in writing. the report also calls for the development of a european strategy for the prevention of and treatment of mental illness. poor mental health, on the other hand, degrades the quality of life of individuals and their families and has implications in the health, economic, educational, social security, criminal and judicial sectors. today, there is a growing awareness that there is no health without mental health. it is important to reflect on some of the data that the report points out, namely: - one in four people have some kind of mental disorder at least once in their lives - depression is one of the most common disorders: it affects one in six women in europe and is estimated to be the most common disease in the developed world and the second leading cause of disability by 2020 - the eu has a high rate of suicide, with an estimated 59 000 suicides per year, 90% of which are due to mental disorders. vulnerable groups such as the unemployed, immigrants, people with disabilities, people who have been abused, people who use psychotropic substances are more likely to have mental health problems. the joint approach to the challenges of mental health is very important and should be taken as seriously as physical health.
in writing. − energy efficiency is essential if the eu is to reduce its energy bill and is a valuable tool for mitigating oil dependence. all nations have much to gain from the sharing of each member state's experience in this field, comparing methodologies and strategies, even if properly framed in the context of each region. for portugal, i regret that the measures being implemented have not been presented to the commission in the framework of a coherent and complete action plan within the regular period, making my country one of the few that have not done so, preventing the sharing of solutions. this first evaluation of the national action plans is a benchmark for eu dialogue and a useful tool for member states to translate the spirit of energy efficiency into pragmatic measures that deliver the expected results by 2020.
in writing. − several member states are drawing up action plans, but there are shortcomings in the achievement of energy efficiency targets. it therefore seems fair to propose that emphasis be placed on the effective implementation of energy efficiency measures, in particular the development of best practices and synergies and better information and advice for end-users. but we must not forget the support that must be given to those who have the greatest economic difficulties. it also seems appropriate to examine in detail, in the context of the review of the action plans planned for 2009, the extent to which they cover all possibilities for savings in the area of energy efficiency, without compromising the sovereignty of each member state. this analysis should serve to monitor the difficulties and, if necessary, take appropriate supporting measures, aiming at the proposed energy efficiency target. it also seems important to support provisions that reinforce the role of the public sector as a role model and recognition of the immense potential for increasing the energy efficiency of buildings for reducing greenhouse gas emissions and for combating climate change, both in terms of adaptation and in combating the causes of climate change.
in writing. − in december 2006, the european parliament and the council adopted an important resolution on the seventh framework programme of the european community for research, technological development and demonstration activities (2007 to 2013). i am glad that i mentioned at the time the enormous importance of ensuring more and better technological development in the field of fisheries and aquaculture. i therefore consider this ep own-initiative report to be timely and of great importance for the pursuit of the objectives of the new cfp reform. the cfp is one of the community policies most dependent on scientific research, since the credibility of the measures adopted under this policy depends on the existence of high-level scientific advice. the technological development of vessels, fishing gear and engines will also be able to ensure the sustainable development of the sector in the future, by improving environmental protection and ensuring the competitiveness of fleets. this report deserves my vote in favour.
in writing. - scientific research into fisheries is essential for an appropriate fisheries management policy, which should take into account the social, environmental and economic components of this activity, which is essential for ensuring food security. it should not only assess the state of fisheries resources and develop models to predict their evolution, but also contribute to improving fishing gear, vessels, working conditions and safety of fishermen and to assessing the impact of different fisheries management systems/instruments on employment and income of fishing communities. however, if scientific research in fisheries is to meet the objectives and needs it is essential that the necessary resources, including financial resources, be made available by the eu and each member state, and we therefore support the recommendations in this regard contained in the report. however, we believe that the development of scientific research necessarily requires the development of its human resources, which implies guaranteeing the rights of these professionals with regard to employment contracts - combating the precariousness of employment ties faced by many of these researchers -, fair remuneration and access to social security schemes, among other labour rights that assist workers.
in writing. − the situation of numerous species of cetaceans, despite the efforts and initiatives undertaken by the iwc (international whaling commission), remains a matter of concern, with many of them at risk of extinction. the issue of whaling for scientific purposes remains a 'cover' for the capture of these mammals, which is why the eu should intervene to review this international position. supporting the continuation of the moratorium opposing all proposals for new types of whaling, accepting that all whaling activities undertaken by iwc members should be under the control of this committee, and supporting proposals to end the practice of 'scientific whaling' outside the control of the iwc, are some of the premises of this report which i consider to be fundamental. ensuring that the eu has a political position on this issue and taking a leading role in pursuing objectives related to respect for biodiversity and sustainable development of our planet are key objectives that the eu should embrace. i therefore vote in favour of this report.
in writing. - regardless of the need to strengthen procedures to combat the use of illegal labour, it is unacceptable that the current directive punishes both the offender and his victims. the aim of this directive is to supplement the shameful 'return' directive - which the ps government has approved - by applying sanctions to employers of irregular workers and by establishing as a general rule and automatically the procedure for the expulsion of these workers, with rare exceptions. the commission has also proposed that the member states should be able to grant the right to vote to the candidates who are not in a position to do so. this directive does not adopt as a principle and central safeguard the protection of migrant workers who, when faced with unacceptable situations, report them. it also fails to fully guarantee the rights of these workers by stating that 'where late payments are not made by the employer, member states should not be obliged to comply with this obligation'. by failing to safeguard the rights of irregular migrant workers, by failing to provide for their regularisation in general and by exposing them to the threat of expulsion, this directive will increase the vulnerability of the situation of these workers and encourage even more covert illegal work situations. a directive which once again exposes the eu's inhuman immigration policy and which merits our rejection!
in writing. the promotion of small and mediumsized enterprises - which represent 99% of the union's enterprises, are responsible for more than 100 million jobs and have created 8 million jobs in the last eight years alone - is central to the small business act. the present diploma illustrates the measures taken. it allows certain companies to benefit from a reduced rate of vat, while at the same time attempting to solve the parallel employment market and calling for the creation of legitimate businesses. in order to facilitate the operation of smes, we cannot infringe on essential principles of the union. in this case, the single market is protected by applying the rules of this document to companies operating only locally, avoiding market distortions. for all these reasons i voted in favour of this report, which i regard as a perfect example of the policies to be adopted by the european parliament in the economic field.
in writing. − in 2007, the european parliament, following the inquiry carried out by the commission i chaired, adopted a resolution containing dozens of recommendations aimed at preventing the repetition of these illegal actions. a process of evaluating the application of those recommendations is now under way, following a methodology proposed by me. the president of the ep recently sent a formal request for cooperation to the presidents of the national parliaments and a hearing will be held shortly. the adoption of a new resolution before the process is complete is a hasty move that generates errors: the error of speaking too soon when there is nothing new to say; the error of giving in to the urge to use it to target people or to make domestic policy; the error of starting by criticising barack obama when we should be investing in the relaunching of transatlantic cooperation and benefiting from the change in the us administration. i shall therefore not be able to vote in favour of this resolution. i shall also be unable to vote against a text which recalls the essential values of respect for human rights, the rule of law and the search for truth. so i will abstain.
in writing. − i did vote for keeping the reference to portugal, including the reference to the responsibilities of the barroso government, despite the fact that i think that the text of that paragraph could be more accurate and mention ‘new factual information’ and not just ‘press information’.
in the light of the recent events in the middle east, the european union has taken a number of measures to help the people of the region. - 'the commission denounces the failure to take any action ... to clarify the extraordinary deliveries programme'; - 'urges the member states, the commission and the council to assist in ascertaining the truth by initiating investigations or cooperating with the competent bodies, and to ensure effective parliamentary control of the activities of the secret services'; - 'calls on the eu, the member states and the north american authorities to fully investigate and clarify abuses and violations of international and national law on human rights, fundamental freedoms, the prohibition of torture and ill-treatment, enforced disappearances and the right to a fair trial in the context of the 'war on terrorism', with a view to establishing accountability ... and to ensuring that such violations are not repeated in the future ...'; the resolution does not, however, demystify or denounce the fundamental issue, namely the so-called 'fight against terrorism', nor does it condemn what it calls the 'limited maintenance of secret rendition regimes and detention centres' by the us. we need a clear condemnation and clarification of the truth, as the pcp proposed in the assembly of the republic and the ps, psd and cds-pp rejected!
in writing. - as with the other two reports discussed and adopted at this plenary session, this one is equally full of ambition. the report also stresses the need to ensure that the eu is able to take full advantage of the opportunities offered by the single market and to ensure that the eu is able to take full advantage of the opportunities offered by the single market. the report (and resolution) is a genuine agenda for the objectives, priorities and interests of the eu's major powers, whether in the balkans (kosovo, bosnia and herzegovina), eastern europe (caucasus, black sea, belarus, georgia, russia), the middle east (gaza strip, iraq), the mediterranean, central asia (afghanistan, iran), africa (chad, sudan, somalia, democratic republic of congo), asia (china) or latin america. this includes everything from the most blatant violations of international law, interference and interventionism, to the thinly disguised ambitions of the eu's great powers to dominate politically and economically. this is the real content and meaning of the so-called and widely propagated 'role of the eu in the world', that is, its ambition to share the domain of zones of influence, markets and natural resources with the usa and japan.
in writing. - as the debate and report on the cfsp show, the european union's ambition in terms of foreign policy is broad. as reality clearly shows, the eu's role in the world does not correspond to this ambition. and yet not only is it increasing, but in most cases the member states agree and act in collaboration. the shared values, principles and priorities of the 27 member states of the european union are much broader than moments of disagreement might suggest. as you'd expect. that is why i am in favour of strengthening agreement on our priorities and criteria for action. but the recognition that there are differences which are fundamentally the result of the existence of different interests and priorities does not lead me to conclude either that the eu is irrelevant in the world or that it is necessary to impose a single foreign policy on all member states, against their interests or even their history. a community is built, and this process, even if slow, is more likely to succeed than the will to impose a uniqueness that is not indispensable.
in writing. - like the report on 'nato's role in the eu's security architecture', this report (and resolution) reaffirms its objective of affirming and consolidating the eu as an offensive political-military bloc, in partnership with the us, within nato. among other important (and clarifying) aspects, this report (and resolution) reaffirms nato's offensive and comprehensive concept, considering that "the updated european security strategy and the future nato strategic concept must be coherent and this should be reflected in the declaration to be adopted at the nato summit in strasbourg/kehl in april 2009" and, going further, "calls for the creation of an eu operations headquarters (...) ". the report (and the resolution) is a genuine denunciation (no matter what its intention) of the militarization of the eu and the so-called 'lisbon treaty' which institutionalises it. those in ireland who denounced the militaristic nature of the treaty and rejected it, whose ratification would mean further militarization of international relations, more arms race, more interference and war, are right. those who, in portugal, like the pcp, rejected this treaty and demanded a referendum and a broad national debate on its grave consequences for the country, europe and the world, for peace, are right.
in writing. - looking around europe, it is clear that the european union has, and should have, its own strategic priorities and concerns in the field of security. geography is a force to be reckoned with, geopolitics is a force to be reckoned with. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. that is a statement of european responsibility. if we want security, we have an obligation to be available to bear the burdens it imposes, whether they be material or human. it also requires an agreement on what is common and shared in this security concern and burden. security comes at a price, and the demand for more multilateralism from our allies comes at an increased cost. the times ahead will be demanding in this area. europeans can no longer be consumers of security without paying for it.
in writing. − i declare that i voted in favour in the final vote on the vatanen report on the role of nato in the security architecture of the eu.

in writing. − europe's geographical situation imposes its own security priorities and concerns. but the integration of europe and the 27 member states into a set of values and a model of society that we call, in the broad sense, the western world, is also defining the threats and our security context. for this reason, the alliance that most eu member states share with the united states, among others, is and must remain a central element of our security. in the year of the 60th anniversary of the atlantic alliance, it is time to rethink its strategic concept and adapt it to the new reality. after the end of the cold war, it is time to move beyond the end of the end of the cold war and adapt concepts to the new reality that is forming: the emergence of the pacific, the greater relevance of asia, the role that russia is seeking to redefine for itself, the threats posed by failed states and global terrorist groups, among others. let us therefore seek to be an active part of this strategic redefinition as full partners of the atlantic alliance.
in writing. - despite its careful language, the report is guilty of what it objectively and consciously omits or tries to hide, for example its failure to denounce and condemn the systematic and brutal violation of international law and human rights by israel - which colonizes the occupied palestinian territories and oppresses the palestinian people - and by morocco - which colonizes western sahara and oppresses the saharawi people. the report exposes the eu's unacceptable immigration policy and its inhuman, criminalising, security-oriented and exploitative character of immigration. the commission also welcomes the fact that the agreements between the eu and the mediterranean countries provide for the financing of centres for immigrants. the report also makes it clear that the model it advocates for the so-called 'union for the mediterranean' is, after all, that of the eu itself, guided by the liberalisation of markets (including energy) and capitalist competition, seeking to ensure the political and economic dominance of the great powers. the aim should therefore be to 'achieve a euro-mediterranean free trade area' and not a 'euro-mediterranean partnership ... based on equal treatment, solidarity, dialogue and respect for the specific asymmetries and characteristics of each country' as we proposed in an amendment we tabled. that is why we are voting against it.
yesterday was international women's day, which was marked against a backdrop of a profound worsening of the situation of women, especially working women, who continue to suffer from inequalities and discrimination. at the moment, millions of women and young girls are faced with a sharp deterioration in their living and working conditions, poverty reforms, redundancies, unemployment, precarious and low-paid work, the spread of poverty and social exclusion, with a strong impact on segments of working women, pensioners, women with disabilities, who are denied the right to have rights in matters crucial to a life in dignity. so, in addition to greeting all the women of the european union and the world, i leave here the call for urgent measures and new policies to guarantee women their rights as full citizens in work, in the family, in society and in politics. it is essential to create the conditions for women to have rights, to guarantee the right to be mothers and workers without penalty and to receive fair wages and pensions that allow them to live with dignity.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, in view of the worsening crisis in the european union and the absence of any objective and effective measures at community level to deal with it, the european parliament, after having agreed last december to a budget for 2009 of some eur 8 billion below that defined in the financial framework for 2007-13, now wants the final budget for 2010 to be closer to these ceilings. the minimum that the european parliament could say, however, is, in our view, very little. the budgetary limits imposed by the present financial framework, which limits the community budget to 1% of community gni, are clearly insufficient to achieve the policy of economic and social cohesion that is being promoted. however, these insufficient limits are not fully used for this purpose, or even respected, let alone fully enforced. the continuing under-budgeting and under-implementation of the structural and cohesion funds, which are two years behind schedule in their implementation, calls for the adoption of a set of measures to ensure the implementation of these financial means, all the more so when the european union and its neoliberal policies are at the epicentre of the causes of the current economic crisis. in the face of rising unemployment, social inequalities and poverty, we reaffirm the urgent need to, among other measures, strengthen the financial means for the structural funds and the cohesion fund, speed up and ensure their full implementation, increase the rate of community co-financing and repeal the application of the n+2 and n+3 rule in these funds, as well as their use for the defence of employment with rights and the enhancement of workers' purchasing power, effective support for small and family farming and fisheries, the defence and development of the productive sectors of each member state, particularly of the cohesion countries, and effective support for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises and the cooperative sector.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in this report on smes we have tried to emphasize the importance of smes, including family businesses and those integrated into the social economy. i would like to congratulate my colleague, mrs herczog, because her report is important in this area. other documents and texts have been produced in this area and it is important that they converge. the creation of such enterprises, by taking advantage of their capacity for initiative, by facilitating the launching of new businesses, by involving the manufacture of new products, by the operation of new services adapted to the needs of consumers and other enterprises, and thus by promoting the development of the european economy. but i would like to mention something which i think is very important, and that is what is referred to in paragraph 47 of the herczog report. it is advisable to improve certain aspects of the legal and taxation systems of the member states in this area, as the current model in most states may discourage the transfer of companies, in particular family businesses, by increasing the risk of liquidation or closure of the company. it is therefore necessary to carefully review its legal and tax framework in order to improve the conditions for the transfer of companies and to promote and implement effective models of transfer of companies to workers as an alternative to their closure. such action would make it possible to safeguard jobs and reinvest profits for the better development of these enterprises. finally, i would stress that support for
