on behalf of the ppe-de group. madam president, mr vice-president of the european commission, ladies and gentlemen, since the tampere conclusions, much has been done to harmonize the member states' legislation in the area of asylum. however, this harmonization was done on the basis of the lowest common denominator, with very different practices and procedures persisting. there are still no equal conditions for access to protection throughout the union, which leads to problems such as secondary movements and multiple applications. in 2008, as the rapporteur has already pointed out, the number of refugees increased to over 12 million people. it is therefore necessary and urgent to start the second phase of the common european asylum system. there can be an identical level of protection throughout the union only if there is a single asylum procedure, for reasons of efficiency, speed, quality and fairness of decisions, and uniform rules on the conditions for qualifying as a refugee or a person in need of international protection. only then can asylum seekers be treated in the same way, regardless of the member state in which they make their asylum application. i therefore welcome the presentation of this action plan on asylum, which has been contributed to by a number of public actors and which sets out the roadmap for the coming years with a view to the implementation of the common european asylum system. i welcome the amendments which are intended to be made to the directive on reception conditions, the directive on asylum procedures and the qualification directive in order to clarify the criteria for qualifying for international protection. i am pleased that commissioner barrot has stressed the need to ensure coherence with other policies that have an impact on international protection and i hope that the same coherent vision will be extended to other common areas. finally, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, giusto catania, on the work he has done, which will deserve the approval of the epp-ed group.
madam president, this is a brief point of order on this week's agenda. the 11th of march is the european day of remembrance for the victims of terrorism. this day was first proposed by this parliament in a vote on 11 march 2004. we were then discussing a balance of the area of freedom, security and justice. that was the day of the tragic bombings in madrid. the original proposal was for 11 september, but in view of the tragedy that occurred on that day, parliament voted to make it 11 march. the european council, a few days later, on 25 march, if i remember correctly, adopted this date as the european day of remembrance for the victims of terrorism. we have a plenary session tomorrow, 11 march, and i saw on the agenda that there is not even a single recall. i wonder if something is planned on this subject at this week's sitting.
in writing. − member states remain responsible for controlling their borders, but only a global and common policy approach will address the fundamental challenges of managing borders and migratory flows. an area without internal borders cannot work without a sharing of responsibility and solidarity in the management of external borders, especially when we take into account that the eu's external borders are crossed by more than 300 million travellers every year. a genuine integrated border management must be able to meet two fundamental objectives: to strengthen security and to facilitate the crossing of borders by persons seeking to enter legally and for legitimate reasons. but we cannot continue to approve new and important initiatives without a detailed master plan for the eu's border strategy. it is also necessary to assess existing systems in order to consider whether there is an effective need to create new instruments, their feasibility, reliability, interoperability and costs, and whether the protection of the fundamental rights of individuals is taken into account in the most appropriate way.
in writing. - a legal system that is permeable to fraud, difficult to enforce and often not enforced, is an invitation to violation - if not to simple ignorance - of the law. in view of the available data, this is likely to be one of the difficulties of the different european immigration legislation. it is well known that the deterrent effect of a legal provision depends more on the likelihood of its application than on the penalties attached to it. these concerns imply the recognition of the need for european authorities to cooperate in the enforcement of existing legislation and also in the attempt to adapt the legal frameworks to the reality described in several reports. finally, both in the name of solidarity and in the name of fairness, it is necessary to stress the need to take account of the burden that the management of the external borders represents for the member states concerned.
in writing. − although in some cases the reasons for someone deciding to immigrate may be similar to those for asylum seekers, the two systems must be sufficiently distinct, both in legal terms and in terms of administrative procedures. this important caveat being made, one point to be taken into account is that, by virtue of the effective opening of borders within the schengen area, what is decided in one state on this matter may have implications in another. at the same time, the idea of europe can be seen as a whole in the eyes of an asylum seeker who sees the 'european union' as a homogeneous space and, in his perception, opposite to the danger he is fleeing. finally, an asylum seeker fleeing a real threat to his life will hardly be able to choose the point of entry into europe or be able to carry out the necessary administrative procedures required of an applicant for immigration. all these considerations make coordination and collaboration between member states necessary. it is not clear from the foregoing that asylum should be transformed into an alternative gateway to immigration, much less a way of avoiding the illegality of certain migratory flows.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this report as it underlines the breadth and persistence of inequalities between men and women in the performing arts and their impact on society as a whole, insisting on the absolute need to promote and encourage women's access to all artistic professions in which they are still in a minority. as the report points out, the percentage of women in artistic professions and official positions in the field of culture is low and women are underrepresented in positions of responsibility in cultural bodies and in academies and universities where some arts are studied. we therefore agree with many of the proposals here, stressing the need to promote women's access to all the arts and other professions in which they are in a minority, and encouraging member states to remove the barriers to women's access to management positions in cultural institutions as well as academies and universities. we also stress that discrimination against women hinders the development of the cultural sector, because it deprives it of talent and skills, and we note that talent needs contact with the public in order to be recognised.
in writing. the proposal for a directive aims to review and bring together in a single text seven separate directives on industrial emissions. the commission proposal claims to provide for an integrated approach, aiming to integrate environmental aspects into the licensing of installations as comprehensively and as balanced as possible, with the aim of effectively limiting emissions through best available techniques (bat), the application of which should be more consistent than hitherto. as the report says, this legislative process may have implications for 52 000 industrial plants in europe, and we have therefore supported some proposals for derogations for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises which should not be subject to the same obligations as large industrial units. but we are in favour of greater intervention in industrial units with incinerators and co-incinerators and greater supervision than the european commission proposes. the amended text thus gives some value to public consultation and the role of environmental ngos, also takes account of the interests of micro and smes and takes away some discretion from the european commission, which is why we voted in favour of it in the end, hoping that in portugal there will be greater government intervention in supporting and monitoring air quality.
in writing. − the wide differences in the legal systems of the member states often impose very costly procedures for companies wishing to start their activity abroad, especially smes with smaller structures. the creation of this degree is another step towards removing these obstacles, especially in a sector which is crucial to the european economy. the creation of the 'european private company' allows smes to establish their subsidiaries with the same status, regardless of the country in which they are based, doing business in their country or abroad, with the same degree of ease. the time and money that this measure, under the small business act, saves smes points to a clear direction for future european enterprise policy. for these reasons, the psd meps support the report.
in writing. - in the face of the worsening economic and social situation in the various member states, the eu has not yet taken any effective initiative other than to protect financial capital. immediate action at community level is urgently needed to help meet the needs of workers, the productive sector and micro, small and mediumsized enterprises effectively by mobilising the necessary financial resources. however, the eu debated and passed a 2009 budget as if nothing was happening - a community budget that, in relative terms, is the lowest since portugal's accession to the eec - once again demonstrating its class character. the ep has been unable to hide from the reality of the depth of the capitalist crisis (and its policies). the resolution now adopted thus timidly states that the community budget for 2010 should be closer to the limits set in the financial framework 2007-2013 - which is clearly insufficient and not even being complied with - while also recognising that the category of expenditure is 'insufficient'. as 'a boiled cat in cold water is afraid', let us hope that this pronounced concern and intention are not just momentary sighs, in view of the elections to the ep, and that, as always, the ep does not remain only for the good intentions.
in writing. − we welcome the fact that language issues are now seen by the ep as a 'key principle' of its priorities for the eu budget for 2010: - stresses the fundamental principle that all member states should have access to comprehensive and quality services enabling them to work, express themselves and obtain all documents in their mother tongue (...) - considers that every effort should be made in 2010 to ensure that members of all nationalities and languages are treated equally in the possibility of performing their duties and exercising their political activity in their own language, if they so wish; - i know . stresses, (...) the principle of the democratic legitimacy of all members and their right to full multilingualism; considers, therefore, that the budget can and should be used to achieve this objective (...) ; however, we must not forget that the proposals put forward by the cpp members in the budget - requiring the availability of all the official eu languages at meetings (whether these are held in the community institutions or in external meetings within the framework of parliamentary work) - have been successively rejected over the years.
in writing. - despite the good intentions expressed, the report maintains and even goes into greater detail about the policies which are at the root of the problems experienced by many small producers, particularly in portugal. in the name of what they call 'promoting the quality of european agricultural products', they are increasing production costs for those who are already having difficulty maintaining their production, especially small producers, such as the small or very small cheese factories producing serra da estrela cheese of undeniable quality. it is unacceptable that, in addition to the fact that producers have to face new requirements to continue producing without the appropriate financial compensation, they should also be burdened with the costs of the requirements of 'official control' of health. contrary to what is being said, production of real quality is in serious danger of disappearing. it is unacceptable to apply harmonization of production and marketing standards to both small producers and agro-industry. the application of these standards is ruining the productive and cultural diversity of countries like portugal. it is important to reverse this trend and promote local production and consumption and to consider agriculture as a sensitive activity which is not compatible with this model of commercial liberalization, which is unsustainable from an environmental point of view and poses enormous risks to human health.
in writing. - i agree with the report, because i believe that we need to reduce the bureaucracy and complexity of the system of standards, so that the regulation and quality control of agricultural products can be made easier. this simplification would therefore lead to a reduction in administrative costs for public entities. i also welcome the special attention given to designations of origin and the request to the commission to bring this issue to the wto agenda.
in writing. - we voted against the report, as they did not even accept a proposal to raise concerns about the abuse of market dominance by large companies, particularly in the case of large supermarket chains, which abuse their purchasing power to force down prices paid to suppliers in the european union and in third countries. nor have they shown the determination to investigate the impact of the merger in the supermarket sector on small businesses, suppliers, workers and consumers and, in particular, to assess the abuses of purchasing power which may result from this merger. the resolution adopted maintains the full line of action in defence of competition and against public services, following up on the infamous bolkestein directive, and always insisting on the need to comply with the rules of the internal market. and, unfortunately, even when he talks about the crisis and the difficulties of the economies, he insists on the commission's vigilance so as not to jeopardize competition. in other words, in the face of the crisis that neoliberal capitalism has caused, the solution is more of the same, which is unacceptable.
in writing. − although 99% of eu enterprises are smes (23 million) - responsible for 80% of new jobs created in the eu in recent years - the rules normally created are mostly aimed at the 41000 large european companies, fostering obvious imbalances in terms of competitiveness. it is time to reverse this trend and invest in the sectors of the economy where wealth is truly created, with a merit-based reward policy, putting european smes alongside their counterparts abroad. moreover, due to its flexible nature, smes are companies accustomed to being at the forefront of innovation in their fields, so the sba is an important step forward in the implementation of the lisbon strategy. the report also calls for the establishment of a european union-wide network of national and regional authorities responsible for the protection of the environment.
in writing. - despite all the phrasing and apparent good intentions in defence of smes, the report has other objectives, namely: to promote free competition, the internal market, that is, in fact to support economic and financial groups; to insist on the liberalisation of services, including public services, and, under the cover of pseudo-support for small and mediumsized enterprises, to increase the exploitation of workers. in fact, on behalf of 91.5% of the companies in the european union, which employed fewer than 10 people in 2003, they want better conditions to destroy essential public services, deregulate the labour market, and call into question social and labour rights. it's neoliberalism in its most visible role. we therefore voted against the report in favour of effective measures to support micro, small and mediumsized enterprises, of a different policy that safeguards their role and important contribution to industrial, agricultural and fisheries production, to employment with rights, to trade and to meeting the basic needs of the population.
in writing. - it is well known that smes account for more than 90% of jobs in europe, but with the crisis many of their workers have already fallen, or will soon fall, into unemployment. the commission's move to speed up procedures for the structural funds is a welcome sign. globalisation coupled with the current crisis has altered many of the circumstances that have underpinned decisions taken at european level in the past and that were considered correct at the time. in this light, i believe that, for example, some aspects of regional and cohesion policies should be reviewed. we must also examine the financial conditions for smes, which are crucial, especially when they have to repay loans in times of low economic activity. i therefore support this report because it is precisely at this time that we must think about smes and their contribution to innovation, economic growth and employment. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption, and the council will be consulted on it.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we are now finishing the discussion of the third package of measures on maritime safety, erika iii. this process is over three years old and in it we strongly advocate for more safety for passengers, oceans, seas and shipping lanes. as a portuguese and a madeiran, you will appreciate that i have been particularly committed to this debate and its main objectives. ensure that member states consistently fulfil their obligations as members of the international maritime organisation, adopt the conventions of the organisation and fully implement the mandatory provisions. the negotiation process was difficult. for parliament, the erika iii package has always been a package and never a set of measures that are not being implemented. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy, and to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. we have now reached political agreements on six of the eight proposals. my proposal and that of my colleague gilles savary were left open. we have now reached agreement that these two proposals will also be included in the final text of the erika iii package. thanks to parliament and its perseverance, the slovenian and, above all, the french presidencies, to whom i am grateful, and finally the common will of all, we can now conclude this third maritime safety package, in which it is important to emphasize the ratification of international conventions by the national method, a position which i advocate with respect to the different systems of ratification existing in the different member states. pending the mandatory adoption of the imo audit regime, member states shall submit their maritime administrations to an imo audit and publish the results thereof. member states shall establish an internationally certified quality management system for maritime administration activities. before allowing a ship to fly its flag, member states shall ensure that international rules and standards are respected. member states shall ensure that ships flying their flag which are subject to port state vessel inspection are brought into compliance with the applicable imo conventions. if a flag of a member state is included in the black or grey list of the paris mou for port state inspection of ships for two consecutive years, the state is required to submit to the commission a report on the reasons for its performance. in parallel to the common position, member states will confirm their willingness through a joint declaration to ratify the main international conventions on maritime safety by 1 january 2012, to apply the imo flag state code and its maritime authorities audit system and to encourage the imo to strengthen compliance with these two instruments worldwide. in conclusion, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the adoption of this third maritime safety package is an important victory in the european legislative process. the commission, the council and the european parliament have proposed, debated, reconciled and agreed a text that improves the lives of citizens, businesses, promotes greater safety on the seas and oceans and prepares our future. i'm honored to be a part of this process. when we vote on this package in the near future, we will be honouring the victims of all past and recent maritime disasters, from prestige to erika, from bolama to estonia, and helping to prevent or mitigate the consequences of similar events in the future.
the rapporteur. - mr president, i would like to begin by thanking the french presidency for giving us the opportunity to see this package through to completion and, in particular, its vice-president tajani and the commission for the important contribution they have made to the final stage of the negotiations. i would also like to thank all of you for your comments and contributions and to extend a special greeting to all the rapporteurs, especially my colleague jaromír kohlíček and, if i may, rosa miguélez ramos, the shadow rapporteur, for ensuring that disputes between member states such as the one that occurred with the prestige do not happen again and that everyone recognizes their responsibility when the worst happens. i would like to thank mr paolo costa, with whom i have worked throughout the process, especially on the report on the responsibilities of passenger carriers, as rapporteur for the socialist group, for ensuring that all means of maritime transport guarantee the protection of their passengers, that means of financial compensation for passengers are in place when the worst happens and that those potentially most affected are also the most protected quickly and even in advance, regardless of the fault. sufficiently clear, accessible and informed legal remedies are available. as i have said, the adoption of this third maritime safety package is an important victory in the european legislative process. the commission, the council and parliament have agreed on a text that improves the lives of all, promotes greater safety on the seas, oceans and inland waters and prepares our future. by voting on this package tomorrow, we honour the victims of all past and recent maritime disasters and we honour citizens and businesses. i hope, mr vice-president, that what will be agreed tomorrow in this package will be implemented so that citizens can turn the expectations now created into effective reinforcement of security and their rights.
mr president, the revision of the eurovignette directive should be an instrument to stimulate more sustainable and environmentally friendly road transport. we have simply, in my view, taken the wrong path with the result achieved in the commission. we have not chosen measures to encourage more sustainable transport of goods. instead, we have sent the wrong message at this time of global crisis, through a review which will only worsen the already precarious situation of many transport companies, largely small and mediumsized enterprises, which constitute the majority of the european business fabric. we know that measures that aim to combat pollution or that stimulate technological innovation, such as cleaner engines and smart vehicles, are key. the problem, which raises serious difficulties, is the imposition of a congestion charge. the so-called rush hour is primarily caused by local motorists travelling for work, business or leisure. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's transport policy is not disrupted by the introduction of a new system of charges for congestion. we are still acting unilaterally, for example, by not taking action on the differentiation of working hours, accident prevention or road planning and maintenance, and by calling into question the very mobility that defines our societies and the market economy. we are also contradicting other measures taken to promote the economic, social and territorial cohesion of the union. for my country, portugal, and for all the peripheral countries, this measure is nothing but a measure to strangle economic activities, since we will be mere net contributors of any toll. finally, mr president, with this tax we are undermining the important environmental objectives, leading many small and mediumsized enterprises to greater difficulties and even bankruptcy, thus directly contributing to the increase in unemployment and social instability in european societies. the choice is ours, and let us have no doubt that we will be called to account for the consequences of our decisions.
i thank all those involved, and especially my colleague el khadraoui, who has worked very hard with great openness and availability of consensus channels. the proposal under discussion will allow member states to introduce charges to cover certain environmental externalities and to generate significant revenues to be used to improve the european road network and minimise the environmental impact of part of road transport. on the other hand, it could represent enormous costs, especially for the most peripheral countries, such as portugal, where i come from, which is why i urged copel and strongly opposed the mandatory extension of the geographical scope to all road axes. i also opposed the application of the costs of congestion for only a few, a measure which creates unfair competition and decriminalises those who pollute the most, while recognising the need to account for these costs. the application only to transeuropean road networks or to routes used regularly and significantly by international freight transport, leaving it to a member state to determine which routes it introduces any charges on, is a minor evil which could be further minimized by an application of this proposal postponed beyond the difficult global crisis we are currently experiencing.

question no 31 by silvia-adriana ţicău (h-0068/09) subject: measures to promote products and services that contribute to energy efficiency and the promotion of renewable energy at the 2008 spring european council, heads of state and government decided to reflect on the possibility of reviewing the energy taxation directive to promote an increase in the share of renewable energy in total energy consumption. increasing energy efficiency is one of the fastest, safest and least expensive solutions to reduce the eu's dependence on energy sources from third countries, to reduce energy consumption and co2 emissions and to reduce the energy bill payment costs of european citizens. in the context of the need to increase energy efficiency, can the commission indicate what measures and financial and fiscal instruments it envisages to promote products and services that contribute to energy efficiency and the promotion of renewable energy?
question no 32 by mr giorgos dimitrakopoulos (h-0100/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: more flexible interpretation of the stability and growth pact the commission is asked whether, and why, at a time of such severe economic crisis, it persists in its view that the deficit reduction of all countries facing this problem should be achieved within a period of two years rather than three, which, according to the available data, seems to be the more logical timeframe. how does this insistence reconcile with the view of the eurogroup president in favour of a more flexible interpretation of the stability and growth pact (statements of 20.1.2009 to the europe agency)?
madam member, we shall indeed be finished at 8 p.m., as scheduled. we started later and we'll finish later. but there is no supplementary question, it seems, from mrs avril doyle? would you like to put your supplementary question, madam deputy?
member of the commission. − in answer to the question by mr dimitrakopoulos, the commission, when proposing the deadlines for a member state to return to a sustainable position of public finances, will take into account the room for manoeuvre available to the member state concerned. a call for rapid fiscal consolidation can be expected only in cases of danger of a public finance crisis, taking into account the financing needs of the whole economy. as for the second question, which was a double question, the answer to the first part is ‘no’. the answer to the second part – on countries with low corporate tax – the stability and growth pact assesses the global fiscal position of a member state, not the specific tax structure of each member state. in reply to the third question, on whether there are any member states without excessive deficit, there are of course certain member states without excessive deficit, as can be seen in the charts which the commission regularly publishes.
question no 33 by mr pedro guerreiro (h-0125/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: the end of tax havens has the commission already proposed or does it intend to propose the end of tax havens, particularly in the european union? has the eu taken any decision to propose to its member states the closure of 'tax havens' on their territories? what measures will the commission take to put an end to tax havens, to combat financial speculation and to curb the free movement of capital, particularly at eu level?
member of the commission. - mr president, since the end of the 1990s, the commission has pursued a determined policy of combating fraud, tax evasion and harmful tax competition. a key element of this policy is the promotion of transparency in tax systems and the exchange of information between tax administrations. recently, this policy has been confirmed by clear statements by the g20 to attack the non-transparent practices of certain jurisdictions, often described as tax havens. the commission reinforced its policy in this area in late 2008 and early 2009 by presenting two proposals. the first is to strengthen the exchange of information provided for in the savings directive. the second proposal is to propose that all member states align their standards for exchanging information to the most open level, in particular by ensuring that banking secrecy cannot be invoked by a member state to refuse to provide information that other member states would need for the taxation of their residents. in may 2008, the council decided to promote this policy of good tax governance covering the principles of transparency, exchange of information and fair tax competition to third countries and instructed the commission to negotiate clauses in agreements with third countries. the commission intends to present a political initiative in the near future to highlight the coherence of this policy and the key elements to ensure its success. the commission considers in particular that the implementation of coordinated measures at european level would be likely to respond to the concerns expressed by the honourable member.
in view of what has been said, it seems that "there were more voices than nuts", that is, "tax havens" and their end are not in question, but i would ask the following question: how does the commission intend to dissuade banks from operating in offshore centres, and how has it disclosed this intention? and what effective measures do you propose to combat the financial speculation which is at the root of the present financial and economic crisis?
question no 35 by mr eoin ryan (h-0055/09) subject: the use of the european parliament in the european parliament subject: information and communication technologies (ict) and the information society the commission has placed the development of icts and the information society at the heart of its programmes, which hold great potential for the european economy and society. however, what steps is the commission taking to ensure that certain categories of european society - in particular the elderly or people with low incomes - are not left behind or even forgotten in this process?

almost seven years after the barcelona european council, most member states are far from meeting the targets set. and yet childcare facilities are an essential condition for reconciling work, family and private life for both men and women. it is also a condition for the promotion of equality. i would remind you that family responsibilities are keeping more than six million european women out of the labour market. and yet there are 15 member states with a coverage rate below the european average that is far from the barcelona targets. for example, the czech republic, which currently holds the presidency of the european council, has a rate of less than 10% for coverage for the under-3 age group. it is therefore not surprising that the czech presidency's programme proposes a debate on the revision of the barcelona objectives. and more seriously, the czech presidency, and i quote, 'intends to take action on parental childcare and its relationship with employment policies, stressing the importance of home childcare as a real alternative to a professional career'. end of quote. you read it and you don't believe it. but it is true, the czech presidency wants to send women back home, it wants europe to go back years and years and european women to throw away the result of years of struggle for equality. yes, because the authors of such a proposal do not intend to send men home to take care of the children themselves. women have the same right to professional development as men. i would like to give the example of portugal, which has launched an ambitious programme of building childcare facilities, helping to stimulate the economy and create jobs while meeting the barcelona targets.
madam president, commissioner, i asked to speak in disagreement with my colleague edite estrela and that is what motivated me to speak when she caricatured the intentions of the czech presidency. no one wants to send women home, the problem is that there are women who want or would like to stay at home. there are even organizations that seek to defend them! women who are ignored, despised, discriminated against because they want to devote themselves to the family and we do not listen to them. and this is a question of freedom, it is a question of freedom of choice that the left likes to evoke so much but that, here, it forgets. it is a question of whether or not to respect the freedom of association of couples. it is about being able to provide those who want to do so with a better quality of life, with better parenting and with better parenting. that's what our society needs to do, too. we're not going to go there, we're not going to solve the problems of birth and happiness of people with prejudice. we solve with policies that are adjusted to reality and to the spontaneous wishes of the people. we're not going to get there with an obsession with the state and the market. let's go with a fair look at the family!
on behalf of the ppe-de group. the possible closure of qimonda will put at risk almost 2000 jobs in the north of portugal, which in a few years has gone from being one of the most industrialized regions in europe to one of the poorest in europe. it must be understood that the industrial fabric of northern portugal was based on traditional sectors, where textiles were of great importance, and precisely at the moment when it is in a decisive phase of conversion with restructuring processes that are always costly and painful, this case arises that, if implemented, will have a very strong impact not only on the region but also on the country. i know that qimonda's continued operations depend primarily on market forces and the will of the shareholders. but it will also be easy to recognise that, as qimonda is considered one of the main portuguese exporting companies and as it is also a key player in the conversion of the economic fabric of the region, we cannot accept that market forces alone should decide its future. this is why the portuguese and german authorities have been addressing the issue at the highest level, most recently through the portuguese president and chancellor merkel. and that is also why, commissioner špidla, if the portuguese government has not yet invited you to visit the region which is experiencing a real social emergency, i am inviting you, i want you to be able to see the seriousness of the situation, to support the efforts being made and to mobilise all the instruments at the disposal of the european commission to prevent distrust from spreading further throughout the region.
on behalf of the pse group. other in the current context of global financial and economic crisis, qimonda is a paradigmatic case. it is a company that uses cutting-edge technology, employs highly skilled workers and promotes research. qimonda is in line with the objectives of the lisbon strategy. the portuguese government has done everything possible to find a solution to make this undertaking viable, but the solution depends on the involvement of the german federal government and the state governments of bavaria and saxony. the portuguese government has already proposed to make available eur 100 million for this purpose. as i said, it has done everything it can and will continue to do, as was acknowledged by the workers of the german firm during the recent official visit of the president of the portuguese republic. the european commission and the member states have taken measures, and well, to save many banking institutions and to support certain industries such as the automobile industry. why not support qimonda as well? if we leave qimonda to its fate, the consequences will be very serious, not only will thousands of workers be thrown out of work in germany and portugal, but also an invaluable european intellectual heritage and many community funds that have been applied to qimonda will be lost. keeping qimonda in operation in germany and portugal is of such strategic importance to europe that it well justifies the support of the european union. commissioner, we must be consistent, and if we are, we will do everything to save qimonda. the point is, qimonda is not just any company!

the rapporteur. - mr president, mr president of the commission, ladies and gentlemen, the present crisis is the greatest the european union has ever known. she's unfortunately far from her end. bankruptcies are still not contained and unemployment is continuing to rise. never before has the european project been subjected to such a severe test. and our common response will depend not only on the strength of the relaunch, but probably on the very continuity of the european project, at least on the speed of our deepening and enlargement. we did not create the european union to limit ourselves to a single market in times of prosperity, or to return to national selfishness in times of crisis. the european project is a political project, a guarantee of peace, freedom and democracy, but in economic terms it is based on competition as well as solidarity and cohesion. and it really lives on the ability to give quality and opportunities for progress to all citizens, whatever their background. today, in this crisis, citizens are looking to europe for protection and action to help them pass urgently, without serious social disruption, the current critical phase to help them rediscover their future, to relaunch employment and enterprises around new and more sustainable development vectors. the lisbon agenda and the environmental commitments are inspiring designs, but they must be given substance and action as a matter of urgency. in this sense, parliament's voice to the council and the commission is clear, it is strong, it is audible. the convergence we have achieved in the vote in the economic and monetary committee illustrates this common will. and i hope that the vote that will take place here today will continue to prove that. we are working together, the various rapporteurs, the various parties, and i hope that this message will be conveyed and understood by the commission in this way. i would like to welcome the shadow rapporteurs, mr hökmark and mrs in 't veld in particular, and i hope that with the same determination today's vote will lead us to put this message into practice and to take it forward. the lessons to be learned from the causes of this crisis are of paramount importance today. it is indeed a very important guide which we must take up, the larosière report. it is a good basis for work, it recovers much of what we have already proposed here. however, its conclusions must in fact lead to immediate and timely action by the commission. and it is important that the european union should be present at the next g20 meeting on this matter. i think that in this context there are elements which are symbolic and i hope that parliament will vote today unequivocally against offshore and tax havens. it is not enough, however, to correct past mistakes, particularly in the area of financial supervision and regulation. today the damage is done and a relaunch programme that is commensurate with the union's responsibilities is needed. we welcome the commission's rapid initiative, but we are aware, and we must be clear, that the means and instruments of intervention are manifestly insufficient. parliament votes in favour of the commission's efforts to make the instruments available more flexible, more anticipatory and more rapid, but we must not forget that 85% of the resources available today are in the hands of the various countries that make up the european union. the countries of the union have never been so different from one another as they are today. some countries have the power, they have the tools to intervene, others are completely vulnerable and their toolbox is completely empty. there are countries with no room for manoeuvre at national level, there are countries unable to resist the violence of the simultaneous forces of the internal market, the single currency and globalisation, and among these, the new countries that have just joined the european project are among the most penalized. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i think that at the moment our message from parliament is broken down into a number of very clear, very precise messages, but one common idea, and that common idea is the objective: the commission's proposal is to be adopted by the council and the council, and the council will be consulted on the matter.
the neoliberal lisbon strategy has been one of the european union's essential instruments for promoting financial deregulation, privatising public services, liberalising markets and world trade, deregulating labour relations and undermining workers' rights, as exemplified by the proposals for the directive on working time and flexicurity. it is pointless to continue to insist on its deepening when the economic and social crisis which the implementation of this strategy has helped to create is worsening. what is needed is a break with the policies of neoliberal capitalism, which is responsible for the increase in unemployment, precarious work and poverty and which has widened social, regional and territorial inequalities. what is needed is an integrated european strategy for solidarity and sustainable development that focuses on the protection of productive sectors and public investment, through the effective strengthening of community funds to support countries with weaker economies, that is respectful of nature and creates jobs with rights, that promotes public services, increases purchasing power and makes a fair distribution of income to reduce poverty. the commission and the council are doing exactly the opposite.
the rapporteur. - madam president, mr president-in-office, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, the crisis is deeper than we expected and unemployment will be higher than we foresaw. there are good reasons to believe that the european stimulus provided is not enough, but it is clear that it is taking too long to reach the citizens. parliament's position is strong and clear, it has been and i hope it will continue to be. our aim is to maintain employment and to create new jobs through cohesion, solidarity, both territorial and social. at this time of crisis, citizens cannot accept a europe that does not give them an answer, a europe that is powerless in the face of the problems they are facing. what will parliament ask the commission to do? the commission is of course asking you, through these reports, to coordinate national interventions, to use all the means at your disposal at the moment to intervene and to give you all the conditions as a budgetary authority to make this happen. he asked the commission to launch a clear european initiative for employment and said it was important to have an agenda with a timetable for implementing the financial market regulation measures and to get credit into the real economy. but what is the council being asked to do? the council is called upon, above all, to rediscover the political will which is at the heart of the construction of the european project. the european union is competition, but it is also cohesion and solidarity. and there can be no single market without that guarantee, solidarity and cohesion. that is why we all delegated to the european level the national autonomy we had before joining this project.
in writing. it will only be possible to understand the serious socioeconomic situation that characterizes the eu countries, including portugal, if we are aware of the objectives of this "integration process" and how its policies are at the heart of the causes of the current capitalist crisis, of which the eu is one of the epicenters. during the last 23 years the eec/eu has promoted the circulation of capital and the financialization of the economy, liberalized and boosted privatization, concentrated and installed overproduction, relocated and destroyed productive capacity, promoted economic dominance of some at the expense of dependence on others, encouraged the exploitation of workers and the increasing transfer of productivity gains from labour to capital, centralized the wealth created, increased social inequalities and regional asymmetries, all under the baton of the great powers and the big financial and economic groups - this is the origin of the incurable capitalist crisis. it is not the 'crisis' but the policies inherent in capitalism that are at the origin of unemployment, precariousness, low wages, the degradation of living conditions, poverty, disease, hunger, the growing difficulties faced by workers and populations. we therefore welcome the big demonstration of the cgtp-in, the great centre of portuguese workers, on 13 march, to change course, for more jobs, wages and rights!
rapporteur. − madam president, it is just to change the wording in paragraph 93 concerning sound state finance to ‘as soon as possible’, instead of ‘when economic conditions allow’, as agreed with the shadow rapporteurs.
in writing. − the first amending budget for 2009 concerns the mobilisation of the eu solidarity fund for romania following the floods that struck that country in july 2008. in view of the direct loss estimated at some eur 471.4 million, only eur 11.8 million is proposed to be mobilised under this fund (and only now!), which once again demonstrates the urgent need for its revision. the aim of this fund is to enable a rapid, effective and flexible response to 'emergency situations' in the various member states, and we therefore support, despite all the shortcomings, its mobilisation in favour of romania. however, the eur 11.8 million will be budgeted at the expense of the reduction in the funds allocated to the european regional development fund - convergence objective. in other words, the 'solidarity' with romania is financed through the funds allocated to the economically less developed countries and regions, including romania itself! this is what we can call solidarity between the 'poor', i.e. between the so-called 'cohesion countries'/convergence regions... we disagree with the use of 'cohesion' funds - especially in a context of deepening socio-economic crisis - when there are other funds, such as those for the militarisation of the eu.
in writing. - the crisis we are experiencing, more than an economic and financial crisis, is above all a crisis of confidence, and its most dramatic sign is the high level of unemployment. but unemployment involves not only loss of income, for it also leads to loss of confidence in oneself and in others. if we are to restore confidence, we must have a very clear medium-term strategy. the role of political leaders is decisive in this respect, because of the signals and messages they convey. prudence, safety, truthfulness, resisting the easy-going propaganda of unattainable goals, and self-praise are some of the good practices that can help restore trust. on the other hand, jobs must be created and, for that, the conditions must be created for investment by companies. we must act quickly, because if nothing is done at this level, the financing difficulties faced by the most deficit countries in the euro zone will lead to deepening recession, to a continuing increase in unemployment, to loss of income for firms and households. i therefore supported the report by mr andersson, who proposed that the employment guidelines be maintained in 2009.
in writing. - it is not understandable or acceptable to extend or postpone the right of all members to address the european parliament in their own language and to have all documents drawn up in the official languages. several years have passed since the accession of some of the countries whose language use is still limited, notably ireland and the czech republic, but the necessary linguists have not been found. the justifications put forward are vague and inconsistent, but the financial priorities have not been formed, which makes us doubt their objectives. we cannot accept that the inalienable right to cultural and linguistic diversity in the eu is called into question, which would also affect portuguese. we cannot accept this discrimination. we have once again shown our uncompromising commitment to safeguarding the cultural identity of each member state and all national languages as working languages, and in this respect we could only vote against this decision. it is, after all, the cultural and linguistic transposition of the eu's budgetary policies, which give priority to investment in armaments rather than to the promotion of culture and the safeguarding of employment.
in writing. − in the european union (eu) we have seen rising levels of poverty, precarious work and inequalities, which may worsen in the context of the current economic and financial crisis, as forecasts point to a downward trend and an increase in the number of unemployed. this has been helped by the policies enshrined in the lisbon strategy and the european employment strategy, which promote financial deregulation, market liberalisation and precarious employment links. so what was needed was a break with these policies. but in the face of worsening social and economic conditions, the eu's response (or lack of response) reflects its class choices, insisting on the continuation of policies that promote the accumulation of colossal profits by the big economic and financial groups, to the detriment of the living conditions of workers and populations. what is needed is a reversal of the current macroeconomic policies and the defence of employment and workers' rights. an alternative policy is needed to ensure a fair distribution of resources, stimulate economic activity, create jobs, strengthen the role of the state in the economy, boost demand, encourage the growth of micro, small and medium-sized enterprises and strengthen investment, taking into account the needs and specificities of each member state.
in writing. - the lisbon strategy was conceived in a context and for an economic context clearly different from the one we are currently in. this does not, however, imply a revision of all the concepts underlying it. the distinction between the exceptional nature of the present circumstances and the policies to be pursued to promote european development and competitiveness in the long term must be made. but it is important not to take away from this distinction the idea that the crisis situation requires measures contrary to good policies. on the contrary, i did. the response to the present situation, even if it requires exceptional measures, must be guided by the ideas of good policies, of the option of investing in innovation and in europe's competitiveness, otherwise they will not help to respond to the crisis and prepare the member states of the european union for the next phase of the world economy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament's resolution on the fight against climate change. the european union must maintain its leadership position in international climate policy and make every effort to reach an agreement in copenhagen that will reduce carbon dioxide emissions into the atmosphere and limit the global temperature increase to less than 2oc above pre-industrial levels. in the context of the current financial and economic crisis, reaching a new agreement to combat climate change in copenhagen is crucial. the economic crisis and the climate crisis can be combined with important economic opportunities to develop new technologies and create jobs.
in writing. - there are positive aspects to this resolution which we value. we stress the points where the european union is urged to actively seek an agreement in copenhagen that takes account of the latest scientific reports on climate change, to work towards stabilising target levels and temperatures that offer a strong chance of avoiding dangerous climate change, and to provide for regular reviews to ensure that targets are in line with the latest science. the call for a significant increase in financial resources to enable the necessary mitigation initiatives in developing countries is also welcome. but we disagree that, even if only in recitals, it insists on the eu's emissions trading scheme, even admitting that it can serve as a model for the development of emissions trading in other developed countries and regions. we also disagree with the economic view which clearly influences several points in the resolution.
in writing. - although some specific positive proposals were adopted, which we voted in favour of, particularly on tax havens, unfortunately most of our group's proposals were rejected and the main line of the report is the continuation of neoliberal policies with one or another pink brush for the voter to see on the eve of an election campaign. among our rejected proposals, i would highlight those calling for a significant increase in financial resources and a more rapid application of the means to support employment, a reorientation of programmes to support the most vulnerable groups, including programmes to guarantee decent living conditions and access to high quality public services for all. i also regret that they rejected proposals such as those which considered the amount of the recovery plan (1.5% of eu gdp) to be insufficient to successfully deal with the current crisis, noting that the eu will fall far behind countries such as the us and china. i also regret that the criticism of the commission for linking the relaunch plan to the deepening of neo-liberal 'structural reforms' and strict observance of the stability and growth pact has been rejected, when what was needed was to eliminate them and change policies.
in writing. - the elisa ferreira report on the european economic recovery plan, although it contains positive elements, suffers from the same problem as the plan itself: a picture of the situation without a full understanding of the causes of the current crisis being definitively established, a sum of initiatives that it is hoped will restore the confidence of economic agents without, however, so far identifying signs of this effect and a weak capacity for european mobilisation. it is also true that the report does not provide much in the way of practical solutions, because the european parliament has little capacity to do so. the same applies to the european commission. only 15% of the plan's budget will be managed at community level. the answer will therefore have to be found at european level, yes, but first and foremost through the political will of the member states to coordinate their responses with the current economic situation. the impetus must come from the member states. if he comes. because there are worrying signs of a lack of political will in europe at the moment. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. the major issue that this crisis brings is the rise in unemployment, a situation which can only be reversed by increasing investment. in order to get more investment, credit must be accessible and cheap, but all indications are that it will be scarce in the near future and much more expensive for more vulnerable countries, such as portugal. these countries face increased financing difficulties, and i therefore strongly support the possibility of a single central issuer of european public debt at the euro area level, which is the scenario most compatible with the long-term sustainability of the euro. in the present circumstances, it is imperative to reactivate the european credit market by providing responsible lending to viable businesses and households. financial aid to banks and businesses must also be well targeted, temporary, transparent, cost-effective and strictly controlled. the solidity and solidarity of the european project may be at stake, and it is therefore essential to act in a coordinated manner and to respect the rules of the internal market without resorting to protectionism. i support the report on the european economic recovery plan by my colleague elisa ferreira because it is in line with these broad guidelines.
in writing. - do not count on us to highlight the hollow 'european economic recovery plan' which will be largely self-financed by each member state (the 'european solidarity' at its best...), and which does not call into question the neoliberal policies which are at the root of the worsening of working and living conditions for the overwhelming majority of the population. it is therefore not surprising that the majority of the ep rejected our proposals which: - denounced that, at a time when the eu's socioeconomic crisis is worsening, the eu budget for 2009 is 'the lowest ever'; (i) insisted on strengthening the structural funds and the cohesion fund; - stressed that the additional 'advances' of these funds would mean a reduction in community funding for the coming years; - criticised the under-implementation of these funds, particularly in the context of worsening socioeconomic conditions in the eu; - demanded that these funds be considered as an expenditure objective and proposed an increase in the rate of community co-financing and the abolition of the n+2 and n+3 rule in respect of these funds; - insisted that these funds should be effectively used to promote real convergence, thus abandoning their continued subordination to the neoliberal objectives of the 'lisbon strategy'; - they insisted on fighting the relocation of companies.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the sis ii system is fully operational. the second generation of sis represents a community approach to the need to strengthen security at the external borders and involves important innovations such as biometric data and the interconnection of alerts. we agreed that this entry into operation could only happen when we have a robust system that is fully operational 24 hours a day. i believe that the time has come to take responsibility, to make a thorough assessment of the situation and to find solutions which will make this project technically viable and restore its already fragile credibility. we know that several tests were carried out last year, the final result of which was negative, namely the 'operational system test'. the council and the commission decided to set a deadline of four months in an attempt to resolve the existing problems, although without much success, as can be seen from the results obtained in december 2008 with the repeat of the tests. although some improvements have been made, there are still, as far as we know, major problems with the performance and robustness of the system, the loss of messages, the quality of the data and the process of synchronisation between the national copies and the central system. the sis ii will never be able to start operations until these problems are solved. i express my doubts about the ability of the contractor to solve all these problems in such a short time that it was not able to solve them earlier with more time. i expect an independent audit of the project to be launched to ascertain responsibility. i have no objection to the alternative technical scenario of the evolution of 'sis i for all' to sis ii, provided that the legal framework approved for sis ii is fully respected. an evaluation and comparison report on the two scenarios is due to be submitted by the end of march. parliament wants access to this study and on behalf of the ppe-de group. madam president, commissioner, i would like to take up the words already spoken here years ago by my colleague eija-riitta korhola on 13 march 2006. she described the situation regarding access to clean water to us as follows: the numbers are alarming, 3,900 children die every day from lack of clean water, a fifth of the world's population, that's about 1.1 billion people who lack clean water. more than 40% of the world's population has no access to running water and sanitation. three years have passed since these findings and what is happening? what is happening is that the global picture remains worryingly the same, and that cannot but worry us. we are facing a serious sanitation crisis today that calls on all of us. i would remind you that this problem affects the poorest and most underdeveloped regions of the world, particularly sub-saharan africa. this remains the area most affected by poor water quality, particularly in rural areas and in the slums surrounding large cities. but the problem is vast. i have here with me a unicef brochure dating back to 2001, but essentially their verification remains and is impressive. what does it tell us? it tells us that this billion is spread out a little bit around the world. these 1 billion people without clean water: 4% in the middle east and north africa, 4% in central and eastern europe, 19% in south asia, 25% in sub-saharan africa, 42% in east africa and the pacific. and if you look at the indicators within each of these spaces, it's the east and pacific and sub-saharan africa regions that give us the most alarming numbers, with 24 percent and 43 percent of their populations, respectively, still lacking access to safe drinking water in 2000, at the beginning of the decade. it is worth noting the health complications, some of them fatal, that arise from these deficiencies, and how they affect the development and progress of populations deprived of the quality and abundance of this essential good, as well as the border tensions that access to water causes and how these risk to worsen if nothing is done to prevent them. the european union, as a global actor, cannot, as a major contributor to the global effort to tackle this problem, refrain from taking part in the main debates on this subject. i welcome the relations which the commissioner has established with us here. i therefore also welcome the holding and european participation in this fifth world water forum. this will be another opportunity for all the main actors to discuss the issue objectively and prepare a clear approach to this problem. i can only support this effort, which is also shared by the whole development committee, to promote subsidiarity and, since there are many responsibilities here at local level, i can only sympathize with the other concerns of our committee. water, ladies and gentlemen, is a commodity essential to life, to the life of each of us, to the life of humanity.
madam president, commissioner, anyone who visits sub-saharan africa easily recognizes, in most countries, the enormous weakness of their health systems and the extremely negative impact that this weakness has on the lives and health of the populations that these services are supposed to serve. the indicators constantly published at international level continue to confirm this. and to that extent, it is absolutely disturbing that simple, practical gestures, even if not very elaborate, not even particularly costly, could be enough to save many lives. european financial support can be crucial in this respect and we must always bear in mind that cooperation in the field of health is truly strategic and directly crosses not only one of the millennium goals but many of the millennium goals. the court of auditors found that, i quote, 'community funding for the health sector has not increased since 2000 in proportion to its total development aid, despite the commitments made by the commission in relation to the millennium development goals and the health crisis in sub-saharan africa'. end of quote. and it recognised, and i quote again, 'the commission has contributed significantly through its funding to the creation of the global fund to fight aids, tuberculosis and malaria, but has not paid the same attention to strengthening health systems which was supposed to be one of its priorities'. end of quote. according to the court, this was because, i quote again, 'the commission did not have sufficient experience in health matters to ensure that the funding of the aid it granted to this sector was used in the best possible way'. end of quote. this is therefore a strong challenge that the court of auditors is making directly to the european commission, and i am following it. for our part, i would like to reiterate the same challenge, which is now based on the objectivity of this data and this assessment. health care is already part of it, but it must be increasingly part of our development aid priorities and deserves increased funding. optimising the way aid is delivered, bearing in mind the apparently antagonistic needs for coordination in its management and proximity to the beneficiary populations, is a service that can save many lives. the european commission must respond positively to this challenge and i urge it to do so. mr bowis has just made a very moving speech to us here and has been able to put faces, human faces, in the dry cold of these figures from the court of auditors. the challenge for us, commissioner, is that our cooperation should be able to put a look of joy and hope on those very faces. and for that, commissioner, it is essential that the figures in the area of health in our cooperation should be different.
