the changes in latin america should encourage the european union to develop new cooperative relations with its countries, paying greater attention to social and cultural aspects, and supporting development in a framework of mutual respect for the diversity of levels of development and political choices of its peoples. unfortunately, these are minor aspects in the proposals that the european union is putting forward. in general, its main interest is economic, aimed at securing the business of the largest european economic and financial groups, a situation to which social organizations, particularly in brazil, have warned as we have seen in the last visit to brazil of the parliamentary delegation for mercosur. at a time when, for example, in mexico, the majority of the population is suffering the consequences of a severe economic recession and the overwhelming majority of the mexican banking sector is controlled by foreigners, mainly european banks, it is regrettable that the european union continues to use its agreement with mexico more as a gateway to the united states than to support local development and is contributing to the destruction of its small and mediumsized enterprises, its productive fabric, particularly its industrial fabric, because of its insistence on free trade, the liberalization of strategic sectors and the commodification of essential goods such as water. a fundamental change is therefore needed in the european union's policies on partnership agreements, in order to give priority to cooperation and economic and social development, contributing to the creation of jobs with rights, social progress, the promotion of the rights of indigenous peoples, the protection of their forests and biodiversity, with recognition of the sovereign right of latin american countries to quality public services, control over strategic sectors of their economy and respect for the decisions of the institutions chosen by their peoples.
mr president, as the czech presidency is not in the house, i shall speak in english because i believe the message will get through quicker. i draw your attention to paragraph 1(l) of the proposal for a recommendation on the partnership with brazil and also to the communication from the commission of september 2008 on multilingualism. the fact is that to establish and to develop a strategic partnership with brazil we speak in portuguese. when we go to the usa or australia, we speak english; when we go to mexico or colombia, we speak spanish; when we go to brazil or angola, we speak portuguese; when we go to senegal or côte d’ivoire, we speak in french. this is key to communication; this is key to doing business. this draws attention to what i called a few years ago the ‘european world’ languages: linguas europeias globais in portuguese. i mean that some european languages have the ability to establish a very intimate and close connection with various parts of the world, and these are: english, spanish, portuguese, french and, to a lesser extent and on different grounds, german and italian. the commission fully understood this and put it in the communication, but, unfortunately, there were misunderstandings in the council – mainly on the part of the germans, i believe – and the council passed a much weaker line in this matter. i have to clarify that this does not affect, by any means, the equal value of official languages in the union. this has to do with the internal vision of multilingualism, and we all agree that every citizen has the right to speak, to read and to be answered in his own language. however, this adds another dimension in the broad field of the external value of multilingualism. to have those european world languages in today’s globalised world, in today’s globalised economy, in this global village, which is cultural, economic, social and political, is a most valuable asset for the entire eu, which we must take full account of and full benefit from. that is why i ask that these languages be properly introduced and managed inside the youth external services and also taught in our schools, as a common asset, as second, third or fourth languages, because these languages, as our relations with brazil clearly show, increase our eu capacity to relate closely worldwide: to mingle more, to really share, to be part of the same club. this is my appeal to the council and i greet and thank the rapporteur for her support.
in writing. - although the present situation shows that the neoliberal lisbon strategy is one of the instruments responsible for the worsening economic and social situation in the european union, the report insists on its implementation, which we disagree with. however, there are positive aspects in the report, aspects which we support, particularly in relation to the defence of the needs of researchers, their rights in terms of working conditions and social security, family reunification, the rights of women researchers and access for young people, the call for increased financial resources for research and for the involvement of a greater number of researchers. but it is not clear how the proposed european research strategy will guarantee equal rights for all member states and general access for researchers, especially young researchers, to the european research partnership, especially from countries like portugal, which is not at the centre of policy decisions in a european union that increasingly operates around a directory of the great powers. that is why we abstained on the report.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament's resolution on the problem of the degradation of agricultural land in southern europe, because i consider it necessary to include in the cap guidelines instruments aimed at combating the effects of climate change and protecting soils. the importance of establishing a european drought observatory and of strengthening the eu's coordinated fire response capacity should be underlined.
in writing. - the attitude of the ppe in rejecting several of the proposals in this report and in having its alternative proposal, which we rejected, approved is regrettable. the fact is that, despite a number of shortcomings, we agree with many of the points made in the report that has been presented, namely that agriculture is the best way to prevent soil degradation and that a well-founded strategy is needed to help maintain this activity. we also consider the role of the agricultural population in combating desertification and the crucial role of the producer in maintaining the vegetation cover of the regions affected by persistent droughts to be important. we also agree with the statement that intensive farming, promoted to a large extent by agro-industry, is a negative contributor to soil erosion, rendering it unproductive. we believe, however, that more should have been done to hold eu agricultural policies and governments such as those that have governed portugal accountable, because they have been the ones that have encouraged the over-exploitation of land and water and environmental degradation. we still believe that these problems can be overcome by a break with these agricultural policies. we are in favour of linking agricultural aid to production, to enable the growth of agri-food production in countries like portugal and, in general, the modernisation of their primary sector.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament's motion for a resolution on the 2008 progress report on turkey. given the slowdown in turkey's reform process, it is necessary for the turkish government to show political will to continue the reform process it committed to in 2005, towards a more democratic and pluralistic society.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament resolution on water resources in the context of the world water forum, as i believe that there is an urgent need to define global policies on water management and water resources, which will enable the millennium development goals (mdgs) to be met, namely to halve the number of people without access to safe drinking water by 2015. despite the global financial crisis, it is considered necessary for member states to strengthen support to the least developed countries through official development assistance and cooperation on adaptation to and mitigation of the effects of climate change.
in writing. - the water sector is also caught up in the privatisation and liberalisation campaign of the majority in the european parliament. it is true that it is stated, albeit in the conditional, that 'water is a common good of mankind and should constitute a fundamental and universal right' and that 'water should be considered a public good and be subject to public control'. but what follows is serious and unacceptable. to consider that, although water may be under public control, if it can be granted, "in whole or in part", to the private sector, its management is to keep the part of investment in infrastructure for collection and supply in the public domain, giving the private sector the profitable part, the collection from consumers. these experiments have already been carried out in several countries, particularly in latin america, where prices have risen exponentially and quality has deteriorated. we also cannot accept that agriculture should be held responsible by putting agribusiness and small farmers on an equal footing and attacking the latter with high water prices. when the capitalist crisis is accentuated, water seems to be a desirable asset to enable the profits that capital so needs. we still believe that water should be maintained exclusively as a public good, both in its abstraction and its supply.
in writing. i have voted in favour of this report. brazil was the last of the brics to enter a summit with the eu, which took place in july 2007 during the portuguese eu presidency. it was also a natural reflection of the relations that portugal has always maintained with brazil. as i said here in september 2007, this is a country whose 200 million inhabitants speak one of the most widely spoken european languages in the world, portuguese, and whose historical, civilizational and cultural traditions are closely related to those of europe. the various political pacts that have marked history down to our day bear witness to this. these relations will cement other bridges with latin america. given its recognised potential and brazil's current economic and political performance at regional and global level, this strategic partnership should not be seen as a future impediment to other partnerships with mercosur. it should be hailed as an example where the eu has achieved the necessary consensus on common commercial and political interests. it should be noted that both sides consider multilateral action to be necessary, based on the united nations system and within the framework of the wto. finally, i must say that i am somewhat curious about the future scope of the cooperation protocols for education and culture.
madam president, i would ask the honourable members to excuse me for taking a few minutes. i would like to speak about china. there was a motion for a resolution today on china, on the case of mr gao zhisheng, a well-known lawyer, who was arrested. there's a fear he'll be tortured; his family has just been accepted into the united states and there are fears for his life. but unfortunately, because only three subjects can be discussed, the proposal concerning mr gao zhisheng could not be accepted. we had intended to put it to the march ii part-session. we were told that there would be no emergency in march ii because, as a rule, when there are two plenary sessions in the same month, there are no emergencies in the second. i doubt that interpretation. this interpretation relates to the double sessions in september and, before that, in october, during which the budget was discussed. the fact that there are two sessions in march is due to the elections; this is a very special case. this means that human rights issues could not be dealt with until the end of april, which is too late. i therefore ask the presidency, firstly, to look into this matter and, secondly, to express to the chinese embassy our great concern about him. to this end, i can send you the case of mr gao zhisheng, because no one really knows where he is and there are fears that he is being tortured and that his life is in danger.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the development of the acp countries. what is happening there, and which we deplore, namely the assassinations of the president and the chief of staff, we cannot forget that it is still the result of all the difficulties and all the divisions that persist there and that, too, are still continuing and that result from that colonial past. it is also necessary to take account of the fact that guinea-bissau remains one of the poorest countries in africa, and this means that the european union should pay greater attention to cooperation in the areas of public health and education, with a view to improving the living conditions of its population and overcoming the difficulties that a large part of the people of guinea-bissau still face, particularly women, mothers and children. it is essential that the european union's solidarity support for these populations be strengthened. support is also needed for education, drinking water and, in some cases, even agricultural production to ensure food for the entire population. but this support must be provided without outside interference and with respect for the sovereignty and choices of its people.
madam president, the truth is that we knew the order to expel the ngos was coming, and that is why i reaffirm that my preference is for a firm and clear line that is also pragmatic. it is very easy to play the hero in the coffee shop, miles and miles away. those of us who visited darfur in july 2007 and went to al-geneina and al-fashir, to nyala and kapkabia and to many idp camps around those cities, are fully aware of the suffering of the people of darfur and the tremendous job that ngos do there. it is therefore essential to protect the remaining ngos and to work by all means for them to stay, and also to fully support those that will continue, including religious charities. i also support the exerting of more pressure on china, which, on the one hand, does not apply the needed pressure on the khartoum authorities and, on the other hand, delays or blocks more effective measures at un level. i also support mr tannock’s idea that ‘any way out is a good way out’. if president al-bashir goes away and his regime is put out of the way, that would be a tremendous relief and help for the people of darfur and sudan. that is not impunity – impunity is to continue as you are for many years.

the rapporteur. - the commission's communication entitled 'multilingualism, an asset for europe and a shared commitment' is part of a long series of documents produced by the european parliament, the commission, the council, the committee of the regions and the economic and social committee, in which the issue of multilingualism is addressed from various angles. in my report i reiterate the positions previously taken by the european parliament and i generally agree with the commission's position on the enormous competitive advantage represented by the cultural and linguistic diversity of the european union and also on the need to support language teaching and cultural and educational exchange programmes within and outside the union. i also support the importance of language as a factor of social inclusion. i would like to stress the importance of dialogue with other regions of the world, taking account of the linguistic, historical and cultural links between the countries of the union and third countries. i am referring to the need for policies to support translation, both literary and technical. i am addressing the issue of multilingualism in the audiovisual sector, the need to support language teachers, the extension of the development of language proficiency indicators to all the official languages of the european union, without prejudice to other languages spoken and studied in europe, including classical greek and latin. as regards language teaching, both in school and in adult life, among many other aspects, i maintain that learning the mother tongue is basic to any and all learning. that parents and educators should be able to choose the official language in which children are to be educated in countries where there is more than one official language or where official language and regional languages coexist. i further submit that no child should be deprived of education in the official language of the state. i would point out that nowhere in my report is the importance of regional or minority languages disputed, which i of course recognize and respect, and nowhere is it intended to undermine them. there is not a single aspect of my report that is incompatible with them. but in the meantime an alternative proposal has emerged, endorsed by my socialist, liberal and green colleagues, which simply abolishes the three points i have just mentioned. the principles of fundamental rights and individual freedom, long established, accepted and practised in the european union, are thus being eliminated under pressure from galician, catalan and basque nationalists, to whom the european parliament would have given in if it voted in this way. these intentions are perfectly visible. in today's spanish newspaper el país, on page 37, it is stated that only three months ago the supreme court ruled that a house should be put on the pre-enrolment list, asking parents in which language they want their children to have their first education, which the catalan generalitat did not do. i don't think the alternative should have been accepted. in the light of the subjects covered in my report, it amounts to an unacceptable protectionism of exacerbated nationalisms and it also clashes in an unacceptable way with fundamental rights and freedoms and even with the principle of subsidiarity, not to mention that this alternative is also contrary to principles already enshrined in previous texts of the european parliament and the council and never put into question. i therefore call the full attention of this house. the alternative presented must be rejected and my report, which was already adopted in the committee on culture and education on 17 february, with 20 votes in favour, 3 against and 8 abstentions, must be adopted. mr president, the european union must be a place of respect for democracy and pluralism, not a place of exclusion or the arbitrary curtailment of fundamental rights and freedoms.
the community is developing and pursuing its action to strengthen its economic and social cohesion by reducing the disparity between the levels of development of the various regions and the backwardness of the most disadvantaged regions and islands, including rural areas. in the light of the current debate on the future of cohesion policy, which is intended to include the so-called territorial cohesion dimension, the following essential principles should be emphasised: first, structural policy should have as its first and foremost objective the promotion of real convergence, acting as a redistributive instrument, in the face of the costs, inequalities and asymmetries fostered by the internal market, economic and monetary union and the liberalisation of international trade, for the economically less developed countries and regions in the european union. secondly, so-called competitiveness cannot be a substitute for convergence in member states and regions which are lagging behind in their socioeconomic development, and cohesion policy and the financial means associated with it should not be subordinated to the competition and liberalisation advocated by the lisbon strategy. thirdly, so-called territorial cohesion should contribute to economic and social cohesion, i.e. it should have as its central objective the reduction of disparities between the levels of economic development of the various regions and the backwardness of the most disadvantaged regions. fourthly, new objectives and priorities must be matched by new community financial resources, i.e. the financing of so-called territorial cohesion must not be carried out to the detriment of the convergence objective. fifthly, the current community financial resources for cohesion policy are insufficient to meet the needs of real convergence, regional disparities, high levels of unemployment, income inequalities and poverty in the european union. sixthly, the strengthening of the community budget for the promotion of economic and social cohesion is a requirement. seventhly, the management and planning of the territory is a matter for each member state. finally, and in addition to other important aspects not highlighted here, we reiterate that it is unacceptable for regions to be financially harmed by the so-called statistical effect, and that measures should be taken to counteract this effect.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking everyone for their work. the three lines defined in the green paper concentration, interconnection and cooperation can provide solutions to some of the obstacles to the union's harmonious development, namely the negative effects associated with the concentration of economic activity, inequalities in access to markets and services resulting from distance and the divisions imposed not only by borders between member states (especially the most disadvantaged ones) but also between regions. we must therefore promote the improvement of synergies between these policies with methods of effective assessment of their territorial impact. that is why i have always advocated the definition of additional qualitative indicators with the aim of improving the design and implementation of the corresponding policies on the ground, taking into account the different territorial specificities. for the time being, gdp remains the only eligibility criterion for structural fund support. however, this definition of additional indicators and the carrying out of territorial assessments should not lead to more bureaucracy or delays, but rather to simplification in the implementation of new policies and actions to support territorial cohesion. the fifth and final interim report, madam president, makes specific reference to the transition regions between the convergence regions and the regions of competitiveness and employment. it must be borne in mind that these regions need a clearer status, with more security and stability for their development.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would first like to congratulate mrs krehl and mr nistelrooij on their reports and on the openness they have shown to the contributions of their colleagues. its reports recognise the importance of best practice as a factor for generating synergies, particularly in the fields of the environment and energy and employment, and link the debate on territorial cohesion to the debate on the future of cohesion in the european union. i agree with the green paper's assessment, share the analysis of the concept of territorial cohesion and subscribe to the recommendations for the future of territorial cohesion contained in these reports, in particular the definition of territorial cooperation, the publication of a white paper on territorial cohesion, the reinforcement of the objective of european territorial cohesion, the integration of territorial cohesion in the future definition of all community policies, the definition of additional qualitative indicators, the evaluation of the territorial impact of community policies and the proposal of means of creating synergies between territorial and sectoral policies, the design of a global strategy for regions with specific geographical characteristics, in particular outermost regions, the creation of a more comprehensive system of transitional and gradual support for the so-called regions and the creation of a transition to articulated territorial governance at european, regional, national and local levels. i therefore ask my colleagues to support these reports and to ask the member states and the commission to follow up on them.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, in the context of this great debate on territorial cohesion and the future of economic and social cohesion policy, i cannot fail to mention the very specific problems of the outermost regions, regions which, because of the combination and permanence of a number of geographical factors, reveal a great economic and social vulnerability, particularly felt in the context of a serious international crisis such as the one we are currently experiencing. i would therefore like to call on the european commission, and in particular on commissioner hübner, to pay particular attention to the effects of the current crisis in the outermost regions. an assessment of these effects in each of the outermost regions, in particular in tourism, construction and the increase in unemployment, would be important to allow a specific european response for these regions. i therefore challenge the european commission to prepare a european response to the crisis for the outermost regions that goes beyond the measures already announced under the economic and social cohesion policy for all european regions. a specific european response to the crisis for the outermost regions... (the president cut the speaker off)
the rapporteur. - mr president, my report is in line with all the documents on multilingualism that precede it, whether they are from this house, the council or the commission. the alternative presented is an attempt to bring to the european parliament some nationalist quarrels which take place in spain. the spanish newspaper el país reported yesterday that the spanish supreme court ruled three months ago that there should be a space on pre-enrolment forms for parents to indicate in which language they would like their children to be educated, and that the catalan authorities are not complying with the ruling. the supporters of the alternative do not want this right to be recognised for parents in countries with more than one official or regional language. they do not want to recognise the vital importance of learning in the mother tongue for the success of education in general and of learning other languages in particular. they do not want full intelligibility between the languages spoken in a country that is in this situation, especially with regard to the elderly and the legal system of health, administration and employment. they do not accept that in these countries one language should not be promoted at the expense of the rights of speakers of another. this contradicts everything that this parliament and the other european bodies have been saying. so i am deleting points 11, 12, 14 and 17 in the alternative. in the face of these points, such negative positions conflict with fundamental rights and freedoms and are in clear violation of the principle of subsidiarity. the report does not attack or undermine the so-called minority languages. it respects them and recognizes their value, but it does not for that reason fail to formulate general and basic principles. this house cannot be an instrument of exacerbated nationalism or regional or local hatred and aversion. our own responsibility as members of the european parliament is at stake. i therefore ask you to vote against the alternative and in favour of the report which i support.
in writing. i voted in favour of the maria martens report. one year after lisbon: the eu-africa partnership in action, as it reiterates the need to strengthen the links between the european union and africa, especially in the current context of global economic instability. i would like to stress that the eu-africa summit in lisbon in 2007 was mainly due to the work of the portuguese presidency of the council of the european union. however, there is still a long way to go to build on the joint strategy outlined at the time, particularly in the areas of peace, security, governance, human rights, regional integration, health and education. the european union needs to create a specific financial instrument for the implementation of the strategy, as well as a significant involvement of civil society.
in writing. - we voted against this report because we consider that the pressure which continues to be exerted to complete the process of ratification of the treaty of lisbon, in disregard of the result of the referendum in ireland, is unacceptable. if they had complied with the rules of the treaty in force and respected the sovereign decisions of the irish people, they should have withdrawn from the draft treaty of lisbon. what we are dealing with here is another anti-democratic position. it is unacceptable that the european parliament should talk about strengthening the eu's transparency and the participation of citizens in decision-making processes when the majority of its members have spoken out against holding referendums in their countries on the draft treaty of lisbon, precisely because they fear the majority opinion of their peoples. it is equally regrettable that they do not understand the opposing views of all those who consider themselves deceived by a capitalist integration which worsens inequalities, increases poverty and unemployment, contrary to what they had promised. even some of the positive points in the report are framed in a context which is more aimed at misleading public opinion and citizens through propaganda campaigns than at actually guaranteeing democratic participation and changing policies to meet the just aspirations of workers and the population.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report by mrs constanze khrel because it highlights best practice in a number of areas, including the environment and sustainable energy. this is the case of the socorridos hydroelectric power plant in the autonomous region of madeira, which has been recognised under the regiostars initiative. the report by mrs khrel also acknowledges major difficulties in preparing them, namely: - difficult to understand regulations, - unclear co-financing rules, - few opportunities for the exchange of experience, - few possibilities for interregional cooperation, and insufficient regional structures for such cooperation. the indicators should be strengthened, improved and thus the community's know-how in this field strengthened, particularly as regards:- respect for the principle of equal opportunities, guarantee of the principles of partnership and innovation of action,- rigor in the organization of projects, efficiency in the use of resources and clarity in their duration,- increase in the transferability of knowledge, so that it can also be applied in other regions of the european union. all these aspects are recognised in mrs khrel's report.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the dagmar roth-behrendt report on the cosmetics regulation (recast) because i consider it essential to strengthen the safety of cosmetics in order to ensure the protection of consumers' health by means of minimum requirements which must be met before the product is placed on the market. however, i regret that the obligation to identify the expiry date has not been introduced for all products, including those with a shelf-life of more than 30 months (article 15). it is important to emphasise that while the use of the expired cosmetic product does not cause any problems for the health of the consumer, it is also true that it does not promote the improvements it proposes.
in writing. - in this report, the european parliament broadly endorses the position of the european commission, which has decided to recast council directive 76/768/eec of 27 july 1976 on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to cosmetic products. the directive, which has been amended 55 times since its adoption in 1976, has become too complex and outdated, and no longer provides the necessary legal certainty in this constantly evolving field. with this recast, the commission intends to address the problem of legal uncertainty and to correct inconsistencies by establishing a series of definitions and implementing measures. in order to avoid divergences in national transposition, the commission has changed the legal form of the act from a directive to a regulation. another objective is to improve the safety of cosmetic products. as the cosmetics directive currently in force does not fully provide for the requirements for safety assessment, the commission sets out in this proposal the minimum requirements in this area. the rapporteur has reinforced the safety aspect in order to ensure the protection and health of consumers. what we think is right.
in writing. - i generally approve of the roth-behrendt report and the compromise reached with the council, but i regret a failure which i believe will be detrimental to consumer protection. the current directive and the proposed regulation stipulate that only cosmetic products with a shelf life of less than 30 months must have an expiry date. often, for economic reasons, this time limit is defined as more than 30 months. in this way, manufacturers ensure the disposal of all their products by not displaying a deadline. although the use of the cosmetic product after the expiry date does not normally cause health problems for the consumer, it does not promote the improvements that the consumer hopes to obtain. unfortunately, the legal services of parliament and the commission have, in my view, wrongly understood that this provision could not be changed in a recasting process.
in writing. - in this report, the european parliament agrees, with slight amendments, to the european commission's request to amend directive 98/8/ec on the placing of biocidal products on the market, which it considers to have become necessary after an evaluation of its application revealed that the ten-year period, until 14 may 2010, provided for the evaluation of active substances used in biocidal products with a view to their inclusion in the community positive list would not be sufficient. therefore, the transitional period during which the market for biocidal products will continue to be regulated by national legislation would expire without the establishment of the community positive list. in practice, this would mean that important products, such as disinfectants used in hospitals, would have to be withdrawn from the market as from 15 may 2010. to avoid this undesirable effect, the commission proposes to extend the transitional period by three years, until 14 may 2013. if these three years are still not sufficient, the commission shall introduce the possibility of extending the deadline further by means of a comitology decision. the rapporteur does not want this possibility to serve to postpone the whole process indefinitely, and she proposes limits.
in writing. - i approve the sarbu report and the commission proposal for a technical adaptation of existing legislation. the current directive provides for a transitional period (2000-2010) during which the biocidal products market will continue to be regulated by national legislation. the commission also proposes a 10-year programme for the evaluation of active substances used in biocides with a view to their inclusion in a positive list to be drawn up by the commission. as the current level of progress in the programme for the revision of this directive will not allow its completion by 2010, as was envisaged, it is necessary to make the necessary adjustments so that transitional periods for the most backward dossiers are possible, hence the relevance of this proposal for adaptation.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. female genital mutilation (fgm) not only causes very serious and irreparable damage to the physical and mental health of women, but is also a violation of fundamental human rights. because of the irreparable damage it causes, fgm must be treated by society as a very serious crime and be fought firmly. it is estimated that in europe some 500,000 women have been victims of this crime, and it is essential that the european commission and the member states work together to harmonise existing legislation in order to prevent and eradicate fgm in the european union.
in writing. i voted against the motion for a resolution which is an alternative to the vasco graça moura report. multilingualism, an asset for europe and a common commitment, because it runs counter to some of the principles that have been defended by the european parliament by abolishing the prerogative given to parents to choose the official language in which their children will be educated, in countries or regions with more than one official language or with regional languages. the alternative motion for a resolution, on the other hand, merely refers to the need to give special attention to pupils who cannot be educated in their mother tongue, undermining the basic principle of the importance of mother tongue learning in general education and in the learning of other foreign languages.
in writing. - it is a very positive document overall, and we subscribe to its assumptions and conclusions. the central question is whether it is applicable, since much remains to be done to ensure that, in the light of the present and the political developments in the european union, we can arrive at what is claimed to be necessary in the document. in some of the questions raised, such as the hegemony of some european languages over others, we start from a clearly negative situation. we have to point out in this connection the linguistic and cultural isolation to which certain national policies have led some peoples in the european area, with the consequent discrimination that follows. what is at stake is the cultural heritage of the european peoples themselves and its proper transmission to future generations. we also want to highlight the importance of the role of teachers, translators and interpreters as professionals whose work is essential for the teaching, dissemination and mutual understanding of the languages spoken in the eu. we therefore regret that the alternative proposal was adopted because it impoverishes some of the aspects of the graça moura report, in particular the protection of the mother tongue and the prerogative given to parents to choose the official language in which their children will be educated in countries or regions with more than one official language or with regional languages.
in writing. - the issue of multilingualism is of great importance in the european context. that is why i have taken part in a number of initiatives to promote and ensure its full respect. the europe i believe in is also the europe of linguistic diversity. for these reasons, i refer to the original report by mrs graça moura. however, like the author of the report, i do not accept that a correct idea of respect for linguistic diversity and individual and family freedom can or should be used as a weapon in a discourse of exacerbated nationalism. no, that must not be the nature of the discussion here. the promotion of multilingualism is, and should be, done in the name of respect for the diversity, the linguistic and cultural variety, of europe, in this case. but this is not the place for any other kind of fighting, especially when it involves the rejection of subsidiarity and freedom.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the lambert van nistelrooij report, given the importance of territorial cohesion in ensuring that the european union continues to follow a path of sustainable development and solidarity, increasingly turning its territorial cohesion and diversity into an asset. the green paper represents an important step towards deepening the union's territorial cohesion and shared competences and is also a stimulus to a broad debate on this subject at the level of the european institutions, national and regional authorities, the economic and social partners and the other actors involved in european integration and territorial development. i would like to stress as a positive aspect the value attached to the potential of the european territory and its diversity, as well as the three guidelines for a more balanced and harmonious development in each region: reducing the negative effects of differences in density, linking territories to overcome distances and cooperation to overcome divisions.
in writing. - i congratulated mr van nistelrooij on his report and particularly on his willingness to accept the various amendments to the draft report. this report deserves my full support! the commission therefore linked the debate on territorial cohesion to the debate on the future of eu cohesion policy, considering that the reform of the latter should incorporate the conclusions of the former. i agree with your assessment of the green paper, share your analysis of the concept of territorial cohesion and subscribe to the recommendations for the future of territorial cohesion that it presents, in particular on:- definition of territorial cohesion;- publication of a white paper on territorial cohesion;- strengthening the objective of european territorial cooperation;- integration of territorial cohesion in the future definition of all community policies;- definition of supplementary qualitative indicators;- assessment of the territorial impact of community sectoral policies and proposing means of creating synergies between territorial and sectoral policies;- design of a comprehensive european strategy for regions with specific geographical characteristics;- creation of a comprehensive system of gradual transitional support for transition regions;- and- creation of multi-level territorial governance. that is why i voted in favour!
in writing. the european parliament has voted in favour of the report, but has not yet adopted the commission's proposal. these proposals emphasised: structural policy should have as its first and foremost objective the promotion of real convergence, acting as a redistributive instrument for the less developed countries and regions in the european union; territorial cohesion will not be subordinated to the objective of reducing the backwardness of the least favoured regions; the new priorities will have to be matched by new community financial resources, especially as the current community financial resources are insufficient to meet the needs of real convergence; the need to strengthen the community budget in order to promote economic and social cohesion at european union level; the management and planning of the territory are the responsibility of each member state; measures should be taken to cancel out the so-called statistical effect; or that competitiveness should not be a substitute for convergence in member states and regions lagging behind in their socioeconomic development and that cohesion policy and the financial means associated with it should not be subordinated to the competition and liberalisation advocated by the lisbon strategy. one possible answer is that, at the very least, the proclaimed cohesion policy is indeed at risk...
in writing. - the debate on territorial cohesion is much more than a debate on the budget or on community policies; it is a debate on the guiding principles of one of the founding and fundamental lines of the idea of europe as we conceive it. the revision and the search for more appropriate solutions for cohesion policy in an enlarged europe of 27 member states, which is now going through an economic crisis, deserve our agreement and our applause. but this does not imply that we agree with or applaud the whole contents of the green paper. the three central guidelines of this review are correct, in the light of the criteria we have set out: reducing the negative effects of differences in population density, linking territories to overcome distances, cooperating to overcome divisions. but this cannot, by the very nature of the matter at hand, imply that the intensity of structural aid depends on the number of inhabitants concerned. on the contrary, i did. the idea of territorial cohesion itself must be based on the idea of making a space from which people are fleeing attractive and viable. that is one of our objectives and it must be clearly pursued. the idea of cohesion is basically an idea of solidarity to which we are all bound and from which we all ultimately benefit.
in writing. − this report is of crucial importance in the context of the eu's work on cohesion policy and cooperation and development policy. this line was also largely supported by the european parliament in its written declaration p6_ta(2008) 0199 adopted on 8/5/2008 of which i had the honour of being one of the first signatories. i therefore vote in favour of this report, because all kinds of investment and further research in this area are crucial and urgent, especially in the current context of the economic and financial crisis. so concrete and rapid action to make the system more credible and coherent is now what is required of the european commission and the council.
in writing. statements are being made that would have been unimaginable a year ago. but at the same time, the contradictions between those who are most responsible are one after the other, which shows, on the one hand, the attempt to evade responsibility for the serious consequences of neoliberal policies, and, on the other hand, the creation of a kind of dust which makes it difficult to see the paths they are following, which are basically the same, with very slight changes. it's, once again, the beginning of the end. something has to change to keep things the way they are, which visconti so well treated in his leopard. so while the british prime minister, gordon brown, said that the market exists to serve us, adding that we are not here to serve the market, without adding substantive measures that effectively discipline the market, limiting itself to insisting on measures of coordination and regulation of tax havens, the president of the european commission, durão barroso, insisted on the liberalisation of international trade and the insistence of the doha agenda. nothing was said about measures to end privatisations of public services and strategic sectors of the economies. nothing was said to end the liberal lisbon strategy or to defend jobs with rights, putting an end to attempts to change labour legislation for the worse.
i also join my colleagues in congratulating our rapporteur on the excellent work he has done in the committee on budgets and on the way he has fought for the rights and prerogatives of the european parliament. according to the judgment of the court of justice of 6 november 2008, the european parliament has full co-decision powers in this area. moreover, the european investment bank is clearly mandated to support economic and social development in a sustainable way for the integration of developing countries into the world market in the campaign to fight poverty and for these states to respect the rule of law, respect human rights and fundamental rights. we will be here to ensure that the eib's investments will meet these criteria.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, the report contains some aspects which, in a mild way, point to what we have been denouncing for a long time, namely that the current community budget falls short of the requirements which are required for a real and effective promotion of the proclaimed economic and social cohesion at the level of the european union. this is all the more so when, in the midst of the crisis, the insufficient ceilings agreed in the current financial framework for 2007-2013 are not respected (less than around eur 29 billion between 2007 and 2009) and the budgeted funds are not implemented as 'spending' objectives, in particular as regards convergence and agriculture and fisheries. on the other hand, new priorities are being added which contradict what we believe should be the aim and the central priority of the community budget: an instrument of redistribution in the face of the costs, inequalities and asymmetries fostered by the internal market, economic and monetary union, and the liberalisation of international trade for the economically less developed countries and regions of the european union. a community budget which gives priority to real convergence, based on social progress and the safeguarding and promotion of the potential of each country, the sustainable use of natural resources and the protection of the environment with the aim of economic and social cohesion, in addition to effective development aid. we also reaffirm that the community budget must be based on a fair contribution from each country on its gross national income, and we reject outright any attempt, more or less covert, to introduce european taxes.
at this point it should not be forgotten that we had a similar debate here last november, when it was already clear that we were facing a crisis in the capitalist system which required fundamental changes to improve the purchasing power of the majority of the population. that is why we insist on increasing wages and pensions and reforms through a fairer distribution of income. this remains the basic measure which can increase demand and thus ensure that the car industry and its components continue to have a secure market. unfortunately, the social situation is becoming more and more complicated, because the politicians are not taking the necessary measures, and unemployment and precarious and low-paid work are increasing. that is why we insist on new policies that give priority to employment with rights, that support industrial production in the countries of the european union, that oppose the strategy of the multinationals that use the pretext of the crisis to reduce jobs, increase the exploitation of those who work and increase profits. the european union's industries need to be properly supported in order to maintain and create more jobs with rights. but particular attention needs to be given to countries with weaker economies, such as portugal, by strengthening financial support to prevent unemployment and to support micro, small and medium-sized enterprises in the automotive and related industries, component manufacturers and small repair shops.
commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i agree that it is urgent that the vis should be able to start operations, and we need a community visa code. i acknowledge baroness ludford's work, but in good conscience i cannot support the compromise for a number of reasons. first, i disagree with the use of external service providers. when we approved sis ii, with the agreement of all the political groups, this parliament did not accept that private entities could have access to data, and at that time it was only car registration plates, which justifies the radical change in parliament's position, allowing private entities to collect this data, which is sensitive personal data, such as fingerprints. these companies may retain this data for one week, in accordance with the circumstances set out in the undertaking. secondly, there should be better data protection. diplomatic protection is essential to ensure effective protection of the personal rights of individuals. how can the protection of data collected by private companies be guaranteed, since such diplomatic protection has not been established? have we forgotten the swift case, with the transmission of data collected by american authorities? if this kind of situation has happened in a country like the united states, imagine what could happen in less developed countries with less protection of fundamental rights. third, the cost of visas is rising and varies from country to country. the commission proposal was aimed at preventing visa shopping. by giving private entities the possibility to charge a fee which will be added to the visa price, a difference will be created between the cost of visas from one member state to another. we are thus encouraging what we really wanted to combat, namely visa shopping. if the cost of a visa is 60 euros in one country and 90 euros in another, where will most of the applications be concentrated? especially in the case of multi-member families. not to mention the need to review the visa facilitation agreements which have been so strongly supported by this parliament, such as the ukraine and serbia. for all these reasons, madam president, i cannot accept this compromise.
the summit response was not ambitious enough. where is the determination to end tax havens and offshore companies? where is the decision to moralize the salaries of the directors of financial institutions? mr president, it is the rich who are paying for the crisis. because they're the ones who caused it. as regards the employment summit, it cannot be a small summit, because employment is not a small problem, it is a bigger problem affecting individuals and families. that is not how we can restore public confidence. finally, i hear you say that you do not need the socialist proposals. that's an arrogant claim. if the defenders of the neoliberal doctrine had listened to the warnings of the socialists, we would not be in this crisis today. isn't that how they learn? if you have not understood the gravity and the depth of this crisis and if you have not understood what has caused it, i fear the worst. i fear that the crisis will continue for many years, and that is not what we want.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament's legislative resolution on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on novel foods, as it simplifies the procedure for authorising and placing on the market novel foods and makes the authorisation procedure more efficient and transparent, giving consumers greater possibilities for informed choice. it should be underlined that novel foods should be placed on the market only if they are safe for consumers and should not mislead them. on the other hand, where new foods serve to replace other foods, they must not be inferior from the nutritional point of view.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this report because it takes a positive view of the question of new foodstuffs and the more serious proposals from the right, which were intended to insist on gmos, were not adopted. as we had argued in the committee on agriculture, genetically modified organisms must be excluded, and new foods must not present health risks or mislead the consumer and, when consumed as a substitute food, must not present nutritional disadvantages for the consumer. the rapporteur believes that the new novel food regulation aims to achieve a high level of food safety, consumer protection, environmental protection and animal health, while respecting the precautionary principle, in accordance with regulation (ec) no 178/2002 laying down the general principles and rules of food law, establishing the european food safety authority and laying down procedures in matters of food safety. all other objectives are of secondary importance. on the other hand, novel foods must not endanger or mislead consumers. where novel foods serve to replace other foods, they should not be considered by consumers to be inferior in nutritional terms.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament's legislative resolution on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on substances that deplete the ozone layer. this regulation, now recast, is the main community instrument for ensuring compliance with the montreal protocol on substances that deplete the ozone layer and ensuring not only greater protection against the destructive effects of uv radiation but also a reduction in the greenhouse effect. the european union must maintain its position as a world leader in this field, as it has done in the past.
in writing. - without questioning the need to ensure that community financial resources are used properly and in a timely manner (which is far from the case), the report points out some less correct and too ambiguous considerations. for example, it should be clarified that the resources which are by nature limited are defined by the eu, more specifically by imposition by the so-called six-nation charter countries which set the community budget at around 1% of gni. it is also important to clarify what should be understood by negative priorities and positive priorities, without which it is not possible to accept a principle that these negative priorities should be revised downwards in order to give way to more pressing priorities. if by negative priorities we mean, for example, the so-called new priorities such as the implementation of the neoliberal policies of the lisbon strategy, the security policies of the fortress europe or the militarisation of the eu, we say that we cannot agree more. however, if these are the positive priorities and the multiannual and overall strategic objectives (as they are), we are decidedly against it.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' in the definition of the term 'competent authority'. as we have said, although in a very mild way, the report makes some pertinent points which are in line with what we have been complaining about for a long time: that the current community budget is not meeting its needs or that every year substantial sums remain unbudgeted (less than about eur 29 billion between 2007 and 2009). the european parliament has been a member of the european parliament since the beginning of the year, and the european parliament has been a member of the european parliament since the beginning of the year. the ep also states that the additional policies should not alter the balance between the main categories of the current mff or undermine existing priorities, although it has removed with our vote against the mention of the two main policies under headings 1b and 2, respectively, convergence and agriculture and fisheries, and has instead placed the emphasis on the new priorities, i.e. the neoliberal objectives of the lisbon strategy (including so-called public-private partnerships), climate change or the militarisation of the eu.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution on the future of the motor vehicle industry. as the eu is the world's largest producer of motor vehicles and one of the largest private employers, support for this sector is key to responding to the current financial and economic crisis. i therefore advocate coordinated political action at european level to promote the adoption of measures to support industry, to ensure that producers and suppliers in the automotive sector have access to credit; to stimulate demand for new vehicles, including the granting of incentives for the withdrawal of old cars from circulation and the purchase of green vehicles; to provide financial support for skilled workers through the full use of the globalisation adjustment fund and the european special fund and to promote research and investment.
in writing. - given the importance of the automotive industry to the european economy, it is understandable that european governments and the european union as a whole are determined to respond to the present situation and to prevent the consequences of a crisis, which is intended to be temporary and which is being fought to be temporary, from making the industry irrecoverable. we support that concern. there are, however, some safeguards to be noted. if the primary objective is to save jobs, it must be in line with the ultimate objective of ensuring the survival of viable industries. this means that we must take advantage of the extraordinary investment in this sector to update, modernise and prepare it for greater competitiveness. the idea that it is possible, or desirable, to isolate and save an economic sector from competition, when it is healthy and fair, is an economic error and a political deception. despite all its failures, overcoming the crisis in the automotive sector, as well as the crisis in general, and preparing for the next moment is at the heart of the lisbon strategy: more competitiveness, more innovation, more jobs. let us help to recover the automobile sector, but let us not try to reinvent the wheel.
mr president, saved from the ashes left by the bush administration by the election of obama, the transatlantic partnership is no longer sufficient to solve the main challenges faced by humanity, but it is still necessary. europe must take advantage of this opportunity and articulate with the us the exit strategy for the current global crisis, upholding human security – and that does not only mean reforming the international financial system but regulating the whole globalisation process and investing in a sustainable economy at the global scale. we need more and stronger europe to help obama close guantánamo, end the secret prisons, and to define an alternative strategy for the security challenges in afghanistan, pakistan, iran and sudan, and put in place justice and peace for israelis and arabs. we need a stronger europe and a genuine partnership with the us to achieve the millennium development goals. only with an eu capable of sharing the burden and assuming its global responsibilities, an eu which is not just the sum of its parts, will we be taken seriously in washington, be able to influence the policies of the obama administration and be in a position to enable the genuine transatlantic partnership that the world still needs.
unlike our colleague ana gomes, who spoke earlier and who believes that the transatlantic relationship has been reborn with the election of obama, i think the news about the end of that relationship was clearly exaggerated. this report once again proves this and i commend its author for it. europe and the united states have long been indispensable allies for prosperity, development and globalisation. europe and the united states have long shared common challenges and even common adversaries, even if some, particularly on this side of the atlantic, are reluctant to acknowledge it. and for a long time both europeans and americans have known what must be done to respond to the needs of a world that remains unjust, unequal, dangerous, and now in global crisis. but the crisis must not make us retreat, nor must we fail to show diplomatic firmness, political and military commitment to our obligations as allies, nor the return of the collectivist economy or the reappearance of protectionism, which would be fatal to the revival of our economies. thanks to an economy that has opened up on a global scale, europe and the united states now have strong allies in japan, india, brazil and various asian countries. despite the current crisis, there are many people in the world who continue to look to our countries and aspire to live like us one day. for all these peoples, europe and america must once again be the alliance that leads the economy of prosperity, that shares it and makes it global. this is also why the next g20 summit is so important, not because it is an opportunity to know who is closest to barack obama, but because it is an opportunity to prove that we have answers and leadership, cooperating with the new powers in the reforms that are needed, but knowing that only the economic model based on the creative power of men will allow to create wealth - i say wealth and i do not say greed - jobs, development, and overcome the crisis. the fact is that, mr president, otherwise it will not be possible, at least in a lasting way, to create new jobs or to show real solidarity with those who, in these difficult times, are most in need.

the following are statements by the council and the commission on european consciousness and totalitarianism.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the debate i would like to be involved in would be one aimed at putting an end to any measures to help those most in need because they have become unnecessary. unfortunately, that is not the case at the moment we live in europe and the world. in the european union, many families affected by unemployment or social exclusion have seen their income become insufficient to meet their most basic needs and therefore owe them our solidarity. the commission's initiative under discussion deserves our applause, especially since it is now a question, as in the past, of finding another easy channel for disposing of surpluses. that's not the case now. the financial allocations for this programme are justified in being increased. i also congratulate the commission for proposing that this support be covered by the agricultural budget. there is no greater solidarity than that of farmers, and there are no communities that are more involved in helping one another than rural communities. i am sure that european farmers are very proud to share a part of the agricultural budget with those who need it most. my group will therefore reject the liberal proposals which call into question the legal basis of this regulation. the commission proposal can and must be improved. the siekierski report and the amendment which the socialist group will table in the plenary are good contributions to improving the report, particularly as regards the eligibility of storage costs and the full community financing of the programme. i therefore call on the house to approve this report and on the commission to accept parliament's contributions.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal for a regulation on the distribution of foodstuffs to the most deprived persons. poverty is one of the major problems facing the eu at the moment and, as early as 2006, there were almost 79 million people at risk of poverty, so the need for food aid programmes is clear. since the commission's proposal aims at distributing food to the most needy people and better planning so that the funds can be used more efficiently, and also taking into account that since its inception in 1987 the food distribution programme has already benefited over 13 million people, i think that the continuation of this programme is both necessary and positive.
in writing. - the free trade agreement with india is another step forward by europe and india towards making the world economy freer and expanding its advantages. and it's also a particularly significant signal in today's environment. the conclusion of this agreement is therefore to be commended. but his analysis does merit some more critical reflection. for free trade to work effectively, a set of rules must be respected to prevent counterfeiting, the use of products prohibited in the countries of destination must be avoided, indications of origin must be clear, information must be available, transparency must be ensured and international agreements must be respected. another relevant aspect is the idea that the benefits of free trade are mutual. in other words, the opening up of frontiers is not just about opening up the markets of developed countries to products from third countries. the virtues of trade are the mutual possibility of exchange, the openness of the economy, and they should be extended to developing and fast-growing countries. and that will only happen if the barriers to trade and investment in those countries are also reduced.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on food prices in europe because i think that political action is needed to deal with the rise in agricultural and food prices and the discrepancy between the producer price and the price paid by the consumer. in europe, the price paid by the final consumer is approximately five times the production price, a situation that has affected low-income households, whose food expenditure accounts for the largest share of the household budget.
in writing. − the internet is a major technological advance, providing the interconnection of its users, the creation of personal, professional and pedagogical relationships, the dissemination of knowledge and the dissemination and promotion of culture. but internet crime has proliferated, and it has become a preferred tool of action for criminal networks, since it is easily accessible, cheap and quite powerful. in the last year, cases of abuse against children on the internet have increased by 16%, with no effective punishment or even the ability to find these criminals in most cases; or terrorism there are already some 5000 terrorist propaganda websites that function as a means of radicalization and recruitment, while also serving as a source of information on terrorist means and methods. i recognize the need and urgency to find solutions and to create the appropriate legislative instruments to combat crime without falling into excessive and unnecessary control through censorship and the secret surveillance of internet traffic.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the stavros lambrinidis report on strengthening security and fundamental freedoms on the internet, as i believe that the european union needs to take action to reconcile the fundamental rights of internet users and the fight against cybercrime, in order to protect citizens, especially children. i believe that this requires the development of standards on data protection, security and freedom of expression.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on safe ship recycling, as i believe there is a great need to address this issue as an integral part of the life cycle of a ship. end-of-life ships should be considered as hazardous waste because of the hazardous substances they contain and should therefore be covered by the basel convention. i am pleased to see that there is a community will to improve ship dismantling practices.
in writing. - european environmental protection objectives can only be implemented to a large extent if they are integrated into the various sectors of community policy. the resolution therefore seeks to speed up all the measures that the union must take to ensure that the dismantling of end-of-life ships is carried out in conditions which are safe for workers and adequately protect the marine environment. the global phase-out of single-hull tankers and the scrapping of old vessels being withdrawn from the market, many of which contain dangerous substances, make us foresee an uncontrolled expansion of installations which do not comply with international standards in south asia, and may even extend to african countries. all these reasons lead us to support this motion for a resolution and the measures aimed at meeting international safety and environmental standards.
commissioner vladimir spidla will have to speak at this stage of the debate. he'll explain better than i will the reasons why he has to do it. i'll give you the floor right away.
ladies and gentlemen, commissioner barrot will be replacing commissioner špidla in the final part of this debate.
mr president, mr vice-president of the commission, ladies and gentlemen, i voted in favour of this report, as did my colleagues from the epp-ed in the libe committee. this is due to the excellent work done by the shadow rapporteur, patrick gauber, in trying to find a balanced compromise. i also welcome the work of the rapporteur, kathalijne buitenweg, and i support her call for everyone to avoid radicalizing positions and to seek the widest possible consensus. as in all compromises, there are points where we can make our opinion prevail and others that we find more difficult to accept. this is a commitment which must take account of the legislation, practice and different cultural traditions in 27 member states. i welcome points such as the 10-year deadline for adapting buildings to enable disabled people to access goods, services and resources; and if structural difficulties persist which cannot be overcome, alternatives can always be found. i also sympathize with the concerns we have expressed about the insurance companies - that they have been taken into account, for example, with the involvement of medical advice. i cannot accept, however, that the reference to the principle of subsidiarity in matters of family, matrimonial and reproductive law, which was approved in committee, should be deleted, these are the exclusive competence of the member states. the same applies to article 8, which the epp's amendment no 90 seeks to remove, because, in view of the legislative traditions existing in many member states, it is not possible to accept the inversion of the burden of proof, since it will create insurmountable legal problems. if these key points are finally adopted in plenary, i shall not be able to vote in favour of this report. but i can never in good conscience vote against a directive which prohibits discrimination against people regardless of their religion or belief, disability, age or sexual orientation. it is also a question, mr president, and i conclude, of defining the kind of europe we want to help build. i am clearly in favour of a europe that will fight without cease any form of discrimination.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday 2 april 2009. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used effectively. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in response to the global financial crisis, the plan for the relaunch of the european economy states that cohesion policy makes a significant contribution to public investment by the member states and regions and should act as a way of recovering from the current crisis. it specifically suggests that action be taken in the priority areas of the lisbon strategy in order to achieve growth and jobs. all instruments put into operation seek to achieve this purpose and to accelerate the achievement of results. in this sense, the extension of the scope of the european globalisation adjustment fund and the streamlining of the european social fund have a particular responsibility to respond to the multiple situations of social and economic emergency that need support. i am sure, today as in the past, that its action will be all the more effective the more complementary its actions and its scope are. however, as no new funds or new lines of action on the ground are planned, it is particularly important that the european social fund is used to its full potential in combating unemployment and the rapidly increasing competitiveness pressures in the european economy resulting from the current financial crisis and economic slowdown. i would like to stress that the regi committee has repeatedly identified simplification as essential to improving the management and implementation of the structural funds, as the european commission is now proposing. we have been asked for urgency on this matter, however, and we have taken it into account in adopting this package of regulations, and this house has never shied away from its political responsibilities. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. however, we stress the need to start an evaluation process of this fund now with a view to a further review as soon as possible.
in writing. - concrete steps have been taken to make european citizenship a reality, from which portuguese emigrants in other community countries benefit in particular by enjoying a wide range of rights and obligations, including equal treatment with nationals of the host state. the most significant development was undoubtedly the adoption of the citizenship directive which established an unconditional right of permanent residence for eu citizens and their families residing in the host state for a period of 5 years. however, there is still a lot to be done, as obstacles still exist, mostly resulting from incorrect implementation of this directive by member states. i welcome the commission's initiative to publish a guide to this directive so that information on the rights which citizens can enjoy is available not only to citizens but also to the authorities at regional and local level in the member states. it is essential to continue strengthening the social and political ties that bind eu citizens together. the lisbon treaty should make a significant contribution to this, in particular through the 'citizens' initiative' which will allow citizens, under certain conditions, to have a right of initiative.
in writing. - although it includes a number of points which naturally merit our agreement, as well as others which can be described as 'politically correct', this ep resolution entitled 'the problems and prospects of eu citizenship' would be laughable if it were not serious. let's see. the ep: - welcomes the fact that the lisbon treaty makes it possible for one million union citizens from different member states to collectively invite the commission to submit legislative proposals, and is convinced that this legal right will contribute to significantly increasing awareness of union citizenship among europeans; - reminds that transparency and democratic participation must be achieved through various forms of partnership between the eu and the member states, regional and local institutions, the social partners and civil society; finally, a tongue-in-cheek that makes us think that this 'citizenship of the union' thing is very nice as long as citizens are prevented from deciding what really matters, namely by making it impossible for them to vote on the 'lisbon treaty' or, if that is not possible, by forcing them to hold referendums, as many as necessary, so that they say yes... hypocrisy at its finest...
in writing. the aim of the proposal is to limit consumer exposure to pharmacologically active substances used in veterinary medicinal products for human consumption and their residues in foodstuffs of animal origin. foodstuffs imported from third countries are covered. after a long process, the text of the common position was obtained, reflecting the compromise reached through the negotiations between the three institutions. the main points adopted in the new text are as follows: - reference values for action: now defined as the residue level of a pharmacologically active substance established for control purposes for certain substances for which no maximum residue limits have been set in accordance with this regulation; - import: member states shall prohibit the import and placing on the market of food of animal origin containing residues resulting from the unlawful administration of pharmacologically active substances which are not subject to classification in accordance with the text. therefore, imports of food products from third countries containing residues resulting from the illegal administration of substances the use of which is prohibited in the european union should be prohibited in order to safeguard public health.
