in writing. − the economic and financial crisis europe is going through should be seen as an opportunity to take measures that will help not only the recovery of member states but also the awareness of the populations towards more sustainable behaviour. the possibility of improving the energy efficiency of housing buildings by allocating funds up to 4% of the erdf to each member state has a double benefit: on the one hand, it reduces household fixed energy expenditure, on the other hand, it reduces national consumption, contributing to energy security and reducing fossil fuel imports and greenhouse gas emissions. i welcome this report and hope that the member states will be able to integrate this budget into their national energy efficiency action plans and implement it in a responsible and pragmatic way. i hope that this money will particularly benefit small island regions, since it is in these regions that energy production has the least means of being realised, thus requiring responsible energy use by all. investment in energy efficiency is one of the most important tools for this purpose and should be a priority for member state governments.
in writing. − i agree with a common visa policy that facilitates legitimate travel and implements measures to facilitate the visa application and processing process (reduction of costs, simplification of the issuing procedure, use of multiple-entry visas, longer validity periods). it is also urgent to combat illegal immigration by further harmonising national legislation and consular practices. i recognise the need and urgency to make the common visa policy more coherent, in particular by integrating all the provisions governing the issuing of visas and decisions on refusal, extension, annulment, revocation and reduction of visas into a single code. i commend the rapporteur, mr lax, for the excellent compromise he has succeeded in obtaining, but i regret that the weak compromise obtained in the report on the common consular instructions (which should be included in this proposal) ultimately undermines the merits of this proposal. the exemption and reduction of the value of visas for children, which was achieved here, is an example of this, but it does not produce the expected effects because of the additional fees to be charged when the service is provided by external companies. for all the reasons which have already led me to vote against this report, i cannot support it fully, and i shall therefore abstain.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the regulation on a community eco-label scheme, as i consider it of the utmost importance to encourage the sustainable production and consumption of products. the eco-label is appropriate to guide consumers to the recommended eco-friendly products on the market and to encourage the production and consumption of products with good environmental performance.
in writing. as the report shows, the eco-label is a voluntary label whose purpose is to promote, at european level, the promotion of high-efficiency products with a low environmental impact throughout their life cycle. to this end, ecological quality standards (which we call 'criteria') have been developed for each specific product category. there are currently 26 product categories, 622 authorisations and more than 3000 products and services - detergents, paper, clothing (including footwear and textiles), tourism, campsites, etc. - which have been awarded eco-labels. this brand, and the flower that symbolizes it, can be considered as a dynamic element which, thanks to the constant updating of the environmental criteria for the products bearing it, encourages companies to operate in a virtuous circle of efforts aimed at increasing the overall ecological quality of the products placed on the market. but there appear to be several gaps in the existing system, taking into account the experience gained over the almost ten years of existence of this certification, which points to the need for more decisive intervention to close certain gaps in the system. the commission's proposal for a new regulation, which meps want to see improved, is part of this context.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on voluntary participation by organisations in a community eco-management and audit scheme (emas), as it helps organisations to identify, monitor, measure and report on their environmental impact. emas was first introduced in 1995 and extended in 2001 to public and private sector organisations. this new revision is an opportunity to make the system more attractive and simple for small and medium-sized enterprises and also an attempt to get emas to have the same level of participation as iso 14001 (which is the leading environmental management system in europe) currently has. it is also important to note that the recognition of emas as a benchmark in environmental management systems is in line with the eu's objective of combating climate change.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on 'equal treatment for all', which includes several amendments to the european commission's proposal, strengthening the protection of citizens, including people with disabilities who are victims of discrimination. according to 2008 eurobarometer data, 15% of european citizens claim to have been discriminated against in the past year. this is an unacceptable situation, and i welcome the fact that this text has been adopted by the european parliament, despite the incomprehensible vote against by the right. i believe it is essential that legislation prohibits direct and indirect discrimination, multiple discrimination or discrimination on grounds of association, sex, race or ethnic origin, religion or belief, disability, age, sexual orientation or gender, and in areas such as social protection, education and access to goods and services such as housing, transport, telecommunications and health.
in writing. - another resolution deeply rooted in a view of interference and stigmatisation towards belarus, assuming the character of gross interference in the internal affairs of that country. beyond the mystifying and manichean language, the goal is clear: what the eu does not accept and seeks to overcome is the assertion of belarus's sovereignty and the definition of an autonomous policy, not subordinated to eu/nato/us interests. the eu therefore uses blackmail and sanctions, which it claims can be lifted if belarus takes the measures it requires, for example: the imperative to include the democratic opposition of belarus and civil society in the eu-belarus dialogue, to make full and effective use of the possibilities of supporting civil society and democratic development in belarus through the european initiative for democracy and human rights (eidhr) or financial support to the independent belarusian television channel belsat. would any of the eu countries accept such conditions? the hypocrisy of this position is evident, especially when the 'european family' of the so-called 'democratic nations' has not uttered a single word of condemnation of the real massacre perpetrated by the israeli army against the palestinian people in gaza or of the criminal flights of the cia, with which it is, moreover, complicit.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the resolution on totalitarian regimes. i believe that europe will not be united until it is able to achieve a common vision of its history and to conduct an honest and thorough debate on the crimes committed by nazism, stalinism and the fascist and communist regimes in the last century. i think that the process of european integration is a success in that it has enabled the construction of a european union which today encompasses the countries of central and eastern europe which lived under communist regimes from the end of the second world war to the beginning of the 1990s, and has helped to secure democracy in southern europe in countries such as greece, spain and portugal, which suffered from long-standing fascist regimes.
in writing. the european parliament's shameful resolution is part of the operation of distorting historical truth carried out by the reactionary and revanchist forces that were defeated in the second world war. these are the same forces that, for example, rehabilitate in their countries those who collaborated with the nazi barbarity. the aim is to whitewash nazism and fascism and condemn communism, that is to whitewash the executioner and the oppressor, condemning their victims and their oppressed. the aim is to erase the decisive contribution of the communists and the soviet union to the defeat of nazi-fascism, their role in improving the living conditions of the workers, their contribution to the liberation of peoples from the colonial yoke, their role against exploitation and war after the second world war. in portugal, no one like the pcp has fought for freedom, democracy, peace, human rights, decent living conditions for the portuguese people, freedom for the peoples colonized by fascism, for the achievements enshrined in the constitution of the portuguese republic, which is now 33 years old. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the resolution is all the more serious at a time of acute crisis for capitalism, which has made the struggle for peace, democracy and social progress the great demand of our time.
in writing. the resolution now adopted makes clear the aims and objectives that the majority of the ep wants for eu-russia relations: that they 'be based on the principles of liberalised and open markets and reciprocity of investment rights between the partners, and therefore require that, in return for close and beneficial economic ties, the russian government guarantees the property rights of foreign investors'. in other words, the aim is to pressure russia, for example, to give up its immense natural resources - in particular natural gas and oil - to condition its productive capacity and to make its labour available for exploitation and to the interests of the great powers and big capital in the eu, which wants to have access to what should belong to the russian people. the resolution puts all the pressure on russia, but there is no mention of nato's eastward enlargement and the installation of the new us missile system in europe. we reject this kind of relationship. we support the establishment of fair and mutually respectful relations between the parties, based on the interests of their peoples, respecting the principles of non-interference, disarmament and denuclearization.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the resolution on health concerns associated with electromagnetic fields, as this is a matter which directly affects all european citizens exposed to electromagnetic fields, both at home and at work. over the past few years, exposure to man-made sources of electromagnetic fields has increased substantially, mainly due to increased demand for electricity and increasingly advanced wireless technologies. i therefore believe that it is of the utmost importance to ensure a high level of protection for all consumers, without, however, jeopardising the operation of mobile networks and the development of new wireless technologies.
the rapporteur. - mr president, mr vice-president of the european commission, mr president of the committee on budgetary control, ladies and gentlemen, we are now faced with the completion of the most profound reform ever of the european parliament, with the creation of statutes for both its members and assistants, with the end of the unacceptable wage discrimination between its members, with the end of a system which is not very transparent in the payment of our travel expenses and with the end of a discriminatory pension system. as rapporteur for parliament's discharge, as a member of the committee on budgetary control for ten years now and as a spokesman for the socialists, i am very proud of what we have achieved and i think it would not be too much to ask to see our public take note of these changes which it has insistently called for. that said, i am today, as i have always been, absolutely in favour of full transparency in the use of public funds and i fully agree with the views of the european ombudsman on this matter. i am, as i have always been, against the use of public funds to cover private risk. i am today, as i have always been, against voluntary pension systems which ignore wage differences, creating inequalities in treatment. i think it is unacceptable to make absolutely unfair generalizations, to present all members of the european parliament as having the right to two pensions. as the author of this report, i would like to stress that after ten years as a member of the european parliament and after brief periods in the portuguese parliament and the regional parliament of the azores, i will now be retiring from parliamentary work without being entitled to any national, regional or european pension. i would like to say that those who think that the denial of the rights of their representatives, which are common in our societies, contributes to making europe better, are deeply mistaken. on the contrary, i am convinced that the only way to overcome the mistrust of any citizen in his representatives is to limit to a minimum the establishment of specific rules for the parliamentarians who are responsible for their approval. i believe that the only question that parliament should decide on is the relative place of the member within the european administration. i regret the lack of clarity in the past between public duty and private interest in the pension system, but i would like to see recognition of the work of all those who, particularly in our committee on budgetary control, have fought tirelessly for the accuracy and transparency of european accounts. i would like to pay a warm tribute to all of you here, and i also wish that the work which our committee has done so far will be continued in the next legislature with the same vigour and the same commitment to a more rigorous, fairer and more united europe as it has done so far. the parliament that will be elected in june will operate under much more transparent and equitable rules, and that is something that all of us here today have reason to celebrate.
on behalf of the pse group. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the main objective of mr romeva's report, which i congratulate on its openness, is to ensure that the rules of the common fisheries policy are respected. respect for these rules and a european approach to fisheries are the best way to safeguard the interests of the sector. if all the workers in the sector - from the crew on board to the fish sellers - do not comply with the rules, their survival is doomed. the attempt to apply european rules without reflecting the diversity of european fleets will also contribute to failure. i therefore proposed that the commission proposal be adapted more closely to the reality of small-scale fleets - although i would like to go further - which are somewhat spread throughout the european union, particularly in the octs, without ever forgetting that a common fisheries policy requires adequate control measures. as rapporteur for the fisheries budget, i have repeatedly lamented the insufficient level of compliance with european rules and have called for better control by the member states, transparency of inspection results and a stronger community inspection policy, provided that this is accompanied by financial measures to support the sector. we would certainly like to go further, but i cannot help but congratulate the rapporteur on the proposal and the measures he has put forward, and i expect the commissioner to give a full answer on this matter.
i am not saying that the european union should be a political force, but i am saying that it should be a political force. the law defines the extent and boundary of the territorial waters, the exclusive economic zone, and portugal's rights to the contiguous seabed. the state shall not alienate any part of the portuguese territory or the sovereign rights it exercises over it. article 5 of the constitution of the portuguese republic could not be clearer. in line with this and in order to ensure that the provisions of the portuguese basic law are respected, we have therefore tabled an amendment to the proposal for a regulation which states that this proposal respects and does not affect the competence and responsibility of the member states to monitor compliance with the rules of the common fisheries policy. however, the amendments of the committee on fisheries, although in some respects they minimize the negative aspects of the unacceptable proposal of the european commission, do not safeguard principles which we consider to be central. among other burdensome and inappropriate aspects, it is unacceptable that the commission has the power to carry out unannounced and independent inspections in the exclusive economic zones and territory of the member states, that it can arbitrarily prohibit fishing activities and suspend or cancel payments of community financial assistance to a member state, or that a member state can inspect its fishing vessels in the exclusive economic zone of any other member state without its authorisation. i would like to end by recalling what this parliament itself has approved: the importance of control in fisheries management, which is the responsibility of the member states. we hope that once again you will not now take what has been said for what has not been said - as, unfortunately, has been your habit.
mr president, commissioner, this proposal of yours is absolutely fundamental. anyone who reads the court of auditors' report on the state of play of the common fisheries policy can have no doubt that this initiative by the european commission was absolutely indispensable. but it is also true that our rapporteur has done an absolutely exceptional job here, and has taken account of many of the specific features - particularly small-scale fishing - and has taken account of some of our suggestions. i would like to congratulate him very much on the exemplary work he has done. but i would like to say that i am in favour of subsidiarity. but there can be no subsidiarity in control if there is no subsidiarity in the logic of the common fisheries policy. that is the challenge facing the commissioner in reforming the common fisheries policy, and i wish him every success in meeting this challenge, which is essential for all fisheries in europe.
on behalf of the pse group. i would also like to congratulate the commission on this legislative initiative and our rapporteur, represented here by our colleague mrs carmen fraga. i think it is true that we were faced with an absolute urgency to simplify the legislative body in this area. but i also think we have to go much further, particularly in two fundamental areas, the first of which is rejection. i think that the reform we are about to introduce should simply prohibit any rejection as a matter of principle. rejections must be absolutely forbidden. and secondly, i think that the principle should be established that all vessels in regions where the regional or national authorities so understand it should comply with standards that are stricter than those of the european union. these are two fundamental principles which are missing here and which i hope will be taken into account in the reform of the cfp.
in writing. according to eurostat, the eu's ageing population will become a reality in the medium term. immigration could act as an important stimulus to achieve good economic performance in the eu. in this sense, it is necessary to recognise the essential nature of the migration phenomenon for the eu. the fight against illegal labour is crucial as it absolutely violates the rights of migrant workers; we must promote a european policy which favours the regularisation of illegal immigrant workers, ensuring that they do not fall into summary return procedures which violate their rights. these workers are already in a position of inferiority when they arrive in europe and it is clear that a proper management of migration flows could benefit both the eu and third countries. the recurrent under-utilisation of skills by migrant workers must be tackled. these workers are often subject to work requiring much lower qualifications than they possess, a phenomenon particularly notable among women. i think that the commission should pay particular attention to the issue of the recognition of skills and the encouragement of lifelong learning, while also ensuring the right of immigrants to learn the language of the host country as a means of social, professional and cultural integration into the european union.
on behalf of the pse group. madam president, the crisis has, among other things, made it possible to highlight the strong imbalances between the countries of the euro area, perhaps the main conclusion of the report on the economic and monetary union of the 10, but above all the extreme vulnerability of the countries which, being members of the european union, do not belong to the euro area. that is why it is absolutely essential to increase the funds available at european level to support balance of payments crises, and that is why we welcome the move from 12 to 25 and then to 50 million euros of the necessary funds. however, it is more important to tackle the root causes of problems, not just their most obvious manifestations. today, the enlarged europe and the eurozone must reinterpret the meaning of european solidarity and the objectives of cohesion and real convergence. the capacity of european horizontal policies must be seen in this light, but new financing instruments such as eurobonds must also be reviewed. financial assistance to the balance of payments is necessary but it is far from sufficient to ensure the sustainability of the european project and the single european currency.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the resolution on the obligations of operators placing timber and timber products on the market, because illegal logging is a serious and growing problem with very serious environmental implications such as loss of biodiversity, deforestation and forest degradation. moreover, it can be blamed for almost 20 percent of global carbon emissions. the european union, as a major consumer of timber and timber products, has an obligation to take effective measures against deforestation and illegal logging, measures which should also prevent the marketing of illegal timber products. legislation against illegal logging is needed to show consumers that products are from legal sources, that companies that comply with these rules will not be harmed and that companies that opt for illegal timber products lose their market.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this report because we consider it important to maintain stocks of crude oil and/or petroleum products. the creation of reserves is a matter of great economic and social importance for each country, including its security, and therefore any kind of speculation on these reserves should be prohibited, contrary to what has been happening, and their ownership and administration should be public, to defend the interest of the eu countries. however, we do not agree with the powers that this proposal for a directive confers on the european commission, in particular the possibility for the commission services to carry out checks on safety reserves and specific reserves in the member states. the countries should build up their own reserves of these products, but it is a matter of sovereignty to administer them and to define their maximum or minimum levels. we also reject any attempt to use the creation of these reserves as yet another justification, albeit a mitigated one, for a policy of interference, as if it could be inferred from the creation of a favourable investment climate for the exploration and exploitation of oil reserves inside and outside the european union.
in writing. − this initiative is part of the overall strategy for the protection of critical infrastructure. the eu's economy and the safety and well-being of its citizens depend on the existence and proper functioning of a set of infrastructures that provide essential services such as health services, telecommunications, energy and transport networks, financial services, water and food supplies, etc. while some member states already have strong protection measures and structures in place, in others the situation is still very precarious. it is therefore important that there should be a better and more efficient exchange of information and good practice, which will only be possible through the establishment of this information and communication system. this will strengthen dialogue and information availability on shared threats and vulnerabilities, as well as cooperation and coordination between member states. at the same time, the development of appropriate measures and strategies to limit risks and promote adequate protection is encouraged and the safety of citizens is enhanced. i also support the inclusion of a three-year review clause, which should allow for any improvements that may be needed, including the possibility of creating a rapid alert system.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal on a critical infrastructure alert network (cirn). some member states' infrastructures consist of material resources, services, information technology equipment, which, if damaged, can have very serious consequences for health, safety and economic or social well-being. transport, telecommunications and energy systems are crucial sectors for the development of member states and are also increasingly interconnected sectors, where some member states are dependent on others. for this reason, the need for a single system for linking and sharing information between different authorities in the field of critical infrastructure protection, exchange of good practices and an early warning system is of the utmost importance for the development of the european union.
in writing. − the protection of officials is a responsibility which remains within the competence and the legislation in force in the host state. in order to improve cooperation and communication between member states in this area, a european network for the protection of officials was set up in 2002. the purpose of this initiative is to broaden the definition of official provided for in article 2 of that decision to include any person who, whether or not holding a public office, considers himself or herself to be threatened by his or her influence and contribution to the public debate. this dutch proposal arose following an incident in 2008 with a former dutch mep who, following a speech she gave at a seminar in the european parliament on islamic radicalisation, was the target of threats to her physical integrity. as the epp's shadow rapporteur, i support this initiative which aims to extend the scope of protection of human rights, and in particular to promote the right to freedom of expression.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal on the integrated approach to equality between men and women in the work of committees and delegations. the own-initiative report shows the progress made in committees and delegations and reinforces the need to adopt and implement gender equality strategies. the call for gender equality is not against men. it is for the benefit of society as a whole, thus benefiting women and men and families. an integrated approach to equality implies the reorganisation, improvement, development and evolution of policies with the aim of integrating the equality dimension into all policies at all levels and at all stages by the actors associated with key policy decisions. it is therefore necessary to adopt and implement a strategy accompanied by concrete objectives for an integrated approach to gender equality in community policies within the remit of parliamentary committees and delegations.
in writing. the third energy package should gradually develop the energy market, which until recently was based on a monopoly system. the move towards liberalisation implies genuine and sustained competition and highlights the importance of a stronger energy regulatory cooperation agency with clearly independent powers. the main objective of the agency shall be to assist the regulatory authorities in carrying out, at community level, the tasks carried out by the member states and, where necessary, to coordinate their action. the agency will also ensure the supervision of the internal markets in electricity and natural gas and contribute to all efforts to improve energy security. i would stress the key role of this agency in the future of energy policy in europe, which we want to see characterized by more competitive and diverse conditions, away from the monopolies of the past, together with conditions of increased safety and greater efficiency, for the benefit of consumers. these are the reasons why i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - i welcome the adoption of this report, which is an integral part of the energy package that was adopted today, as i believe it is another important step towards improving the quality of life of europe's citizens. the possibility of better interconnections between electricity grids, the existence of strong and suitable regulators ensuring market transparency and transnational cooperation are all very important factors in ensuring that final consumers can enjoy a truly fair and competitive service. shared responsibility and cooperation between member states in both electricity and natural gas are the cornerstones of a genuine european energy market, which is to be fair, dynamic and sustainable.
in writing. the third energy package will make up for the many structural shortcomings of the past. we must also remember the discrimination against new energy suppliers and a certain lack of transparency in terms of prices and choice of supplier. finally, with this package we can hope to complete the liberalisation of the internal energy market in the union. the adoption of this third package and of this proposal in particular will make european energy markets more competitive, sustainable and secure. consumer rights are expected to be at the heart of the process of opening up markets in the face of the agreement reached, which includes the issue of separation of ownership, independence of national regulatory authorities, as well as conditions for a clarification of responsibilities between the national authorities, the agency and the european network of transmission system operators. i voted in favour of this report in the hope that consumers will have a more transparent market, with access to detailed information and the possibility of changing energy suppliers without any cost.
in writing. − i welcome this report, aware that it will be another instrument for improving consumer information, contributing to a more transparent market, where the choice between products is made in a conscious and informed way, based on simple standards but with scientific and technological justification. in my view, this report represents another step towards an energy-sustainable europe by allowing consumers to choose tyres that lead to lower fuel consumption in their vehicles. on the other hand, the possibility to choose a tyre according to its specific behaviour is another tool for the consumer to protect himself and to equip his motor vehicle in a way appropriate to his driving style and the environmental conditions in which he is travelling. i also note the healthy and technologically-oriented competition which this system will impose on the tyre market, leading to technological developments of the various brands being able to be compared and appreciated by consumers in a clear and objective way.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the resolution on the labelling of tyres with regard to energy efficiency. since almost 25% of total co2 emissions come from road transport, reducing the energy intensity of vehicles is a very important challenge for the european union. the creation of a labelling system that ensures adequate information on fuel efficiency and grip, among other characteristics, will allow consumers to make informed choices when purchasing tyres. since tyres account for 20 to 30% of total fuel consumption, enhancing sustainability should be an integral part of an approach to reducing fuel consumption and consequently carbon dioxide emissions into the atmosphere.
in writing. - i am opposed, in principle, to the legislator intervening in the market and fixing prices. i believe that it is up to the market to set its own prices in accordance with the essential principles of fair competition. this is precisely what is lacking in roaming and what justifies the european parliament's intervention to fix maximum tariffs below which telecommunications operators are now obliged to compete. i voted in favour of this regulation not being limited to calls made or received, but also including sms and data roaming. an eu that promotes the free movement of its citizens is not compatible with market rules that end or limit themselves to crossing a border. today, the overwhelming majority of citizens continue to be reluctant to use their mobile phones when they are abroad for fear of the roaming bill. i voted in favour of this regulation precisely because it requires cheaper rates for roaming customers, while at the same time strengthening the rules on price transparency. this is a concrete example of the way the eu is in our daily lives. from now on, when european citizens return from holidays or a business trip abroad, they will remember that the reduction in their mobile phone bill is due to the eu.
in writing. - the completion of the internal market is a fundamental objective of the union and therefore our joint efforts to achieve the best possible conditions of competition in the various economic sectors are very important. the globalization of trade, commerce and communications is no exception. the report on the mobile communications market is therefore important. the result of the agreement at first reading is a victory for parliament for the regulation to enter into force in july 2009. this report sets the conditions for greater transparency and consumer protection and, moreover, for fairer and clearer competition for everyone, from industry to consumers. i therefore voted in favour of this report.
in writing. − nuclear energy will play a key role in the energy future of our societies. therefore, and given the prospects for future installations in europe, it is essential to establish a common european framework for nuclear safety. mr hökmark's report will make it possible to create the best and safest conditions for future nuclear installations in europe. a clear framework with strong and independent national regulatory bodies will be established, as will a system for the licensing of nuclear installations and a system for the inspection and control of nuclear installations. the future importance of nuclear requires excellence in the conditions for its implementation and the conditions for the exchange of information necessary to establish the same and higher safety conditions. i therefore voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - we regret the significant rejection by the majority of the ep of our amendment which, in line with and in pursuit of respect for the constitution of the portuguese republic, that is, national sovereignty, stated that this proposal for a regulation respects and does not call into question the competence and responsibility of the member states to monitor compliance with the rules of the common fisheries policy. we regret the significant rejection by the majority of the ep of our amendment proposals which prevent the european commission from carrying out unannounced and independent inspections in the eez and the territory of the member states; and from being able, on a discretionary basis, to prohibit fishing activities and suspend or cancel payments of community financial assistance to a member state; or from a member state being able to inspect its fishing vessels in the eez of any member state without its authorisation. the proposal for a regulation, which will continue to be negotiated by the eu institutions, if approved as it stands, will represent another step in the disregard of national sovereignty, by incorporating requirements regarding equipment and procedures which have been pointed out as completely inadequate for portuguese fisheries. that is why we are voting against it!
in writing. - a common approach to immigration in the eu is imperative. in an area without internal borders there can be no fragmented and incoherent approach, because any action or immigration policy taken by one member state necessarily has consequences for all the others. i therefore support the creation of a common european immigration policy based on a sharing of responsibility, a high level of political and operational solidarity and mutual trust. the adoption of the pact on immigration and asylum was a major step in this direction, initiating a more coherent, controlled immigration policy linked to the labour market needs of european countries, as well as to the resources available in housing, health and education. allied to a firm fight against illegal immigration. the excellent report by mr busuttil follows this line, the only problem being an amendment tabled by the socialist group which calls into question the firmness of our fight against illegal immigration, since there is no doubt that any immigrant legally resident in the territory of a member state will have the right to vote, under the conditions laid down. i therefore support the alternative resolution proposed by the rapporteur, who has once again tried to reach a compromise that can be acceptable to all.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the resolution on a common immigration policy for europe, since immigration is one of the main challenges facing europe today. in trying to overcome the problem of immigration, it is possible to adopt policies that can turn it into an opportunity. immigration is a common concern in the countries of the european union and therefore they must act together to find an appropriate response to this problem. this report seeks to cover all the dimensions of immigration, supporting the creation of a common european policy based on a high level of political and operational solidarity. it also includes measures to enable immigrants to participate in the civic and political life of their societies, including political parties and trade unions, and to vote in local elections.
in writing. - a report with 91 articles obviously includes some points with which we agree; however, this report, despite its attempt to gild the pill, is deeply negative, and so we have clearly rejected it. it strongly supports the creation of a common european immigration policy, welcoming the institutional implications of the lisbon treaty, in particular the extension of co-decision and qualified majority voting to all immigration policies, and welcoming the adoption of the european pact on immigration - that is, an inhumane immigration policy of a security-oriented, criminalising, exploitative and selective nature. if there were any doubts about the eu's aims, the report clarifies them: migration remains necessary to meet eu labour market needs (around 60 million migrant workers by 2050), so migration profiles should be established with labour market needs as a central aspect of these profiles and the attractiveness of the eu for highly skilled workers should be increased, notably through the blue card i.e. an inhuman view of immigrants, which sees them only as labour to be exploited. more than a common policy, what is needed is a policy that defends the rights of immigrants and tackles the root causes of immigration.
in writing. the report sets out the guidelines for the development of the trans-european transport network (ten-t), currently 30 priority projects, inspired mainly by national interests, but also by the interests of multinationals, which will have some eur 5.3 billion of community co-financing. it is part of a vision of transeuropean transport networks in the light of the growth of the internal market and competition and capitalist concentration, and therefore insists on the need to integrate... the objectives of the lisbon strategy and argues that the ten-t should be adapted to changing market conditions, meet the following criteria: economic viability, improvement of competitiveness, promotion of the single market... and be developed on the basis of public-private partnerships (for example, by allocating a percentage of the revenue from road infrastructure tolls to the financing of ten-t projects). the majority of meps are not resisting, one month before the end of the current parliamentary term, the call on the european commission to propose by the end of its mandate a legislative initiative on the opening of national rail passenger transport markets from 1 january 2012. finally, in response to the interests of the major economic and financial groups in expanding their dominance in eastern europe, it emphasizes the priority of links between western and eastern europe.
in writing. − this report assesses the implementation of the 30 ten-t priority projects whose delays have important consequences for the future of sustainable european transport policy. despite the difficulties, it is essential to continue to invest in the changes needed to establish intermodal connections, with citizens and freight transport in mind. the various challenges of today, ranging from security to regional issues, social, economic and financial habits and the environment, must be taken into account. it would therefore be important to stress the need for an integrated and more coherent approach to the transport network. the importance of cross-border links is clear for the advancement of the european transport network, hence supporting better interconnection between all modes of transport and in particular those which have had the most difficulty in their transformation and development, such as rail or maritime transport. they must establish better links with ports and airports, and the proper implementation of the transeuropean networks projects will certainly contribute to this objective. the report reflects some of these concerns, but not all of the text reflects the order of priorities that i consider appropriate. that's why i voted against it.
mr president, i too would like to join in the congratulations already addressed to our rapporteur for his work, to the various political groups, to the commission, to our committee in the person of its president, and say that not only is the existence of blue bags absolutely unacceptable to us, but also that this so-called gentlemen's agreement, which will be 39 years old, has, in my opinion, no reason to exist today. we're not an amateur club today. no, i'm not going. we are a people's europe. we all have to answer to him. we in the european parliament, with the reforms we have just put into effect, are fully available from this next mandate to be fully responsible for the management of all our accounts. the council can do nothing else. it is absolutely regrettable that the council has not taken the opportunity afforded it to be here. that's exactly why we picked this time. and we would like to say that this is certainly not going to be the case and that we will do everything we can to hold the council to account for the way it implements its budget.
mr president, commissioner tajani, i would first like to congratulate mr teychenné on the quality of his report. on the maritime package to be signed tomorrow in an area where i was rapporteur for the socialist party on the obligations of the flag state and also on the responsibilities of passenger carriers, i said that in the european union people should come first and in this union passengers must also come first. the teychenné report deserves my full support because it strengthens the regulatory framework for both bus passengers and, in an area which is dear to me, for sea and inland waterway passengers, namely 25% of the ticket price in the case of delays of one to two hours, 50% of the ticket price in the case of delays of two hours or more or 100% if the carrier does not provide alternative transport or information services. i would remind you that these compensations are payable in the event of cancellation or serious delay of travel up to one month after the passenger's request. in the case of bus transport, this report recognises the need for additional measures for disabled people and persons with reduced mobility. for all passengers, the right to a refund of the current price in the event of cancellation, overcrowding or a serious delay of at least two hours of the journey becomes a reality. i have tabled an amendment, mr president, to the effect that this regulatory framework should also apply to the outermost regions. i am sure that legislation of this quality is the same, that it will not be questioned by anyone and will be appreciated by all european citizens, including those from the remotest regions such as the azores, madeira, the canary islands and the french overseas departments.
the joint discussion of the reports is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow, 23 april. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
the question period is now over (b6-0227/2009). the following questions were put to the commission. part one
question no 28 by sarah ludford (h-0142/09) subject: the use of the european parliament in the european parliament subject: electronic commerce and services what other measures will the commission take to ensure that the barriers imposed on consumers by virtue of their country of residence or the place of registration of their payment card, which prevent them from accessing the most competitive prices and a range of goods and services, such as music downloads, train and plane tickets, dvds, computer games - in particular those sold online - for sale anywhere in the eu, are completely removed?
question no 29 by mr claude moraes (h-0149/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: eu strategy to combat alzheimer's disease and the effects of the disease in february, the uk announced its national dementia strategy which will ensure investment in a network of memory clinics, improve support for people affected by the disease and launch wide-ranging public awareness campaigns. in addition, on 5 february parliament adopted in strasbourg written declaration 0080/2008 - p6_ta (2009) 0081 which encourages the recognition of alzheimer's disease as a european public health priority. what initiatives has the commission taken in this field in terms of research, prevention and public awareness? does the european commission envisage the possibility of creating a european strategy to combat alzheimer's disease similar to that of the united kingdom, taking into account in particular the recommendations of the european collaboration project on dementia already submitted to the commission?
question no 30 by mrs rodi kratsa-tsagaropoulou (h-0163/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: threat of recession in south-eastern europe and consequences for the european economy recent analyses by international financial institutions and credit rating agencies predict a significant economic slowdown in south-east europe, highlighting in particular the risk of default on loans by consumers and businesses. such a recession could have very serious consequences for the economies of the member states of the union, given the significant investments of western european companies and banks in the region. does the commission consider it appropriate to adopt measures to support banks in the countries of south-east europe, within the framework of the national action plans of the european neighbourhood policy, in collaboration with these countries and, where appropriate, with the european investment bank? how does the commission intend to use the possibilities of the available funding instruments, such as the pre-accession mechanism and the european neighbourhood and partnership instrument, to stimulate local economies and to avert the possibility of recession?
question no 31 by mr gay mitchell (h-0131/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: state aid to non-community countries in this period of economic turmoil it is imperative that all the nations of europe stand on an equal footing and do not have to worry about jobs going to non-community european countries which give state aid to companies in difficulty. if eec or efta member countries such as switzerland are to reap the benefits of increased trade with the eu bloc, then it is imperative that they respond by acting under the same state aid rules. the current procedures for dealing with this situation are slow and cumbersome and do not protect those who are currently losing their jobs. how does the commission intend to strengthen these aspects of competition law and ensure a rapid and efficient procedure for dealing with complaints?
question no 32 by mr zbigniew krzysztof kuzmiuk (h-0165/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: discrimination against polish shipyards in the light of the commission's endorsement of state aid to the automobile sector after having allocated several tens of thousands of euros to their banks, the governments of several member states of the european union have now decided to support the automotive sector. the united kingdom, germany, france and italy want to contribute to this economic sector with aid amounting to a few tens of thousands of euros, a proposal which the commission accepts in principle without restriction, although this method undoubtedly distorts competition in this market. without questioning this support, i would like to ask why the european commission has previously questioned the state aid granted by the polish government to our shipyards. the commission's position has led to the closure of two polish shipyards and the dismissal of several tens of thousands of workers employed and subcontracted by these yards. compared with the commission's recent endorsement of aid to the motor industry, is this decision not discriminatory?
question no 33 by giovanna corda (h-0171/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: online sales of perfumes, clothing and branded products commission regulation (ec) no 2790/1999 oj l 336, 29.12.1999 (and the guidelines on vertical restraints) lays down the rules on distribution agreements, covering in particular products such as perfumes, clothing and other 'brand name' products. under the scheme, it is prohibited to sell, in particular online, a large number of products where the market share of the supplier is less than 30%. does the commission consider that this archaic system should be maintained at the expense of healthy price competition and consumer choice and for the exclusive benefit of the economic interests of a few large groups whose revenues come for the most part from these comfortable, legal, exclusive rights?
question no 34 by georgios papastamkos (h-0172/09) subject: state aid to small and medium-sized enterprises can the commission state what additional measures, compatible with community state aid rules, are available in the member states in this crisis period to help small and medium-sized enterprises, and in particular to support their access to finance?
question no 36 by mrs marie panayotopoulos-cassiotou (h-0154/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: research and professions related to the sea marine science, technology and research are of fundamental importance for the sustainable development of marine-related activities and contribute to broadening the field of marine studies and deepening qualifications and competences related to the maritime professions. in this context, what action does the commission intend to take to promote research in this sector and to give european citizens more and better career prospects in sea-related activities? will the economic crisis affect this objective negatively?
question no 37 by mr emmanouil angelakas (h-0158/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: assessment of creativity, innovation and technological development in the member states given that 2009 is the european year of creativity and innovation and given the programmes available to the eu, namely the 7th framework programme for research and technological development (2007-2013) and the competitiveness and innovation framework programme (cip), does the commission have statistical data on the funds absorbed so far in each member state by the sectors covered by the two programmes? what are the most popular sectors in percentage terms per member state? has the response of smes to these programmes (in percentage) been assessed?
question no 38 by mr justas vincas paleckis (h-0174/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: bonus - initiative 169 by the commission the bonus (network of marine science funding organisations in the baltic sea) project is of great importance for bringing together national and regional research programmes on sustainable development in the baltic sea region and for coordinating, developing and implementing them through joint cooperation activities. for this reason, lithuania and the other baltic sea states strongly support the transition from the era-net+ project to initiative 169. can the commission indicate the new measures planned for bonus - initiative 169? will the proposal be submitted during the term of office of the present commission? what are the possible interferences that hindered the transition from bonus to initiative 169?
question no 41 by marian harkin (h-0137/09) subject: the use of the european parliament in the european parliament subject: communication with citizens given the importance attached by the commission to effective communication with citizens, especially at a time of economic crisis when many citizens are fearful about the future, and given that the consultation process is an effective tool to give citizens the opportunity to directly influence the political process at the level of the european union, does the commission not consider that more needs to be done to inform citizens about the european union's consultations through the media and other forums at national, regional and local level in order to achieve the participation of more people and grassroots organisations?
question no 42 by mr david martin (h-0155/09) subject: the role and participation of the commission in the forthcoming european elections can the commission explain what impartial role it will play in order to increase participation levels in the european elections?
questions which were not answered in due time will be answered in writing at a later date (see annex). the commission's question period is closed. (the sitting was suspended at 8 p.m. and resumed at 9.05 p.m.)
mr president, my colleagues roth-behrendt and sacconi have already presented our group's position very well. we therefore consider it essential to change the legal basis. health care, commissioner, is not a commodity. it is therefore incomprehensible that the commission has not included article 152 of the treaty. the requirement of prior authorisation for hospital and specialist patient protection care is also indispensable. only with the requirement of prior authorisation can safe and quality care be guaranteed. i would like to conclude by wishing my colleague john bowis well, and his case proves that crossborder services already work without this directive.
mr president, it is unacceptable that under the pretext of the application of patients' rights to health care, the european commission and the majority of parliament actually want to call into question the national and public health service, as is the case in portugal. by seeking approval of this proposal on the basis of article 95 of the treaty on european union, which provides for harmonization in relation to the internal market, it seeks to liberalize the sector, which is unacceptable. this is a sector where the logic of the market and profit must not prevail in order to serve the interests of economic and financial groups. health can't be a business. that is why we are proposing to reject this proposal from the european commission. to ensure crossborder healthcare, there are already regulations and agreements that can be improved without compromising the competences and rights of the member states in relation to the ownership and management of their national health service, which we believe should be public and universal, accessible to all.
in writing. - this proposal clarifies the rules for being a road haulage operator. the new rules are presented with the aim of increasing the safety and excellence of this business and ensuring common criteria for the financial management of these companies. the requirement to have a trained manager responsible for managing the company's traffic, as well as to demonstrate its financial health, are signs of this new way of looking at the activity. other important elements of this text are the points concerning the protection of personal data, the creation of a register with a public and a confidential section, and the termination of so-called post office box companies. the conditions for access to the profession, namely reputation, financial health and professional capacity, correspond to a clarification of this business, which we hope will allow it to prosper in a more transparent way, ensuring better protection and safety for customers.
in writing. − prices and reliability of energy supply are critical factors for the eu's competitiveness, and increasing energy efficiency is one of the most cost-effective ways for the eu to meet its co2 emission targets, create jobs, reduce business burdens, address the social impacts of energy price increases and reduce its growing dependence on energy from abroad. the energy performance of buildings currently accounts for about 40% of energy consumption and the recast of the directive will improve the current situation. all relevant stakeholders should be informed of the benefits of energy performance improvement and should have access to relevant information on how to proceed. it is therefore important that financial instruments supporting the improvement of the energy efficiency of buildings are accessible to local and regional authorities.
in writing. - the report by mr albertini seeks to contribute to creating the conditions for a clearer picture of the use and operation of bus transport. by addressing issues relating to the rights of persons with reduced mobility and by establishing clearer rules in the event of death or accident of passengers or in the event of loss or damage to luggage, this report is working towards greater safety for passengers and businesses alike. the commission is also proposing solutions for compensation and assistance in the event of cancellation, delay or interruption of travel. this will create the conditions for better information for passengers before, during and after the journey and clarify their rights and the responsibilities of operators, with the aim of making them more competitive and safer.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the brian crowley report on the term of protection of copyright and related rights because it promotes european artists and european music. the european parliament's proposal introduces greater benefits for artists, who are protected throughout their lives, similar to what happens in the usa and in accordance with the european principles of valuing creativity and culture. i think that extending the term of protection from 50 to 70 years will promote investment in musical innovation and result in more choice for the consumer, allowing europe to continue to compete competitively with the world's largest music markets.
in writing. - portuguese music industry officials consider this to be a matter of great importance for the european and portuguese music industry. they also stress that the commission's proposal to extend the term of protection of recorded works for artists and record producers meets a need if europe is to remain competitive with the world's largest music markets. the support of creators and performers is evident when almost 40 000 artists and musicians signed a petition calling on the european union to close the gap with other countries that already provide for a longer term of protection. the extension of the term of protection is expected to promote reinvestment in a very wide variety of new music resulting in greater choice for the consumer. it should also be noted that the recording industry is a major contributor in terms of employment and tax revenue and is a major exporter of intellectual property. for these reasons, which were mentioned by those responsible, i voted in favour of the compromise text voted on today. this approval will enable a consensus to be reached between the council and parliament and will facilitate the adoption of the directive by the council.
in writing. the establishment of the second marco polo programme is a major step forward, as it provides the necessary financial assistance for measures to increase and improve the environmental performance of the freight transport system. the proposal follows the evaluation of the effectiveness of the marco polo programme itself, which concluded that, so far, only 64% of the modal shift target has been achieved - far short of the estimated targets. the new marco polo is expected to have better financial conditions to pursue its objectives, which now also include projects relating to motorways of the sea and projects relating to measures to avoid traffic congestion. i believe that this programme, which seeks to stimulate and support projects for the shift from road freight to sea, rail and inland waterway transport, should have all the conditions to help reduce congestion, pollution and transport which is more efficient and environmentally sustainable.
in writing. the creation of a genuine internal rail market is of the utmost importance for the objectives of a sustainable european transport policy. that is to say, for the future of europe and its transport. it is also important that this sector is an integral part of the measures that will contribute to the success of the lisbon strategy. rail freight is also a very important factor among the various transport work areas. the creation of a european rail freight network with trains running in good condition and crossing easily from one national network to another will hopefully allow for improvements in the use of infrastructure and facilitate more competitive freight. i think it is essential to support measures to improve the situation of the rail freight sector, with a view to its being fully integrated and integrated into the whole of the future european transport network.
in writing. - the serious issue is that the report was adopted without changing the basis of article 95, namely the consideration of health in the internal market as a commodity, which is unacceptable. it would therefore have been better to reject the commission's proposal, as we have argued. but, unfortunately, most of them did not accept our position. patients' rights in cross-border healthcare do not therefore safeguard the exclusive competence of member states to decide how to organise and finance their healthcare systems, including their competence to set up prior authorisation systems for the purpose of hospital treatment abroad. the right of citizens to health, as well as the rights of professionals in the sector, are not guaranteed. the need was for greater solidarity and coordination between social security systems in the different member states of the european union, particularly with a view to implementing, strengthening and responding more adequately to the rights and needs of users of health services. for all these reasons, we voted against the report.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal on patient safety. although the quality of healthcare in europe has improved substantially due to the development of medical science, medical acts may in some cases cause harm to the health of patients. medical errors or infections caused by treatment are some of the causes of undesirable effects that could be avoided. the report makes important proposals: improving information collection at local and regional level; better information for patients; increasing the presence of nurses specialised in infection control; promoting education and training for health personnel; paying more attention to hospital infections. measures that i fully support.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on rare diseases, as i consider that concerted action in the field of rare diseases is necessary at european and national level. although the incidence of each rare disease is very low, there are always millions of europeans affected because there are many rare diseases. i think it is of the utmost importance to support independent organisations, to provide access to information on rare diseases, to set up specialised centres in the different member states, to set up training courses in existing centres and to mobilise experts and professionals. the creation of adequate resources for immediate action in the area of rare diseases is necessary.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the challenges of deforestation and forest degradation, as i consider that deforestation causes very serious environmental damage which is difficult to correct, such as the imbalance of water resources, desertification, impact on the climate and the disappearance of biodiversity. there is a need to ensure greater coherence between forest conservation and sustainable management policies, as well as the internal and external policies of the european union, and therefore an assessment of the impact on forests of the european union's energy (especially biofuels), agricultural and trade policies is needed. i also think that financial support for developing countries to stop tropical deforestation is of the utmost importance. reducing deforestation will play a very important role in mitigating and adapting to climate change.
in writing. - the current rapid growth of cities, together with the concentration of european populations in urban centres, are the data that this european parliament report seeks to analyse, trying to contribute to the much that needs to be done in this field. in the light of the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality, proposals have been put forward which i consider to be important. the most important points in this parliament's position are the warning about the dispersion of measures which may suffer from inconsistency, not only as a legislative body but above all in the field of implementation. i agree with the need for a coherent approach, taking into account the incentive to optimise the various modes of transport in urban centres through better planning. i also support the continuation of research and innovation in this specific field and the commission's collaboration with the member states, contributing to the exchange of information on good practice in the various countries. finally, i would like to stress the importance of european industry in developing technologies that can improve the management, safety and energy efficiency of urban transport for european cities.
mr president, the g20 was important above all because it created a space for multilateral dialogue and made it clear that without multilateralism the crisis cannot be solved. but it is a starting point, not an end point, and the role of the european union must be strengthened, it must be clear and it must be mobilizing. at the moment we have no indication that this is happening. we have a very important script, the larosière report, but the commission has been slow to implement it, has been slow to react. look at the reaction to hedge funds, for example. and yet the real economy in europe is still showing no signs of recovery, and the policy that has been followed of waiting to see means waiting for worse numbers, for a situation that is getting worse every day. look at the latest estimates from the international monetary fund and the oecd: 27 million people out of work. this is a huge problem. the commission also owes this parliament an explanation of what it intends to do, what is actually in the field of its initiative and what is the concrete state of play in the policy of coordinating the initiatives of the member states. but we must not wait any longer. the political will to act must be there now.

the report by angelika beer, on behalf of the committee on foreign affairs, on a proposal for a european parliament recommendation to the council on the non-proliferation of weapons and the future of the treaty on the non-proliferation of nuclear weapons (npt) [2008/2324 (ini) ] (a6-0234/2009) follows.
on behalf of the pse group. – mr president, i would like to congratulate mrs beer on an excellent and timely report. this is the year of all nuclear debates. the us is preparing its nuclear posture review, nato is reviewing its strategic concept and the world is getting ready for the 2010 npt review conference. all of this is happening in a special context. the us is once again being led by a president, barack obama, who espouses the vision of a world without nuclear weapons. this is not the time for timidity or hesitation. our voters would not understand if this parliament prepared a report on the npt which was less ambitious than that of the current us administration. of course, us leadership on this issue is welcome. after all, the us and russia are the undisputed global nuclear hegemons. however, europe cannot sit passively by while moscow and washington discuss its strategic future. we need to present our own npt narrative, based on a strict balance between the non-proliferation and disarmament agendas. this is what this report is all about and this is the spirit of the socialist plenary amendments. why not support the model nuclear weapons convention and the hiroshima-nagasaki protocol, both of which are promoted globally by civil organisations and political leaders? it is our role as parliamentarians, unencumbered by the weight and limitations of executive power, to show our governments, and therefore the council, the way in this field. i hope the house can support the pse amendments.
the president-in-office of the council. - mr president, madam member, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to express my appreciation for this debate. as i mentioned at the beginning of this debate, a watershed moment has begun in the existence of the npt and the arms control process. i'd like to thank all of you who spoke for your comments. i would like to express my unequivocal agreement with the support for verifiable, concrete and realistic steps in this area, as expressed by mr zieleniec, and also to endorse the statement by mr onyszkiewicze, who said that disarmament is a slow process and that it is all the more important to support those very realistic steps. at the same time, i agree with the need to draw attention to the misuse of a possible peace programme for the production of fissile materials, as pointed out by mrs harms, and also with the need to take a firm stance against those countries which misuse a peace programme for potential armaments, and i agree with mr claeys on this point. i would also like to emphasise that the european union will continue to participate in the review of the npt and will actively contribute to the success of the whole process. the union will present concrete proposals for the future action plan for the 2010 evaluation conference under all three pillars of the treaty to the forthcoming preparatory committee in new york in may. we will present our proposals in the form of joint declarations on the individual pillars and in the form of working papers. in preparation for the 2010 review conference, the council intends to develop a revised and updated common position. the european union will seek a successful outcome and will work together with key partners to ensure broad support for our objectives ahead of the preparatory committee meeting. new opportunities in the field of nuclear disarmament must also be seized, and the european union is determined not to remain a passive spectator. the european union is made up of the member states which have nuclear weapons, the member states which do not, the member states which use nuclear energy and the member states which oppose its use. as a whole, it can and is determined to play a significant role. the council is committed to informing the european parliament regularly of future developments, both as regards the outcome of the preparatory committee's deliberations and more generally in the context of preparations for the conference to be held next year.
member of the commission. − mr president, i would like to thank all those who have taken part in today’s constructive debate. this is indeed a subject of great significance. for our part, the commission will continue its work in support of the npt treaty through our work in verifying the proper use of fissile nuclear materials under the euratom treaty. it works to thwart attempts by terrorists to gain access to chemical, radiological and nuclear materials, through a strengthened community regime of export controls on dual use, support for the efforts of the iaea to secure vulnerable nuclear materials and tackle nuclear smuggling – which is very important – and the promotion of a worldwide nuclear security and safety culture using the substantial resources available under the stability and nuclear safety instruments. i look forward to continued cooperation with parliament in taking these objectives further and i count on our cooperation in the future.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on friday, 24 april. the competent authority shall inform the applicant of the reasons for the refusal.
in writing. although it contains aspects which we consider to be positive, the present proposal for a european parliament recommendation falls short of the requirements that must be met in the field of nuclear disarmament, particularly when it focuses solely on non-proliferation. what is needed and required is the implementation of a whole programme prohibiting the use and production of nuclear weapons, promoting their total and complete dismantling, the creation of zones free of these weapons, the relaunch of negotiations on nuclear disarmament and the strict application of the non-proliferation treaty, including by the current nuclear powers. a programme of nuclear disarmament, necessarily accompanied by the demilitarisation of international relations, respect for the united nations charter, the end of colonialism, the principles of non-interference and the peaceful settlement of international conflicts. a programme which calls for the non-militarisation of space; the reduction of conventional armaments and military expenditure (and not their increase, as the us administration defended at the recent nato summit); the end of foreign military bases; the rejection of the militarisation of the eu and its transformation into a political-military bloc; the non-overlapping of nato and the un in security matters and the dissolution of political-military blocs.
