author. − mr president, gender responsiveness is a measure of good governance anywhere in the world, but even more so in afghanistan after the suffering endured by women for decades there. there cannot be true peace and recovery in afghanistan without priority being given to respecting the human rights of women. this shia family law allows marital rape, approves child marriage and bans wives from stepping outside their homes without their husbands’ permission. women’s human rights and dignity cannot fall victim to pre-electoral negotiations with islamic fundamentalists. the international community present in afghanistan must put much more pressure on president karzai and the afghan authorities to come up with proper laws which respect the human rights of women, and policies that are committed to enacting those rights and respect their dignity. like the shia family law, the delaying of the afghan media law, which was passed by the afghan parliament months ago by two thirds of the parliament, is a tool by president karzai to continue control of the state media, vital propaganda ahead of the presidential elections. the international community cannot allow this to go on. this law is fundamental to ensure that there is freedom of expression and of the media in afghanistan. without that, anything that we are doing in afghanistan is not worth it. it is crucial that action is taken on these two laws and that the international community makes sure that the authorities in afghanistan abide by their commitments, by their words, in terms of human rights, and respect in particular the rights of women.
author. − mr president, some in this chamber want to present the people’s mujahedin as heroes or a true alternative to the iranian regime. they are neither. in my trips to iraq, i have heard kurdish, sunni, shia, christian, turkman leaders and others complain about the role of the people’s mujahedin as a tool of saddam hussein in the 1988 anfar campaign, which culminated in massacres such as halabja. that is what the iraqi delegation which was here this week also confirmed to us, while assuring us that the iraqi constitution binds the government of iraq to fully respect the human rights of the residents of camp ashraf, who, with the help of unhcr and icrc, wish to leave for iran or any other destination, or wish to stay as political refugees abiding by the laws of iraq. we must understand the reluctance of the iraqi government to let camp ashraf continue to be a nuisance to their good neighbourly relations with iran. for iraqis, iran cannot be wished away. it is there. it is a powerful neighbour. it is true that the people’s mujahedin are no longer on the terrorist list, but they are still an opaque cult which brutalises those of its members who wish to defect. above all, the people in camp ashraf are human beings whose human rights have to be respected, irrespective of the fate of the organisation itself or of its past. they are to be treated under the 1951 refugee convention and no one – i repeat, no one – should be forced to return to iran. but let us make one thing clear. this resolution is not about the iranian regime, which has been repressing its people, mismanaging the country and destabilising the middle east for decades. anyone who presents a vote for the pse and green amendments, which aim to balance the tone of this resolution, as a vote for the iranian regime, is either arguing in bad faith or has simply run out of arguments. the spirit of our amendments is quite simple. we want to present the whole picture of human rights violations and threats in and around camp ashraf. for example, we request that all camp ashraf residents be allowed to be interviewed by the icrc and unhcr at a neutral location and without people’s mujahedin officials in attendance, in order to clarify their real wishes. also we must call on the mujahedin leadership to stop controlling the lives of the residents of camp ashraf, namely by not letting them leave the camp. above all, we express our concern about the reported practices of mental and physical manipulation and severe human rights violations within the cult. in short, this is about the individual human rights of the people in camp ashraf. let us have these people and their human rights in mind when we vote.
mr president, i too would like to call for a vote on this joint resolution as it stands. the amendments which have been tabled are deeply misleading. it is absolutely false that there is one, one refugee from camp ashraf who has been transferred from the alternative camp to europe or even inside iraq with the support of the high commissioner. i defy anyone to ask the high commissioner if ever one was transferred. all of this is mere falsehood with the sole purpose of facilitating a massacre. that is what it is all about, and i would ask the authors of this, which is absolutely shameful, to withdraw these amendments, which are an insult to this parliament.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament's resolution on women's rights in afghanistan because i consider the new draft law on the personal status of shiite women unacceptable. the legislation, recently passed by both houses of the afghan parliament, imposes severe restrictions on women's freedom of movement, legitimises marital rape and promotes discrimination against women in the areas of marriage, divorce, inheritance and access to education, which is not in line with international human rights standards in general, nor with women's rights. i think that the european union should send a clear signal that this bill must be annulled, since its content is contrary to the principle of equality between men and women, as enshrined in international conventions.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the joint resolution in support of the special court for sierra leone, as it is important to ensure that the perpetrators of violent crimes in the light of international humanitarian law, that is to say war crimes and crimes against humanity, are punished and serve their sentences. created in 2000 by the united nations and the government of sierra leone, this was the first international tribunal to be funded through voluntary contributions, the first to be established in a country where these crimes allegedly occurred, and the first to indict an african head of state for war crimes and crimes against humanity.
in writing. - the residents of ashraf are one of the visible faces of the iranian regime's oppression and resistance to that violence. the association that has been repeatedly attempted between the iranian resistance and terrorism is, as far as newspapers, politicians and courts have been able to ascertain, unjustified. on the contrary, the situation in ashraf is public knowledge and several people, including members of parliament and journalists, have visited the camp and drawn their conclusions. the residents of ashraf are protected persons under the geneva convention. for these reasons, the signal given by the european parliament is of the greatest importance: the residents of ashraf have the right to be protected and not to be handed over to the iranian regime under any circumstances. it is a matter of basic respect for human rights. we therefore hope that this resolution will have consequences. finally, a note on the iranian regime. the mistakes made at the beginning and during the intervention of the countries allied with the united states in iraq must not now be added to the mistakes made in the withdrawal. if, at the end of this process, the fundamentalist iranian regime has strengthened its influence in the region, in particular by influencing iraq's internal affairs, that region will have been further away from peace and the world more threatened.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the rumiana jeleva report on the united nations convention on the rights of persons with disabilities, whose powers will be shared for the first time by the community and its member states, as it advocates respect for individual dignity and autonomy and promotes non-discrimination, inclusion in society and acceptance of people with disabilities as part of human diversity.
in writing. - profiling is already used today in numerous areas ranging from law enforcement, administrative and customs border controls, and the fight against terrorism itself. there is increasing interest in using this research technique, which is based on the collection of information about individuals from various sources, and may include more sensitive data such as ethnic origin, race, nationality or religion. however, the use of these techniques has evolved considerably without having had the opportunity to discuss and come to a conclusion as to how and when they can be used, when their use can be considered legitimately necessary and proportionate. as is clear, the necessary safeguards must be put in place to protect the fundamental rights and freedoms of individuals. this situation is all the more worrying if we consider that there should be data interchange between the various data systems, as is the case with sis ii, vis and eurodac. i would like to thank the rapporteur, mrs sarah ludford, for her initiative and for the opportunity which she has given us to start this debate on the basis of this report, which i consider to be quite balanced and which respects the commitments negotiated between us.
in writing. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work. i think this report will complement other existing initiatives on pesticides, agreed at the end of last year. this report introduces a number of important changes, such as changing the wording of plant protection products to pesticides, broadening the scope to include biocidal products and including pesticides for commercial non-agricultural uses. with this regulation the european union will ensure a much safer use of pesticides.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal on the ecodesign of energy-related products, as current consumption patterns have a very significant environmental impact, particularly with regard to greenhouse gas emissions and pollution. i think it is important to change consumption and production habits without incurring significant additional costs for both firms and households.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the schnellhardt report on the regulation laying down health rules for animal by-products not intended for human consumption, as i consider that the proposals contained in this document constitute a substantial improvement in the safety of these products, in particular by ensuring traceability throughout the treatment process. food safety and consumer protection in the eu are thus strengthened.

in writing. − the g20 summit and the awareness of the need for a coordinated and cooperative response to the current world economic situation is an expression of the positive aspects of globalisation. there are no longer any isolated powers, autonomous economies or globalised countries to be dispensed with. on the contrary, those countries that are far worse off than these victims of the crisis, but that have not joined globalization, as most african countries have, still have their problems and remain outside the solution. that is the problem that is not being addressed. the other lesson of these times is that the only alternative to a market economy is a market economy that works better. that's the way to go. finally, it should be stressed that the capacity to respond to the crisis depends very much on whether or not there has been capacity to reform national economies and to create conditions for flexibility. in addition to responding to the financial crisis, we must respond to the transformation of the world economic paradigm. otherwise we will be faced with a profound but temporary crisis without resolving the structural inadequacy of our economies.

the joint debate on the reports of astrid lulling, on behalf of the committee on women's rights and gender equality, on a proposal for a european parliament and council directive on the application of the principle of equal treatment to men and women engaged in an activity in a self-employed capacity and repealing directive 86/613/eec (com (2008) 0636 - c6-0341/2008 - 2008/0192 (cod)) (a6-0258/2009) and edite estrela, on behalf of the committee on women's rights and gender equality, on a proposal for a european parliament and council directive amending council directive 92/85/eec on the implementation of measures to encourage improvements in the safety and health of pregnant workers and workers who have recently given birth or are breastfeeding (com (2008) 0637 - c6-0403/2008 - 2008/0319 (cod)) (a6-0267/2009) will follow.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to thank the shadow rapporteurs, the rapporteurs for the opinions of the committee on employment and the committee on legal affairs, the secretariats of the committee on women's rights and the pse group, the social partners, the ngos, the experts who took part in the public hearings in brussels and lisbon, the representatives of the commission and the council and my assistants. i appreciate all of your support and suggestions. the proposals in my report are therefore the result of a very participatory process and many working meetings with all those interested in providing the european union with a maternity leave directive appropriate to our times. the main objectives of my amendments are: firstly, to strengthen the rights of working women during pregnancy, after childbirth and during breastfeeding; secondly, to promote the reconciliation of work, family and private life; and thirdly, to help europeans to have the children they want and thus increase the birth rate. i therefore propose extending the protection against dismissal to 12 months, exempting women from night work and overtime if they so wish, and reducing breastfeeding time by two hours a day without any loss of rights. but the most innovative proposal in relation to the european commission's proposal concerns paternity leave - community legislation should enshrine the principle of shared leave in order to facilitate a more equitable sharing of private and family responsibilities between men and women and thus promote the quality of life and well-being of children. parents should be associated with family responsibilities from the birth or adoption of a child. it is also necessary to combat the economic, social and cultural prejudices associated with the right to paternity leave and to change attitudes. while, for example, in sweden, a man who does not share parental leave is regarded by society as a bad father, in the countries of southern europe the opposite is true: fathers are coerced by employers and coworkers to not take the leave to which they are legally entitled. so i propose that two weeks of paternity leave be mandatory, non-transferable, 100% paid and without loss of any professional rights. it is well established that the sharing of family responsibilities between men and women is the first step towards the indispensable reconciliation of family and professional life. women have the same right to work as men, but they cannot work as much outside the home as men and three times as much inside the home. family life is one of the reasons why there are more women unemployed than men. on the other hand, it is common in admissions surveys to ask candidates whether they plan to marry or have children. women see their personal lives ravaged and end up being harmed if they manifest the natural desire to be mothers. maternity cannot be seen as a problem for employers, for the economy. on the contrary, it is a service to society, as it allows the low birth rate and the ageing population to be tackled while ensuring the sustainability of the social security system. so it is unacceptable that women should be penalized for being mothers. but the truth is that they often don't advance in their careers, they don't get the usual productivity or profit-sharing bonuses, and they have to submit to the most insignificant and least rewarding tasks professionally. we must change this situation.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social partners are able to take part in the negotiations. − the the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of workers' rights and the right to health. ladies and gentlemen, women are often faced with the choice between professional and family life, especially in the matter of motherhood, which ultimately hinders their physical and mental health and their ability to complete themselves as persons. we must also ensure that women's rights and protection are not restricted in accordance with the european social model, and we would like to include parental leave in this framework, which, as we have seen, was not the commission's choice. commissioner, someone who is 18 weeks away from the workplace needs special training to get back to work so as to ensure the continuity of his legitimate career prospects and not suffer a double penalty in his working life. we must ensure that this is the case: providing job protection for 12 months is essential to ensure the ability to reorganize domestic life as one returns to work, to training and to new rhythms. the change from six to twelve months is therefore common sense. it is crucial that legislation on the rights of selfemployed women is enacted in each member state, and to do so is a clear political signal on which we would like to hear the commission's reaction. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done on this subject. in conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, we believe that this line is more consistent with the european social model we are striving for, and this is not just a women's issue, it is a social issue.
i would like to refer to the report by mrs edite estrela on the improvement of the safety and health of pregnant workers, which is a small step in the protection of motherhood and fatherhood, which are fundamental values and rights of our society. on behalf of my group, the european united left/nordic green left, i have advocated greater progress in the defence of maternity and paternity, in particular 22 months of 100% paid leave. in the report, only 20 months are enshrined, and not all the time you have a 100% guarantee of remuneration. i do not understand the position of the epp, which does not want to accept this either. this small step, which is envisaged in the report, must be approved here in the promotion of the rights of women, children and families. it is not enough to say that we stand for these rights. we need to approve small steps in this promotion and in this defence.
the rapporteur. - madam president, commissioner, i am shocked by the european commission's position on my proposal for paternity leave. it is a conservative position, out of touch with reality and unacceptable. i did not say, commissioner, that parental leave was necessary, but that it was necessary to take paternity leave. if paternity leave is not covered by this directive, why did the commission propose to extend the legal base, and not just article 137 on health protection? why did you introduce article 141 of the treaty on the promotion of equal opportunities for men and women? commissioner, ladies and gentlemen of the ppe, motherhood and fatherhood are important social values which must be respected and which must be associated. this is where the link is made, this directive links paternity leave with maternity leave. and you're thinking of the children, commissioner. it's thinking about the best interests of children, because if they have the support of their parents from an early age, they have a healthier psychological and physical growth. and you are also aware, commissioner, that paternity leave is provided for in the national legislation of many member states. does the commission intend to fall short of the reality, of what already exists in the member states? i am very surprised, commissioner, at these positions. that is why, because there is this lag between the european institutions and reality, the citizens are moving away. that's why the abstention rate is high. we must give the citizens a reason to go to the polls, and what is at stake, commissioner, is to pass legislation that serves the citizens. if citizens realise that the decisions taken in the european parliament improve their lives, they will certainly have more reason to go to the polls next june.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, madam president, we welcomed the failure of the european parliament and the council to agree on a change in the directive on the organization of working time, because what was at stake was to make worse what exists today, both in terms of the average working day and the length of stay. what the european commission and the council wanted was to open the way to a further devaluation of work, an attack on the right of trade unions to negotiate and collective bargaining, by accepting simple administrative provisions to regulate the organization of working time and its payment, calling into question the right to stay and the right to breaks, in a 100-year retreat in labour rights. the obligation to pay in full for all hours of work, including breaks, whether in the health and emergency services, the fire service or any other sector of activity, remains in force, in accordance with the existing case law. we will continue to give voice to the workers' struggle against the notorious opt-out and for the value of work, in defending the shortening of the working day without loss of pay as an important condition in times of recession for job creation and reducing unemployment, in promoting the health and safety of workers and the reconciliation of work and family life. we want a real social europe which will not be forgotten after the european parliament elections.
two issues were highlighted in this negotiation: the so-called length of stay and the opt-out clause. as regards the length of stay, i would say that we were very close to a compromise, but in the final part the council inexplicably backed down. as for opt-outs, parliament could never accept their indefinite generalization, because in practical terms this would mean, quite simply, deregulation of the labour market. if there are 15 countries today which use the opt-out, it is because the level of permanence is inadequate to the reality. the residence scheme is not appropriate to the present situation, especially in the field of health. in parliament's proposal this was solved, and it would not be necessary for so many countries to opt out. this was acknowledged by the presidency during the negotiations. let me be clear, opt-out has nothing to do with flexibility. flexibility is fully achieved through the annualization of the reference period, which was proposed by parliament as early as 2005. parliament has always fought in this process so that we could at least have a glimpse of a future date for the end of the opt-out. but a blocking minority in the council not only accepted this, but wanted to make the application of opt-outs the rule rather than the exception. and i want to remind you that the opt-out was accepted in 1993, but as a clear exception. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware of the fact that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. science and labour law exist in europe because it has long been accepted that one party is at a disadvantage and therefore must be protected. the council's blocking minority, with the rigidity it has shown, wanted to do away with this protection, which is totally unacceptable to me for those who defend the fundamental values which are at the basis of the european social model.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the use of animals for scientific purposes is a relatively sensitive subject which confronts us with controversial and irreconcilable sentiments if we look at it from a one-sided point of view. it is not possible to discuss this issue from the standpoint of defending the interests of animal rights alone, nor to do so from the standpoint of the interests of science alone or from the standpoint of obtaining results at the lowest possible cost. no one is indifferent to the suffering of other living beings, and even less so when it comes to animals as close to us as primates. but our sensitivity is even higher when we are faced with the suffering of human beings caused by accident, war or disease. to combat pain we cannot deny science the tools it needs, including the use of guinea pigs. this report reflects the balance that can be struck between the contradiction of values and feelings we are facing and reveals an effort at compromise which only ennobles parliament and the members who have been most committed to it. i therefore congratulate those with whom i have had the closest contact in this debate, the rapporteur neil parish and the shadow rapporteur for the pes, roselyne lefrançois, for their excellent work and for the consensus they have managed to establish on a subject as difficult as it is controversial. my group, the pse, will therefore vote in favour of this report and the amendments which we intend to make to improve it further, confident that in this way we will be contributing to reducing animal suffering without compromising scientific progress in favour of human health and the viability of european research.
the commission's proposal for a regulation on the marketing of poultrymeat for human consumption, even after antimicrobial treatment, is not acceptable. i would therefore like to thank everyone who has worked with me on this report, including my colleagues and the rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety. we hope that the european commission and the council will take account of this report and the opinion of the european parliament in defending public health, food safety and poultry producers in the european union.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal on the marketing of poultrymeat. the proposal aims to update the rules on the marketing of poultrymeat which have been in force since 1990 and adapt them to the new market situation. it also aims to safeguard the stability of market prices in this sector, to facilitate the marketing of products and to ensure consumer safety and high quality standards for food products. i think that the treatment of poultrymeat with decontaminating substances is unacceptable and that is why i have advocated cold treatment.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the recovery of petrol vapours. gasoline contains volatile organic compounds (vocs) that evaporate inside the fuel tank, filling the empty space above the fuel. when a vehicle is refuelled, the vapors are pushed out of the tank by the incoming fuel and, if not captured, escape into the atmosphere. the commission proposal aims to recover the gasoline vapours which are emitted into the atmosphere during the refuelling of passenger cars. the implementation of gasoline vapour recovery systems at service stations with high capture efficiency is of great importance for improving air quality.
in writing. - transparency is a fundamental principle of the eu. decisions should be taken as openly and as closely as possible to the citizen, thereby giving the institutions greater legitimacy in the eyes of european citizens, while contributing to an increase in trust. the regulation adopted in 2001 was undoubtedly a significant step in this direction, but several amendments appear to be necessary and should contribute to making the european decisionmaking process more understandable, to increasing the level of transparency and to improving the institutions' practices. the present initiative therefore aims to introduce these improvements. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european parliament. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of personal data. i therefore support the rapporteur's proposal to refer this initiative back to the libe committee.
in writing. - i voted in favour of rejecting the european commission's proposal on the organisation of working time for persons performing mobile road transport activities, as i consider it unacceptable and discriminatory that this proposal excludes selfemployed road transport workers. all persons engaged in mobile road transport activities should be protected by community rules limiting the number of hours of work per week. the aim is therefore to ensure the protection of the health and safety of these workers and road safety.
in writing. - it was very important that the majority of the european parliament had approved the proposal which we support to reject this proposal for a directive which the european commission had put forward on the organization of working time for persons engaged in mobile road transport activities. the proposal for a directive was a step back from the existing system, both as regards selfemployed workers and those who are not selfemployed, and as regards current working hours, particularly at night. i had already tabled this proposal in the committee on employment and social affairs, and it had received the majority vote in favour. but the rapporteur (epp) insisted on bringing the report to the plenary, persisting in the attack on essential workers' rights. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of workers' rights and the protection of the environment.
in writing. - we welcome the rejection of the proposal by the european commission, chaired by mr barroso, to exclude so-called self-employed road workers from the directive on the organisation of working time for persons performing mobile road transport activities, as a result of the rejection proposal we have tabled. in fact, in the committee on transport and tourism of the european parliament last february we had already tabled a proposal calling for the rejection of this unacceptable initiative by the european commission. this is the best response to a new attempt to intensify competition and exploitation of road workers, by jeopardizing their labour rights and road safety. what is required is the defence and improvement of the rights and working conditions of road workers, combating precariousness, respecting periods of rest, rest or stay without prejudice to their wages and the labour legislation or collective agreements in force in each member state. it is necessary that the rules on working time, driving and rest periods apply equally to all professional drivers, including self-employed drivers, in order to ensure their safety and road safety, avoiding excessively long working hours and rest periods or inappropriate working standards.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on trade in seal products, since it is based on two assumptions: the prohibition in the european union of trade in certain seal products and respect for the traditions and cultures of the indigenous peoples of the arctic. some 900 000 seals are killed annually in large-scale commercial hunting (this figure does not take into account the number of seals that are caught but not recovered or the number of unreported killings), 60% of which occur in canada, greenland and namibia. norway and russia are the other countries engaged in large-scale commercial hunting of these animals. in the community, sweden, finland and the united kingdom (scotland) hunt seals on a small scale, mainly for fish stock management and pest control purposes. i think this agreement will protect the seals from cruelty and at the same time it will protect the culture of the inuit communities. i also think that this regulation ensures that unscrupulous trade will end and that harmonized rules will be created that will change the whole internal market.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the protection of animals used for scientific purposes. this proposal covers the protection of animals used for scientific purposes relating to human or animal health or animal welfare. every year in the eu, approximately 12 million animals are used for scientific purposes, including 12 000 non-human primates. the directive provides for the obligation to carry out ethical tests and to subject experiments involving the use of animals to authorization. the proposal also includes certain invertebrate species and foetuses in their last trimester of development, as well as larvae and other animals used in basic research, education and vocational training. i therefore believe that the proposal is aimed at better protecting animals used in experiments and at strengthening animal welfare rules in the face of developments in scientific research.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal on pollution from ships, as i believe it is essential to combat a continuing practice in maritime transport, namely the illegal discharge of pollutants into the sea by certain ships. i think that this amendment is absolutely necessary to prevent environmental disasters and the deterioration of water quality by means of penal sanctions which are sufficiently severe to deter potential polluters.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the directive on the indication of energy consumption by means of labelling. environmental issues, and in particular energy efficiency, are of great importance and are fundamental to combating climate change. the aim of the recast directive is to enable the labelling of all energy-related products, including those in the domestic, commercial and industrial sectors. given the urgent need to combat climate change and the european union's commitment to achieve a 20% increase in energy efficiency by 2020, i believe that simple, clear and easily recognisable labelling can influence consumers to make more informed decisions and contribute to the promotion of more energy-efficient products.
the european union must not be left to the mercy of the european parliament, which is the only institution in the world that can do so. we must not ignore the responsibilities of the leaders of the european union and of all those who have put the defence of production and social and labour rights on the back burner in order to give priority to the liberalism of the lisbon strategy, to the irrational criteria of the stability pact, to the guidelines of the european central bank, with its false autonomy which has in fact always served only the interests of economic and financial groups. the current forecasts of the european commission, which point to a fall in gdp of 4% this year and to stabilization only in 2010, accompanied by a loss of 8.5 million jobs in these two years, predicting that the unemployment rate will reach about 11%, is very serious and demonstrates the ineffectiveness of the measures taken so far. who can remain indifferent to this exponential growth in unemployment, which may reach 30 million unemployed in the european union? the community must therefore make an urgent break with the neoliberal policies which have aggravated the crisis of capitalism. the creation of jobs with rights, the defence of community production, the improvement of public services and the fair distribution of income to reduce poverty must be given priority. it is urgent that economic and social cohesion should no longer be a mere phrase without a clear content and that the principle of 'save whoever can' should be maintained. a supplementary community budget is needed to ensure solidarity and to increase levels and support for the most affected populations and the most fragile economies. to create jobs with rights for young people, women and the unemployed, to increase the purchasing power of the population, to boost demand and to support micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. instead of continuing to insist on the draft treaty of lisbon, what is needed is to respect the sovereign decision of the irish people. instead of continuing to insist on giving priority to the financial sector, what is needed is to end tax havens and give priority to the productive sectors of the european union, ending the stability pact and replacing it with a pact for development and social progress. instead of continuing to liberalize regulations and flexibility in employment, we must focus on public investment, which will boost the productive sectors, prevent redundancies, reduce the average working day without loss of wages, curb unemployment, and make quality public services universal in health, education and training, research, housing, justice and the environment. that is what the people of our countries expect of us. if you want more citizens to take part in the european elections, give the answer that the workers and the people of our countries want and deserve.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, mr president of the commission, at the beginning of this parliamentary term one of the main topics of debate was the institutional question. we started with the treaty of nice and five years later we will still have to take some important decisions in nice. the re-election of president barroso, which i naturally support, is certainly one of them. i hope, however, that the treaty of lisbon will enter into force quickly, not only because of its virtues and the improvements it has made over time, but also to allow the institutional debate to stabilise. i hope above all that our energy will then be better used and directed towards the europe of results, which the president of the commission has always spoken about. if the irish vote yes in the next referendum, it will be more because they have realised that europe is a safe haven, especially in times of crisis. reality is thus our most powerful ally but can also be the most fearsome of adversaries. today, the modern promise of europe is above all that of prosperity and well-being for the people, and that promise depends very much on what we are able to decide, including at the next european council. above all, we need political will to respond to the economic situation. protectionism, state control, fear of globalisation are not helping us. we need to unleash the creative energies of the economy, give them space, insist on reform and law enforcement and, of course, support the most vulnerable. this will contribute to a europe of results and not to an abstraction from which citizens will inevitably remain aloof. as i leave parliament, madam president, this is the hope i want to express, this is the hope that animates me.
madam president, mr president of the commission, mr president-in-office, we are in an unprecedented crisis. it was not the time to discuss internally how we should organise ourselves, so i hope that the treaty of lisbon will be ratified quickly so that we can face the world scene united and united as europeans. as regards the economic crisis, in the report i authored, which parliament approved by a majority, it was admitted that the recovery plan, which the commission had presented in the meantime, was not sufficient. today we know for sure that he is not enough. a crisis that started in finance is spreading to the real economy, then to employment and then to society. he's not enough and he's blurry. every crisis contains an opportunity, and this is the commission's opportunity to respond to the real problems of europeans by means of serious financial regulation, not the lightest and most piecemeal possible, as is the case at present, not made up of national and individualistic plans but of a genuine european initiative, not by means of scattered measures but by concentrating on the real challenge of employment. in every crisis there is an opportunity, this is the opportunity for the commission and europe to respond to the real problems of the european citizens and i hope that this will happen through a new approach to the recovery plan.
in writing. the eu tries to hide the 'sun with a sieve', but it is clear how much of the continuing deterioration of the living conditions of workers and the population is due to the eu. over the last 23 years, the eec/eu has promoted the circulation of capital and the financialization of the economy, liberalized and boosted privatization, concentrated overproduction, destroyed and relocated productive capacity, promoted economic domination by some at the expense of dependence on others, intensified the exploitation of workers, centralised wealth as never before, increased social inequalities and regional asymmetries, all under the baton of the great powers and the big financial and economic groups. the european commission's catastrophic 'spring' forecasts are nothing more than a portrait of the consequences of the eu's neoliberal policies - decided and implemented by the right and social democracy - for portugal: more than 600,000 unemployed, loss of real wages, two years of recession, growth in public debt and a budget deficit that will once again exceed 6%. but much worse than these predictions is the current reality for millions of portuguese who see their difficulties increase day by day. on 7 june, the portuguese will have another opportunity to say enough by voting for the cdu!
the rapporteur. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, in the last few months since the report on the renewed social agency was drawn up, the effects of the economic, financial and social crisis affecting europe and the world have been worsening. every day we see more layoffs, more businesses closing, more families in dire straits. more than an economic and financial crisis, i feel we are experiencing a crisis of confidence. according to the latest eurostat data, in february 2009 more than 19 million men and women were unemployed in the european union. in the face of this picture, if nothing is done, the increase in unemployment will surely lead to more poverty, more social exclusion, more insecurity, more crime and above all more mistrust. let us be aware that unemployment, the most visible aspect of this crisis, does not only mean loss of income for those who are unemployed and their families. unemployment is discouraging and can lead to a loss of confidence in themselves and in others around them. even before the crisis we are facing today, the member states of the european union were already confronted with the social problems resulting from weak economic growth, a complicated demographic situation and the difficulties of living with an increasingly globalised world economy. in this report i have tried to reflect these concerns as clearly and as pragmatically as possible. i know that a social agenda is a very broad concept and so i have tried to come up with a balanced report and to present in a clear and concise way what is really a priority. first, the european union institutions can play a major role in reaffirming the importance of the social models and structures of the member states, helping to build consensus on the importance of their universal access, quality and above all sustainability. secondly, we must use all the instruments at our disposal to ensure that people are better and more fully integrated into the labour market. the third priority stems from the conclusion that much remains to be done for the full mobility of citizens within the european union. fourthly, i think the european union has to play a much more active role in promoting social and environmental standards in its external relations with emerging powers such as brazil, russia, india and china, and this is particularly important when it comes to trade agreements. the fifth priority which the commission has tried to implement, even today with this vote which we are also going to have on the report on the globalisation funds, concerns the flexibility of the european structural funds. in order for european citizens to understand and cope with the changes brought about by the current context, the social dialogue must be strengthened with the aim of increasing transparency in decisions on social adjustment and economic restructuring. i often say that we must move from a phase of conflict to a phase of cooperation in relations of social dialogue. the open method of coordination, an essential complement to european union legislation, should also be strengthened. social policies cannot be a blanket of loose actions and ideas, but must be better coordinated between economic, labour, social and environmental initiatives. it is essential that social policy should go hand in hand with economic policy, aiming at a sustainable recovery not only of the economic but also of the social fabric. there is one point on which i want to be very clear, the crisis we are experiencing cannot be used as a pretext for reducing social spending, but it must be said that if this is not the time to cut social spending, it is also the time to continue decisively to implement the necessary structural reforms, so i have to congratulate the commission and president barroso who, in very complex circumstances, have managed to get europe to deal with the problems of the crisis in a coordinated way... (the president cut the speaker off)
the rapporteur. - at this stage of the debate i would like to make three comments. firstly, to say that social policy is not the monopoly of any political force in this house. the epp, the political force i represent here, has made a very decisive contribution to social policy during this mandate. i was very active in several reports, in several dossiers, i recall the review of the european social fund, the globalisation adjustment fund, flexicurity, the report on the european social model, the working time directive and now the european social agenda. i am convinced from the experience of this mandate that it is possible to reach a broad consensus on social policy issues. but we must be more demanding in terms of achieving consensus in political action. and here i have the feeling that at the level of the commission, at the level of the european institutions, we should create much more incentives for local and regional funding, provided that there is convergence between different entities and actions so that social problems can be solved. the third comment i wanted to make had to do with the trust problem. trust cannot be decreed, it cannot be legislated, it depends to a large extent on the behaviour of the institutions. i believe that a culture of cooperation in policy-making helps to restore confidence. i think that during this term of office, here in the european parliament, we have set a good example in the sense of cooperation and the result of this work of the renewed social agency is clearly based on this idea. i agree with the commissioner when he says that the social dialogue must be at the centre of the debate, but more than the social dialogue, i think we are now in a time when social policy must be at the centre of the political debate. i therefore welcome the words which have been spoken here in the context of the summit on employment and social policy. i think it is appropriate at this stage to discuss this matter. finally, as my last speech in this parliament, i would like to congratulate all my colleagues, and the commissioner and the commission, on behalf of the president, for the way in which they have cooperated with me during these five years of intense and exciting activity in this european parliament. thank you so much!
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, in the light of the european union's so-called 5 billion package for the european economic recovery plan, it is useful to recall the resolution this parliament adopted on the mid-term review of the financial framework for 2007-13, which states that the own resources ceiling represents 1.24% of the european union's gni in payments and that in fact it has been less than 1%; that significant margins remain annually up to the limit set by the multiannual financial framework, more than 29 billion in payments in the last three years; that there are margins between the ceiling of the multiannual financial framework and the ceiling of the european union's own resources, more than 176 billion from 2010 to 2013. that said, it begs the question: why, in the face of the worsening economic situation, is not at least the multiannual financial framework being complied with? why does the european union choose to withdraw 2 billion from the agricultural margin when thousands and thousands of farmers are facing increasing difficulties? do farmers need support first to cope with rising production factors and falling producer prices or broadband internet? to ensure this neutral adjustment, from which other budget lines will some eur 2 billion be withdrawn? cohesion too? how will the almost €4 billion for energy projects and the nearly €1 billion for - let us say - the promotion of broadband in rural areas be redistributed? will the so-called unfair return apply? where is the much-vaunted solidarity at european union level? or has the mountain finally given birth to a mouse once again?
this year european wealth will fall by 4% and unemployment will rise to 26 million. the world and the european union should have done more, better and sooner in regulating financial markets. i welcome the work of this parliament, much of it in a climate of urgency, but i stress the contribution of the socialist group, which should have been more recognized in due course. i regret that the commission's reaction was slower, more partial and more light-hearted than the situation required and demanded, as illustrated by the recent proposal on hedge funds. the adoption of the capital requirements directive is another step in the right direction. we know that it is not ambitious enough and falls short of what is desirable, but today the most important thing is to give a clear signal to the institutions and the financial markets that business as usual is over. i therefore commend the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs, and in particular pervenche berès, for their efforts to reach a compromise. this directive allows for the introduction of clear rules, but much work will still have to be done during the forthcoming review of some of the most controversial issues, in particular the level of the value of the securitisation hold. today it is essential that this parliament ensures that this directive is adopted, sending a clear message to the european citizens that things are changing and will continue to change and that we care about them above all.
the rapporteur. - madam president, i would ask the services to bear in mind that the valid language version for all amendments is the portuguese version. secondly, i would ask that the order of voting be changed, that commission amendment no 43 be voted before amendment no 83 for reasons of consistency. finally, i would remind you that this directive which we are about to amend is 17 years old, and is clearly out of date. the revised directive will not come into force before the end of the next parliamentary term. so we're legislating for the future, not the immediate. and above all, we are legislating for the good of the citizens, giving them more reasons to vote in the elections next june. i therefore ask for the support of my colleagues for my report.

in writing. − the european commission communication com ((2007) 281 has issued a challenge to all european institutions: the time has come to look at brazil as a strategic partner, a leading economic actor in latin america and a regional leader. this partnership was launched on 4 july 2007 in lisbon during the portuguese presidency of the european union and the european parliament adopted on 12 march 2009 a recommendation to the council in which it stated that the strategic partnership should allow for the establishment of a regular structured dialogue between the members of the national congress of the federative republic of brazil and the members of the european parliament. despite this declaration of principle, i note with sadness that parliament, despite my appeals to the president of this house, persists in the anachronistic option of making brazil the only one of the bric countries with which it does not maintain an autonomous parliamentary delegation, thus denying its own deliberation and revealing a deplorable inertia and shortsightedness in the face of the real importance of brazil in the world. i hope that future colleagues, particularly the portuguese, will help to change this regrettable state of affairs and establish fruitful and direct communication with the brazilian congress. i voted against it.
in writing. - millions of europeans depend on the internet, either directly or indirectly, for their lifestyle. limiting, restricting or conditioning it would have a direct negative impact on the daily lives of the population and of a large number of micro and smes that directly depend on this resource to carry out their activity. it was therefore important that our group's proposal to maintain free trade between users not controlled or promoted by the intermediary should have been approved with our vote in favour. however, it seems that the council is not prepared to accept this amendment by the majority of the european parliament which runs counter to the limitation agreement agreed in the negotiations with the council. but it's a small victory, considering you stopped a bad proposal from passing. congratulations to all those who defend the free movement of internet and free software. it is a fight we will continue to ensure that citizens' rights are protected and that end users have unrestricted access to services.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the astrid lulling report on equal treatment for men and women in selfemployment, although i think that more should have been done to strengthen women's rights and maternity protection. in europe, selfemployment, which accounts for 16% of the active population, remains a minority activity. only one third of the selfemployed are women. this proposal should remove obstacles to women's access to selfemployment by providing for specific measures or advantages to facilitate the exercise of selfemployment. i think that the spouses who work for us should have a very well defined occupational status and social protection equal to that of the selfemployed.
, in writing. - this partial improvement of the european globalisation adjustment fund falls short of what is needed in the serious crisis we are experiencing and has not even taken into account some of the proposals we have made to increase the community contribution to 85% of the amount to be allocated to the unemployed, or even to double the value of the fund so as to cover more people who are victims of business closures. that is why we abstained. the amendment to the rules of the european globalisation adjustment fund, which was adopted today, is intended to enable the fund to intervene more effectively in co-financing the training and placement of workers made redundant as a result of the economic crisis. the new rules extend the scope of the fund and provide for a temporary increase in the co-financing rate from 50% to 65% in order to provide additional support from the fund during the financial and economic crisis. but countries in financial difficulty will make little use of the fund, as they still have to bear a high share of co-financing.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the programme for granting financial support to projects in the field of energy. the european parliament's investment proposal, based on an agreement reached by the council, is based on three pillars: interconnection of gas and electricity networks, carbon capture and storage and offshore wind energy projects. as such, the proposal lays down procedures and methods of financial support which aim to stimulate investment in the creation of an integrated european energy network and which, at the same time, reinforce the european union's policy for the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions. there is a need for immediate action to stimulate the european economy and therefore it is important to have measures that ensure an appropriate geographical balance and a rapid implementation. in portugal, projects for the interconnection of gas networks (infrastructure and equipment) and also projects to strengthen the interconnection of the electricity network with spain are eligible.
in writing. - if there were any doubt as to the real objective of this proposal, it would be sufficient to quote the wording in the text adopted today, namely to ensure the removal of the obstacles which continue to hamper the proper functioning of the internal market, as stated in article 2 of the proposal, which clarifies that the general objective is to improve the conditions under which the internal market operates. it is, however, at least once more a paradigm shift that, after the failure of the so-called plan to revive the european economy and the so-called european solidarity, the first and only proposal to create a community support programme so far is directed at financial services! it seems that we are not facing one of the major crises of capitalism, with the worsening of unemployment, the destruction of the productive apparatus or the growing inequalities and difficulties of the peoples and workers. the proposals we have been making - to strengthen the community budget, to set up community programmes to support the productive fabric, to defend employment with rights and public services - have been rejected, but when it comes to supporting the financial market and the proper functioning of the internal market, the community's financial resources are not lacking. this is unacceptable. that is why we are voting against it.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the protection of animals at the time of slaughter. every year millions of animals are killed in the european union. many animals are subjected to forms of treatment that cause unnecessary suffering, not only during rearing and transport, but also at the time of slaughter or killing and related operations. the suffering of animals in slaughterhouses must be avoided, including animals reared for the production of food and other products. the proposal seemed to me to be balanced and consistent with the community's objectives of animal protection and welfare. i agree that large-scale slaughter of animals should be carried out in accordance with humanitarian standards and limit the suffering of animals. i did not, however, vote in favour of the amendment which removed the ban on the use of reverse or other unnatural restraint systems for cattle, as i believe that this practice is harmful to the welfare of the animals.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the silva peneda report on a renewed social agenda. in the context of the current economic crisis, it is crucial that social policy is linked to economic policy with a view to a recovery of the european economy. european social models are faced with a number of challenges, including demographic change and globalisation, to which they cannot remain immune. as a consequence, they need to be modernised in the long term, while preserving their original values. europe must be ambitious in its social policy, especially now that we are going through a serious crisis. i think, however, that the commission's renewed social agenda is very unambitious, comes too late and is not fully adapted to the challenges created by the financial and economic crises. social and employment policies must be strengthened to reduce or avoid job losses and protect europeans from social exclusion and the risk of poverty.
in writing. - this is a report with many contradictions, but which, in essence, insists on the existing orientations of neoliberal capitalism, albeit attenuated in one or another point, but without changing the underlying policies that are at the origin of the current economic and social crisis. the guideline is the same as always. the crisis is now being used to sell the recipe for more of the same: flexibility, the internal market, public-private partnerships, etc., ignoring that the policies of the european union are also at the origin of the crisis and have contributed to its worsening. the correct concerns it includes do not address or respond to the root causes of the problems identified, namely economic policies, precariousness, liberalization and privatization of public services, etc. alternative responses are lacking, notably in terms of strengthening the role of the state in the economy, in strategic sectors and in the deepening of quality public services, or, furthermore, in the defence of wage and pension increases, although it addresses the need for a better redistribution of wealth, but without pointing out ways to achieve it or advocating breaks with the policies that have aggravated social inequalities.
on behalf of the pse group. some consider the durban review conference a success. the final document respects the five red lines set by the european union and reflects a genuine global consensus, unlike the previous versions which were the subject of so much intense controversy. but unfortunately, it is not this document that will be etched in the memory of those who, out there in the world, watched the debates in geneva. it was rather the deep divisions caused by a conference devoted to a theme of universal vocation, such as the fight against racism, that mobilized the world's attention. europe has once again demonstrated the fragility of european unity on the most politically polarizing issues, as was the case at this conference. nothing would have been more symbolically important than a collective exit from the european union from the room in the face of the provocative and unacceptable intentions of an iranian president who, despite the change of tone in washington, is determined to put his country on a collision course with the west. unfortunately, some european countries were no longer in the room to express their displeasure at a speech whose sole purpose, as the un secretary-general pointed out, was to accuse, divide and even incite. unity is strength, and the european union, by being divided, has shown weakness. the fight against racism and hate speech by ahmadinejad and others deserved better.
the question period is now over (b6-0231/2009). the following questions were put to the council. we have here minister kohout on behalf of the council. i take this opportunity to welcome the decision taken today by the czech senate, which has given rise to good hopes for the future ratification of the treaty of lisbon.
question no 1 by mr manuel medina ortega (h-0205/09) subject: protection of european cultural creation in view of the weakening of european cultural creation due to the proliferation of unauthorized copies of products of this creation, what measures does the council propose to effectively protect these products?
question no 2 by mr gay mitchell (h-0207/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: the use of pesticides in view of the well-known scepticism of the czech president, václav klaus, about climate change, will the council ensure that the opinion of the overwhelming majority of eu citizens and member states, who support the scientific veracity of man-made climate change, will be respected, particularly with regard to the preparation of the copenhagen climate summit and the forthcoming swedish presidency?
question no 3 by avril doyle (h-0210/09) subject: the treaty of lisbon and the czech republic can the council comment on the possible deadline for the czech republic to ratify the treaty of lisbon?
question no 4 by mr bernd posselt (h-0213/09) subject: croatia, macedonia and south-eastern europe what is the council's view of the possibility of concluding accession negotiations with croatia this year, of indicating the date for the start of negotiations with macedonia this year and of defining the european perspective of the states of south-east europe, including kosovo, which is situated between croatia and macedonia?
question no 5 by mr claude moraes (h-0215/09) subject: protecting the most vulnerable from the economic crisis in the conclusions of its meeting of 19 and 20 march, the european council stated that in order to deal with the social impact of the current economic crisis, 'particular attention must be paid to the most vulnerable and to the new risks of exclusion. 'how is the council committed to protecting the most vulnerable, such as new migrants, the elderly, ethnic minorities, the disabled and the homeless, so as to prevent them from being pushed to the margins of society?
question no 6 by marie panayotopoulos-cassiotou (h-0217/09) subject: european employment pact and the european employment pact how does the council assess the proposal for the adoption of a european employment pact, which could be an important instrument for preserving social cohesion and promoting the development and economic recovery of the eu, which is suffering from the consequences of the world crisis?
question no 7 by mr justas vincas paleckis (h-0219/09) subject: the situation in the former yugoslavia subject: health care abroad the proposal for a directive on the enforcement of patients' rights in crossborder healthcare (com (2008) 0414), for which the european parliament is due to give its opinion in april, contains common provisions on the arrangements for reimbursement of medical expenses abroad. the aim of meps is to ensure that, as far as possible, patients in eu countries receive healthcare abroad (for example, by reimbursing higher medical costs in advance, to avoid reserving certain care for the rich), while in the council the tendency is to limit these rights and to allow member states to decide what healthcare abroad they wish to reimburse their citizens. how does the council see the contradictory positions of the european parliament and the council being reconciled? what compromises will be possible?
questions which were not answered in due time will be answered in writing at a later date (see annex).
on behalf of the ppe-de group. – mr president, this is my last speech in the plenary, so it is a bit different. a day will come when federal and cosmopolitan parliaments will join to govern the world. the dream of a shared humanity without frontiers is being born right here in this place of representation, where freedom grows stronger and democracy grows wider. the enlightenment’s visionary idea of a union of peoples gives the first steps within the magic of our rooms and our debates. in this union machiavelli weakens, for we were able to replace the sovereignty of powers with the sovereignty of men. the paradigm of cosmopolitanism settles in our institutions and in our decisions and is now drawing a share of the action. a new anthropocentric vision of law and politics is emerging, and the post-national identity is taking form in the streets of europe. the sublime dignity of man is now the principle that brings together national and international law. it is the ultimate rule for our original coordination. what the european union and its parliament represents is a moral view without precedent in the history of mankind – a collective moral view that entered the strategy of the european states and founded their integration. european people’s mutual commitment is truly the assumption of their common humanity. in fact, isolation and egoism fell on that day of the treaty of rome. now justice emerges precisely from the virtue of politics, like a bridge between kant and aristotle, between freedom and happiness. the peoples of europe know that the only legitimacy is that which comes from human rights, and that the only authority belongs to the powers that respect them. they know that the emancipation of history is only possible through a project of political sharing and global justice. a day will come when the peoples of asia, of the americas and of africa will get together. the dignity of men will cross all cultures, from goethe to pessoa, from bach to tchaikovsky, from mohammed to buddha. human rights as a universal law, as common rule beyond all differences, and europe, to be an example, requires more constitution, more decentralisation, more politics, more expansion. at this moment of saying goodbye, i want to tell you how proud i am to have shared such an adventure with you. (applause)
mr president, mr vice-president, ladies and gentlemen, the european commission has adopted as a matter of urgency an amendment to regulation (eec) no 95/93 on the allocation of slots at airports, the main purpose of which was to suspend the 80/20 rule, that is to say, to prevent slots which have been acquired in the past and which have not been used from being auctioned. it is not a principle ad eternum, not a right of property, as the chairman of the committee, mr paolo costa, has just said, but a transitional measure. this suspension is based on the awareness that the economic crisis has caused a general decline in air passenger and cargo traffic, with a substantial impact on domestic carriers and other economic sectors and, consequently, at a worrying time for employment. we therefore have a duty not to force airlines to fly at economic and environmental costs that are not necessary just to maintain their schedules. hence my support for the suspension of this 80/20 rule. that said, i would like to ask whether this committee will be sufficient to respond effectively to the global crisis affecting this sector or whether, as i believe, the commission should consider and propose a programme of support for this sector to put it, after the crisis, on a path of stability and growth. we remember that many companies, such as my country's flagship company, have overcome economic crises and have consolidated their economies to find themselves today in a crisis situation which is difficult to overcome, a crisis which they did not create but which they suffer from. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, this is the last speech i shall make in this term of office and it may be the last, since it depends on the voters. and so i could not pass up this opportunity without expressing my gratitude for the support and cooperation which i have always received from my colleagues in my modest contribution to the construction of a european project and to the response to the citizens. i would therefore like to express my gratitude and thanks to you, mr president, to mr tajani and to all my colleagues in the party. i remember those who have just spoken here, simpson, gilles savary and others from other parties, such as the chairman of our committee, paolo costa, with whom i had the pleasure of working on several reports, and georg jarzembowski, who i will not forget, as a leader of his party in the transport sector, who was always cooperative, often saying no to me, saying he understood me, but always with great elegance and with great democratic sense. at the very least, this is what i am taking home, to consolidate what good democracy should be, a democracy of respect for pluralism and the pursuit of the objectives that are ours.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the mainstreaming of the gender dimension in the eu's external relations. greater integration of women into politics, including external relations and diplomacy, is essential for the successful implementation of the eu's external policies, including in the areas of aid, development, enlargement, neighbourhood policy, conflict resolution, security and peacebuilding and international trade. despite the plurality of policy documents adopted at eu level on gender equality and women's rights, actual commitment to gender equality and women's rights remains low and budgetary resources specifically allocated to gender issues are insufficient. it is important to stress that mainstreaming gender requires not only high-level political statements, but also the political will of eu and member state leaders.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the jo leinen report. this report provides a detailed analysis of the new powers of the european parliament in the light of the lisbon treaty, in particular the new powers of co-decision, the new budgetary powers, the new approval procedure, the new powers of control, the new rights of information and the new rights of citizens. the final result shows that the european parliament will strengthen its powers, including co-decision, and increase its capacity to influence decision-making, thus strengthening the democratic legitimacy of the european union.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the zwiefka report, as i think it is of great importance to establish a procedure which allows member states to negotiate international agreements on matters falling within the exclusive competence of the ec, in cases where the ec has not decided to exercise its competence. in other words, portugal is currently prevented from concluding international agreements to speed up judicial cooperation, in particular in matters relating to divorce, marriage annulment, among others, because it is considered that the ec has partially acquired exclusive competence over these matters. the proposal allows the commission to authorise the conclusion of such agreements where the community itself does not intend to conclude or has not concluded an agreement on the same subject with a third country. i think it is very important that the negotiation of this regulation should be as rapid as possible, since it is in the interest not only of portuguese citizens but also of the citizens of the rest of europe.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the deprez report. this regulation lays down a procedure for the negotiation and conclusion of bilateral agreements between member states and third countries. i think it is very important to establish a procedure which will allow member states to negotiate international agreements in cases where the ec has not decided to exercise its competence. for example, portugal is currently prevented from concluding international agreements to speed up judicial cooperation, in particular in matters relating to parental responsibility, maintenance obligations, divorce, among others, because it is considered that the ec has partially acquired exclusive competence over these matters. the proposal allows the commission to authorise the conclusion of such agreements. given the close ties that portugal has with certain countries, especially the cplp, and the significant number of immigrants in several countries, it is very important that, in the area of family law, portugal can speed up the recognition of the rights of portuguese citizens in those countries by concluding or revising bilateral agreements. and while i am using portugal as an example, i think it is equally important for all eu citizens that this regulation is negotiated as quickly as possible.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the 2008 annual report on human rights. this report has two main objectives. on the one hand, it is intended to be a documentary reference base for knowledge, debate and evaluation of the actions that took place during the year, with the aim of improving, correcting or developing future actions. it also aims to inform the widest possible public about the actions taken by the european union to promote human rights worldwide. i think it is very important to have a debate to establish priorities, to identify issues requiring european intervention, and to keep up and periodically assess situations requiring increased vigilance. this report also includes the issue of women's rights and shows that there is a gap that needs to be filled in the development of specific eu policies and actions in favour of women's human rights.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the impact of the lisbon treaty on the development of the eu's institutional balance. this report analyses the impact of the treaty of lisbon on the development of the institutional balance of the european union, stressing the importance of the implementation of the new provisions and the first appointments. the eventual entry into force of the treaty of lisbon by the end of 2009 requires a political agreement between the council and the european parliament to ensure that the procedure for the election of the next president of the commission and the appointment of the future commission always respects the new powers that the treaty of lisbon gives the european parliament in this area. the report therefore makes a number of recommendations with a view to taking stock of the institutions and stresses that the lisbon treaty strengthens each of the european institutions in its field of competence.
