on behalf of the gue/ngl group, in another grotesque exercise of distortion of reality, we are once again confronted with the disgraceful operation of interference by the european parliament in venezuela. in fact, this operation is part of the manoeuvres of those who conspire against the democratic and sovereign process of emancipation and social progress initiated a decade ago by the venezuelan people and reaffirmed in 14 electoral processes. once again, what really bothers the promoters of this initiative is that despite all the problems, threats, dangers and interferences, the venezuelan people have been an example that it is worth fighting for and it is possible to build a country and a world that is more just, more democratic and peaceful. this is demonstrated by the achievement of broad areas of popular participation, the reduction of poverty rates, social inequality and unemployment, the fight against illiteracy and the extension of educational provision at all levels, the access of millions of venezuelans to health care, the national network of food markets at subsidized prices, the de facto nationalization of the state oil company and strategic sectors of the economy, the use of productive land by peasants, solidarity with other peoples, among many other examples. and let us ask: what is the moral character of this parliament, which wants to impose a draft treaty rejected by the french, dutch and irish peoples, which approves the inhumane return directive, which violates the human rights of immigrants, many of them from latin america, and which has not a word of condemnation for israel's barbaric aggression against the palestinian people in the gaza strip, in order to teach lessons in democracy and respect for human rights? for the umpteenth time, we say: stop pretending you can lecture the world.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to correct my colleague mr guerreiro, who is misinformed, because according to the undp indicators, the united nations plan, venezuela has not reduced poverty by one tenth in the last ten years. chavez has been a demagogue and a dictator. unfortunately, with support at the ballot box, but with a lot of intimidation on the citizens, as this case of manuel rosales once again confirms. i was with manuel rosales. i had the honor of meeting him on a visit to venezuela. and i deeply regret the fate that has befallen him, because he wants to be a free man in his country and he cannot be a free man in his country. this is the problem of venezuela. i met him as the elected governor of zulia state. he was elected by his people as mayor of maracaibo, and he cannot live in his country because in venezuela people are persecuted and slandered. they are being slandered, as is happening miserably with manuel rosales. it is the worst punishment that can be inflicted on a politician, and we who are politicians in this house should all be aware of it. we can fight for ideas, but we don't have the right to slander anyone to persecute them and put them in jail. this is what's happening in venezuela. it is time, ladies and gentlemen, to define a strategy for latin america. a strategy, an active diplomacy for latin america. i am sure that we are on the side of social progress, in line with the many millions we invest every year in development and cooperation, but we are 100% on the side of democracy, 100% on the side of pluralism, 100% on the side of fundamental freedoms. long live the free venezuela!
madam president, the prime minister has said that the czech presidency has been a success. i'm sorry, mr. fischer, but i have to disagree. the prime minister will be the least responsible, but the czech presidency was not at all consensual. it started with the artwork controversy - controversy that favours the artist, but not the presidency. then internal political instability damaged the image of the european union, that is, of all of us. it is true, mr fischer, that the czech republic has succeeded in ratifying the treaty of lisbon, but it lacks the signature of president václav klaus and we agree that having a eurosceptic president when you are the president of the european union does not help. the czech president gave several signs of euroscepticism, refusing to use the union flag. but more important is the fact that the treaty of lisbon has not been signed. it is a disrespect for all of us and for the citizens of europe. but the czech presidency was particularly bad on the maternity leave directive. i know what i am talking about because i was a rapporteur on that report. the presidency's intervention on this matter was very negative and was greatly helped by mrs lulling, who boycotted the vote on my report. the czech presidency was against extending maternity leave to 20 weeks, it was against including paternity leave, which is essential to ensure that men and women share family responsibilities and thus promote gender equality. mr. prime minister, men are as much needed at home as women are in the workplace. men have as much right to accompany their children as women do to their professional development. mr. prime minister, please don't underestimate women's rights or their capabilities.
the world is facing one of the most serious crises of capitalism, with dramatic consequences for workers and peoples. in the eu, this crisis is a consequence of the neoliberal policies that have been pursued - policies enshrined in the treaties and the so-called 'lisbon strategy', which the lisbon treaty seeks to institutionalise, with increased breadth and depth. instead of seriously attacking the causes of the crisis, the council reiterates the main lines of the policies which caused it, in what constitutes a worrying and irresponsible leap forward, maintaining that: - the intention to approve the lisbon treaty, now by a deception which consists in making what is exactly the same and has already been rejected by the irish people appear different;- the free and unregulated movement of capital and the existence of offshore;- the liberalisation of markets, the privatisation of public services and the increasing financialisation of the economy; - the deregulation of industrial relations, the devaluation of wages, the intensification of exploitation, with the defence of flexicurity; - unemployment is not adequately tackled, and large sums of money continue to be channelled to support the financial sector, while the same attention is not paid to the productive sectors.
since dag hammarskjöld sweden has distinguished itself as a member of the un. so he knows that without bodies with legitimacy and representativeness that are universally recognized, there will be no political force to conclude a kyoto ii, the doha round, the millennium development goals, or regulate the global economy, let alone defend human rights. it is regrettable that the swedish presidency resigns from the informal and contested g20 framework. it is tragic that the eu, the locomotive of the world that mr barroso referred to, quoting the un secretary-general, is lacking leadership and strategic vision on this matter, while president obama proclaims his commitment to the governance of the world, but on the other side we hear only the pope urging the imperative to reform the un security council and the bretton woods institutions. mr president, why is the swedish presidency refraining from leading europe to force reform of the un security council by demanding a seat at the table for european union with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon?
the anti-social programme of the swedish presidency of the council of the european union is a dangerous piece of the deepening of neoliberal policies, reflecting a real leap forward by the leaders of capitalism in the european union, forgetting that their policies are the main cause of the economic and social crisis. although in presenting the programme the swedish presidency referred to the people and the problems of unemployment, it did not point to a single measure to change the existing policies that have caused these problems. on the contrary, i did. he stressed in particular the need for free competition policies in a wide range of areas, including services and external trade, and he placed everything on the recovery of financial markets, the return to the so-called paradigms of the stability pact, and the defence of neoliberalism, which will certainly result in new attacks on social and labour rights. it also did not forget the insistence on a new referendum in ireland on the draft treaty of lisbon, already scheduled for 2 october, persisting in blackmailing the irish people to move faster in the destruction of public services, in the reduction of social rights, including in the areas of social security, health, water, social protection and workers' rights, and it is not difficult to guess that new proposals for directives are expected which will try to take up lines of proposals that were rejected in the previous legislature.
the coup d'état which took place in honduras on 28 june is an act against the free political expression of the honduran people and their most basic democratic rights. since then, the government has de facto been taking repressive measures against the popular movement that is taking to the streets, has established a regime of blocking information, curtailing freedoms, persecutions, illegal detentions, disappearances and even murders of members of the resistance organized to the coup. we have had the opportunity to witness all this during the recent visit of a delegation from the confederal group of the european united left - nordic green left to honduras and nicaragua, where we met the legitimate president of that country, manuel zelaya. in these circumstances, the reaction of the european institutions is ambiguous to say the least. the unacceptable silence of some is compounded by the calls of others for the parties to do everything in their power to reach a political solution quickly, treating equally what is different, concealing responsibility, as if there were not a democratically elected president on the one hand and a coup government on the other that arrested him and expelled him from the country by illegally taking power. the most basic respect for democracy requires that the european institutions firmly and forcefully condemn the coup, implement measures which at international level contribute to increasing pressure on the coup government in power and its isolation, and do not recognise or support any electoral process which is not carried out without the prior restoration of democratic legality in the country.
mr president, it is very worrying what is happening to the former german footwear factory, rhode, in santa maria da feira, now called the luso-german footwear society. this company once employed about 3,000 workers but, after the problems in germany, it reduced its workforce and now has about 1000 workers, mostly women, most of whom are now with reduced work and wages, fearing that after the elections in portugal there will be the closure of the company. we know that unemployment is increasing in this municipality, and that it has already reached many thousands of workers, particularly in the shoe and cork processing sectors, so we call for special and urgent action to prevent another serious attack on production and employment in an area so plagued by unemployment.
madam president, commissioner, we are once again talking about fires, because every year fires consume part of the european forest and with climate change and global warming the future does not look any better. natural disasters cannot be avoided, but they must be prevented. the commission is therefore proposing to reduce the greenhouse gas emissions and to define an appropriate forestry policy, which recognizes the multiple environmental services provided by forests, in particular the carbon sink. it is fair to acknowledge that the european system works better and that in several member states a great deal of effort and investment has been made in prevention and immediate action. for example, in my country, portugal, a plan has been adopted which provides for land reorganisation, the creation of a specific budget for firefighting, the revision of legislation and a programme for the recovery of the lost areas, and yet we still have fires. more and better work is needed in all the member states affected by the fires and the european commission must also adopt the proposals put forward by the european parliament in various resolutions. there are several documents that point to solutions. the barnier report and other documents tabled by parliament have already been mentioned. i myself was rapporteur on a report on natural disasters in the committee on the environment and also made some proposals. it is important to tell member states that they are invoking financial reasons for not being as quick to improve the mechanism, which is more expensive to remedy than to prevent. we also need an international agreement to combat climate change at the copenhagen conference, an ambitious agreement, and we are still waiting, commissioner, for the fire directive, as has already been done with regard to floods.
mr president, i also welcome the commissioner and the honourable members. this is a moment for solidarity, but it is also an opportunity - i would even say it is an obligation - to work on improvements, changes and adjustments. adjustments, as soon as possible, with regard to the european union solidarity fund so that it is used and so that this use is made in time to deal with these disasters, and improvements and changes with regard to forestry policy. we need a european forestry policy which aims to promote forestry and at the same time prevent fires. it should be noted that in many member states, such as my own in portugal, there is still no register of all the land, so it is not known who owns it, which creates difficulties in terms of parcels, reforestation and prevention policy. the question which also remains for this policy of prevention is a suggestion: why not include forestry prevention measures in the economic recovery plan for the european economy, in the plan to revive the european economy? if we were to promote actions to clean up forests - and the result of these actions could, for example, be used for energy production by means of biomass power stations of the right size for this purpose - if we were to act in this way, we would certainly be friends of the environment, we would certainly protect it and at the same time create jobs, which is also one of the objectives of europe at the moment.
mr president, in addition to all the demonstrations of solidarity with this scourge of forest fires which every year affects our southern countries, including greece, italy, spain and portugal, it is urgent to take all the necessary measures to reverse this situation, which destroys our forest heritage, property, animals, biodiversity and, even more seriously, many human lives. it is therefore time to face up to the causes of this scourge and to take account of the growing and serious neglect of the rural world. it is time to change the common agricultural policy to focus on prevention, which also means focusing on the multifunctionality of agriculture, including the mediterranean forest, supporting family farming and giving small and mediumsized farmers and young people the opportunity to settle in the rural world and thus contribute to the prevention measures that prevent this scourge every year.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, the commissioner's statement makes it clear that there are economic and financial sectors and groups in the european union which will benefit from this agreement with south korea, but there is the other side of the coin, commissioner, which she has not considered here, particularly with regard to certain more sensitive sectors such as the textile and clothing sector and the employment of workers. it is therefore worrying, as the industrial sectors and workers' organizations in our countries have pointed out, that the european commission continues to ignore the serious situation in the textile and clothing industries, particularly in my country, portugal, where unemployment is reaching alarming levels, precisely in the regions where these industries predominate, in the north and in certain areas of the centre. we therefore insist on the need for a coherent and concerted strategy to defend european union industry, particularly labour-intensive sectors, either through urgent regulation of the world trade market or through public policies that support investment, innovation, differentiation, vocational training and the creation of jobs with rights.
mr minister, commissioner, there is no choice in the matter of the geneva convention and human rights. the only choice is to honor the agreements we signed. the case law is clear: sending refugees to countries which have not signed the geneva convention on refugees, when they reach european shores, is a violation of the geneva convention. this is not an abstract legal point, this is a real argument. when we, through frontex, through the member states, send refugees to libya, we are violating the convention, all the more so when we know that 75% of the people who arrive on european shores, according to figures from the italian government itself, are seeking asylum, and 50% of those 75, about 38% of the total, are entitled to humanitarian protection. now, political choices lead to moral choices, and we are faced at this moment with a moral choice. is it fair, is it moral, that more than 14,000 people have died trying to reach european shores in recent years? is it morally right that so many of these people risking their lives should have been granted asylum in the first place? will they have to risk their lives? no, it's not necessary that they have to risk their lives. we have been saying this for a long time, a purely repressive immigration policy, like the one that has been followed, puts us in front of these life-or-death choices of people's lives and makes us all co-responsible for this life-or-death choice. so it is not, at the beginning of this mandate, throwing money at frontex, money that frontex cannot spend, money that the commission says is not advisable to give to frontex at this time. that's not gonna solve our problem. what can solve the problem for us is to review the mandate of frontex and then frontex could need more money. for that frontex would have to cooperate, give full information to the unhcr - which it does not at the moment - for that frontex would have to imply in its own policy humanitarian concerns - which it does not imply - and this is all the more serious, ladies and gentlemen, because at the moment the proposal for funds for refugees, while increasing frontex, is decreasing funds.
in writing. - with a view to replacing some provisions of bilateral air services agreements previously concluded between member states of the european union and the government of mongolia, this proposal focuses on aspects which i consider essential, both at the technical level and in terms of fuel taxation and pricing practices. i think that the reservation on the importance of respecting community competition law is particularly relevant, since some provisions of previous bilateral agreements were clearly anti-competitive. i therefore support the report by my colleague brian simpson because it is in line with these general guidelines.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the eu solidarity fund for france because i believe that this fund contributes to responding in an effective and flexible way to situations created by natural disasters, in this case the storm that last january hit the south-west of france causing great damage. this solidarity financial instrument, which is granted in cases where the damage caused by a disaster is so severe that national resources are not sufficient to respond effectively to the crisis, is intended to stimulate economic recovery and to respond to the needs of the affected member state.
in writing. - i believe that solidarity between the member states of the european union, and in particular european support for states affected by disasters, is a clear sign that the european union is no longer just a free trade area. by adopting special aid instruments such as the european union solidarity fund, the community it aims to build 'united in diversity' demonstrates its ability to remain united in adversity, even in situations of particular human and material demand. i can't help but congratulate you on that. i hope that the solidarity fund will not be used too often - that would mean that europe will not suffer many serious emergencies - but i also hope that its structure and availability will be increasingly improved and frequently assessed so that it can meet any real needs quickly and without bureaucracy. i am reminded of the fires that have been ravaging my country, particularly those of 2003, and i recognise the importance and usefulness of the existence of mechanisms such as this fund. i believe that the particularly serious situation in france in january of this year justifies its mobilization. the overwhelmingly favourable vote in the committee on budgets confirms the goodness of this measure.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal for a european parliament and council decision on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund, which provides additional support for portuguese workers affected by redundancies in the textile sector and suffering the consequences of profound structural changes in world trade. the mobilisation of eur 832 800 under the fund aims to reintegrate workers into the labour market through personal employment plans, designed in conjunction with the workers, which provide for the development of personal skills and labour market integration strategies.
in writing. − eu support, in particular the european globalisation adjustment fund, is key to helping the unemployed, who are victims of relocation in a globalised context. in recent years, and particularly as a consequence of the world economic crisis, portugal has felt the effects of the difficult reabsorption and reintegration of the unemployed into the labour market. many companies have moved because of the low labour costs in countries such as china and india, with negative effects on the national economy as a whole. the approval of egf funds to help these workers is essential not only for improving their personal and family situation but also for the whole national economy, since the ultimate objective of these exceptional measures is to help these workers to find and keep a new job.
in writing. - i am voting in favour because i believe that this support is important for portuguese workers. however, i think that the portuguese application was not very ambitious. it should be noted that the portuguese government requested from the egf an amount of eur 833 for each worker affected by redundancies in the textile sector, while spain requested for the same sector eur 3006 for each worker. it is also surprising that at a time of economic crisis and rising unemployment, when the effects of globalisation are being felt, the egf is little sought after and used by the member states. in fact, the egf has an amount of eur 500 million available for the current year for all member states, and only about eur 60 million has been used so far.
in writing. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work. although it is a small sum, it is only a palliative in view of the serious unemployment situation in portugal, particularly in these regions. as is known, portugal made its request to the european commission in january this year for this amount, and it concerns 1,588 redundancies recorded from february to november 2008 in 49 textile companies in the north and centre of the country. however, eur 3 306 750 was also approved to support the 1 720 workers made redundant by 30 textile companies in catalonia, spain. but what was really needed was a policy of support for production, particularly in the textile sector, to prevent further closures and redundancies.
in writing. − recognising the negative impact of globalisation on europe's industrial regions, the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) builds on the solidarity of the european project and brings it closer to the citizen by providing support to workers most affected by these changes. the northern and central regions of portugal, whose economy was based on traditional sectors such as textiles, have been modernizing since the early 1990s, trying to adapt to a rapid increase in competition. textiles absorb about 15% of the labour force in these areas of the country and almost 98% of unemployment in the sector nationally is concentrated in the two regions. the deterioration of the economic environment that has affected these regions, along with others - particularly the outermost regions such as madeira, where tourism is of great importance - has had worrying consequences for social cohesion, especially as a result of the increase in unemployment. i am therefore supporting the mobilisation of eur 832 800 from the egf following the dismissal of 1 588 textile workers in the northern and central regions of portugal, which should be used wisely to reconvert these workers and reintegrate them into the labour market in a rapid and sustainable way.

in writing. what is at stake in this election, too, is what the eu will be heading towards in the coming years. the current president of the commission, who is running for a new term, is a symbol of one of the possible courses: the eu represents the interests of the major economic groups. the eu is a result of bureaucratic and anti-democratic functioning; of political and ideological conservatism; of the accentuation and institutionalization of deep inequalities and of social, regional and national relations; of militarism and external interventionism; of the institutionalization of neoliberalism as a single acceptable economic system. but this is not, and never has been, the only possible course. the opposite of this is a social europe of the workers and the people. an eu that values democracy in its participatory aspect, not by reducing it to the formal representative sphere. an eu that respects the will of the people and the decisions they democratically express. it defends public services and workers' rights as essential instruments for social development and cohesion. an eu of free, sovereign and equal states, which defends and promotes the protection of nature, peace and cooperation between peoples.
the statement which mr barroso has just made as president-in-office of the commission is a reaffirmation of the fundamental pillars of the european union as we know it, of a capitalist, federalist and militarist european integration which the treaties of maastricht and nice enshrined and which the draft treaty of lisbon seeks to deepen. if there were any doubts, here are his statements on the importance of the draft treaty of lisbon, in continuing, moreover, the anti-democratic pressures of the european leaders, and which he has also accompanied, on the people of ireland, who are obliged to hold a new referendum on 2 october. even if it now tries to promise some amendments to the serious violations of social and labour rights which the european commission, of which it is still president, carried out in the previous mandate, it has never gone to the bottom of the issues or to the causes of the current crisis of capitalism that we are experiencing. in practice, he wants to continue the same policy which gives priority to free competition, militarism and the interests of economic and financial groups, particularly the most powerful countries, as was evident when he said that 'we are the champions of globalisation'.

in writing. - i welcome this vote which re-elects dr josé manuel durão barroso as president of the european commission. portugal is proud to have a portuguese, with recognized competence and quality, such as dr josé manuel durão barroso, at the head of the european commission. it is a matter of great pride to note the merit of his work during the first term, from 2004 to 2009. this merit was recognized by the european parliament in this vote. in fact, the majority achieved is very significant and exceeds the one required by the treaty of lisbon. over the past five years, dr josé manuel barroso has demonstrated strong global leadership. the energy-climate dossier, the services directive, the chemicals regulation are just a few examples of success and leadership. he was also a leader in finding solutions and putting forward concrete proposals to resolve the economic crisis which still affects us. the european parliament has just given a signal that europe is strong, that it has a strong leader and that it can therefore move forward with confidence and hope towards a more prosperous and more united europe.
in writing. - the political guidelines for the next commission term, now presented, are clear as to the intention of its president to continue the main lines of the guidelines developed in the mandate which is now ending. these are the guidelines at the root of the deep economic and social crisis we are experiencing and whose dramatic consequences - unemployment, inequality, poverty and exclusion - have fallen mercilessly on the workers and peoples of europe. in portugal, the consequences of implementing the policies made possible by these guidelines have been the destruction or outright weakening of essential productive sectors - agriculture, fisheries and industry; the attack on workers' rights, the devaluation of wages and the corresponding increase in unemployment and precarious work; inequality in the distribution of incomes, the divergence from the eu average is now evident; the failure of the policies of deregulation, liberalization and privatisation of key sectors, dismantling public services and commodifying essential aspects of our collective existence. to insist on these guidelines is to insist on the concentration of wealth, on divergence rather than convergence, is to anticipate new and heavy eruptions of a latent systemic crisis in the nearer than distant future.
in writing. - the work carried out by president barroso over the past five years has been marked by a great deal of pragmatism, seriousness and firmness in pursuing europe's highest interests. the global leadership in the fight against climate change, the sense of opportunity in bringing europeans closer to the oceans through the launch of the new european maritime policy and the ability to generate consensus around the financial perspectives in a europe of 27 are some of the examples that justify his continuity at the head of the european commission. economic growth, the focus on innovation and people's skills, and the fight against unemployment are key pillars for the continuation of the process of european integration, and they are also durão barroso's priorities for the future. at a time when the world economic and financial situation is not the best, europe needs a strong leader capable of giving life to the european project. i believe, for the above reasons and more, that mr barroso is the ideal president to lead the eu's destiny in the next term.
in writing. − a strong and ambitious european commission presidency is essential if europe is to regain its position as a world leader in seeking a balance between economic and political objectives and social and territorial cohesion. priorities must be set in a pragmatic way, without neglecting the values that are at the origin of the european project. we have a number of challenges ahead of us, such as the reform of the community budget, which europe must tackle by showing that it is capable of defining the global agenda on a number of issues such as the fight against climate change and the regulation of financial markets. at a time when unemployment is high in several member states, a sustainable way out of the current economic and social crisis must be sought. this objective must unite the member states on the basis of the principle of solidarity and the strengthening of the european single market. i believe that josé manuel durão barroso has the qualities that will enable him to continue to meet the expectations we have placed on him with success, and i consider it a matter of pride for portugal to hold this office, and i understand that it is an advantage for a small, isolated, remote and ultraperipheral region like madeira to have someone in the commission who knows its reality in depth, so i support his re-nomination as president of the european commission.
in writing. - unfortunately, extreme drought and forest fires have increased both in frequency and in extent in southern europe. the damage caused is enormous, both in terms of loss of human life (11 people died this summer alone), and in terms of reduced economic activity and environmental degradation, in particular by contributing to the acceleration of desertification, in which in the last decade some 400 000 hectares of european forest have been disappearing every year. climate change is contributing to the increase in natural disasters, but many cases remain unpredictable or have criminal origins. this is why scientific research needs to be developed in order to improve risk assessment mechanisms, prevention systems and the means of combating this phenomenon, and to provide the necessary financial resources. we need a european strategy for dealing with natural disasters and greater interoperability and coordination between the various community instruments. member states should strengthen cooperation and coordination in order to ensure solidarity and the availability of additional resources for rapid mobilisation in response to these disasters. i recommend that the presidency of the council urgently take a decision on the regulation for the new solidarity fund, in order to increase transparency and make it more flexible to mobilise it in an emergency.
in writing. − southern europe has been hit by catastrophes (drought and fires) that have affected human lives, local economies and ecosystems and have contributed to the progressive desertification of large areas that were once populated, worked and cared for. more than a problem of this or that country, this issue and its serious consequences have already assumed a transnational character and clearly deserve a european response. as i have said before, the european union will win if it remains united even in the face of adversity and if it is able to mobilise means such as the solidarity fund, systems and methods capable of stopping the causes and mitigating the consequences of these scourges and of giving them a flexible, prompt and appropriate response. in addition to the european commission - which should take a leading role in finding solutions and applying best practices - the entire forestry sector should be called upon to share knowledge, define solutions and identify ways to contribute to a diversification of forest-related activities. a portuguese children's song says that a tree is a friend. the european union must repay this friendship and thus defend the future of the rural world.
in writing. the reason for our vote in favour is the need for effective solidarity and the immediate mobilization of financial resources to deal with the dramatic social and environmental situation in the areas affected by the fires this summer and their populations. however, we believe that the resolution should place greater emphasis on the effects of some of the community policies, in particular the common agricultural policy, which promote the abandonment of land and production systems, and on activities which contribute to a preventive approach to the problem of fires. the consequences of the cap, particularly in countries such as portugal, are now among the underlying causes of the fires which, year after year, devastate vast areas of the territory. we also believe that the possible and desirable cooperation between member states in combating forest fires should not be used to open the way to the subtraction of important elements of the sovereignty of each state, in particular with regard to civil protection and measures of control and intervention in the territory.
in writing. - as a result of reforestation by the regional government, together with initiatives by nature protection associations, recent studies show that the madeiran forest has grown by almost 5 000 hectares in the last 36 years, a fact all the more remarkable because it is contrary to the national trend. this invaluable heritage must be protected and, if we are to be prepared to respond to fires, i think it is essential to have a balanced allocation of resources between means of fire prevention, detection and suppression. i voted in favour of this resolution because i believe that this policy can be promoted at european level through a strategy of solidarity which seeks to coordinate the response to fires and to prevent risky behaviour effectively. with a special focus on the outermost regions, this approach should involve both public authorities and forest land owners and focus on clearing forests, planting indigenous species and rural re-population. criminal behaviour should also be adequately punished at national level. the flexibility of procedures associated with the use of existing or new structural funds, such as the eu solidarity fund, will allow for a faster response to the population affected by the loss of agricultural land, housing and livestock following fires and other disasters.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the rights of children are protected. what does this mean? does that mean that if i have a cinema in vilnius and i want to put up a poster for the movie "brokeback mountain", i can't do that? does it mean that if i give a lecture on homosexuality in a public place, in a theater or in a university in lithuania, i can't do it? does this mean that i can, as has already been discussed in the lithuanian parliament, pay a fine of up to eur 1 500 or do one month of community service, in accordance with the amendments to the penal code which are currently being debated in lithuania? appoint a committee to see what is the disclosure or non-disclosure of homosexuality? where, in books, in theaters, in movies, in advertising? vilnius is one of the european capitals of culture this year, with great merit and certainly with great joy on the part of all europeans. but being european capital of culture also brings responsibilities, the responsibilities of bringing european culture for the best of reasons and not being seen this year for the worst of reasons. i therefore call for a vote in favour of our resolution and for the fundamental rights agency to give an opinion on this very serious matter. that is certainly the least that the members of this house can ask for.
on behalf of the s-d group commissioner, the commission and the council have been slow to react to the deep crisis affecting the sector. we all agree with that. the dramatic situation in which we find ourselves calls for swift and decisive action, or else we shall be witnessing the ruin of many thousands of farmers throughout europe. my group has made a great effort to reach the compromise contained in the resolution we are discussing here today. i hope that the proposals deserve your attention, commissioner, because only by acting simultaneously to reduce supply and stimulate demand is it possible to rebalance the market and bring prices to profitable levels for producers. i regret that the undertaking did not include the temporary suspension of the increase in quotas or the introduction of a temporary premium for the reduction in production. however, the measures we are proposing make a good contribution to overcoming the crisis more quickly. it is up to you, madam commissioner, to follow up on this.
i am sorry that the commissioner has not gone to the bottom of the matter, has not recognised the need to review the decisions taken on the abolition of milk quotas and, on the contrary, wants to continue deregulation of the sector against farmers. we therefore insist on the need to maintain the quota system, albeit with adjustments, adapted to the needs of each country, including the cancellation of the 1% annual increase until 2015. does he not think that, at a time of such a serious crisis in the dairy sector, more important than deregulation of the sector and the liberalisation of international trade is the defence of agriculture and the dairy sector in the member states, with a view to promoting the rural world and employment with rights? does he not think it essential to create at community level an extraordinary fund for the support of the dairy sector, with a focus on the producers and the countries most affected, and to define new aid for milk and meat production? i consider it necessary.
in writing. i come from an or, the azores, where milk production is the economic, social and environmental pillar. in the last few weeks we have been talking to the producers, their organisations and the processing industries. they all agree. it is crucial that the commission gives a more effective, global response, urgently, to deal with the current situation. the financial flows are stopping in the sector. it is of the utmost importance that the ors are given particular attention by the eu and included in its responses to mitigate the effects of the current crisis. it is the opinion of all that we must stop the increase in quotas. in an unbalanced market with surpluses, any country that increases its production harms all the others. they also believe that we should continue to maintain quotas as a mechanism for regulating supply and stabilising their activity. in the current context of full liberalisation, it is urgent to study its social, environmental and economic effects in these regions and to devise specific measures to prevent the collapse of this activity responsible for our beautiful landscapes, the quality of our environment and our rural environment and the progress and economic convergence we have achieved.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the proposal for a council regulation concerning the 2009 and 2010 intervention periods for butter and skimmed milk powder because i consider that these measures constitute the continuation of a positive measure, with immediate effect, in the regulation of the surplus supply affecting the milk and milk products market. the fall in prices on the world market for milk and milk products over the last 12 months was caused by the general increase in production and the fall in world demand due to the economic and financial crisis, which justifies the continuation of public intervention for butter and milk powder.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the european parliament resolution on the lithuanian law on the protection of minors from the harmful effects of public information, as i believe that the law adopted by the lithuanian parliament on 14 july 2009 prohibiting 'the direct dissemination to minors of public information promoting homosexual, bisexual or polygamous relationships' because it has 'a harmful effect on the development of minors' should be urgently reviewed. in accordance with the principles of the european union, all forms of discrimination and in particular discrimination on grounds of sexual orientation should be eliminated and the fundamental rights agency should therefore issue an opinion on the law in question and its amendments in the light of the eu treaties and community law.
in writing. i supported and voted in favour of amendment no 28 to this resolution which called on the commission and the member states to take immediate additional measures to intervene on the current level of community production by means of a temporary freeze on the quota increases decided upon during the last reforms of the common agricultural policy, because this was in the interests of all portuguese milk producers and, in particular, had the support of azorean milk producers. we therefore regret that this amendment did not have the support of the majority of members of the european parliament.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the joint resolution on the crisis in the milk sector, as i believe that urgent measures are needed to overcome the deep crisis in the sector, in particular by stimulating demand and bringing the market back into balance. it is regrettable, however, that the compromise reached in the european parliament does not include the temporary suspension of the increase in quotas or other measures to reduce production, which would be very important in supporting european farmers in coping with this crisis.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: stabilisation of the milk and milk products market will not be achieved by single measures, such as not increasing quotas, designed to mitigate the immediate and short-term effects of this crisis. on the contrary, medium- and long-term solutions must be considered and the causes of the poor functioning of this market and the best ways of maintaining production in a sustainable way, without neglecting the right of consumers to a fair price, must be assessed. i would point out in this connection the particularly fragile competitive situation of the outermost regions, such as the azores, which are heavily concentrated on dairy farming, and i regret the lack of sensitivity on the part of the european commission and, in the national case, the poor management of this process by the portuguese government. in times of crisis, the capacity of our governments to act and defend national interests is particularly relevant. unfortunately, that was not the case. i would like to highlight the success of the amendments which i have co-authored and which advocate increasing de minimis payments. it's not the ideal solution, but it's a useful contribution.
in writing. - i vote in favour of the resolution, although i consider it insufficient. at the moment, to stabilise prices, it is urgent to withdraw the 2% annual bonus above quota. it is also necessary to continue the milk quota system after 2015 or to replace it by another regulatory mechanism. it is imperative to adjust milk production to european domestic demand by ensuring a fair price for its production. the commission is proposing to set up a european observatory to help monitor and regulate the market from production to distribution.
in writing. the resolution adopted contains positive measures which are necessary in the current context of the deep crisis in the sector. that is why we voted in favour. however, these are only one-off measures which do not solve the underlying problems of the sector, in particular of small and medium-sized producers, for whom the situation is expected to worsen in the future, given the announced intention to abolish milk quotas. the fight that the dairy farmers are waging, beyond their immediate aims - to sell their produce at prices that will ensure their survival - also has a wider meaning and scope, which is linked to the kind of agriculture we want in the future. a neoliberal model of agriculture, which promotes the flooding of the market with products from countries with greater productive capacity, which promotes intensive production in some countries and agricultural abandonment and food dependence in others, must be opposed by a model based on the concept of food sovereignty and food security, on the right of each country to produce in a sustainable way. a model in which public production control instruments - quotas, adapted to the needs of each country - are essential.
in writing. - europe is highly dependent on energy imports. to reduce energy dependence, action must be taken on energy efficiency, diversification of energy sources by increasingly using renewable sources, and diversification of countries of origin and transit. the consolidation of the internal energy market at european level is also very important for increasing energy security. the existence of an electricity network and a gas pipeline network covering the whole of europe should be a priority. in this respect, two of the projects included in the economic recovery plan are of great importance for portugal: the electricity link between portugal and spain, which will contribute to the consolidation of the iberian electricity market; and the link between spain and france, which will prevent the iberian peninsula from becoming an energy island. the commission and the council are asked to pay particular attention to encouraging the development of renewable energy projects in the southern european countries with great renewable potential. much of the alentejo region has the highest solar potential in europe as has been demonstrated by maps of solar radiation distribution.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament resolution on the external aspects of energy security because i believe that the creation of a genuine common energy policy will be decisive in ensuring the security of energy supply in the european union. however, a properly functioning internal energy market and diversification of energy sources will also be of great importance in preventing future crises and disruptions in energy supply. in this sense, i think that increased investment in renewable energy and energy efficiency should be a central element of european policies.
in writing. − the european union's energy dependence, the strategic need to reduce it and the challenges to the union's stability posed by this fragility are well known and widely discussed. my country has a level of dependence that is well above the european average, which shows the absolute failure of successive governments in this area and raises particular fears of a possible crisis in the energy markets. given the difficulties in relations with some of the main suppliers and the limits in the diversity of energy sources and the existence of insufficient supply capacity, i consider it important for all member states that the european union can act as a bloc in defence of common interests and is able to impose them in a very demanding negotiating context. i also believe that states cannot escape their own responsibilities and should opt for a variety of 'energy pathways' in which all the main alternatives, including nuclear, should be studied, evaluated without prejudice and put into practice when they prove useful, giving priority to the most efficient, safe and clean forms of production.
