in writing. − the establishment of the special committee on the financial and economic crisis could prove to be fundamental in preparing the future of the eu. this can be achieved not only by discussing and presenting measures but also by identifying mechanisms that can be put in place to prevent a similar situation from occurring in the near future. it is therefore essential to learn the lessons of this crisis by analysing its causes and consequences. it is also relevant to correct the failures of the financial system that led to the current situation by adopting better legislation, duly justified. we cannot risk creating such a tight regulatory environment that could lead to a delay in recovery, or to the eu becoming an unattractive financial market in a highly competitive environment. the commission may continue to be in place beyond the 12 months provided for, in order to allow for the monitoring and assessment of the measures to be taken in the context of the current crisis.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i just wanted to express, not only on behalf of the epp, as many members have already done, but in particular on behalf of the portuguese epp members, our great satisfaction with the result of the referendum in ireland. we believe that this clearly strengthens the prospects for the development and evolution of the european union and creates the conditions for a new stage. we would naturally like to congratulate all those who contributed to the treaty of lisbon, in particular the commission and the commissioner present here and, of course, the portuguese presidency of the european union, which played a very important role here, and therefore we would also like to say that we are pleased to note that, despite all these events and while we are still awaiting the position of the czech republic, it will be with great joy that the portuguese will see the name of their capital linked to a decisive step in the evolution of the european union and i would like to say that we can also count on another flag in this perspective, which will also always be a flag of the portuguese meps of the epp, which is the flag of the national parliaments. we believe that the treaty of lisbon is a decisive step towards incorporating the presence of national parliaments into european democracy. and so we believe that this dimension, which is the most important dimension of democracy, which is the representative dimension and not, as we sometimes hear in this house, the referendum dimension, that what is fundamental and essential in democracy is representation and not direct voting, the lisbon treaty is also a great step towards increasing the participation of national representative democracies in the european process. we are therefore very pleased with this decision of the irish people.
the victory of the 'yes' in ireland is good news for europe and for me as a portuguese and a staunch europeanist. it represents the victory of common sense and rationality over demagogy and manipulation. the irish have made it clear that they have faith in the european project. the main obstacle to the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon has been overcome with the victory of the 'yes' vote. president klaus has no excuse and must respect democracy and the parliament which elected him and which has already approved the treaty of lisbon. the treaty of lisbon opened a new cycle. the european union will be more efficient in decision-making and more effective in external relations. more democratic and more transparent, closer to the citizens and less dependent on governments. the new treaty will make the european union better able to combat climate change and respond to the challenges of globalisation.
i am glad that the irish people voted yes in the referendum on the treaty of lisbon last sunday. it is hoped that the long ordeal of ratification of the treaty will finally come to an end. i am sure that the czech republic will also ratify the treaty. it would be incomprehensible, it would be unacceptable, if the future of europe were to be hampered, blocked, i would say, by a personal opinion and in total disregard, in total disagreement with the majority of its parliament. we all want the institutional impasse in which we find ourselves, which has lasted for almost 10 years, to finally come to an end. i therefore thank the irish people, because the will they have expressed gives us hope for a stronger, more prosperous, more united europe and at the same time adds the responsibility, the obligation of all european institutions to act in such a way as not to betray that same will. in fact, this is the wish of the vast majority of european citizens. i hope that we can all live up to the expectations of a europe that is increasingly leading, more cohesive and more dynamic in the world of democratic values, social and fundamental rights, economic growth and, at the same time, environmental protection.
mr president, madam minister, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, a brief word to welcome the historic and sweeping result of the referendum in ireland and to highlight the decisive role that fine gael and its leader and future prime minister of ireland, enda kenny, have played in taking the lead in this process. the need to bring the european union much closer to its citizens became clear. they are the main beneficiaries of all the union's action. it is essential that everyone is aware of the impact and benefits that europe has in our daily lives. europe is our living space. what is good for europe is good for each member state, is good for its citizens. this is certainly the case with the treaty of lisbon. this treaty makes it much clearer that one can be both a proud and committed national and a staunch supporter of the european project. and the european union, after too long a debate on its organisation and functioning, will be able to concentrate much more, and with new and much more effective instruments, on the real problems facing europeans: competitiveness, growth and employment. i would like to conclude, mr president, madam minister, with a proposal for future generations. given the lack of information on the values, competences, objectives and way of working of the european union, the european parliament should propose, at the end of compulsory schooling in each member state, a new compulsory european studies discipline. in an objective, real and concrete way, young europeans will know what our principles are, who we are, what we do and where we want to go. it is by learning from a young age that they will understand, throughout their lives, the potential and the enormous usefulness of the european identity of this unique project of voluntary sharing of sovereignty on a continental scale.
mr president, madam minister, madam commissioner, i hope that with the result of last friday's vote europe will be able to emerge from a crisis concerning the treaties and that it will thus be possible to have a stable institutional body appropriate to an organisation of 27 states, quite different from the one that was the basis of nice. the fact is, mr president, that a treaty is not a work of art, a legal work, which can be admired. it has to be useful, it has to have an effect. and that is why, as important as the debate on, for example, who will be president of the council is politically, it is more important that the ratification process be completed and that the treaty actually enter into force. the result in ireland makes it clear that europe is not built against the people. whether through representation or through referenda, this approximation is real. and, by the way, it is important to point out that, as democratic as it is, voting no is voting yes. and some seem to confuse that very reality. i hope that with the ratification of ireland, the czech republic and poland, a situation of de facto solidarity between the states can be created. and that we move towards a situation in which this great step is joined by the sequence of what is schuman's europe: a europe made of small steps, made of everyday things and, fundamentally, of the enlargement of european ideals.
the outcome of the referendum in ireland on the treaty of lisbon is a key step in the construction of europe and in strengthening europe's role. the lisbon treaty allows the eu to step up its role in energy, climate change, science and cooperation with developing countries. with the lisbon treaty, the fight against climate change becomes a specific objective of eu environmental policy, recognising that the eu has a leading role at international level in the fight against climate change. for the first time, a chapter on energy will be included, setting the eu's policy objectives of ensuring energy security, promoting energy efficiency and developing renewable energy. the lisbon treaty lays the foundations for the creation of the european research area, strengthening european action in an area that is key to economic growth and employment. the lisbon treaty introduces, for the first time, a specific legal basis for humanitarian aid and defines poverty reduction and eradication in developing countries as the main objective of eu development cooperation policy.
the outcome of the second referendum on the treaty of lisbon in ireland does not erase the anti-democratic nature of a process which, from the outset, has been disrespectful of the will of the people. we will not let the 'no' of the french and dutch peoples to the 'constitutional treaty' and the deception of translating it into the treaty of lisbon be forgotten. history will also record the disregard for the 'no' vote of the irish people in the first referendum and the unacceptable blackmail and interference that followed, culminating in a campaign of colossal proportions that included the shameless use of irish state and european union resources. neither the ratification process nor the content of this treaty legitimises the continuation of policies which are at the root of the serious economic and social crisis in the eu, particularly in portugal, and which this treaty will deepen. we will continue with unwavering determination to combat neoliberalism, federalism and militarism in the european union. we will continue with great confidence the struggle for another europe, for the workers and the peoples, and against the renewed threats to social and labour rights, democracy, sovereignty, peace and cooperation, with which we are now confronted.
mr president, there are statements of optimism about the beginning of the end of the crisis. unfortunately, these statements are daily contradicted by reality. the situation in the north of portugal is illustrative of this. in the district of braga alone, at least ten companies have closed in recent weeks, throwing more than three hundred workers into unemployment. many others are in danger of closing. the situation is particularly serious in the textile and clothing sector, one of the sectors in the european union most affected by the increasing liberalization of world trade. in the face of this dramatic situation, solutions other than the political guidelines which gave rise to it are urgently needed. it is urgent to apply safeguard measures, in particular in categories to be indicated by the member states, to enable employment and the activity of these enterprises to be maintained and promoted, and to make progress in the creation - a subject which has already been the subject of a resolution in this parliament - of a community programme to support the textile sector with adequate resources, especially for the most disadvantaged regions which are dependent on this sector.
in portugal, prisa, a spanish company which is a known supporter of the socialist party and a majority shareholder in a television channel, tvi, ordered the closure of the friday evening television news about three or four weeks ago. i would like to ask you, mr president, whether your group has the same intention of studying what is happening in other countries or whether it is just looking for political chicanery like the one we are witnessing this morning.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the böge report on the mobilisation of the solidarity fund for italy, as i believe that the european union must respond as quickly as possible to the request for assistance made by italy in order to mitigate the dramatic consequences of the earthquake that struck the abruzzi region of italy in april 2009, killing 300 people and causing considerable material damage.
in writing. - as i have said before, i believe that solidarity between the member states of the european union, and in particular european support for states affected by disasters, is a clear sign that the european union is no longer just a free trade area. by adopting special aid instruments such as the european union solidarity fund, the community has shown that it is able to stand united in the face of adversity, in situations of particular human and material need. i cannot but welcome this and support once again the mobilisation of this fund, this time to help the victims of the earthquake that struck the abruzzi region of italy in april 2009. i reiterate my wish that the solidarity fund should not be used too often - that would mean that europe would not suffer many serious emergencies - but i renew my wish that its structure and availability should be progressively improved and frequently assessed, so that it can meet any real needs in a rapid and non-bureaucratic way.
in writing. the text approves the mobilisation of the solidarity fund for italy following the earthquake in april last, which killed 300 people and caused considerable material damage. the total direct damage caused by the earthquake is estimated at more than eur 10 billion and has been considered, according to the fund's intervention criteria, as a 'major natural disaster' and as such falls within the main scope of the legal basis. the commission is therefore proposing to mobilise the european union solidarity fund to the tune of eur 493 771 159. we would point out that the proposal for a revision of the regulation establishing the fund, which was put forward by the commission and on which the ep voted with our critical vote in may 2006, is still pending in the council. we believe that it is important, among other things, to safeguard the continued eligibility of regional disasters and the possibility of recognizing the specific nature of mediterranean natural disasters and adapting the fund - in terms of deadlines and eligible actions - to the specific nature of natural disasters such as drought and fires.
in writing. - i consider it essential to increase the efficiency of criminal proceedings, while ensuring the proper administration of justice. this framework decision will contribute to the prevention and resolution of conflicts of jurisdiction, ensure that the court before which proceedings are brought is the most appropriate and bring greater transparency and objectivity to the choice of criminal jurisdiction in situations where the matter of fact falls within the jurisdiction of more than one member state. i hope that this will prevent parallel and unnecessary criminal proceedings, without however leading to an increase in red tape when it comes to cases where more appropriate options are readily available, such as more flexible instruments or agreements already made by the member states, which should prevail. the existence of situations where the same persons may be subject to parallel criminal proceedings, relating to the same facts and in different member states, may lead to violations of the ne bis in idem principle, which should be applied effectively throughout the european judicial area. i also support greater involvement of eurojust from the very beginning of the process.
in writing. − coordinated action by the member states of the european union in preventing and resolving conflicts of jurisdiction in criminal proceedings is essential for more effective justice in the conduct of such proceedings. thus, the concentration of a criminal case affecting several jurisdictions in a single member state is desirable, on the basis of objective criteria and the necessary transparency, not only to avoid wasting time and resources, but also because of the costs involved and the fact that greater coherence and efficiency in the conduct of the case are essential. direct contact between the competent national authorities, as soon as possible, is therefore essential to determine the competent court and the respective transmission of the case. in this context, it is important to recall the rights of the accused throughout the criminal proceedings, since one of the fundamental objectives of the european union is to offer its citizens an area of freedom, security and justice without internal borders.
in writing. - the reason for our vote against is the strengthening of the role of eurojust which this report seeks to enshrine. the council's proposal for a framework decision better safeguarded the fairness of the decisions taken from a legal point of view. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the principle of ne bis in idem is respected, namely that a person is not tried twice in the same criminal proceedings. the rapporteur acknowledged this, but the amendments reinforce the role of eurojust in matters which are the exclusive responsibility of the member states. by this means, and by its early intervention in the proceedings, eurojust is placed in a position of oversight over the member states' instances, and is deprived of the possibility of reaching an agreement as to who has jurisdiction over the proceedings. we cannot accept that all this should be done merely on the grounds of 'waste of time and waste of resources'. the transfer of powers from the states to the eu in the field of justice, as in other matters, impoverishes its sovereignty and has shown itself to be not in the interests of citizens in defending their rights, freedoms and guarantees. this is, in our view, another example of that.
in writing. − the establishment of the special committee on the financial and economic crisis could prove to be fundamental in preparing the future of the eu. the experience of its members is essential for the successful pursuit of the work and proposals to be presented by this special committee. i believe that the list of members includes members of this house of repute and with considerable experience in the various areas affected by the current crisis. they can thus contribute to the discussion and presentation of relevant measures to correct the failures of the financial system that led to the current situation, and will be of great value in preparing for the possible adoption of future and better duly justified legislation. i also believe that this commission may have to remain beyond the 12 months provided for, and its composition may be revised, so as to allow for monitoring and evaluation of the measures to be taken in the context of the current crisis.
in writing. - i welcome the agreements reached at the g20 summit in pittsburgh. i'm sure they're a step in the right direction. the immediate priorities should be to ensure sound and sustainable growth of the real economy, to ensure the proper functioning of the capital and credit markets, to support and promote employment and to protect people from the negative impact of the crisis, with particular attention to the poorest and most vulnerable. the rapid increases in public debt and budget deficits are worrying. the importance of establishing long-term sustainable public finances should be reinforced in order to avoid burdening future generations. however, i regret the lack of an assessment of the main regulatory and supervisory failures that caused the financial crisis. it is a priority to understand what has happened at this level, and thus to avoid repeating the mistakes of the past.
in writing. − the g20 committed to reaching an agreement in copenhagen at cop15 and it is essential that the eu continues to take a leading role in the negotiations in order to establish an ambitious and equitable pact. the copenhagen agreement can boost economic growth by promoting clean technologies and securing new sustainable jobs in both industrialized and developing countries. a consensus on financing and technical support for clean and renewable energy and energy efficiency in developing countries is essential to reaching a solid agreement in copenhagen. it is important to define a concrete model in order to maximise the chances of concluding the agreement in copenhagen. the international agreement should ensure collective reductions of greenhouse gas emissions in line with the recommendations of the 4th assessment report of the intergovernmental panel on climate change (25-40% by 2020, relative to 1990) and should establish a long-term reduction for the eu and other industrialised countries of at least 80% by 2050, relative to 1990.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the european parliament's resolution on the g20 summit in pittsburgh on 24 and 25 september 2009, as i believe that the current economic difficulties should be used as an opportunity to further the objectives of the lisbon strategy and to reiterate the commitment to combat unemployment and climate change, as well as to create a european strategy that will result in a sustainable long-term economic recovery. i regret, however, that the proposal for a tobin tax on financial transactions was not adopted, as it would limit excessive speculation and promote financial stability and long-term investment.
in writing. − in the context of a global economic crisis with serious social consequences, the decisions taken by the g20 are of great importance. it is through a coordinated effort among the g20 members that we can build a financial system that will contribute to more sustainable and competitive economic development in the future, so as to avoid crises like the one we are experiencing. we cannot, as the european union, act in isolation, or else we will adopt rules which are not competitive with other countries and which will put the european economy at a disadvantage in the globalised world in which we live.
in writing. - i welcome the agreements reached at the g20 summit in pittsburgh. as capital has become more global, action to combat and prevent new crises requires the utmost international cooperation. i welcome agreements aimed at economic growth, employment promotion and market regulation, and hope that these objectives will be achieved in a 'territorially extended' way. the use of the financial transaction tax, which helps to control excessive speculation and encourages long-term investment, makes sense only if it is applied on a global scale.
in writing. the resolution adopted today follows the line taken by several bodies of the european union, including the ep, in their attempt to conceal the real causes of the economic and social crisis and, by exploiting it, to accelerate and promote the continuation and deepening of the policies which have provoked it. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. what is needed, and what the resolution ignores, is the need to break with the neo-liberal policies of liberalization and privatization of services, of attack on workers' rights and of destruction of the productive apparatus, policies which are responsible for worsening living conditions, indebtedness, the increase in unemployment, precariousness and poverty. what is needed is the appreciation of work and workers, the defence of the productive sectors and public services, the effective subordination of economic power to political power, the fight against and punishment of corruption and economic crime, and the end of tax havens.
in writing. - first of all, i would like to highlight the fact that the g20 meeting addressed such important issues as sustainable economic growth, employment and the possible phenomena of climate change that could endanger the habitability of our planet. these are global, topical issues, unanimously considered as fundamental to the european growth process. in this respect, i welcome the decision to maintain the stimulus for economic recovery and the clear commitment to a strategy capable of making the assumptions of the lisbon agenda viable, in particular the overall interest in implementing the 'global jobs pact' on the ground. in this respect, the urgency for the creation of an international counter-cyclical jobs fund, ambitious fiscal incentive packages to support job creation and retention, and strong social policies to support the most vulnerable groups should be reinforced.
in writing. - i would like to highlight and welcome the agreements reached at the g20 summit, which were unanimously considered as a step in the right direction. having discussed such important issues as the regulation and supervision of financial markets and the sustainability of public finances, i would like to place particular emphasis on the issue of sustainable economic growth and employment. i welcome the fact that the priorities agreed at the g20 are based on the idea that sustainable growth in the real economy will be the dynamo that will ensure not only job creation but also serve as a guarantee of protection for people from the negative impact of the crisis, especially the poorest and most vulnerable. i also welcome the commitment of the g20 leaders to tackle the international employment crisis by making employment promotion the central element of recovery plans. i am only sorry that no agreement has been reached at the g20 on the global fight against climate change.
in writing. - first of all, i would like to highlight the fact that the g20 meeting addressed such important issues as sustainable economic growth, employment and the possible phenomena of climate change that could endanger the habitability of our planet. these are global, topical issues, unanimously considered as fundamental to the european growth process. in this respect, i welcome the decision to maintain the stimulus for economic recovery and the clear commitment to a strategy capable of making the assumptions of the lisbon agenda viable, in particular the overall interest in implementing the 'global jobs pact' on the ground. in this respect, the urgency for the creation of an international counter-cyclical jobs fund, ambitious fiscal incentive packages to support job creation and retention, and strong social policies to support the most vulnerable groups should be reinforced.
in writing. − developing countries are severely affected by successive crises such as food and fuel crises and climate change, and are suffering deeply from the consequences of the financial crisis and economic recession. it is essential that the eu and its member states assume their responsibility as international actors by fulfilling their commitments on official development assistance and continue to contribute to the achievement of the mdgs. the eu has advanced the allocation of €8.8 billion for development aid, budget support and agricultural financing and proposed €500 million to support social expenditure in developing countries through the flex facility for the benefit of acp countries. it is important that budget support focuses on health, decent work, education, social services and green growth. the commission is asked to find new sources of financing to safeguard the european development fund (edf), and it is important that the edf be integrated into the community budget. there is also a need for greater coherence between eu policies in the areas of international trade, budgetary, climate change and development. development aid should give priority to: (the minutes were approved)
in writing. − although the current economic and financial crisis originated in the us, its consequences have been felt on a global scale, affecting europe and, more intensely, developing countries, either through human costs that push millions of people into extreme poverty or through the weakening of their already weak economies. it is urgent that all actors in official development assistance, in particular the bretton woods institutions, respond swiftly and effectively to this situation. it is also essential that the european union and its member states assume their responsibilities as frontline actors in development aid by fulfilling their international commitments to these countries, by urgently increasing to achieve the millennium development goals and by increasing the volume of official development assistance insufficient to respond to the collateral damage of this crisis in the developing countries. it is also particularly urgent on the eve of the copenhagen conference, when the eu and other international actors will articulate their policies on international trade, climate change, humanitarian aid and development. for all these reasons i support this resolution.
in writing. − the developing countries have been hit hard by the current economic and financial crisis and there is a fear of a sharp stagnation, or even a serious setback, in their growth and progress rates. the eu and its member states, which are major donors, cannot fail to take this into account and consider increasing aid to these countries, lest some of them fall irretrievably into poverty, which could lead to or exacerbate social and political conflicts and contribute to the insufficiency of regions which are trying to pacify and develop at great cost. this increase, which must be guided by flexibility, imagination, solidarity and common sense, cannot be separated from strict donor control over the use of aid, an effective 'traceability' of the amounts made available to the beneficiary countries, from their transfer to their final use. civil society and its parliaments should not be left out of this transparency effort, and the eu should encourage national debates on the destination of the aid received. but in the face of economic decline, europe cannot and must not close in on itself and ignore the burning problems that surround it.
regional disparities remain a challenge in the context of an enlarged european union. it is therefore essential that cohesion policy supports the least developed regions and member states. it is in this sense that we view with the greatest concern the recent amendment to the general regulation on the erdf and the cohesion fund proposed by the portuguese government to the european commission. this amendment provides for exceptions to the general rule of territorial eligibility of expenditure on operations with a spillover effect and on technical assistance, allowing funds for the convergence regions of north-central portugal, the alentejo and the azores to be implemented in the lisbon region. this amendment may constitute a serious breach of the principle of economic and social cohesion, which is an essential pillar of the european project.
the rapporteur. - mr president, mr vice-president of the european commission, ladies and gentlemen, the commission has already taken a number of decisions on the basis of the proposals contained in the white paper on growth and employment. i am in favour of a schengen evaluation mechanism which improves the existing system and makes it more efficient, so as to ensure a transparent and coherent application of the schengen acquis. i am, however, disappointed by these proposals from the european commission. in substance, these proposals retain the rules for the first part of the mandate unchanged and, for the second part of the mandate, namely the assessment of how the schengen acquis is being applied by the member states already within schengen, they merely incorporate the recent improvements introduced in the existing assessment mechanism. the only innovation i welcome is the possibility of unannounced visits. these proposals transfer the overall role of the council in the evaluation mechanism to the commission, providing for very limited forms of cooperation with the member states and removing the european parliament from the whole process, without demonstrating the added value of these. i was also concerned that the evaluation mechanisms were being completely separated for each part of the mandate, which could jeopardise the efficiency and coherence of the system. different rules and assessment systems should not be imposed on those states which want to join schengen and those which have already done so. there are also problems in data protection. i will give you just three examples: firstly, the point on the security of consular premises is incomplete, because the premises of external companies, outsourcing, are not covered. secondly, the security requirements for the sis should also be introduced in the visa section. third, article 7 of the regulation should not only cover risk analysis but also audits and security inspection reports carried out by member states in order to comply with the rules introduced by the sis and vis legal instruments. in addition to the problems i have mentioned and the improvements that can be made, there is a fundamental problem, which is the role that has been reserved for the european parliament, which is an irrelevant role. the choice of legal basis made by the european commission is legitimate, according to our legal service. but it would also be possible to apply the codecision procedure to the proposal for a regulation. between the two possibilities there is only political will. the commission has already made a number of proposals, and i am sure that the commission will be able to make use of them. we must not forget that we are talking about the security of the area of freedom, security and justice, which must involve all the member states and all the european institutions. therefore, codecision should be the procedure chosen. the position of the european parliament should not be merely adjective, but should correspond to its weight in the adoption of basic legislative instruments. i would like to end by thanking the shadow rapporteurs for their support for this position of the european parliament and i would ask vice-president barrot, who has always shown respect for this parliament, to re-submit these proposals by introducing not only substantive improvements but also, as regards procedure, by giving the european parliament an appropriate role.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, the schengen area is approaching its 20th year or is about 20 years old and the evaluation, or standing committee and then evaluation group, is 10 years old. it is therefore time to improve the evaluation procedures and to address the concerns about the schengen area. it is a pity that the commission, as this milestone approaches, has not done enough work and has not made enough progress in creating a more effective and comprehensive evaluation mechanism that could respond not only to the initial concerns of the first years of the schengen area: effectiveness and cohesion between member states and equality to a certain equivalence of procedures, but also to concerns on the one hand, of transparency, on the other hand, of control by citizens (democratic control) and, finally, concerns, which are very dear to this house, of respect for human rights. there are well-founded concerns that efficiency has been benefited at the expense of citizens' rights and it is time to address this gap. i also wanted to talk a little bit about codecision. at the moment, it is a test for the european commission and all those who have defended the treaty of lisbon, propagandizing its democratic virtues, to then live up to their words and lend more parliamentary and more democratic control to the schengen evaluation processes. i can only support the conclusions of the rapporteur, our colleague mr carlos coelho. i think it does a good service to european democracy in demanding that the commission get back to work on its proposals and give us something that is simpler, more effective, more transparent, more respectful of human rights and that gives more parliamentary and democratic control.
the rapporteur. - three final words. - what are they? first of all, i would like to thank the members who supported my report and the personal references they made to me during this debate and to vice-president barrot for his statement to us that he encourages the involvement of the european parliament as a co-legislator by making the most of the possibilities that come with the lisbon treaty. i know this doesn't come as a surprise to me. i know that this is the position that commissioner barrot has held for a long time, but it is good that he, as vice-president of the commission, has made this formal statement to us. secondly, to record something that commissioner barrot said when he said that there were difficult negotiations with the council. we also have this information and we understood that in this matter it would be difficult to do otherwise. we also expected the commission to look at parliament as a partner, a partner with a codecision role, because, as far as the european approach is concerned, both the commission and parliament are certainly identified with the idea that it cannot continue to be merely intergovernmental. and thirdly, to underline two things that i think will remain from this debate. the first to say there can be no break in coherence. there can be no two systems of assessment, there must be only one, both for new and existing ones. and the principle of mutual trust cannot be weakened. it has to be all-inclusive. there must be involvement of the member states in the evaluation process and there must be involvement of the european institutions. the european institutions are not just the commission, not just the council, but also this parliament, and that is why we are calling for codecision.
i would like to support the report by mr busuttil, who approves the european commission's initiative, and he says three simple things. firstly, by this decision, which makes it easier to move around, we are denying the idea that there is a strong europe. secondly, to say that we didn't open the doors in any way. we opened by following the rules, as vice president barrot recalled. the countries have come close to the rules and are in a position to get off the negative list, as he said. i think it is important that we have a european approach and that we cannot pick and choose countries, that is, accept some and not accept others. either you accept the european space or you do not. and finally, as simon busuttil and vice-president barrot have stressed, the guarantees of reciprocity in these agreements are essential. we cannot ask europe to open its doors to others if others do not open their doors to europe, and this is very well guaranteed in these agreements.
ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking vice-president barrot for his statement, although i am in an unfair position, because it would be good to hear the council's position as well. i note that it is easier to exercise parliamentary scrutiny over the commission than over the council. in december 2001, the european commission was given a mandate for the second generation of sis, which is expected to become operational in march 2007. the problems and delays were many. the new system isn't working yet. some predict that it will not be before 2012, others question the feasibility of the project. the delays are now not only in sis but also in vis, both projects developed by the same company. i must say i have confidence in vice president barrot. he is a man who has always shown in his political life his competence, seriousness and european vocation. we know that sis is a project that did not start, that you inherited when you accepted a change of portfolio at the request of president barroso. but there are clear responsibilities here for the services of the european commission and the company contracted to develop the central system. parliament has budgetary authority and we have the right and the duty to ask the european commission for explanations. why do we need sis ii? we need better controls at the external borders, we need more security, we need biometric data and we need the interlinking of alerts. two global technical tests, the so-called milestone tests, are planned, the first on 22 december and the second in the summer of 2010. the purpose of this first test is to verify the stable, reliable and effective operation of sis ii under operational conditions for a period of 72 hours and to verify that the vital core functionalities and data coherence function without any problems and interruptions. there are questions to ask. firstly, is there any risk that this test could be held in december this year? secondly, is it true that in order to reduce the risks, the possibility of reducing the level of requirements or even reducing the number of states that should participate is being considered? thirdly, can the incorporation of these new tests be considered as part of the annual contract with the company or will they be considered as additional requirements entailing extra costs? fourthly, did the detection of technical problems and errors lead to the hiring of additional services? what money was paid with these funds? fifthly, the delays and technical errors which led to the failure of the previous tests resulted in penalties being imposed on the contracting company. - has mr barrot already said what those penalties amounted to? sixthly, does the commission consider that if there is a switch-off to the implementation of an alternative solution, does this entail the termination of the contract with steria? if so, what are the implications for vis? and finally, madam president, the last question: is it true that bulgaria and romania have given up waiting for sis ii and are already planning to integrate it into sis 1?
mr president, the approach to the problem of climate change has been truncated in essential respects and, above all, skewed by so-called 'market solutions'. fossil fuels now provide nearly 85 percent of the world's energy needs. a consistent approach to climate change must focus on reducing this dependence. on the contrary, the main instrument proposed by the european union to curb climate change, the carbon market, should not only not help to alleviate this dependence, but even constitute an obstacle to the necessary change in the energy paradigm. experience shows that trading in emission allowances has not led to a reduction in greenhouse gas emissions, quite the contrary. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. the environmental problems facing mankind today are many and varied, so serious that they threaten the very existence of life on earth as we know it, but they will hardly find a solution within the framework of the irrational system that has generated it.
madam president, considering an action plan to support developing countries in the field of climate change is a matter of basic justice and equality: it is the countries most affected and those who have contributed least to the situation we are in. that is why we must go beyond a declaration of intent. to go beyond the declaration of intent is to be certain and serious about fundamental issues such as financing. as ambitious as the strategy is, if it does not have the funding issues defined, we will not have a concrete policy at all. the council is talking about 100 million euros by 2020. there are various estimates, there are also estimates that speak of the need for 120 billion euros a year to support developing countries, for every year that passes. and so we need our own resources and not just palliatives. it is therefore urgent to define how the financing will be carried out, who will finance it? it takes courage to take on this, to involve all the parties, but also to include the private sector and industry. or do we expect them to volunteer to contribute to this intervention as well?
madam president, mr minister, mr commissioner, the european union should continue to take a leading role in the international negotiations towards an ambitious agreement in copenhagen. the agreement should be based on the principle of common but differentiated responsibility. this principle should also apply to developing countries which, because of their different stages and circumstances, require differentiated measures adapted to their individual situations. all developing countries, except the least developed countries, should adopt national low-carbon development strategies. developing countries will face a cost of around €100 billion to mitigate their emissions and adapt to the impacts of climate change. part of the funding will come from the private sector, but international public funding will be needed in addition to official development assistance. it is urgent to define the architecture of this financing system, the source and the amounts of the financing, in order to secure an agreement in copenhagen. the kyoto protocol's clean development mechanism should also be reformed so that only projects that allow for additional reductions are eligible for funding. the economically more advanced developing countries and the economically more competitive sectors should be gradually excluded from this mechanism, and it should focus mainly on the poorest countries, in particular the african countries.
copenhagen is the great opportunity for a global agreement in good time. for the first time, there is an administration on the united states side that is committed to building solutions, and there are positive signs from other countries, for example, japan. but we need a new approach to climate change that takes into account its consequences on security, economic recovery, immigration and even the fight against terrorism. and let us not forget that climate change requires the contribution of science, technology and economics. the biggest obstacle to an agreement in copenhagen is the question of funding. there will be no agreement without financing for adaptation in developing countries. and it must be strengthened, commissioner, because the funding currently available is not enough. developed countries must lead by example, be ambitious in reducing their emissions by at least 30%, and help developing countries with funding and technology.
i am grateful for the clarifications provided by the european commission, but i would like to recall, as was clear during the debate, that the legal service of the european parliament, while recognising the legitimacy of the legal basis of the commission's initiative, also said that it was possible, on the basis of the treaty in force, that the european commission had taken the same initiative on the basis of a legal formula allowing co-decision for the european parliament. as this has not been the case, i propose that the initiative be returned to the committee on civil liberties so that the european commission can reconsider its initiative and, within the time limit set out in rule 56 of our rules of procedure, we can expect to receive in this parliament an initiative from the commission which respects codecision and which gives this european parliament the intervention it deserves in advocating greater security in the schengen area.
in writing. - i welcome the fact that the european community will be represented in the international renewable energy agency (irena). the international renewable energy agency aims to promote the uptake and sustainable use of all forms of renewable energy, taking into account their contribution to environmental conservation, climate protection, economic growth and social cohesion (including poverty reduction and sustainable development), access to and security of energy supply, and regional development and responsibility across generations. it is also part of the agency's objectives to provide technical, financial and policy advice to governments of developing countries, thus contributing to their transition to a low-carbon society. the use of renewable energy is precisely one of the objectives of the eu's climate and energy package. the agency will contribute to the achievement of the objectives of this package, in particular the objective of increasing the share of renewable energy in energy consumption to 20% by 2020.
in writing. - portugal is the sixth most energy dependent country in the eu-27, which is why investing in 'clean tech' is essential. i am advocating a national renewable energy project, with particular attention to wind energy, wave energy (taking into account the exceptional conditions of the portuguese coast), solar thermal and photovoltaic energy and microgeneration. i also support research and development of methods, technologies and strategies to enable the storage of surplus renewable energy. i am in favour of an energy policy which is in tune with economic challenges and social needs, and which at the same time promotes sustainable development, without being an environmental cost to be borne by future generations. it is precisely because i am constantly concerned about limiting our energy dependence and i am sure that the way forward is towards supporting and developing renewable energy that i welcome the fact that portugal is a founding member of the international renewable energy agency (irena). that is also why i support the european community's accession to the statute of the international renewable energy agency (irena).
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the proposal for a council regulation repealing certain obsolete council acts in the field of the common agricultural policy, as it is necessary to remove from the community acquis acts which have lost their relevance in order to improve the transparency and legal certainty of community legislation, with a view to the interinstitutional agreement on better law-making of the european parliament, the council and the commission. this was recently reaffirmed in the commission communication 'a simplified cap for europe - a success for all', for which i am responsible for the report to the european parliament on behalf of the group of the party of european socialists and democrats. in the context of this strategy, it is therefore appropriate to delete from existing legislation acts which have ceased to produce real effects.
- whereas it is essential that all those involved in the common agricultural policy (cap) are aware of the legal framework in force and the rules applicable at any given time; recognising that legal certainty requires that obsolete acts should not remain in force indefinitely in the community legal order; believing that, given the fundamental importance of the cap for the governments and citizens of the union, it should be as simple and clear as possible as regards its application and the rules in force; i support the commission's proposal to repeal obsolete acts in the field of the common agricultural policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report authorizing portugal to reduce the excise duty rates on liqueurs, spirits and rum produced and consumed in the azores and madeira, because i consider this measure important in supporting the subsistence of small industries in the sector which are engaged in their production and which are in a very unfavourable competitive situation in the face of the liberalization of markets and the increasing marketing of spirit drinks in these regions. the reduction of this tax also contributes to a better economic and social balance in these regions, thus ensuring the maintenance and even the creation of jobs, which are crucial for the protection of local economies.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the hübner report on a proposal for a council decision authorising portugal to reduce the rates of excise duty applied to rum and liqueurs produced and consumed in the autonomous region of madeira and to liqueurs and spirits produced and consumed in the autonomous region of the azores. in view of the specific features of the outermost regions, i consider this extension to be essential for the survival of the local industry of these products and for the protection of employment in the sector.
in writing. - in view of the importance for agricultural activity and, consequently, for the economy and employment in the portuguese autonomous regions of madeira and the azores of the production of rum and spirits respectively, and of liqueurs in both regions, i consider it essential that the reduced rate of excise duty on the consumption of these goods should continue to apply, as this will not lead to a situation of unfair competition within the internal market. the increase in the retail price caused by the elimination of the application of excise duty rates, which would make these products even more anti-competitive compared to similar products imported from the rest of the eu, thus undermining the survival of traditional products, would be dramatic for the local industry and the regional economy at a socio-economic level due to the impact on existing family farms in those regions.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the existing derogation granted in 2002 to allow portugal to reduce the excise duty on rum and liqueurs produced and consumed in madeira and on liqueurs and spirits produced and consumed in the azores. since the beginning of this process, i have made every effort to ensure that this measure, which expired at the end of 2008, is resumed with the utmost urgency. after winning unanimous support in the committee on regional development, this result is confirmed by today's vote, which maintains the tax reduction with effect from january 2009 until 2013. madeiran rum and spirits producers face permanent obstacles related to their ultra-peripheral geographical situation, insularity, difficult terrain and climate and very small farms. if they were to lose this exemption, they would have to increase their selling prices, which would affect their activity and the employment generated by it, with serious consequences for the region.
in writing. - i support this agreement negotiated between the ec and the republic of mauritius establishing a visa-free entry and short-stay visa regime to facilitate the movement of their citizens. citizens of the union and nationals of mauritius travelling to the territory of the other contracting party for a maximum period of 3 months in any 6-month period shall be exempt from the visa requirement. with the exception of the united kingdom and ireland, which are not bound by this agreement, and a territorial limitation in respect of france and the netherlands, where this agreement only concerns their european territory. i would remind you that any person travelling for the purpose of pursuing a paid activity during a short stay should not benefit from this agreement and should remain subject to the relevant community and member state rules on visa requirements or exemptions and on access to employment. this agreement may be suspended or terminated, but such a decision may only be taken in respect of all member states. i also support the provisional application of the agreement pending its entry into force.
