in writing. - as i said earlier on the report by reimer böge (a7-0021/2009) on the earthquake in abruzzo, i believe that solidarity between eu member states and european support for states affected by disasters is a clear sign that the european union, by adopting special aid instruments such as the european union solidarity fund, is showing that it is able to stand united in the face of adversity, and i welcome this. therefore, and considering the possibility of amending budgets being presented by the commission 'in the event of unavoidable, exceptional or unforeseen circumstances', including the earthquake in italy, i voted in favour of adopting this report on amending the budget of the european union so that the residents of the affected region can see more quickly a repair of the damage caused by the earthquake and a normalisation of living conditions through the mobilisation of eur 493.78 million from the eu solidarity fund.
in writing. - the mobilisation of the european union solidarity fund for italy has been approved for the amount of eur 493 771 159, and since this fund has no budget of its own, it is necessary to make an adjustment to the community budget to ensure that the agreed amount is made available. while agreeing with the need to make funds available as quickly as possible, we regret that the proposal put forward by the european commission implies, among other budget lines, a reduction in funding for important community programmes under the previous and current multiannual financial framework. this is the case, for example, with the reductions planned for the european agricultural guidance and guarantee fund, the financial instrument for fisheries guidance - the community programmes of the previous community framework 2000-2006 - or the financial instrument for the environment life+. in our view, apart from the necessary adjustment of the solidarity fund to provide it with a budget line with its own resources, its financing should not be at the expense of the community programmes referred to above, while at the same time insisting on an increase in military and propaganda expenditure, which could have been used to raise the funds now allocated to the solidarity fund.
in writing. - unemployment is one of the main problems affecting the european economic area. even before the outbreak of the current financial crisis, which has intensified and aggravated some of the symptoms previously felt, the serious impact that globalisation and the resulting business relocation have had on the lives of many populations was already evident. if we add to this the present distrust of the markets and the retreat in investment, it becomes clear the particular difficulty of the times in which we live. in this respect, and despite my preference for the regulation of markets, i believe that the exceptional nature of the crisis justifies exceptional measures to combat it. the european globalisation adjustment fund is one of the mechanisms available to the european union to assist workers made unemployed by the current economic situation. i believe that the case of the workers of nokia gmbh in the german region of bochum justifies european aid, as was done in the past for portugal. the european union cannot but work together to promote a more robust and creative european market, which generates investment and jobs. only then can this problem be tackled effectively, seriously and sustainably.
in writing. − the case concerned a request for assistance from germany for redundancies in the telecommunications sector, in particular in respect of nokia gmbh employees, which met the eligibility criteria laid down in the european globalisation adjustment fund regulation. but the fact is that the mobilisation of this fund only mitigates some of the serious consequences of the economic and financial crisis, and therefore a break with the neoliberal policies that are causing a real economic and social disaster in many countries of the european union, particularly in portugal, is necessary. so, although we voted in favour of this report, we cannot fail to point out both the inadequacy of the measures, which are merely palliative, and the injustice of the regulation itself, which benefits more countries with better incomes, in particular with higher wages and unemployment benefits. we therefore insist on changing policies and on the need for a real plan to support production and the creation of jobs with rights.
in writing. − the creation, with the schengen agreement, of a european area without border controls was an important step in building an open internal market with free movement of people and goods. however, in order for this area to retain its potential, which i recognize, it needs an effective mechanism for assessing its implementation by the various member states. i therefore support the strengthening of schengen monitoring powers, in particular through assessments (questionnaires and on-the-spot visits, announced and unannounced) and ex post evaluation mechanisms, in order to ensure adequate monitoring of the way the various member states act, cooperate and control their external borders within the framework of the schengen agreement. i know that any weakness or malfunctioning of the system poses serious risks to the internal security of the union and calls into question the schengen area itself, as an area of freedom but also of security. i do not agree, however, that the community character of this evaluation should be strengthened by strengthening the powers of the european commission to the detriment of the intergovernmental character which has hitherto prevailed in the schengen evaluation group. i therefore vote against the commission's proposal.
vice-chairman of the ppe group. mr president, mr president of the commission, i would like to begin by congratulating you on this first debate on what this new instrument of political control means for the advancement and development of parliamentary democracy in europe. it is the european citizens, through their representatives, who stand to gain. in view of the latest developments, the irish referendum, the ratification by poland and the latest statements by the president of the czech republic, what is your assessment of the process of entry into force of the treaty of lisbon? has the president of the commission taken any action? and what is your forecast for the possible date of entry into force of the treaty and, on the other hand, do you know whether at this moment and in view of this forecast the commission has already made some efforts to transition from the treaties of the treaty of nice to the treaty of lisbon or whether it is still in a phase of expectation, so to speak, a phase of waiting to see?
vice-chairman of the ppe group. mr president of the commission, i would like to know the following: at this stage when everyone is waiting for the commission to be set up, what is the president's overall perspective? the question is whether we will now have a functioning commission by the time the czech republic has finally ratified it, or whether we will make progress in the appointment of new commissioners.
mr president, to reduce poverty and unemployment, we must create jobs with rights, we must support production, we must improve universal and free public services, that is, we must invest in other policies, we must do the opposite of what has been done. for example, it is with concern that we have just learned of a study by the directorate-general for employment and eurofund which presents two scenarios for the textile sector, predicting a loss of 20 to 25% of community employment by 2020 and, in a third scenario, even pointing to the loss of 50% of the employment currently existing in the european union. how can this be combined with the new proposals being made for the liberalization of international trade without taking into account the consequences in portugal and other countries of the european union, with very sensitive sectors such as the textile sector, agriculture and a universe of micro and small enterprises which are being suffocated by these policies.
i would like to start with the positive aspect: on thursday we will vote on a further eur 3 billion in payment commitments for the structural funds and for social programmes. it is true that the council opposes this policy, it thinks that we should not ask the states for more money. forgive me, swedish minister, but that is really the view of the grocery bills. we are living in exceptional times and i think that the commission, the council and parliament should have had the courage to face the problem of financing this budget head on and make it an exceptional budget. even with the expenditure we will be approving on thursday, this budget is still a budget that is passing by the crisis. it's a continuity budget. we don't need drops of water in seas of milk. we need a different policy for the rural world. we need not only structural funds for our territories, but a european social policy which complements the policy of each of our states. what we really need is courage! we have as many poor people today - 79 million - as we did at the turn of the century. we have enormous uncertainty about the values needed to combat climate change. the key issue we have to discuss is the financing of the european budget and the next financial perspective. that is the point, and don't tell us that there is no money because there is, we can get it from tax evasion, we can get it from taxation of capital gains made in financial markets and tax havens. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is precisely because this is the problem with this budget that it was so important for us as members of the european parliament to set an example of restraint. i made some proposals about travel expenses. in today's world, a member of the european parliament earns a day's travel by salary, by means of a travel allowance, by distance travelled and by time lost. this is not serious and it's not understandable. i hope that on thursday we will at least have the manhood to review this.
i am convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that the european union's budget for 2010 will help us to overcome the economic crisis we are facing. one of the results of this crisis is unemployment. i would therefore like to stress and point out the importance of the amendment we have made to strengthen the erasmus programme in order to promote and facilitate the creation of the first job for young people. i am glad that our main proposals have been accepted both in the budget and in the motion for a resolution. our aim is legislative excellence. we agree with the importance of multilingualism, of course, but we understand that our great aim is legislative excellence, and to that end we must have the means for members to achieve it. we have proposed for greater transparency and rigor a zero-based budget, which should be implemented at the beginning of each legislative cycle. we have also proposed reservations for this objective, the objective of transparency and the objective of rigor, and therefore reservations in the case of, for example, communication policy, where we want to have a cost-benefit analysis. we also reserve the right to try to reduce the bureaucracy that exists, for example, in the hiring of assistants, and we want to 'oblige' the administration to help reduce that bureaucracy. we also agree and advocate that real estate policy should be a long-term policy. in conclusion, i would like to congratulate the rapporteurs, mr surján and mr maňka. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mr lászló surján, on not giving in to the temptation of demagoguery, for example in the case of the milk fund, and on doing the best he could and not trying to sell abroad.
member of the commission. − madam president, i will be very brief. first of all i would like to thank all the members of the parliament for their proposals regarding the budget for 2010, and hope that in the further budgetary procedure we will find proper solutions in order to establish a good budget for 2010. i just wanted to respond concretely to this very important issue of milk funds and say that what mrs fischer boel stated yesterday was the commitment by the commission to come up with a proposal on certain amounts of money, namely eur 280 million. the commission will discuss this issue next week and we will come up with a concrete proposal on this issue according to the rules of procedure. these are my brief remarks regarding the discussion.
in writing. in the midst of the economic and social crisis, with dramatic effects on employment and people's living conditions, the proposed community budget for 2010 falls far short of what is needed. it is unacceptably even short of the agreed amount in the 2010 multiannual financial framework of some eur 6 billion. in view of the serious social situation in the various member states, and particularly in the so-called 'cohesion countries' such as portugal, we consider it urgent to review and increase the community budget and have put forward proposals to this end, such as: - financial reinforcement of structural and cohesion policy; - re-evaluation of the n+2 and n+3 rules, which have so far resulted in a loss of some eur 106 million for portugal under the previous financial framework; - increase in the rate of community co-financing in the structural and cohesion funds. it is regrettable that other proposals with important social and environmental implications were not taken up during the budgetary debate, such as:- a programme for the development of portuguese industry;- a programme to support the textile and clothing sector;- a programme to support small-scale coastal and artisanal fishing;- the increase in the life+ programme.
mr president, commissioner, we are all unfortunately aware of the serious situation in which the dairy sector finds itself in europe. my group has been calling for several months for urgent measures to try to save thousands of farmers who are threatened with ruin. that is why we have taken initiatives and supported initiatives by other political groups to achieve this objective. we cannot therefore fail to welcome the commission's initiatives, however insufficient they may seem to us. that is also why the socialist group voted this morning for the urgency procedure which was requested of us, but we will nevertheless propose and demand that the european parliament be kept informed of the measures which, under the powers which we are now giving to the commission, will be taken in the coming days. we will, however, continue to do everything possible to restore market balances so that they can guarantee adequate income to producers, as reaffirmed in the lisbon treaty.
mr president, commissioner, the proposal put forward by the commission acknowledges the failure of its policy for the dairy sector, but it leaves intact the disastrous underlying political orientations for the sector. the millions mentioned are far short of the needs of compensation for the price falls which producers have been suffering. contrary to what is advocated, we must reverse the course of a cap whose successive reforms have dismantled the instruments regulating the market, quotas and production rights, leading to the gradual abandonment of the activity by many thousands of producers. many thousands of jobs are at stake, whole families who depend on the agricultural activity of the milk sector in particular. this is a matter of concern for vast areas whose rural neglect will be accentuated with significant social and environmental consequences. all this calls for immediate action to establish fair prices for producers, which requires more resources than are currently available, but above all, and i repeat, it calls for measures of wider scope, not just palliative measures, such as the cancellation of the annual increase in milk quotas, their re-establishment at the levels prior to the decision to increase them and the repeal of the decision to end the quota system in 2015.
mr president, commissioner, i too welcome the adoption of the urgency procedure in this house, considering that the inclusion of milk in article 186 is important and urgent. i have argued that a serious situation such as that in the milk sector requires urgent measures to defend the sector, for both economic and social reasons. i come from a country, portugal, and a region, the azores, where the dairy sector is very important and is located in socially very fragile regions where, incidentally, it would be vital to maintain the quota system beyond 2015. these measures now proposed are few in number, but they are indeed necessary and urgent. i am also concerned about the exercise of the new powers to be made available to the commission, especially because it has been quite insensitive to this crisis, moving forward only with weak and late measures and under heavy pressure. in this context, i would like the commission to present an annex to article 186 which gives examples of measures which can be used for the purpose of providing support for the milk sector in order to make it sustainable.
in writing. commissioner, after having fought so hard for the commission to come forward with measures, we are not in a position to reject any contribution that comes to us at this very difficult time for milk producers and their families. unfortunately, the commission took a long time to act. it did so under a lot of pressure from milk producers, the european parliament and 21 member states. the measures which it presented here included the following: 1 the fund of eur 280 million is insufficient in relation to the eur 600 million unanimously considered necessary by the committee on agriculture, which is the committee which is both technically and politically specialised in this area. this political demonstration was approved by the european parliament itself. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the inclusion of milk in article 186 introduces an operational instrument for rapid use by the commission, which should not, however, deprive parliament of its monitoring. we still need market regulatory instruments. we must maintain the quota system and introduce transparency in the milk marketing chain, without which it will not be possible to restore and guarantee stability in europe's fields.
two brief notes: the first on the treaty of lisbon, after the 'yes' vote in ireland and the signature by the president of poland, it is expected that the constitutional court of the czech republic will rule and that president klaus will do what he must do: sign the treaty. the council cannot give in to blackmail by the president of the czech republic. the lisbon treaty is indispensable to the better functioning of the european institutions and has other advantages, including the strengthening of the european parliament's powers and the rights of citizens. hence the urgency of the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. the council and the commission must give president klaus a deadline to behave as the president of a democratic country and a member of the european union. it is not acceptable even to think that the will of any ruler can override the will of the majority. the second note is on the copenhagen conference: ladies and gentlemen, the world needs a global agreement to combat climate change, the world needs to stop global warming, developed countries and developing countries need to join forces and make an ambitious decision. copenhagen is the great opportunity to avoid catastrophe, that's what scientists say. invoking the financial crisis to delay or reduce ambition in copenhagen is not an argument, it is not serious. ambition is what it takes to save the planet.
mr president, this next council should give absolute priority to changing the liberal policies which have been at the root of the serious economic and social crisis. it should be time to face up to the poverty of some 80 million citizens in the european union, including more than 30 million workers whose wages are so low that they cannot get out of the poverty in which they and their families live. it should be the time to make a decisive contribution to the response to the serious unemployment which is continuing to increase and which could reach 30 million next year if appropriate measures are not taken immediately. the great challenge for this council is therefore to break with the neoliberal policies of the lisbon strategy and the stability pact. it is the replacement of the old system by a genuine programme of social progress and development, which promotes quality public services, supports production and micro, small and medium-sized enterprises, and values those who work, creating more jobs with rights, including for women and young people.
the next european council has as one of its main objectives to ensure the success of the un climate change conference in copenhagen in december. the council conclusions on the eu's position at the copenhagen conference are essential for the eu to speak with one voice. it's important to maintain an ambitious negotiating position. the eu must show unity and leadership, in particular through aid to developing countries, which by 2020 will face costs of €100 billion a year to adapt to the impacts of climate change and mitigate their emissions. it is essential to define the architecture of a financing system for developing countries, especially the least developed, and to define the source and amounts of such financing in order to secure an agreement in copenhagen. this agreement will allow for the entry into force of a protocol to replace the kyoto protocol as from 1 january 2013 and will ensure an effective reduction in greenhouse gas emissions while maintaining the competitiveness of european industry in the global world.
mr president, i would like to ask for the floor under rule 157 (1) of the rules of procedure to speak on the motion for a resolution tabled by the ppe group on the joint motion for a resolution on freedom of information in italy and other member states. this amendment is an embarrassment to the european parliament and, in the name of truth, should not be discussed, let alone voted on. their assumptions are false and are based on a fallacy created by the parties in portugal during the election campaign. they have had the proper response from the portuguese electorate. on the other hand, what is an unquestionable fact (and i have the proof here): the mep who proposed this amendment yesterday, on his website, showed his intolerance and inquisitorial attitude by asking the writer josé saramago, nobel prize winner for literature, to renounce his portuguese nationality. it's clear who's attacking freedom of speech.
mr president, my oral amendment is as follows: it deplores and regrets the influence exercised by the portuguese socialist government which led to the decision to close down the national journal of the portuguese television channel tvi and notes that this decision is now the subject of an investigation by the portuguese regulatory authority.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the s&d group resolution on freedom of information because i consider that freedom of the press is one of the pillars of the free and democratic society that i defend, and whenever it is at risk, as it is now in italy, we must be ready to protect it. in italy there is a clear and serious risk to freedom of expression and the press. the fact that the prime minister controls, directly or indirectly, a vast empire of publishers, daily and weekly newspapers and 3 television channels is truly incompatible with the political functions of mr berlusconi and with the requirements of a democratic state governed by the rule of law, a member of the eu. in addition, attempts to pressure and manipulate the public television station have been made. we must not forget that the economic crisis we are living through has further weakened the media, making them more sensitive to pressures from advertising companies or the public authorities. it is indeed urgent that the european commission finally publish a proposal for a directive on media pluralism and concentration, which will clarify the rules to be followed in a matter of such democratically vital importance in all the member states.
in writing. - the relationship between political power and journalism is complex and delicate, but it is clear to me that no one should have the right to impose 'his' truth by censoring ideas, persecuting journalists or limiting freedom of expression and information. freedom of information and expression is one of the basic principles on which the european union is founded and is essential in any democracy. that is why this debate should not be trivialised or used for political ends. when the european socialists table a resolution on freedom of information in italy, they confuse the european parliament with the italian parliament. they show double standards when they refuse to accept the proposal to include other national cases such as the german, hungarian or portuguese cases, which referred to the unjustified suppression of tvi's national newspaper. they don't care about freedom of information in europe, but only in italy... interestingly, on the same day that it is known that portugal drops 14 points in the ranking of press freedom produced by "reporters without borders".
in writing. the portuguese socialist delegation to the european parliament denounces and condemns the initiative of some portuguese meps from the ppe who have tried to tarnish the image of portugal and the portuguese prime minister with the unfounded accusation of interference in the media. this action was taken as part of a whitewashing operation against the italian government of silvio berlusconi's attacks on freedom of expression and the media. the socialist delegation cannot but deplore the fact that some portuguese meps are dragging a debate that some parties opened in the recent election campaign and to which portuguese voters have responded without hesitation overseas. unlike the promoters of this initiative, the socialists do not ask any portuguese citizen to renounce his nationality, but we denounce those who, in defence of any compromises with silvio berlusconi, attack the good name of portugal.
in writing. - commissioner viviane reding appealed to the house during the debate on this issue: i ask you not to use the european institutions to solve problems which, in the light of our treaties, should be solved at national level. i cannot agree more and i cannot but regret the use of such expedients by the european left, particularly by the socialists. i would remind you that in the previous legislature the spanish socialists tried to manipulate the european parliament by obtaining support from outside - which was scarce within the country itself - for a disastrous anti-terrorist policy. this unfortunate pattern of behavior persists. as the president of the ppe group has had occasion to point out, it is clear that there is a lack of faith in these attitudes, which do nothing to increase the people's confidence in the european institutions. i wonder whether the socialist group would be so ready to promote a similar debate on portugal and the repeated interference of the government in the media, which led, in the space of a few months, to the replacement of the director of a leading newspaper (público) and the abrupt closure of a television newspaper (tvi), which has already been expressly condemned by the regulatory authority for the media.
in writing. - we voted in favour of the resolution in defence of freedom of information because we are in favour of freedom of expression and information, pluralism in the media and against their concentration. however, we disagree with some aspects of this resolution which amount to interference in the democratic life of each country, and we have the greatest doubts about a possible directive in this area, particularly in view of the current composition of the european parliament. the fight we are waging for freedom of information and expression, for the defence of the rights of journalists and other media professionals, for the defence of access to information for all and for the guarantee of its pluralism, cannot be used to provide cover for partisan manoeuvres aimed above all at the european parliament's interference in the internal life of countries, using two different weights and measures, as the case may be, in the interests of one or another political force. we therefore abstained on the ppe's proposals on the tvi issues in portugal, despite the fact that the criticism of the pcp on this situation in portugal is well known.
mr president, it is essential that the primary objective of the creation of the european external action service should be the concrete expression of the efforts of the european institutions: to ensure the creation of an effective service capable of articulating, formulating and implementing a european response to the international challenges of the present. it must be more than the sum of its parts, it must be an added value to the present efforts of the member states and the european union. in this sense, it seems to me essential that what makes the union specific should be at the heart of this new reality, and this specificity is the community method. that is why i give my full support to the brok report, which advocates and safeguards close links between the commission and the future service. i would like to stress two points in this connection: the importance of close coordination between the president of the commission and the high representative, who is also vice-president of the commission, in order to ensure its effectiveness and smooth operation. finally, the need for an alliance between parliament and the commission to combat the intergovernmental drift that is looming and which could jeopardize the effective functioning of the service. parliament must therefore be vigilant and ensure that the european external action service is a centre of excellence, representing the best foreign policy expertise.
this is another of the regrettable documents of the european parliament, which seeks to influence the whole functioning of the european union in the worst possible way, using the legal personality which the treaty of lisbon confers on it, but which has not yet entered into force because of the lack of ratification by the czech republic. the report is symptomatic of the militaristic nature of the european union. the aim here is to ensure that foreign policy serves the interests of military expansion of the great powers of the european union, taking advantage of what they have written in the treaty of lisbon to strengthen their decision-making power, even if there are member states with different views. an example of this is the statement in this report: 'military and civilian crisis management units shall be placed under the authority of the high representative, while the command and organisational structure of military personnel may differ from that of civilian personnel; the sharing of security intelligence analysis among the participants in the eeas is of vital importance in supporting the high representative in fulfilling his mandate to conduct a coherent, robust and effective union foreign policy.';
madam president, in this debate i would like to begin by stressing the importance of relations between the united states and the european union, especially at a time of global economic crisis. we need more and more common action on the crisis, the energy market and the fight against terrorism, but we also need more and more focused action, not wasted on more taxation or attacks which are now absurd in relation to a financial system which is necessary for the market. focusing on the financial issue, i would like to highlight the efforts being made, both in the united states and in the european union, towards a policy of better regulation, with attention to the involvement of stakeholders in the debate on the report. coordinated action between the united states and the european union is essential to achieve a more mature state of economic relations leading to a de facto transatlantic market perhaps by 2015. we must also defend atlanticism here. it is also essential to reduce administrative barriers between the united states and the european union in order to create a competitive environment and a more attractive market for all, citizens and businesses. i believe that the transatlantic market can be built on a stable trading basis, which will grow economies and put an end to the danger of new economic and social crises such as the one we are experiencing. in conclusion, madam president, i would like to make it very clear that the conditions are unique and that with more atlanticism a better situation is possible.
i would like to stress the importance of this question raised by mr goerens, a question of the most elementary justice. we all know perfectly well that in a situation of influenza a pandemic, it will be the countries of the south that will suffer most from this problem and the consequences of the spread of the virus, and not the countries of the north. we are watching with total passivity every year the deaths of millions of citizens as a result of pandemics that have either been eradicated or are being treated in the northern countries, a huge list: malaria, dengue, yellow fever, tuberculosis. we have no limits to this list and we are passively watching this problem. we have indeed used the countries of the south as free suppliers of samples of the new strains of the virus so that we can improve treatment in the countries of the north, but we have not yet had an effective response to these problems and therefore to the questions that have been raised i would like to add just a few more. do we really want to treat this problem as a public health problem or as a business? because if it's a public health problem, we have to ensure equal access to treatment in all regions of the world, and to do that, we have to be more effective. on the other hand, i would like to ask, and i hope that is not the case, whether we are waiting for the vaccine stocks in europe to become so overstocked or for the side effects to prove so negative in this plan by then, yes, to have an act of generosity and suddenly start sending the necessary vaccines to the countries of the south. finally, i would just like to ask whether the european union, whether we europeans, whether the commission is comfortable with a system or a model where the northern countries treat pandemics through a prevention model and the southern countries treat them as palliatives or just leftovers.
mr president, pursuant to rule 151 of the rules of procedure, which concerns matters of a personal nature, i would like to state that i consider it improper to use my name in the budget amendments on forced abortion tabled by mr deva and mr szymański. the amendment to which they refer, which i tabled in an opinion on the report 'a special place for children in the european union's external action', read as follows, and i quote: calls on the eu to firmly pursue the elimination of all forms of discrimination against girls (since conception) and commit adequate resources to overcome the subsequent asymmetries. my text seeks to protect girls from discrimination before birth, in particular against sex-selective abortions, but it does not seek to prevent any and all activities linked to termination of pregnancy. my text differs in language and purpose from that of mr deva and mr szymański in the amendments to this budget and it is therefore not right to associate them, let alone to invoke my name in this sense, which i consider to be a reprehensible attempt at political manipulation. i declare that i shall vote against amendments nos 727, 732 and 734 tabled by mr deva and mr szymański.
mr president, i rise to speak under rule 173 of our rules of procedure, which deals with the non-observance of the rules of procedure. i am asking for a change in the order of voting, because i think that the order of voting in the list violates rule 161 of our rules of procedure. i therefore ask that amendment no 70 which proposes an allocation of eur 600 million to support the milk sector, which is in a very serious situation, as we all know, be voted on immediately before amendment no 812, which proposes an allocation of eur 300 million. i would remind you that this allocation of eur 600 million was unanimously approved by the committee on agriculture and deserved the support of a large majority in this house at our last meeting on 17 september, when we voted a resolution on support for the milk sector and the crisis it is going through.
in writing. − the contribution of the european budget for 2010 to stimulating economic growth, competitiveness, cohesion and job protection is key to responding to the recent economic crisis. as a particular stimulus to the european economy, i would like to highlight the european economic recovery plan, which encourages, among others, projects in the energy sector (electricity grids, natural gas grids and carbon capture and storage projects), finances broadband internet measures, bringing the so-called information highways to rural communities, creates a dairy fund as a new challenge for the common agricultural policy, and other community aid programmes, such as the distribution of fruit and milk in schools. i would like to highlight the amendment tabled by the ppe to the draft general budget for 2010, which introduces the promotion of young people's first jobs as an additional objective of the erasmus programme through joint training with companies, traineeships and entrepreneurship courses.
in writing. - i consider the measures announced by commissioner fischer boel following the meeting of the council of agriculture ministers in luxembourg and the agriculture committee in strasbourg (19 october) to be insufficient. the package, which is still to be approved by the ecofin council on 19 november, has a budget of €280 million and will be delivered in the form of a financial envelope to the member states, based on production and annual quotas. according to the calculations, portugal would receive a financial envelope of eur 6 to 7 million to combat the fall in producer prices, which is more than 50% compared to 2007-2008 prices. i think that eur 0.003 per litre of milk produced in portugal (calculations presented by the producers) is too little for a problem which has been dragging on for several months now, especially if the minister for agriculture uses this money for early reforms, as he has already announced. this amount of eur 280 million is an important signal from the european commission, but it is insufficient in relation to what producers really need to get out of the crisis.
in writing. the budgetary procedure is one of the most important community procedures in which parliament, the commission and the council share the decision-making power. today, as the first reading is completed, parliament reaffirms its role as budgetary authority by having succeeded in increasing both the appropriations for commitments and for payments in relation to the council's proposal, although still below what we would like. the epp sees the crucial issues it has been advocating for the relaunch of european economies in the context of the current crisis confirmed, through a focus on cohesion policy that boosts growth and competitiveness, especially of smes, the main engines of job creation. as a psd mep, i have reason to be particularly pleased that a large majority has approved an amendment by our delegation which aims to create an erasmus for the first employment programme, one of the proposals we put on the table in the last european elections. aid to milk producers and the guarantee of greater security for citizens, also with regard to energy supplies, have also been safeguarded. this is not the end of the process, as the three institutions will have to reach a common position which will be voted on at second reading in december.
in writing. − in the current context of economic, financial and social crisis, it was essential that the 2010 eu budget paid particular attention to this situation and that it became an effective instrument to overcome the crisis. i therefore voted in favour of the draft eu budget for 2010, considering that it responds globally to these needs. the european parliament is determined to do everything possible to ensure adequate funding for all activities and policies that encourage growth and job creation and provide solutions for european citizens, including greater energy security, increased support for research and innovation, particularly in the field of clean technologies, the promotion of small and medium-sized enterprises and the enhancement of lifelong learning. in this context, as proposed by the psd meps, i would like to recall the importance of the creation of an erasmus-employment programme, aimed at young people looking for their first job, to help achieve these objectives. finally, i must stress that i disagree with the council's further cuts in budget lines to support the lisbon strategy, as this is contrary to what should be done to encourage growth and economic recovery.
in writing. - i support the surjan report as the possible compromise on the budget of the european union. i regret that the council has further reduced the commission's preliminary draft budget, which in my view was already below what was needed. we cannot have europe with a miserable and manifestly insufficient budget. i particularly disagree with the reductions in the budget lines to support the lisbon strategy. the contrast between the public declarations of priority for combating the economic crisis and the commitment to 'competitiveness for growth and employment' and the appropriations provided for in this draft budget could not be greater. i welcome the increase in appropriations for the area of freedom, security and justice and stress that the construction of a people's europe also requires the proper implementation of these budget lines.
in writing. - i consider the increases proposed by parliament for the different headings and the amount of eur 1.5 billion to finance the european economic recovery plan relevant for the eu to recover from the economic crisis we are in, as well as for strengthening the role of the european union in the world. as i said earlier, i consider it particularly important to allocate the necessary funds so that smes, one of the main victims of the crisis, can benefit from support to enable them to survive this crisis. the increase for the competitiveness and innovation framework programme will allow the promotion of entrepreneurship and innovation, which are essential for the eu's assertion in the global market and for socioeconomic development in the internal market. however, i regret that only eur 300 million has been allocated for the creation of a milk fund. the serious crisis currently affecting this sector would, in my view, justify a larger allocation of funds, initially eur 600 million, to help producers overcome the difficulties they are currently facing. i therefore consider that eur 300 million is insufficient and i hope that the allocation of this amount can still be substantially revised in the light of the needs of the parties affected by this decision.
in writing. - the proposals to increase funding for energy security, research and development, support for smes and lifelong learning; d) the creation of a milk fund of eur 300 million, which is insufficient but is the maximum amount possible (i consider it essential that there be a regulatory mechanism and that milk quotas be maintained); e) the financing of broadband for the rural world from the sea available under heading 2; f) the amendment proposal we have put forward to strengthen and amend the ermus programme so that it is also the first instrument to create employment opportunities for young people. i hope that the budget with payment appropriations of around eur 127 billion will be used in full, as there is a delay in implementing the funds for a budget year.
in writing. - in the midst of the economic and social crisis, with dramatic effects on employment and people's living conditions, the proposal for a community budget for 2010, which is now under discussion, falls far short of what is needed and once again demonstrates the true meaning of 'european solidarity'. instead of responding to the social crisis, a significant part of the budget is for the strengthening of military expenditure and for the support of economic and financial groups, in line with the reinforcement of the militarist and neoliberal orientations of the european union. the european parliament's proposal, although an increase on the council and commission's budget proposal, still falls short of the agreed amount in the 2007-13 multiannual financial framework for 2010 by some €6 billion, with the final amount - to be decided in december - still unknown. we are, however, pleased that our proposal to create a new budget line for textile and footwear activities has been adopted, with a view to establishing a community programme for the sector. a proposal is being put forward to deal with the crisis in the sector, which is being caused by the exponential increase in imports from third countries, particularly in the regions most dependent on the sector.
in writing. - i would like to begin by regretting that the council has further reduced the commission's preliminary draft budget and that, despite the assumption that the priority of the 2010 budget is the citizens and the fight against the economic crisis, there is no adequate funding for heading 1a - competitiveness for growth and jobs. the budget cuts made by the council are taking away funds from the implementation of the lisbon strategy, which is contrary to the fight against the current economic crisis. i would, however, like to congratulate the fact that the amendment by the psd meps, which was an electoral commitment to the portuguese electorate, has been adopted with regard to the creation of the erasmus 1st employment concept as a way of promoting employment for young people and helping to combat the economic crisis. finally, i would disagree with the reductions introduced by the council in heading 1b - cohesion for growth and employment at a time when the structural and cohesion funds are important to stimulate growth and economic recovery, but also because a large number of important policies aimed at combating climate change and supporting growth and employment are financed by this subheading.
in writing. - in this debate on the general budget of the european union, i am showing my willingness to have the particular conditions of the crisis we are currently experiencing taken into account when we discuss the allocation of funds to the different sectors of the european economy. i would like to draw attention to the urgency of setting up a fund for the milk sector, given the difficult circumstances in which producers find themselves, and i hope that an effective measure to support this sector of activity will be adopted. the allocation of the necessary funds to enable smes, one of the main victims of the crisis, to benefit from support enabling them to survive this crisis, is essential. the increase for the competitiveness and innovation framework programme will allow the promotion of entrepreneurship and innovation which are essential for the eu's assertion in the global market and for socioeconomic development in the internal market.
in writing. - i welcome the adoption of the zero-based budget policy at the beginning of each parliamentary term, which i presented on behalf of the ppe. parliament's budget will thus reflect real needs and increase transparency, budgetary rigor and efficiency. i am also in favour of the separation of fixed and variable costs, the latter being justified by a cost-benefit analysis. this cost-benefit analysis in areas such as communication policy is important for better results and resource management. i would point out that this draft budget does not include the requirements resulting from the possible entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, which will probably lead to the drawing up of an amending budget. i would stress that legislative excellence must be parliament's top priority and that the necessary resources should be allocated to this end. i also think it is crucial to adopt a long-term building policy, which should incorporate the costs of maintaining buildings.
in writing. - i welcome the fact that the overall level of parliament's budget falls short of the self-imposed limit of 20% of expenditure under heading 5 (operating expenditure) of the multiannual financial framework. in a year of crisis, it is important for parliament to give an image of rigour and restraint. the adopted budget does not include any adjustments which might be necessary if the treaty of lisbon enters into force, particularly in the legislative field. in this sense, there may have to be an amending budget if the lisbon treaty enters into force. it is important to note that the legislative role is the main priority of the parliament (for the epp), and that the resources necessary to ensure that it does a job of excellence should be allocated to it. as regards information policy, i welcome the agreement on the funding of political parties at european level and political foundations at european level, which should help to strengthen communication with citizens and their participation in eu political life - calls for further discussion on long-term budgetary principles in this area. this report deserves my vote in favour.
in writing. - i would like to congratulate the fact that the overall level of parliament's budget falls short of the self-imposed limit of 20% of expenditure under heading 5 (operating expenditure) of the multiannual financial framework. it is important to note, however, that with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, and the consequent increase in the responsibilities of the european parliament, an amending budget will have to be drawn up and the 20% ceiling on operating expenditure will hardly be maintained. a legislative role of excellence should be the first priority of the european parliament and the necessary conditions should be created for legislative excellence to be a reality. i would also like to highlight the work of the epp shadow rapporteur (mep josé manuel fernandes) who has made a decisive contribution to making this report a document of the utmost importance to the european parliament. i would like to highlight the proposal, now approved, to implement a zero-based budget policy at the beginning of each parliamentary term, which will allow parliament's budget to reflect only real costs and will increase transparency, budgetary rigor and efficiency.
in writing. − since 1998, the european parliament has consistently rejected all initiatives put to it when consulted on detailed matters concerning europol, as long as europol remains in the intergovernmental sphere, without being subject to democratic and judicial control. with the adoption of the decision making europol a union agency, financed from the community budget, and the reinforced role of parliament in monitoring, it is no longer necessary to continue this line of action. i therefore voted in favour of this initiative by the czech republic, which is intended to deal with an administrative issue, namely the updating of the basic salaries and allowances of europol staff in order to adapt them to the rising cost of living in the netherlands. i would point out, however, that it is for the council to take any decision on the adjustment of the salaries of europol officials by unanimity.
in writing. - i voted in favour of including milk in article 186 in order to allow a rapid response to crisis situations by the commission, as this introduces a tool whose use is important. however, the use of this instrument cannot take parliament away from this process, especially at a time when co-decision is approaching, and it is therefore essential that the commission should monitor these interventions. as regards the voluntary change in the supplementary levy system, commonly known as 'fines', to obtain internal means of financing for the restructuring of the milk sector, i think it is a measure that creates national responses, although in my view it would be more appropriate to adopt european responses to a crisis which is general in europe. in conclusion, i think that after having fought so hard for the commission to come forward with measures over the last few months, we are not in a position to reject any contribution at this very difficult time for european milk producers and their families.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the proposal for a regulation on the single market because, in view of the serious crisis in the milk sector in europe, urgent measures are needed to restore market balances and guarantee adequate incomes for farmers, as stated in the objectives of the cap reaffirmed in the treaty of lisbon.
in writing. - these proposals are an acknowledgement of the failure of the commission's policy for the dairy sector. there are several reasons why we are voting against: 1) the amount proposed by the commission for the stabilisation of the milk market is clearly insufficient, being made available only in 2010, and therefore not allowing for a situation of manifest urgency, as would be required, especially in the case of small and medium-sized producers; the inclusion of milk and milk products in article 186 of this proposal gives the commission powers conferred on it by parliament and the council, without the commission specifying the measures to be taken. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the introduction of a new system of quotas for the production of beef and veal. (4) the funds approved are mainly intended for restructuring the sector, which for the commission means the abandonment of thousands of producers, with all the social and environmental consequences that this entails; the proposal will accentuate the existing imbalances in the distribution of funds between producers and between countries, which will mean an aggravation of the difficulties for many producers.
in writing. - reports were voted at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg on three specific measures (eur 280 million for direct aid to producers, temporary rule for calculating quota purchases for redemption and inclusion of the milk com in article 186 (emergency measures)). i believe that these are important measures, but that they are specific and insufficient for a problem which has already acquired very serious structural dimensions: the sector needs useful and effective management mechanisms to be able to intervene in the market when necessary, in stark contrast to the line advocated by the european commission, which advocates a liberal and deregulatory line. the crisis in the milk sector has shown that the common organisation of the market in milk still needs instruments, in particular the quota mechanism, to combat market imbalances. the package, which is still to be approved by the ecofin council on 19 november, has a budget of 280 million and will be delivered, in the form of a financial envelope, to the member states on the basis of production and annual quotas. according to the calculations, portugal would receive a financial envelope of 6 to 7 million euros to combat the fall in production prices, which is more than 50 percent compared to the 2007-2008 prices...
