in writing. the vote on this resolution must not fail to raise two points about its aims and content. first, instead of playing the role of champion of democracy, which it is not, by making 'practical recommendations' to other countries, the european union should first ask itself about the state of democracy in the eu itself. the ratification process of the lisbon treaty is a recent example, among others, of how the so-called 'construction of europe' is moving against the democratic participation of the european peoples, against their interests, desires and aspirations, by putting pressure on them, blackmailing them and disrespecting their decisions about the direction they want for their collective future. secondly, in the field of external relations, instead of promoting the relaunch of the arms race and the militarization of international relations, the eu countries should make a contribution to democracy by promoting a genuine policy of peace and international cooperation, based on respect for the sovereignty of each country and the principle of non-interference, as well as respect for international law and the united nations charter. unfortunately, we are far from that. the eu, with its increasingly marked democratic deficit, cannot set an example, let alone help to 'consolidate' democracy in the world, which is why we are voting against.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the brok report on the institutional aspects of the creation of a european external action service (eeas), which will be extremely important for the union's external relations to become more coherent and effective. it is necessary to ensure that the establishment of the eeas, as a result of the innovations introduced by the treaty of lisbon, preserves and develops the community model in external relations and ensures the interinstitutional balance of the union.
in writing. − the creation of the european external action service is the natural corollary of a process of increasing external acquisition of competences by the european union. it can become an important instrument for the eu's external coordination and assertion if it can resist the pressure of the directories and base its action on complementarity rather than competition with the diplomatic representations of the member states. i regret that it was not possible to obtain support in the committee on foreign affairs for an amendment i had tabled calling for the european languages of universal communication/world languages to be taken into account when defining the future internal linguistic regime of the eeas. the eeas should therefore use as its preferred working instruments the languages best suited to establishing these directly. it is therefore incomprehensible that this parliament, in dealing with this issue, should choose to ignore the external dimension of multilingualism and the strategic character of these languages, which is recognised not only by itself but also by the european commission.
in writing. - eu-us relations are without doubt the strategic partnership of greatest importance to the eu. we have a shared responsibility to promote common values such as respect for human rights, democracy, stability and peace, but also to find the best responses to the various global dangers and challenges, such as the economic and financial crisis, climate change, nuclear proliferation, the fight against international crime and terrorism, poverty eradication, among others. as regards cooperation in the fields of justice, police, internal and external security, i would point out that in order to achieve success in future negotiations it is essential that security measures do not undermine civil liberties and fundamental rights, without forgetting the need to respect privacy and data protection. i would like to reiterate that transfers of european personal data to third countries should only take place if two fundamental principles are taken into account: the principle of necessity and the principle of proportionality. full compliance with european and national data protection legislation and appropriate procedural safeguards should also be ensured.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution on the forthcoming eu-us summit and the transatlantic economic council (tec) meeting, which stresses the need for closer transatlantic relations in a context where major international challenges require a coordinated global response. i stress the role of the tec in promoting and ensuring a coordinated response to the global financial crisis and the importance of transatlantic relations in reaching an international agreement at cop 15 in copenhagen, including an appropriate financial package to help developing countries combat climate change, both in mitigation measures and adaptation measures.
in writing. − due to the strategic importance of the us for the eu and vice versa, a transatlantic partnership should be strengthened at the next summit in order to strengthen the capacity for action to deal with the challenges we currently face: from the current severe economic and social crisis, to issues such as disarmament, the fight against terrorism, climate change, respect for human rights, etc. coordinated action between the us and the eu is also key to achieving a more mature state of economic and trade relations between the two, leading to the creation of a de facto transatlantic market by 2015, with reduced bureaucracy and, consequently, a more stable and attractive framework for companies in both markets, and providing them with lower costs of doing business. moreover, if equivalent measures, particularly in the context of financial regulation, are not taken by the us, the eu could find itself in a competitive disadvantage, which would be extremely harmful for the european economy. i therefore reiterate the need to defend the eu's interests through a coherent and coordinated policy between the eu and the us in the various areas of common interest.
in writing. the main objective of the new eu-guinea conakry partnership agreement is to strengthen cooperation between the european community and the republic of guinea in order to foster the establishment of a partnership framework for the development of a sustainable fisheries policy and responsible exploitation of fishery resources in the guinean fishing zone, in the interests of both parties. the financial contribution to the protocol is fixed at eur 450 000 per year for fishing opportunities for highly migratory species. the whole of this amount will be used to establish a national fisheries policy based on responsible fishing and the sustainable exploitation of fishery resources in guinean waters. the above is in line with the purpose of the cfp in the area of fisheries agreements with third countries, and therefore the proof of this document deserves my vote in favour.
the european union has a duty to help the people of iran, and to help the people of iran. the majority of shiites live side by side with sunnis, zoroastrians, christians, jews, and baha'is. the persian majority shares the country with a number of ethnic minorities that make up almost half the population: azeris, arabs, kurds, baloch and others. cities are teeming with a middle class and a modern youth, ready to live in 21st century iran. all this turmoil and complexity scares the regime that preferred a simple iran: simple in religious fanaticism, in the isolation of the country and simple in a public opinion intimidated by violent repression. this resolution describes the systematic violation of human rights by iranians in their country, including the frequent application of the death penalty, including against children, the stoning of men and women, the gross limitations on freedom of expression and the persecution of religious and ethnic minorities. the european parliament is sending two different messages with this resolution. the first is for the people of iran: europe sees in the iranians, and especially in their youth, the hope of a future in which their country will embrace democracy and freedom and take on a leading role in the region, as it deserves. and the second one for the iranian regime: we tell him that iran will never realize its undisputed potential as long as violence and obscurantism are the main features of a political regime that invokes the values of justice and peace in vain and that continues to brutally oppress its people.
the european parliament has already expressed its concern about the situation in iran, and we are therefore calling on the iranian authorities to take all necessary measures to ensure that the elections are held in a democratic manner. these millions of iranians, inside and outside the country, expect something from us, and therefore the starting point cannot be other than that of solidarity and that of collaboration in helping these millions of iranians who are fighting for democracy and human rights and who do so, it is good to remember, taking much greater risks than those that diplomacy usually fears. so the starting point, i said, cannot be another. it is true that western policy has often been a simplistic and ignorant policy with regard to iran. it is true that too often europe has been a follower of policies that have proved wrong about iran. it is true that we have often been unwilling to accept that iran should be accorded the respect which it certainly craves as a major regional power. as an exiled iranian artist said after the protests, the west did not want the islamic republic and now we don't even have a republic. however, none of this can excuse a regime which has been a liberticidal regime, an oppressive regime and now a regime which is based on an increasingly unsound foundation of fraudulent elections and oppression of its people. the iranian people expect solidarity and support from the european parliament, and this is what we are trying to do with this text.
in writing. i welcome the strong will expressed by eu heads of state and government at the european council last october to continue to take the lead in the fight against climate change. european leaders agreed to the estimate that by 2020, the total costs of mitigation and adaptation measures in developing countries should amount to around eur 100 billion annually. the eu strengthened its negotiating position by agreeing on the funding needed to help developing countries, and in particular the poorest countries. however, i am concerned that there is no clear decision on the eu contribution and the share of the total burden that will fall on each member state, taking into account the contribution capacity of each country. the success of the copenhagen conference depends on reaching a broad political agreement on the most important points, particularly on the commitments to be made by all the parties involved.
in writing. other at a time when europe has finally managed to overcome the crisis of the treaties in which it was immersed and lisbon is finally ratified by the 27 member states, i welcome the existence of a new legal and institutional framework which will make it possible to adapt the functioning of the union to its present size, strengthening the powers of the european parliament and paying particular attention to the role of national parliaments in european construction. this is the moment we have been waiting for, and with the adoption of the treaty of lisbon, the union has the opportunity, with its new institutional framework, to tackle the main tasks it will have to fulfil in the immediate future. i cannot help stressing here the action that is expected from the european union in combating the crisis, in reviving the economy, in strengthening confidence in the markets, and in paying particular attention to the evolution of the unemployment rate in europe. now that we are seeing tentative signs of economic recovery, we must therefore concentrate our efforts on reviving the european economy, with particular attention to the primary and secondary sectors - with particular emphasis on agriculture - which have been badly affected by the crisis, and on the creation of a european supervisory structure.
in writing. unfortunately, the european council once again gave high priority to the adoption of the conditions guaranteeing the entry into force of the so-called lisbon treaty by the end of 2009. the czech republic has therefore adopted the positions which enabled it to ratify the convention quickly. for the leaders of the european union, the important thing is the institutional issues that will allow a faster progress in the capitalist, federalist and militarist integration of the european union. the economic, financial and social crisis therefore took a back seat. the fact that little progress has been made is the result of great pressure from important sectors in several member states, as in the case of the milk crisis. even then, the council settled for a mere €280 million increase in the 2010 budget. however, the proposals which the european commission has just put forward following the council's guidelines, both on the excessive deficit and on the announced increase in the retirement age, are extremely worrying. instead of responding to the serious social problems of poverty and unemployment, they announce measures which will aggravate the social situation and the glaring inequalities that exist.
in this debate we must not forget the serious implications which the transposition of this directive on the liberalization of services may have in several countries, particularly those with the most fragile social situations, making a crisis situation such as the one we are experiencing even more serious. even with all the care in the various member states, if immediate measures are not taken to defend social and labour rights and to protect the most vulnerable sectors, including in the areas of public services, we could have even more serious problems. liberalisation could increase unemployment, poverty and inequalities among the most vulnerable, to the benefit of only large service companies and economic groups, especially in the wealthier countries. so at this time of crisis, one of the essential measures is to postpone the deadline for transposing the directive on the liberalization of services and to study adequately the possible social implications of the application of this directive.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, first of all, i would like to express my full support for the question raised by mr reul and, above all, to highlight the underlying political issue which is at issue here, which is precisely the joint research programming. what has happened in the joint programming on neurodegenerative diseases and in particular on alzheimer's is that in the middle of the process the rules have changed, we no longer have a report, we now have a resolution, so our capacity for codecision on this matter has been eliminated, we no longer are members of parliament, we have become consultants. and so i would like to know whether at least what we recommend will be taken into account or not. the programming of research activities, in whatever field, is a political choice, not a technical one, and in this respect the role of parliament must be valued and strengthened. it seems to me that setting priorities that are political means that they must be scrutinized, they must be transparent, they must be democratic. what has happened with regard to joint decision-making and joint scheduling of alzheimer's research is a process which must not be repeated and if it is repeated again, please let us know in good time! i hope, therefore, that the last thing that can happen to us is that the decisions and recommendations which parliament has made on this matter are not taken into account.
i welcome the initiatives and actions taken at european level in the fight against neurodegenerative diseases and in particular alzheimer's disease. member states have been developing research efforts in this area. it is important to encourage cooperation between member states by ensuring greater coordination in scientific research and technological development, avoiding fragmentation. joint programming of research activities is a valuable tool for reducing fragmentation by involving member states, the public sector and the private sector at european level. this joint programming instrument will be key to the future of the european research area. the construction of the european research area is at the heart of the research policy of the lisbon treaty. i am concerned, however, about the implementation of this joint programming instrument in terms of the bureaucratic complexity and the delay in the administrative procedures. i therefore ask what plans are there for the use of a process of economies of scale which combines efficiency, simplification and speed of administrative procedures in order to promote excellence and encourage the european-wide collaboration which the importance of this subject requires.
in writing. dear president, ladies and gentlemen, the commission's communication to parliament presents an optimistic analysis of the expected benefits of research cooperation between member states and of the coordination that the commission can carry out, in the context of the 7th framework programme, in the field of neurodegenerative diseases, in particular alzheimer's disease. the measures proposed in the communication are based solely on the principle of the best use of existing resources and programmes: the commission has also adopted a number of proposals for the following programmes: - the health programme; - the 7th framework programme; - the eu disability action plan; - the open method of coordination; - the statistical programme. will optimum use of these resources be sufficient to achieve the expected results? what coordination mechanisms does the commission propose that could not have been put in place before the communication? what's the added value of this one? what new practical means does the commission intend to adopt to promote research cooperation in a context where resources and teams exist, albeit dispersed? will it be enough to raise awareness of the problem and use existing instruments to solve it?
in the last month, the regions of europe have been particularly evident. the european week of regions and cities has consolidated the position of the regions as a key part of the solution to europe's response to global challenges. president barroso, commissioner samecki and the hundreds of debates that followed have concluded that, leaving no room for doubt. at a more specific level, the important meeting of the conference of presidents of the outermost regions of europe, which included members of the european parliament for the first time, was a milestone in regional policy for the construction of europe. global challenges and european responses are a great opportunity for a new strategy for the ors. these regions, like the azores, beyond the permanent constraints that everyone is accustomed to hearing about, present a set of potentials that give clear opportunities to make our progress and give europe a clear advantage in new areas essential to responding to global challenges. the president of the commission is therefore called upon to put a clear end to the palace coup which the commission's 'no-paper' indicates was being prepared while we, members and european citizens, were advocating the ratification of the treaty of lisbon and its importance in defending territorial cohesion... (the president withdraws the floor from the speaker).
in portugal, particularly in the north, the economic and social crisis is worsening and unemployment is rising, making this region one of the poorest in the european union, as recent eurostat documents prove. large companies continue to announce a reduction in activity and employment. one of the most serious examples is that of qimonda in vila do conde, which is in the process of laying off more than 600 workers, in addition to the approximately 1000 it has already laid off in the last year. it is, in effect, the destruction of one of the largest and most important companies in an industrial sector strategic for technological development. we cannot accept this situation. it is unacceptable that neither the european commission nor the council has found an alternative to the dismantling of this sector of microships, of nanotechnology. it is regrettable that they remain indifferent to the worsening unemployment and the growing agony of vast regions of the european union.
the survey carried out by the european agency for safety and health at work reveals worrying findings, noting that the generalization of unemployment is having a negative impact on the safety and health of workers. according to the survey of 27 000 respondents in the 27 member states, 6 out of 10 workers believe that the economic crisis and rising unemployment are degrading working conditions. 75% of the respondents say that the deterioration of their health can be attributed to the work they do. this information confirms the data from a eurostat study, which reveals that 27 million workers are victims of accidents or occupational diseases and that 137 million are exposed to risks on a daily basis. this week, another serious accident at work killed five portuguese workers in andorra. this situation requires a rapid response from the european union and the member states, which involves creating jobs with rights, valuing work and workers, and penalizing employers who, in order to increase their profits, cut back on the means of prevention and protection of workers.
the european commission has recently presented a draft communication on budgetary reform which includes references to cohesion policy which raise concerns and perplexities, particularly for the outermost regions. the debate on the community budget is urgent, but we believe that the post-2013 financial framework must be built on the basis of solidarity and territorial cohesion, which is crucial for regions like madeira that are facing permanent difficulties and therefore need permanent aid. it is unacceptable to try to shift the focus of cohesion policy from the current approach in terms of regions to one of value-added sectors, a move which could lead to the abolition of objective 2, which currently benefits about two thirds of european regions. it is inconsistent for the commission to try to reorient the allocation of funds from a regional perspective to a national or even a european one, rejecting the criterion of proximity which has always been at the heart of cohesion policy. it is intolerable to include as a variable in the calculation of the allocation of the structural funds the length of the member state's membership, dividing the states into new and old, which would render meaningless article 299 (2) of the treaty as regards access to the structural funds by the ors.
the current economic crisis is leading to precarious employment. economic hardship leads workers to blindly fall in line with job offers that escape labour standards and the scrutiny of police and government authorities. unfortunately, in the european union we are already witnessing a number of cases of labour exploitation, which often amount to genuine slavery of both european and non-european citizens. on the other hand, we have seen an unusually high number of deaths of migrant workers, including many compatriots, many portuguese citizens, even though they are working in a regular situation, accidents that have happened at the workplace and especially in the construction sector. it therefore urges and calls on the european union and its institutions, and all member states, to cooperate strongly and effectively in order to prevent such situations.
the application of the principle of economic and social cohesion is particularly important in crisis situations, which requires not only a significant increase in the community budget and a fair sharing of wealth, but also other policies that give priority to social protection and inclusion, the creation of jobs with rights for all and access to universal and quality public services to guarantee fundamental rights for all citizens. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women in matters of social security is a very good one. we hope that after this debate, commissioner, there will be initiatives from the european commission to this end.
the global financial crisis has had a profound and global effect on the movement of people in the world. in this context, greater political and social integration is needed. a stronger and more united europe. the european union must guarantee the protection of all fundamental rights in its own territory, otherwise it cannot advocate a policy which it does not put into practice. i therefore advocate a new concept of sociality in the european union, capable of guaranteeing minimum social rights to every european citizen. for example, minimum standards in the areas of health, education and social pensions are needed and should be guaranteed at the level of the european union. i consider it crucial that these minimum standards be established, since they add to the homogeneity of employment conditions by acting as a regulatory effect on the movement of firms and labour. it is obvious that the union's financial support cannot be conditional on policies which are the sole responsibility of the member states, nor on those policies themselves. however, this does not mean that greater political and social concertation is not sought. on the contrary, this integration is urgent!
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, we condemn the military coup d'état in honduras on 28 june and the continued violation by the coup plotters of the tegucigalpa/san josé agreements of 30 october. it is unacceptable that the coup plotters continue a clear strategy of systematic delay and subterfuge to delay the return of power to constitutionally elected president zelaya. the european union must demand the immediate and unconditional replacement of president zelaya. without this, it is impossible to continue with the electoral process, because it becomes unviable and illegitimate, since there are no minimum conditions to ensure that citizens can exercise the universal right to vote directly, secretly and free from coercion or any kind of threat. the european union and this european parliament must take a clear stand in condemning and repudiating these positions of the coup plotters. they demand the immediate restoration of democracy in honduras and refuse to send european observers or accept any results of elections under these circumstances.
in writing. the liberalisation of world trade has resulted in significant job losses and the deterioration of the social situation in several regions and countries, such as portugal, which are more dependent on certain productive sectors, such as textiles and clothing. this situation, in the context of the present deep economic and social crisis, calls for a different approach. the adoption of rules on designation of origin could contribute to the enhancement of production in the various member states. but much more is needed. the protection of productive sectors is required by a broader and more effective set of measures, including: - the implementation of safeguard and defence mechanisms against aggressive exports, giving priority to local production. - the break with the model based on low wages, low qualifications and precarious employment. - the existence of public instruments for controlling production and regulating markets, which guarantee the defence of the principle of food sovereignty and food security. - an effective fight against the relocation of firms. the defence of each country's right to produce in a sustainable way is a requirement for the future. in the name of a new economic, social, energy and environmental rationality, which the neoliberal model not only does not guarantee, but makes impossible.
in writing. the eu's strategy for the region of the former yugoslavia was defined some five years ago in the thessaloniki agenda. a european perspective for the peoples of the western balkans was safeguarded, where the issue of visa liberalisation was envisaged. the slovenian presidency decided to open negotiations in 2008. five countries in the western balkans are negotiating such liberalisation, but according to the european commission's report, although all have made considerable progress, only three (macedonia, montenegro and serbia) meet the conditions for such liberalisation to be achieved in the near future, and bosnia and albania have not been considered as being suitable. i agree that we cannot set a precedent by lowering the criteria that have been agreed. we are ready to welcome bosnia and albania when they are ready to meet the conditions that have been set.
in writing. - the visa regime advocated here, and which we voted against, is a major step backwards for these countries of the former yugoslavia, whose citizens, during the period of socialism, could travel without visas to the countries of the present european union and who are now required to have a visa. on the other hand, this regime is full of contradictions. it is true that it provides for visa facilitation, but it still requires a set of unjustified procedures and requires the inclusion of biometric data in passports, posing serious risks to the right to privacy and data protection and violating the rights of citizens of those countries. in parallel, the unacceptable agreements for the 'repatriation of persons residing without authorization', whose access to the visa facilitation system implies their signature, remain in place, which, in addition to violating the rights of immigrants under these conditions, constitutes an unacceptable pressure and blackmail on these countries. the report also includes the dialogue with kosovo on its admission to this process, which implicitly constitutes its recognition and violates international law and serbia's sovereignty over the territory.
in writing. - although i voted in favour of this report, i believe that special attention should be paid to this process. the possibility of exempting the citizens of these countries from the visa requirement must be based on certainty as to the conditions under which this is done. in particular, taking into account the need to combat illegal immigration, trafficking in human beings and crime. for all these reasons, i believe that the epp's reservations about albania and bosnia are justified, and that the countries which have been given this opportunity should be kept under constant surveillance. the security of europe's borders must be a concern of european policy, otherwise, by facilitating entry, we will be unable to comply with and enforce the rules within the eu.
in writing. − the european commission's proposal aims to create a new european microfinance facility for employment, which aims to give the unemployed a fresh start and open up entrepreneurship to some of the most disadvantaged groups in europe, including young people. this instrument will broaden the range of financial support specifically for new entrepreneurs in the current context of reduced credit supply. however, i cannot agree with the european commission's proposal to reallocate part of the budget (eur 100 million) of progress - which is a community programme for employment and social solidarity - to the european microfinance facility. reinvesting progress funds would be a bad sign, since progress is targeted at the most vulnerable social groups. the european microfinance facility should have a separate budget line.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this resolution, as we support the position of the committee on employment and social affairs, which disagrees with the european commission's position that it intends to withdraw funds from progress to finance a microcredit programme. if the european commission wants to create a microcredit programme, it should do so with fresh money and not at the expense of progress. in any event, the european parliament has not yet completed its examination of the commission's proposals for the microfinance facility and the commission should therefore refrain from adopting the specific measures concerning the financial allocation of the progress programme until the legislative procedure on the microfinance facility has been completed. the commission is therefore strongly opposed to the adoption of the draft commission decision establishing the annual work plan for 2010 of the progress programme, divided into separate sections. on the other hand, we also think that it is more appropriate for the commission to withdraw the draft decision establishing the annual work plan for the 2010 progress programme, divided into separate sections, and to present a new proposal.
in writing. - i welcome the european union's commitment to intensifying its relations with russia, thus contributing to greater stability, security and prosperity in europe. one of the most important and central areas of cooperation in eu-russia relations is precisely energy and energy security. it is essential to create the stability needed to provide security of supply to eu member states and their consumers. i hope that the energy dialogue and the commitments resulting from the next eu-russia summit will contribute to greater transparency and long-term viability of the energy sector and that in turn this sector can help to establish new structural relations between the two blocs through the development of trade and economic cooperation. the eu and russia are also working together on climate change to secure a comprehensive agreement at the copenhagen conference. i would stress the strategic nature of eu-russia relations and their contribution to the process of mutual understanding and trust, which is fundamental to the process of establishing peace and stability on the european continent.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution because i consider that this summit, in the run-up to the copenhagen climate change conference, is of particular importance and can be an excellent opportunity to strengthen relations between the eu and russia. this partnership can play a decisive role in the world, taking into account the effects of the economic and financial crisis, the preparations for the copenhagen conference and the signing of a future agreement establishing an early warning mechanism that ensures greater energy security between the eu and russia, thus promoting greater cooperation in this field.
in writing. − as europe celebrates the 20th anniversary of the fall of the berlin wall, eu-russia relations are of particular relevance and deserve careful reflection. it is now clear that the wave of liberating and democratizing optimism after the fall of the iron curtain has suffered multiple setbacks and that russia is far from being the democratic rule of law that was then idealized and for which the whole world longs. it is therefore natural that this slowness should be disappointing. that said, we must firmly denounce the revisionist efforts of a part of the european left which seeks to cover up the heinous crimes of communism and to invent past perfect worlds which offend the memory of those who fought for liberation from soviet totalitarianism. this summit is an opportunity to strengthen eu relations with russia and to do so in a consistent and productive way for both sides, without neglecting the firmness and attention that are essential on issues such as energy, defence, democracy and human rights. the award of the sakharov prize to memorial signifies the extent of the work still to be done and reveals the demanding attention of this house to russia. i hope that the other european institutions will follow suit.
in writing. the next eu-russia summit will take place 20 years after the fall of the berlin wall. the symbolism of the occasion serves to remind us of two key ideas that should be kept in mind. firstly, the values of freedom, respect for fundamental rights, democracy, peace and the sovereignty of states are still very much relevant, they guide the european union in its internal and external policy and they should guide a state like russia, whose role in international society is, and should be, fundamental. on the other hand, we must stress with concern that russia has not taken on the role of a player guided by these values. but the passage of these twenty years also serves as a reminder that europe's relations with russia are different today, based on dialogue. russia is not an ally of europe with whom we share values, it is a neighbour with whom we share a geographical space, differences and common interests, as is the case with the situation in afghanistan. this realism must be the starting point for the future establishment of the new cooperation agreement. the defeat of communism does not mean the end of differences, it means, in these relations, the end of confrontation.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the joint programming of research activities to combat neurodegenerative diseases because i consider it necessary to strengthen measures to promote research at european level in the field of these diseases, in particular alzheimer's disease. considering that 7.3 million people suffer from alzheimer's disease or related diseases (a number that is estimated to double by 2020), the promotion of prevention and early diagnosis and treatment of neurodegenerative diseases is of particular importance. i believe that article 182 (5) of the lisbon treaty, which provides for the codecision procedure for the creation of a european research area, could provide a more appropriate legal basis for future joint programming initiatives in the field of research, through greater involvement of the european parliament.
in writing. - we voted in favour of this motion for a resolution because we know that neurodegenerative diseases such as alzheimer's and parkinson's affect more than seven million people living in the countries of the european union. we welcome the commission's proposal for a pilot project for a joint research programme in this field, although we consider it to be insufficient. but we recognize its importance in reducing the fragmentation of the research effort, leading to a gathering of a critical mass of skills, knowledge and financial resources. further progress is needed, in particular in a disciplinary approach covering social research into the well-being of patients and their families, in the promotion of 'healthy mental lifestyles' and in the substantial improvement of the living and health conditions of the general population. we know that neurodegenerative diseases like alzheimer's and parkinson's are one of the biggest challenges in mental health, so the fight against these diseases must address a triple challenge: providing care on a daily basis to an increasing number of patients; improving the conditions under which much of this care is delivered, which means supporting more families and caregivers; and securing much more resources for research to bring the disease down.
in writing. − this report provides a clear and comprehensive overview of the activities of the european ombudsman in 2008. the new layout and presentation of the statistical data have helped to make this report clearer and more comprehensible than the previous ones. the ombudsman has seen an increase in the number of complaints that have been sent to him, although of the 3 406 received in 2008, only 802 fall within his mandate. i would like to stress the positive aspect of the fact that in 36% of cases an amicable solution has been found. i think that the number of inadmissible complaints is still very high and that an information campaign should be launched among the european citizens to increase their knowledge of the ombudsman's functions and powers. the european ombudsman is responsible for ensuring that citizens' rights under eu law are respected at all levels in the union and that the eu institutions and bodies comply with the highest standards of administration. it is important to ensure that citizens receive prompt and well-founded answers to their requests for information, complaints and petitions, while at the same time strengthening their trust in the eu and its institutions.
in writing. - given the importance of the role of the european ombudsman in fostering transparency in the relationship between the eu and european citizens, i reiterate my position that constructive relations have been developed between all the community institutions and bodies.
in writing. − the entry into force of the lisbon treaty will modify the current eu budgetary framework and will lead to the need to adopt legal acts for its implementation, in particular the adoption of the new regulation on the multiannual financial framework, the financial regulation on principles concerning the adoption and implementation of the budget and the adoption of the new interinstitutional agreement. as the whole procedure for adopting these new measures will take a few months, i agree with the rapporteur that transitional guidelines should be accepted as soon as the treaty enters into force. these guidelines will be relevant to allow the implementation of the budget and the adoption of amending budgets and, if necessary, for the 2011 budgetary procedure.
in writing. - i welcome the imminent entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, which strengthens the role of the european parliament in several areas, including the budget. the report by alain lamassoure on transitional procedural guidelines deserves my agreement and i therefore voted in favour of it, since transitional measures are necessary as long as the treaty of lisbon is not in force. i would like to take this opportunity to congratulate the rapporteur on his proactivity and the quality of the work he has done. i would stress that transitional measures cannot depart from the general principles enshrined in the new treaty or prejudice future legislative procedures. i would also stress the need to reduce the current number of amending budgets, which has been excessive, and the urgency of the commission presenting appropriate proposals for the adoption of a regulation containing the multiannual financial framework and the adaptation of the financial regulation.
in writing. the report focuses on the transitional procedural guidelines on budgetary matters, taking into account the prospect of the entry into force of the lisbon treaty. the entry into force of the treaty - which we were and are against - by the changes it introduces into the budgetary procedure naturally requires the adoption of these transitional measures, since the budget of the european union for 2010 will still be adopted within the framework provided by the treaty of nice. the subject of this report is not the treaty of lisbon itself, but the need to adopt a procedure to enable the 2010 budget to be implemented. understanding this need, we voted against all amendments to the report which would lead to a blockage of budget implementation, which would be profoundly negative. however, we cannot vote in favour of a report which says in paragraph 1 that the european parliament 'welcomes the imminent entry into force of the treaty of lisbon'. this is what the most elementary consistency demands, in the face of a treaty whose consequences will be profoundly negative for the future of the workers and peoples of europe for the reasons we have repeatedly stated and whose ratification process was profoundly anti-democratic. that is why we abstained in the final vote.
the trade agreement signed on 4 april between the european union and israel, which aims to liberalize trade in agricultural products, processed agricultural products and fishery products, is unacceptable for a number of reasons which we are denouncing here. of course, because it is in line with the neoliberal policies that are aggravating the current economic and social crisis, particularly in agriculture and fisheries, but this is all the more serious as these policies are now being promoted through an agreement with a country that has been violating international law and the most basic rights of the palestinian people, which does not respect the obligations agreed in the road map for peace, keeping gaza under siege, building more settlements, continuing the construction of the wall and the expulsion of palestinians from jerusalem. a country that continues to escalate its violations of human rights, as well as violations of international humanitarian law. we condemn the signing of this agreement, which will involve trade in products originating in israeli settlements in the palestinian territories, and we stress the european union's blatant and unacceptable complicity with israel in the serious violations described above. we express our full solidarity with the palestinian people and defend their right to build a free, independent and sovereign state.
the recent tragedy caused by hurricane ida as it passed through el salvador between november 6 and 8 included about 200 people dead and at least 18 missing, about 15,000 displaced, 80 damaged schools, destruction of agricultural crops and essential infrastructure such as roads, electrical grid, water supply services, communications and health equipment. el salvador is one of the countries in central america with the greatest economic and social difficulties and the european union must provide extraordinary support to deal with the social disaster caused by this natural disaster. the government estimates that more than eur 1 billion is needed to address these losses and implement a reconstruction and risk mitigation plan, given the country's vulnerability to these natural disasters. it therefore calls for the provision of extraordinary funds and the reorientation of available funds from the european union. so here it is, mr president, this appeal, asking you to forward it to the european commission and the council.
i would just like to warn you of the danger of a disease that threatens coniferous forests in the european union, the pine nematode, which is native to america, and which has been detected in the setúbal region of portugal and has already spread to other parts of the territory and to spain. the only effective method of combating the disease is the felling and burning of trees. i note that forests cover 38% of portugal's territory and represent 400 000 landowners, 14% of industrial gdp, 9% of industrial employment and 12% of exports. it is, after all, the forests of the european union that are at risk. this intervention is therefore linked to the need to establish an emergency plan to combat this disease, which, although it is currently confined to the iberian peninsula, will spread to the whole of the european union. a contingency plan which also has sufficient financial resources to put an end to what is now affecting many firms which have closed down and many workers who have lost their jobs. the whole of the european union must do something about this.
mr president, this initiative is intended to enable the customs administrations of the member states to set up a common automatic system of information in the customs field. this information system should contribute to the prevention, investigation and elimination of offences against not only community but also national laws. i welcome the desire to facilitate the exchange of information, to improve and strengthen customs cooperation and to establish procedures for joint action. i have no doubt that a rapid and efficient exchange of information will provide a vital service in the fight against illegal trade activities. when we talk about information systems, however, we must not forget that they contain personal data and that the issues relating to the protection of such data and the privacy of individuals are of paramount importance. i agree with the rapporteur, mr alvaro, that we must be vigilant to avoid any violation of fundamental rights. in particular, the right to privacy, so that personal information to be entered into the system should be limited to what is strictly necessary, without infringing on the privacy of individuals. it must be ensured that the use of such data can only be made for clearly defined and limited purposes within the applicable legal framework. and that data should only be kept for the period necessary to achieve the purpose for which it was entered. it is also important to ensure that access to this information system is transparent and in accordance with the provisions applicable to similar information systems, such as sis ii or vis. that is also why i do not support the amendments proposed by the gue group. as regards data protection supervision, rather than trying to create ad hoc solutions, it is preferable and advisable to maintain and apply the same supervision system that has been decided to apply to other similar systems, not only for reasons of consistency but also to avoid any level playing field in data protection. finally, madam president, i share the view of commissioner kallas and rapporteur alexander alvaro, who regret the hasty way in which the council sought to close this dossier before the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon.
the rapporteur. - madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, as this is the first time i have taken the floor in a plenary session of the european parliament, let me greet you all and convey to you the pleasure, enthusiasm and commitment i bring to my participation in this assembly representing all european citizens. in the light of my task here, i have to present to you today two reports on the allocation of macrofinancial assistance to georgia and armenia, intended to alleviate the budgetary and external account difficulties of these two caucasian countries. both these countries are our partners in the european neighbourhood policy and have been hit hard by the global economic and financial crisis of the last two years. in the case of georgia, the impact of the crisis has also been compounded by the direct and indirect consequences of last year's military conflict with russia, including large numbers of internally displaced persons within that country. the economy of neighbouring armenia has also been affected by the combination of the economic recession and, in particular, the rapid deterioration of the russian economy, given the armenian economy's heavy dependence on trade with its large northern neighbour. this has dragged these two countries into a very difficult economic, financial and social situation. these two proposals for exceptional financial assistance which we are discussing today will reduce the budgetary gap and will have an immediate positive impact on the public finances and balance of payments of georgia and armenia, provided they are implemented immediately, and that is why our two reports fully endorse the need to provide such financial assistance to georgia and armenia. in addition, in my capacity as chairman of the committee on international trade, i proposed to my colleagues in that committee that they approve these two proposals without amendments under the simplified procedure, and that proposal was unanimously approved. however, in the reports we have presented to the house, we have expressed our protest at the delay and the short time in which the commission has brought these agreements to us for discussion, delays which we consider unacceptable in the future, even though we are aware of the natural need for a more expeditious procedure for this type of issue, given its exceptional nature. this position does not mean that my colleagues and i do not have reservations and concerns about the agreements themselves which are proposed to us. and some of us, probably, if we had chosen another option, would have had the opportunity to table amendments, stressing, for example, the importance of certain conditions and criteria linked to assistance in order to improve the transparency of its content and implementation. but that would have meant - and i will end, madam president - that we would not have been able to approve these agreements with regard to these two countries. and so, in view of the factual circumstances and the strategic importance of these two countries in the context of the european neighbourhood policy, i call on all of us to approve these two proposals for macrofinancial assistance without amendments, as proposed by the commission.
the rapporteur. - madam president, i take this opportunity to make three final remarks. first note. the macro-financial assistance to the union's neighbouring countries is justified for two reasons. firstly, because of its political value as a factor of prestige for the union, which derives from the assistance given to countries in difficulty in the framework of a good neighbour policy. secondly, because the union itself is interested in contributing to the financial, social and political stability of our neighbouring countries, as a condition for their development and democratic consolidation, because this is also a condition for our own political stability. the second point is that macrofinancial assistance must, however, be carefully linked to two conditions: that the assistance is intended to respond to the exceptional and transitory financial difficulties of these countries and not to other purposes, and that these countries do not divert the funds we provide them for purposes incompatible with the reason for the union's assistance, namely to increase military expenditure. third and final note. i would like to stress the importance and the need for a general regulatory framework on the allocation of macrofinancial assistance and on the implementation of the relevant agreements with the countries concerned, and i therefore welcome and note the commitment of commissioner kallas in this regard. and i hope that the next commission will live up to this commitment and that we will have a general regulation on macrofinancial assistance in the short term.
mr president, i would first like to welcome the rapporteurs, mr martin and mr corbett in particular, and say that as a member of the ppe i am basically looking at the proposed amendments to the rules of procedure. i would like to emphasize the point which seems to me important here and which has not been brought up this evening, namely the point of the national parliaments. the democratic strengthening of the european parliament is undoubtedly the result of these rules and the treaty of lisbon, but european democracy also results from the link with the national parliaments. and what this regulation, this proposal, is now doing is creating these two democratic legitimacies, strengthening democracy. on the one hand, democracy through the instruments of the european parliament, its instruments of control and its legislative capacity, and on the other hand, democratic cooperation between national parliaments through various instruments. and so here i am - let us say - in full agreement with the proposals made. i do not agree with mr duff when he says that we should put off introducing mechanisms for relations with national parliaments. i think we can do that now, in the light of the lisbon treaty, and that is very important. i would also like to say that i support the proposal by my colleagues mr szájer and mr brok on representation in cosac and, finally, that i am in full agreement with the remarks made here by mr brok and other colleagues on the primacy of parliament and its members over parliament's services.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, a consistent approach to the problem of climate change, beyond the setting of emission reduction targets, requires a realistic definition of the means to achieve them. we consider it significant and revealing that the majority of the members of the committee on the environment have rejected the inclusion in the motion for a resolution on copenhagen of amendments advocating - and i quote - a diversification of the instruments used to achieve the reduction objectives, avoiding a dependence on market instruments and the need to carry out an evaluation of the effectiveness of these market instruments as well as their social and environmental consequences. the preponderance given by the european union to market solutions is indicative of a political and ideological choice at the heart. the aim is to set up a scheme to generate billions of fictitious financial assets in the service of a system which shows that it has learned nothing from the crisis in which it finds itself. the experience of the implementation of the european trading system is a complete denial of the virtuousness of market regulation and a clear demonstration of the ineffectiveness and perversity of its instruments.
madam president, with only a few days to go until copenhagen, it seems more difficult to reach a legally binding text. we're going to have what some have called a two-strikes deal, but that can't diminish our ambition. the european union must continue to lead the negotiations and put pressure on the other partners. copenhagen cannot be a mere declaration of intent. the copenhagen process must include at least the binding political commitments and the timetable for the adoption of a post-kyoto agreement in june 2010 in bonn. the united states, japan and the bric countries and many others must make commitments comparable to those of the european union, as the eu's efforts alone will do little to achieve the goal of limiting the temperature increase to 2°c. the financing of adaptation in developing countries must not jeopardise the achievement of the millennium development goals, especially in african countries, which are the most affected by climate change. copenhagen should also contribute to the shift in the global energy paradigm by promoting renewable energy and energy efficiency. this is the way forward, not just to combat climate change, but also to create jobs.
it is essential that a politically binding agreement emerges from the copenhagen conference. it should contain operational elements that can take immediate effect and a timetable that allows agreement on a legally binding text during 2010. the agreement must involve all the countries that have signed the convention, and it is important that commitments, both in terms of emission reductions and in terms of funding, are clearly identified. while the industrialized countries must take the lead in reducing greenhouse gas emissions, the more economically advanced developing countries also have a role to play, contributing according to their responsibilities and respective capacities. industrialized countries and emerging countries with more advanced economies should be subject to comparable efforts. this is the only way to reduce distortions of international competition. the new agreement should provide for the establishment of low-carbon plans at national level supported by legislative acts. the commitments contained in the national plans should be registered internationally, ensuring greater transparency in all processes. these plans should be binding on all countries except the least developed. but for this strategy to lead to a true third low-carbon industrial revolution, a holistic approach covering all sectors responsible for emissions is needed. it is also essential to define the architecture of the financing so that it is sustainable in the medium and long term. the financing must come from the private sector, the carbon market and the public sector in the industrialised countries, but also from the more economically advanced developing countries. in relation to the implementation of funding, priority should be given to capacity building and adaptation, with particular attention to the least developed countries. in conclusion, i would like to acknowledge the excellent work of commissioner dimas in leading this dossier.
in writing. no one can ignore the extreme urgency of concluding a post-kyoto international agreement to reduce co2 emissions substantially. we have to make sure that the earth doesn't get warmer than two degrees. to do that, global emissions have to fall by at least 30% over the next 10 years. we have the future of humanity at stake and we are running out of time. only in this way can the consequences of climate change, which is already having an impact but which may become the most serious problem of the 21st century, be mitigated. as the least contributors to these changes, we on islands are seriously concerned about this. the european union must continue to play a leading role and speak with one voice on environmental matters. it will have to use all its political weight to get a firm agreement concluded by the world's major powers at the next summit in copenhagen. it is essential to take concrete steps to change the global energy pattern, as portugal and the azores have already done, by focusing on renewable energy and energy efficiency.
a political agreement in copenhagen is essential, but it needs to be global. to put european industry in a situation of enormous disproportion to other developed economies would be economically wrong and ecologically unhelpful. the european effort must be directed towards the search for an agreement which is binding on all. the commission has also been asked to provide the necessary information to the member states. this leaves out the idea of a tax on international financial transactions (tobin tax) to finance climate change adaptation in developing countries. this support, which is needed, must not be done at the expense of the economy, trade, wealth creation. the cost of such a tax to society at large (increased tax burden reflected on all taxpayers and consumers) and its impact on the financial market (reduced liquidity and credit flow to firms and households) cannot be ignored. in addition, the implementation of a global tax raises complicated technical and administrative problems. the way forward in a time of crisis cannot be through more and new taxes which are difficult to collect. the idea of this new tax has to be stopped.
in writing. the eu must maintain a leading and exemplary role in combating climate change. it should be noted that the eu has gone beyond the kyoto targets. i am in favour of the copenhagen agreement to reduce global emissions being binding. i have therefore tabled an amendment to parliament's resolution on this subject, calling for an international sanctioning regime to be included in the final text. i believe that the agreement must be global, timetabled and demanding. if we are not ambitious, we will create a small, unwieldy instrument that will be even less effective than the kyoto protocol, which already provides for international sanctions. it is therefore hoped that effective control will be established and that the agreement will incorporate a review clause so that it can be easily updated. we must also send a clear signal to the emerging industries in asia. china and india cannot be dismissed as being responsible for a large percentage of global emissions, while our industries are making tremendous efforts to reduce them. the united states has a strong responsibility for the success of this summit. i hope that the president of the united states, barack obama, will demonstrate that he deserves the nobel peace prize, because the fight against climate change will contribute to the peace and happiness of all peoples!
in writing. - i welcome the fact that the european parliament has adopted the telecommunications package, thereby strengthening consumer rights and contributing to greater access to information and freedom of expression. in order to achieve the objectives of the lisbon agenda, it is necessary to provide adequate incentives for investment in new high-speed networks in order to support innovation in content-based internet services and to strengthen the international competitiveness of the european union. it is essential to promote sustainable investment in the development of these networks, while safeguarding competition and increasing consumer choice. in order to ensure investment in new technologies in less developed regions, the regulation of electronic communications should be consistent with other policies, such as state aid policy, cohesion policy or the objectives of a broader industrial policy.
in writing. - i support the telecommunications package because i consider the internet to be an essential means for education and for the exercise of our freedom of expression and access to information. this initiative recognises that access to/use of the internet is a fundamental right of european citizens. i congratulate mrs regina bastos, who was the only portuguese member of this conciliation. i'm advocating a free internet without that meaning unregulated. as in the real world, the virtual world of the internet is the scene of illicit and illegal activities from uploading video/music files to encouraging terrorism, to child pornography, ... the european parliament, against many national governments, has succeeded in ensuring that all users enjoy the rights and guarantees provided for in the european convention for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms (echr). thus, any restriction on the fundamental rights and freedoms of internet users, such as a cut-off of access, should comply with the echr and general principles of law and, in particular, be authorised in advance by a judicial decision, while safeguarding procedural guarantees, the presumption of innocence and the right to privacy, without prejudice to specific mechanisms in cases of urgency justified by state security.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report by mrs trautmann because i believe that the current agreement goes far beyond what was possible in the earlier stages of the process, particularly as regards consumer rights. i consider it essential to introduce measures to ensure the rights and guarantees of fixed and mobile telephone users and internet users in the field of freedom of expression and information. it is important to harmonise the internal telecommunications market by promoting competition between undertakings while strengthening the independence of national regulatory bodies from their governments. it was also important to ensure more modern management of radio spectrum by creating mechanisms to make these services more easily available in rural areas.
in writing. - i welcome the commitment reached between parliament and the council to introduce in the framework directive adequate protection for users in the event of restrictions on access to services and applications via electronic communications networks. i consider that the rule of law requires that no one may be restricted in access to information and use of electronic communications networks unless this is done in strict compliance with the principle of the presumption of innocence, and that the restriction of access must be preceded by a prior, fair and impartial procedure which guarantees the right of the persons concerned to be heard and the right to effective judicial protection. i also believe that it is particularly important to strengthen the mechanisms for the independence of national regulatory authorities so that they can be effective market regulators, promoting fair competition between operators, and the mechanisms for cooperation between the various european regulators, so that a more transparent and competitive market can be created, which will mean a gain for users in the quality of the services provided.
in writing. - at the end of october the council adopted most of the texts negotiated with the european parliament on the so-called telecommunications package, with some exceptions included in the trautmann report. the negotiations on this text continued in the conciliation committee, where the text now under vote was accepted. it is true that the agreed text contains some of the proposals put forward by our group in defence of the rights of users. however, it is insufficient, since it allows exceptions to procedural guarantees in cases of urgency, although they must be justified and comply with the european convention for the protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms. but the main problem with the text is the scope, since it refers only to limitations imposed by the member states, not to limitations imposed by private companies. in fact, the european union seems more interested in creating the internal telecommunications market, just to serve the interests of the economic groups that dominate the sector, than in defending the fundamental rights and freedoms of end users, which can only be deserving of our disagreement.
in writing. - the express vote is justified, in particular, by the fact that the new european legislation for the telecommunications sector will strengthen the rights of fixed and mobile telephone users and internet users and boost competition. the most relevant of these new rules will be the strengthening of consumer rights, guarantees of internet access and the protection of personal data, taking into account that the eu is increasingly an area of rights and freedoms.
in writing. - i welcome the agreement reached on the joint draft regulation of the european parliament and of the council on statistics on pesticides, approved by the conciliation committee, which will create a legal framework and establish harmonised rules for the collection and dissemination of statistics on the sale and use of pesticides with a view to their sustainable use.
in writing. − as a member of the european parliament who has always paid particular attention to issues relating to crime prevention, security and police cooperation, i recognise the fundamental importance of europol for the creation of a european security area and for the prevention of trans-european crime, as well as the need to strengthen it at various levels, including those discussed here. but the main question under discussion today is whether the european parliament, less than a week after the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, should renounce its new institutional prerogatives in the field of crime prevention and police cooperation and thus renounce the possibility of intervening in the decision on all these matters under the codecision procedure. i believe that this should not be the case and that this parliament should fully assume its new responsibilities in these matters. i therefore vote in favour of this report and call on the council to withdraw its proposal.

in writing. − four initiatives were presented to us within europol, aimed at introducing new rules on confidentiality of information; implementation with regard to regulating europol's relations with its partners, including the exchange of personal data and classified information; determination of the list of third countries and organisations with which it should conclude agreements; and implementation applicable to analysis files. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. i therefore support their position of not taking a position on the substance of these proposals, rejecting them and asking the commission or the council to make a statement in plenary committing themselves to submitting a new decision within six months of the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. in practical terms, it should be recalled that the council may in any case take a position before the end of the year on the current initiatives, which are merely a consultation of the ep, since they are four implementing measures which are due to come into force on 1 january 2010.

in writing. − the european crime prevention network was set up in 2001, but has so far not produced very positive results, due to numerous organisational failures that have prevented it from developing its potential effectively, and has already been subject to internal review on two occasions. the present initiative seeks to repeal that 2001 decision by proposing a restructuring of the network which in my view appears to be very limited and clearly insufficient to solve the existing problems. it is therefore essential to carry out a more serious and structurally more ambitious reform of this network. the swedish presidency's insistence that the ep should take a decision before the entry into force of the lisbon treaty is therefore unacceptable. not only because it is a weak initiative, but also because it asks the ep to renounce the institutional prerogatives that the lisbon treaty gives it in the field of crime prevention, just a matter of days before the new treaty enters into force.

in writing. − the european prevention network (epn) was set up in 2001 to identify measures and exchange actions in crime prevention and to strengthen the network of national authorities responsible for crime prevention. after seven years, an external evaluation of the repc concluded that there were many areas for improvement in the functioning of the institution. it is of the utmost importance for the security of the european area to develop different aspects of crime prevention at eu level and at the same time to support the prevention and combating of national and local crime phenomena. given the sensitivity of the issues raised in this report, i agree with the decision to ask the council for a new proposal, in co-decision, in accordance with the treaty of lisbon.

in writing. - we have always advocated the need for the eu to provide solidarity aid to countries in need and that this aid should be directed to projects of real interest to their populations. eu aid has, however, shown little solidarity. the interests of big business and financial capital and the great powers almost always override solidarity. this is also the case with the aid to georgia which we have just voted on. the financial assistance is mainly intended to finance the imf recommendations and its policy of structural adjustment, i.e. the insistence on the same neoliberal policies that led to the economic and financial crisis that this country is facing. these same reasons also justify our abstention in the other reports. in addition, there is no guarantee that the funds decided upon will not support, even indirectly, the rearmament of georgia after the attack by its troops on the populations of the provinces of south ossetia and abkhazia, which led to war with russia. we could not accept a decision which could lead to a further militarization of relations between the countries of the caucasus region, whose energy and geostrategic wealth mobilizes the will of the eu and its monopolies.
in writing. − the macrofinancial assistance programme is very important in helping to balance the finances of european nations which have recently emerged from armed conflict and which are facing financial difficulties in their budget deficits and balance of payments as a result of such events. this aid is important if the process of reconstruction in these same countries is to be carried out peacefully, which will only be possible with international help. such assistance also ensures that these areas of instability do not jeopardise security and peace in europe, in particular because of the refugees/displaced people caused by these conflicts. the eu must thus be a space of solidarity, and this support for georgia must be in line with the relevant aspects mentioned above.
in writing. − the macrofinancial assistance programme is also very important in helping to restore the financial balance of european nations that have been through the recent global crisis and have been damaged by the consequences of that crisis in their main trading partners, notably russia in the specific case of armenia. the financial imbalances are budgetary and balance of payments imbalances. this aid is important in order to make armenia face the crisis more consistently and to prevent social instability which could lead to mass exits of migrants, which could cause problems in the european area. the eu should thus be a space of solidarity, with this support for armenia being in line with the relevant aspects mentioned above.
in writing. - in the specific case of serbia, the macrofinancial assistance programme is very important to help its financial balance, as this country, in addition to the global crisis, has also emerged from an armed conflict whose effects are still being felt. this aid is an important instrument for the financial balance of serbia and for strengthening the stabilisation of the situation in the entire balkan region. serbia and its economy play a very important role in the process of regional integration and its participation in european integration is also essential. the eu must thus be a space of solidarity, and this support for serbia must be in line with the relevant aspects mentioned above.
in writing. − the macrofinancial assistance programme is very important in helping bosnia and herzegovina to balance its finances in the face of the adverse effects of the global crisis on its economy. the aid will be reflected in a better performance of its economy in terms of budget deficit and balance of payments. bosnia is also in a sensitive region, so its economic and financial stability is important, thus contributing to the stabilisation of the situation in the entire balkan region. the eu must thus be a space of solidarity, and this support for bosnia must be in line with the relevant aspects mentioned above.
in writing. - i regard this report on the commission proposal to amend the common system of value added tax as a means of achieving greater simplification and harmonization. in fact, by bringing together in a single proposal certain aspects relating to vat on the supply of natural gas, electricity and heating and/or cooling; the tax treatment of joint ventures set up in accordance with article 171 of the ec treaty; the determination of certain consequences of enlargement of the union; and the conditions for exercising the right to deduct vat paid in advance, it will be possible to make vat more effectively applied.
in writing. the council proposal aims to clarify certain provisions concerning the importation and place of taxation of gas and electricity supplies, to include in the directive the derogations agreed for bulgaria and romania when they joined the eu, and to clarify and strengthen the basic rule of the right to deduct which states that it arises only in so far as the goods and services are used by a taxable person and for the needs of his professional activity. however, the text adopted today does not respond to certain specific features of national markets, such as the use of butane and propane gas. in portugal, as in other european countries where citizens have low incomes and where integration into the european natural gas networks is relatively new and extremely costly, the use of butane and propane for domestic use and in micro and small enterprises is an unavoidable reality. in addition, the citizens who use this type of energy are usually the most deprived, so the vat directive discriminates against this population in relation to those with higher incomes. the amendments to the report also seem to limit the scope for action by the member states.
