in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the protection of the marine environment of the north-east atlantic as regards the storage of carbon dioxide streams in geological formations, as i believe that the existence of a regulatory framework and guidelines on the storage of carbon dioxide streams in geological formations will contribute to the protection, in the short and long term, of the maritime zone, if they are intended to permanently retain carbon dioxide in those formations and do not have significant adverse effects on the marine environment, human health and other legitimate uses of european maritime areas, in particular the portuguese and in particular the azores.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the rosbach report on a proposal for a council decision on the approval, on behalf of the european community, of amendments to annex ii and annex iii to the convention for the protection of the marine environment of the north-east atlantic (ospar convention) concerning the storage of carbon dioxide streams in geological formations. however, it is important to ensure that the technology for the geological capture and storage of carbon dioxide, which has not yet been tested, is applied in accordance with the strictest safety standards laid down in the directive on this matter.
in writing. − geological storage of carbon dioxide has been suggested as a possible solution to mitigate the climate effects of an increase in anthropogenic concentrations of this gas in the atmosphere. although this solution raises a number of reservations as to its future applicability - particularly in view of the still early stage of development of the techniques used, their foreseeable high cost and the possible associated risks - it is necessary to continue the studies which have been carried out in this field, taking into account that some of the results obtained so far provide positive indications in this respect. however, it should be noted that the continuation of studies on this possibility, and its possible future application, can in no way compromise the necessary energy paradigm shift aimed at a significant reduction in the current dependence on fossil fuels. in addition, both the environmental impacts and the safety of storage technologies should be rigorously assessed. the resolution adopted safeguards these aspects. that is why we are voting in favour.
president of the commission. − first of all, the president of the council and the high representative are not there representing a part of europe: they represent all of europe. let me say that i was very happy also with the nomination of herman van rompuy. when prime minister reinfeldt put forward his name it was evident that this was the name that could secure consensus around the table. and the fact that he comes from belgium, bringing instinctive support to the community method and being instinctively pro-european – that is a very good tribute to belgium as well. having said this, with regard to the importance of the new member sates, i think you have already made their importance clear by the election of mr buzek as president of the european parliament: someone coming from a new member state, someone who has given so much for the reunification of europe. so, when we are in these positions, i am not here representing one country or one region: we are representing the whole of europe.
mr president, last week the council took note of the agreement between the institutions on the 2010 budget. the consequences of the copenhagen decisions are not, and could not have been, contained in that agreement or commitment. these should be the subject of an amending budget. my question to you is very simple: given the difficulties that have arisen in financing the second tranche of the economic recovery plan for this budget, where does the commission intend to find the resources to finance the first year of the fight against climate change, which will be at least two billion euros? and this value will be increasing every year and therefore where will it get itself?
mr president, i believe that this house would welcome a stockholm programme which is concrete, precise and which really moves towards a people's europe. unfortunately, i think we're a little short of what we could have had. unfortunately, there is a certain tendency in the stockholm programme to be vague, inconclusive and general, and i must say that the lack of democratic control or the lack of intervention, of real intervention by parliament which changes and has practical effects, is part of the problem of an inconclusive and too general programme. i should like to give a concrete example in the case of immigration policies. we all follow the implementation of easo with great interest, but i think that very few steps have been taken in the case of, for example, the opening of legal immigration channels, which would be absolutely necessary, or the directive, for example, on seasonal immigrants, which we have been waiting for so long and which is not yet covered by the stockholm programme.
mr president, i warmly congratulate vice-president barrot and the swedish presidency on the stockholm programme. the stockholm programme is good news for all those who argue that europe is much more than a common market. the construction of a people's europe requires a genuine area of freedom, security and justice. i applaud the balanced weight of the three lines of this triangle, contrary to some more radical views, all three are necessary. security without freedom is dictatorship, freedom without security is anarchy, and justice is not possible without freedom and security. this five-year programme is a return to the ambition of tampere and aims to make significant progress in many areas. it is important, however, to ensure that the objectives set are achieved. there must be effective implementation of the legislation and instruments already adopted. the european citizens will not take us seriously if we do not pass the test of effectiveness. it is not possible, for example, to call for the creation of an entry/exit system by inviting the commission to submit a proposal for this system to start operations in 2015, when it is not even known when sis ii and vis will start operations, given the enormous delays and the set of difficulties encountered. i hope that a common european asylum system will be established by 2012 and that there will be a common immigration policy, both in terms of welcoming and integrating immigrants and in terms of effectively combating illegal immigration. finally, we have a lot to do together at the level of the citizens' europe. there's no point in wasting time on subjects that are not your area of expertise. the fact that the european parliament has been asked to vote on the report by mr glinne, and that the commission has been asked to do so, is a very good thing.
we have always advocated that the european union's policy on the relocation of companies should be reviewed and we have always considered that the globalisation fund is only a palliative for workers who are victims of the strategy of multinationals or the lack of an appropriate industrial policy that focuses on production and quality employment with rights. that is why we insist on other policies of development and social progress. but we also think that irish workers should not be doubly harmed by the profit strategy and social dumping of the multinational dell, which has closed in ireland and received support to move to poland. that is why we support this report. however, we want answers from the european commission on the future. we want a fundamental change in policies, effective control of support for multinationals, a genuine industrial policy and a decisive commitment to the creation of jobs with rights.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's trade policy is not distorted. we are confident, however, that the commission will be able to answer our question. it has to do, as has already been said, with the current state of the euro-mediterranean free trade area, initially planned for 2010. in fact, one of the projects resulting from the barcelona process, which began in 1955, is the creation of a free trade area in the mediterranean by 2010, which should be based on a regional perspective and include a north-south and a south-south network of this free trade area, a goal which, however, remains very distant, and therefore the following questions: does the commission consider that the creation of this euro-mediterranean free trade area in 2010 was a realistic objective from the outset? can the commission substantiate its reply? secondly, parliament is aware that a new euro-mediterranean roadmap will be adopted soon, and possibly a new mechanism to facilitate trade and investment in the region. can the commission provide more details on the actual practical aspects and implications of such a mechanism? thirdly, can the commission comment on the current state of the agadir agreement, on the union's contribution to the reformulation of that agreement and, more generally, on the south-south dimension of the barcelona process: union for the mediterranean? fourthly, can the commission state how it has incorporated the recommendations contained in the sustainability impact assessment carried out by the university of manchester in order to take account of social cohesion and sustainable development in the negotiations, as called for in that study? fifthly, can the commission explain to parliament what was at stake in the renegotiation of the association agreement with syria after it was frozen in 2004? sixthly, can the commission comment on the negotiations with libya, their objectives and the current state of the negotiations? seventh, several mediterranean countries have expressed interest in deepening and/or extending their trade agreements with the union. can the commission first inform parliament about this new generation of association agreements? secondly, to inform parliament whether, in the light of the new powers which the treaty of lisbon confers on parliament in the field of trade, the commission will be able to take account of an earlier resolution of parliament in the negotiation of these new agreements. these are the questions, madam president, commissioner. given the current interest in the barcelona process and the union's southern neighbourhood, we believe that the answer to these questions is of great timeliness and importance.
mr president, we must make two points about the establishment of a euro-mediterranean free trade area. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. these are the words that come to mind, from the dominican lacordaire: between the strong and the weak, between the rich and the poor, it's freedom that enslaves, it's the law that sets you free. we cannot ignore the obvious and significant socioeconomic asymmetries and the very different state of development of the productive systems of the countries of the northern and southern mediterranean. the liberalization of trade, particularly in sensitive sectors such as agriculture and fisheries, has been an unquestionable factor in the worsening of the present economic and social crisis, by the pressure it is putting on the weakest productive systems, on employment and on social rights, by the increase in dependence, particularly serious in the food sector, by jeopardizing the free and sovereign development of all countries. we also recall the situation in palestine and western sahara, which we have already discussed, and which we cannot ignore in this debate.
mr president, this is an important debate because i believe it is the last one in which we are discussing names. it is indeed time to turn the page, to talk about policies, to talk about the challenges facing the european union. i would like to emphasize one: the subject of combating the crisis. it is one of the most unifying days in the history of the european political institutions. it is time to welcome the council's concern and, in particular, the swedish presidency. it is time to congratulate the commission and its president, who have always been very concerned about the fight against the crisis, and to stress that the european parliament itself, through a special committee, has shown a great willingness to discuss this matter. but this is also the time when there have been discrepancies between what is said by policy makers and what has been said by central bank governors, both at national and european level. first, we must be cautious about the end of the crisis. this is not the time to lift measures to support state economies and enterprises. but it's also time to chart the path. central bankers have been warning governments of an ambitious plan to correct deficits. the years vary. they even oppose certain policies, such as those that seek to lower taxes, in a message to some governments. i am sorry that the portuguese government is not following the path of lowering taxes. the way forward must be competitiveness, and the message of this concept must be clear: more competitiveness, more stimulus, more growth, more support for business, more europe to support growth and employment.
in writing. the next european council intends to lay the foundations for the future of the lisbon strategy for growth and jobs. it will do so at a time when unemployment in europe is at historic levels and the economy is in recession, plunged into a deep crisis. eight years later, reality is showing: the declared objectives have failed all along the line. the causes of this failure lie in what has early proved to be the real agenda of this strategy: the deregulation of industrial relations and the correlative devaluation of work, the attack on social rights, the dismantling of essential public services, the privatisation and liberalisation of key sectors of the economy, the imposition of the market as an absolute in growing spheres of collective life. the workers and the people have every reason to aspire to a deep break with this agenda. to a change of course that needs to be made. the change that is required, among other things, is the recognition of education as a right, not as a service, a more marketable sector; the democratisation of access to knowledge and culture; the appreciation of work and workers' rights; the defence of the productive sectors, micro, small and medium-sized enterprises; quality public services for all; a fairer distribution of the wealth created.
the european parliament has voted in favour of the mobilisation of eur 24 million to support workers made redundant in belgium. we voted in favour of the directive because we are on the side of those who need it, those who give their sweat and their intelligence to companies and who are ultimately the victims of an unfair economic system, of unbridled competition for profits and with devastating social effects. that said, the role of this adjustment fund must be assessed. in 2009, only €37 million of the €500 million available was mobilised. the fund is not reflecting the reason it was created. secondly, this fund does not support the unemployed directly, but national safety systems at work. the fund, because they are so different from each other, ends up reproducing the inequalities in the distribution systems themselves. in portugal, the fund supports an unemployed person with eur 500. in ireland, he supports him with 6,000 euros. thirdly, the dell case shows how it is possible to support workers who are made redundant in ireland and the multinational company that made them redundant, which is now receiving another type of public funding, in poland. dell, to set up a new factory in poland, received money while acquiring new positions in the us markets, and in the third quarter of this year, it reported profits of around $337 million. the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the fund to cover the whole of the community.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group, mr president, to ensure that we have serious and binding results in the fight against climate change, there are four principles that must be ensured, and i would like to refer to them again. they were here today too, the vote. the first concerns the need for us to reach an agreement which is legally binding. the second concerns the need to ensure that strict policy targets are met, including targets for emission reductions. we must have ambitious goals in this respect. i think we could have gone further than we did today. the third point is to ensure the necessary public funding to enable us to combat this climate change problem. the fourth and final point - which i think is very relevant to consider - is that it should be global, not just a regional agreement, and this has to be through a process anchored in democratic participation involving all countries. i think that the resolution we have adopted here today is less good - so to speak - than the one that was previously adopted by the committee on the environment. however, i think that what should be safeguarded here is really the result and the effort that has been made over this time and so we leave for copenhagen with a very positive work that has been done in the european parliament. i sincerely hope that we will fight hard and that we will be able to implement what we have agreed here today.
in writing. − at the end of the last parliamentary term, in april, the european parliament decided to postpone the discharge to the council for the implementation of the 2007 budget, mainly due to the lack of transparency in the accountability of the use of the community budget. in particular, parliament considered it important to have greater transparency and closer parliamentary scrutiny of council expenditure on the european common security policy/european security and defence policy (cfsp/esdp). the report adopted today finally gives the council a discharge, since it considers that parliament has obtained a satisfactory response from the council to the requests made in the resolution of last april. it does, however, leave some caveats for the next discharge procedure, namely: the council will monitor progress made by the council in closing all extra-budgetary accounts, publishing all administrative decisions (when these are used as the legal basis for budget lines) and sending its annual activity report to the ep. the council's step towards accountability for the use of the community budget is a slight step forward, but we consider that the information available on cfsp/esdp expenditure is still very insufficient and therefore we remain reserved.
in writing. the new tyre labelling regulation is part of the community's co2 strategy, which sets targets to be achieved through reductions in vehicle emissions. from november 2012, tyres will be labelled in the eu according to their energy efficiency, wet grip and noise emissions. tyres account for 20 to 30% of the energy consumed by vehicles due to their rolling resistance. by regulating the use of energy-efficient, safe and low-noise tyres, we are simultaneously contributing to the reduction of environmental damage through lower fuel consumption and to greater consumer protection through market competition. i therefore welcome the creation of another instrument which represents a further step towards an energy-sustainable europe.
in writing. - i am pleased that instead of a directive there is a regulation on tyre labelling, as suggested by the european parliament. from november 2012, tyres will be labelled according to their energy efficiency, wet grip and noise emissions. european citizens will have more information to choose the right tyres to reduce fuel costs and help reduce energy consumption, and will be able to make a more environmentally friendly choice, reducing their environmental footprint. on the other hand, labelling will lead to more competition between producers. this labelling has environmental advantages. it should be noted that road transport is responsible for 25% of carbon dioxide emissions in europe. tyres, as they account for 20 to 30% of the energy consumed by vehicles, can play an important role in reducing these emissions. for passenger vehicles, the choice of more efficient tyres can result in a saving of 10% in fuel costs. for these reasons i voted in favour.
in writing. - the express vote is justified, in particular, by the fact that this report contributes to two essential points: the improvement of the information available to enable a choice of tyres that is more environmentally friendly and the fact that, by making this choice, we are contributing to greater energy efficiency, since tyres account for 20% to 30% of the total fuel consumption of vehicles.
in writing. − the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) aims to support workers who have been personally affected by redundancies resulting from the profound changes in international trade. the egf finances in particular job-search assistance, specific retraining, promotion of entrepreneurship, support for self-employment and income supplements for special temporary activities. in the long term, these measures are intended to help these workers find and retain a new job. my country, portugal, has benefited from the egf twice: in 2008, following the 1 549 redundancies in the automotive sector in the lisbon region and the alentejo, and in 2009, following the 1 504 redundancies in 49 textile companies in the north and centre of the country. the crucial role of this fund is clear. however, the question put by mrs berès points out that there is a situation which must be clarified by the european commission. we must prevent the allocation of funds or state aid in one member state from resulting in job losses elsewhere in the eu. i therefore agree with the need to ensure effective coordination of european financial aid, preventing companies from seeking to make a profit by creating and destroying jobs.
in writing. - as i have already said, even before the outbreak of the current financial crisis, which has intensified and aggravated some of the symptoms we have already felt, it was clear that globalisation and the resulting business relocation were having a serious impact on the lives of many people. if we add to this the present distrust of the markets and the retreat from investment, it becomes clear that we are living in a particularly difficult time and that there is an exceptional need to have recourse to mechanisms, also exceptional, to help the unemployed and to promote their reintegration into the labour market. in this respect, the european globalisation adjustment fund has been used on several occasions to mitigate the impact of european workers' exposure to the global market. the cases described in this report by mr böge are again worthy of consideration, although there are some doubts whether they will all really qualify, and there is therefore an advantage in presenting the applications separately in the future. i reiterate my conviction that the european union cannot but work together to promote a more robust, free and creative european market, which generates investment and jobs.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report because the egf (european globalisation adjustment fund) is an instrument for responding to a specific crisis at european level caused by globalisation, and this applies to the present situation. this fund provides individual, specific and time-limited support directly to dismissed workers. it is argued that there is no disproportion in the allocation of this fund per worker as has been found. the esf (european social fund) also supports the european employment strategy and member states' policies on full employment, quality and productivity at work, promotes social inclusion, including access to employment for disadvantaged people, and reduces employment disparities at national, regional and local level. this is a crucial fund for strengthening economic and social cohesion. the present situation calls for a strong, correct and rapid implementation of the esf. it is clear that the egf and the esf have different, complementary objectives and that one cannot replace the other. as an extraordinary measure, the egf must have an autonomous financing, and it is a very serious mistake if the financing of the egf, a short-term measure, is made at the expense of the esf or any other structural fund.
in writing. − the eu is an area of solidarity, and the european globalisation adjustment fund is part of this. these supports are essential for helping the unemployed and the victims of displacement in a globalised context. the number of companies relocating is increasing, taking advantage of the low labour costs in several countries, notably china and india, with harmful effects for countries that respect workers' rights. the egf is intended to help workers who are victims of the relocation of companies and is essential to help them to regain access to a new job in the future. the egf has already been used in the past by other eu countries, notably portugal and spain, and it is now up to belgium and ireland to provide this aid.
in writing. - i welcome the amendments proposed to the rules of procedure following the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. i would like to highlight one of the aspects which i consider to be of the greatest importance as we witness the negotiations of the new agreement which will replace the kyoto protocol in january 2013. with the lisbon treaty, the fight against climate change at international level becomes a specific objective of eu environmental policy. the lisbon treaty introduces support for international action to combat climate change in the list of objectives defining its environmental policy. the lisbon treaty also gives europe new powers in the fields of energy, scientific research and space policy. energy is now a shared competence, paving the way for a common european policy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report by mr david martin on the adaptation of parliament's rules of procedure to the treaty of lisbon, as some of the european parliament's internal rules need to be amended in view of the increased powers resulting from the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, in particular the increase in legislative power, which will allow it to legislate on an equal footing with the governments of the member states on a greater number of matters.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' in the context of the directive on the protection of consumers. i believe that the significant increase in the powers of the european parliament, which calls all members of parliament to new challenges, is an important test of their ability to make proposals and their sense of responsibility. i cannot therefore fail to welcome a change of regiment which will bring the chamber more into line with the provisions of the treaties. i would particularly like to highlight the increasingly important role that national parliaments and the initiatives of the member states are playing in the process of european integration. i hope that the principle of subsidiarity, which is the subject of particular attention by the european legislator, will be increasingly respected and fulfilled by all european decision-makers.
in writing. − with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon on 1 december 2009, the rules of procedure need to be adapted to make them compatible with the new rules and powers of the european parliament. with these amendments to the rules of procedure, the european parliament is preparing for the increased powers it will have with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, with the arrival of 18 new meps, the increase in legislative powers and the new budgetary procedure. future cooperation with national parliaments is also relevant here.
in writing. - the lisbon treaty will bring more speed, legitimacy and democracy to the decision-making process in the european union, which is responsible for measures that affect us as citizens on a daily basis. the european parliament in particular will see its legislative power increased by sharing responsibility equally with the european council on most matters dealt with by the institutions. with the treaty of lisbon, the so-called codecision will become the rule and the ordinary legislative procedure. for my part, as an elected member of parliament, i am aware of the challenge this change brings. this report in particular takes up the work already carried out, and almost completed, in the previous legislature, by adapting the rules of procedure governing parliament's work in the light of the new treaty, which is due to enter into force at the beginning of next month. some of the amendments are purely technical and others relate to updates which parliament has taken the opportunity to put into practice. overall, the report reflects a commitment that satisfies the political family to which i belong, the epp, by incorporating in a balanced way issues such as subsidiarity and proportionality, as well as the strengthening of the european parliament's cooperation with national parliaments. for these reasons i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution, which calls for an ambitious and legally binding international agreement in copenhagen, because i believe that the conclusion of this agreement can lead to a new sustainable paradigm, which stimulates social and economic growth, encourages the development of green technologies as well as renewable energy and energy efficiency, and reduces energy consumption and allows the creation of new jobs. i think that the adoption of this resolution, which explicitly states that the international agreement should be based on the principle of common but differentiated responsibility, with the industrialized countries taking a leading role in reducing their emissions and taking responsibility for providing financial and technical support to the developing countries, is in line with a certain global balance. it is therefore essential that the union takes a leading position in this area in order to safeguard the well-being of future generations.
in writing. - it is essential that a politically binding agreement emerges from the copenhagen conference. it should contain operational elements, which could take effect immediately, and a timetable to allow agreement on a legally binding text during 2010. the agreement must involve all the countries that have signed the convention and it is important that commitments, both in terms of emission reductions and in terms of financing, are clearly identified. while the industrialized countries must take the lead in reducing greenhouse gas emissions, the more economically advanced developing countries also have a role to play, contributing according to their responsibilities and respective capacities. industrialized countries and emerging countries with more advanced economies should be subject to comparable efforts. this is the only way to reduce distortions of international competition. it is also essential to define the architecture of the financing so that it is sustainable in the medium and long term. the financing shall be sourced from the private sector, carbon market and public sector in industrialised countries and more economically advanced developing countries.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the copenhagen conference on climate change because i believe it reflects a good parliamentary commitment to the fundamental aspects which should guide the negotiations on a future international agreement on this subject, especially as regards the issues of adaptation, financing mechanisms or deforestation. i repeat that reaching a legally binding international agreement in copenhagen, which is ambitious and realistic and involves all the parties, is also a matter of social justice.
in writing. - as i have said before, it is essential that a comprehensive, legally binding political agreement on climate change is adopted so as not to put european industry in an anti-competitive situation. the european effort must be directed towards the search for an agreement which requires a joint effort, and not just from the eu. the idea of a tax on international financial transactions as the solution to finance climate change adaptation and mitigation by developing countries is not in my view appropriate, as it will sacrifice the economy (especially in a crisis context like the one we are currently experiencing), trade, wealth creation. the cost of such a tax to society at large (increased tax burden reflected on all taxpayers and consumers) and its impact on the financial market (reduced liquidity and credit flow to firms and households) cannot be ignored. i believe that this is not the way to regulate the market and that other less damaging alternatives to the world economy can be considered.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this motion for a resolution because i believe that the eu must maintain a leading and exemplary role in combating climate change. it should be noted that the eu has gone beyond the kyoto targets. i am in favour of the copenhagen agreement being binding. i have therefore tabled an amendment to parliament's resolution on this subject, calling for an international sanctioning regime to be included in the final text. i believe that the agreement must be global, timetabled and demanding. if we are not ambitious, we will create a small instrument that will be even less effective than the kyoto protocol, which already provides for international sanctions. it is therefore hoped that effective control will be established and that the agreement will incorporate a review clause so that it can be easily updated. i also argue that china and india cannot be dismissed as being responsible when they contribute a large percentage of the world's emissions, while our industries are making tremendous efforts to reduce them. the united states has a strong responsibility for the success of this summit. i hope that the president of the united states, barack obama, will demonstrate that he deserves the nobel peace prize, because the fight against climate change will contribute to the peace and happiness of all peoples!
in writing. − the resolution adopted contains positive aspects such as: the importance of maintaining an international commitment beyond 2012; the need for reduction targets to be in line with the latest scientific data; the call for the us to set binding targets that were declared (but not yet assumed) in the last election campaign; emphasising the historical responsibility of industrialised countries for greenhouse gas emissions; the advocacy of energy efficiency and increased r & d & t activities. however, the preponderance of so-called market solutions and particularly carbon trading is unavoidable. this is a political and ideological option which not only does not guarantee the achievement of the set reduction targets, but is in fact the most serious threat to the achievement of the proclaimed environmental objectives. the experience of the operation of the european transaction scheme since 2005 is illustrative of this. carbon trading aims to market the earth's ability to recycle carbon and thereby regulate the climate. thus, this capacity - which is the guarantee of life on earth as we know it - risks falling into the hands of the same corporations that have been degrading the planet, its natural resources and the climate.
in writing. - in the context of the copenhagen conference, it is very important to achieve an ambitious and legally binding international agreement on climate change. however, it is equally important that everyone should be involved, particularly china, india and brazil, given their considerable economic weight and intense industrial activity, and these countries should also commit themselves to pursuing ambitious goals and objectives, similar to those of other countries, albeit with the help, as far as possible, of other richer and more industrialized countries. it is also important that the united states should play its part in this important matter.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this resolution because it sets priorities in fundamental chapters such as freedom, security and justice, in particular as regards reception and integration conditions for immigrants, the fight against discrimination, in particular on grounds of sexual orientation, access to justice and the fight against corruption and violence. it is essential to combat discrimination, whether on grounds of sex, sexual orientation, age, disability, religion, colour, descent and ethnic or national origin, as well as racism, anti-semitism, xenophobia, homophobia and violence. freedom of movement must also be guaranteed to all eu citizens and their families. in conclusion, the protection of citizens against terrorism and organised crime must also be ensured, and the regulatory framework must therefore be strengthened to deal with these very current threats, given their global dimension.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the european parliament's motion for a resolution on the stockholm programme, as i considered that the proposals contained therein clearly and precisely define the priorities for the coming years in the field of european legislation in the areas of freedom, security and justice, in the light of the implementation of the treaty of lisbon. it is essential to strike a better balance between the safety of citizens and the defence of their individual rights, and i would therefore like to stress the importance of applying the principle of mutual recognition to same-sex couples in the eu, as well as the creation of a european court of information affairs and the adoption of measures guaranteeing new rights for detainees.
in writing. - it has been a common practice in this parliament that the promotion of fracturing issues, which go far beyond the competences of the european union, is done by including them in texts on broader issues which would normally merit widespread support. i cannot but regret the use once again of this stealthy method which only serves to discredit this house and to increase the distance between the elected and the voters. it is well known that family law matters are fortunately within the sphere of action of the member states and it is therefore absolutely illegitimate and a clear breach of the principle of subsidiarity for the european parliament to try to coerce them into a unified understanding of these matters by seeking to enforce radical agendas. the recognition by the european parliament of same-sex unions which are valid in only four member states cannot be imposed on the others and constitutes a gross attempt to influence national legislators and public opinion, which deserves the strongest condemnation. when the charter of fundamental rights was adopted, there were fears that it would be misused in the future and that it would conflict with national legal systems. the present situation confirms that these predictions were correct.
in writing. the european parliament has approved by a majority the main lines of the european commission's proposal for the so-called stockholm programme, which are a strong attack on a central issue of state sovereignty such as justice. the expansion of joint actions in the field of police and judicial cooperation and cooperation between secret services, as well as the introduction of an internal security strategy and new measures for data exchange in the eu, are at the expense of the rights, freedoms and guarantees of all those residing in eu countries. the development of a common migration policy based on the classification of immigrants according to a desirability scale, and in its most repressive aspect the use of frontex, violates their rights and ignores the human drama that is being experienced in many countries. the increasing use of surveillance and control of persons, as well as the practice of profiling, based on data mining techniques and their widespread collection, irrespective of whether citizens are innocent or guilty, for so-called prevention and control purposes, is worrying. the large sums of money funneled to the military-industrial complex and its research activities in the field of internal security are also disturbing.
in writing. - the stockholm programme addresses concerns of enhanced security, in particular in the fight against crossborder crime and terrorism, and respect for citizens' rights. the effort, which also stems from the new reality of the lisbon treaty, would lead to a responsible discussion on the essential issues relating to an area of freedom, security and justice at the service of citizens. unfortunately, some people have tried to contaminate the discussion of a fundamental issue, such as the stockholm programme, with a pointless and irrelevant issue of same-sex marriage, without even respecting the legitimate differences in the internal legal solutions of each country of the european union. whoever did this, for the sake of political tactics, did not care about the fate of the stockholm programme. the vote i have taken reflects the priority given to discussing the needs of this area of freedom, security and justice, at the service of the citizens. the commission has also expressed its disapproval of the strategy of those who wanted to contaminate this debate with a fracturing agenda which had nothing to do with it.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution on the state of play of the euromed free trade area project. despite some progress, i regret that the main objectives of the euro-mediterranean partnership have not been achieved, putting into question their achievement by 2010. it is urgent to ensure that the euro-mediterranean integration process is taken up as a political priority of the eu, as the success of this process and the free trade area can contribute to peace, prosperity and security throughout the region.
in writing. - the mediterranean is the cradle of civilisation as we know it. on its shores, ideas and institutions that define the european civilisation have been born, matured and strengthened, inseparable from its history and its future projects. on their shores, too, deep rifts have been created, often resolved by force of arms, which have led to a painful political separation, the gradual alienation of their populations and the development separate, if not antagonistic, from what was once the centre of the world. the eu, which wants to be open to the world and to promote dialogue between its members and third countries, cannot but cherish the idea of a euro-mediterranean free trade area which would make it possible to further strengthen relations between the two sides of the mediterranean and also promote greater south-south convergence. it must be admitted that the results achieved so far do not correspond to the ambition behind this idea. the obstacles are many and of an economic and financial nature, but it is clear that the main obstacles are of an eminently political nature. we must persist and contribute to the re-establishment of a market on a mediterranean scale, which will bring people into closer contact and re-establish the links that have been broken.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on compensation for passengers in the event of the bankruptcy of an airline, as i consider it necessary to strengthen the protection of european passengers in the event of the bankruptcy of airlines by introducing new legislation or by revising existing legislation and by creating a reserve fund for compensation.
in writing. − commercial airlines have been in a deep crisis since the attacks of 11 september 2001, which has been aggravated by the current economic and financial crisis. the number of bankruptcies and the unpleasant situations of real helplessness in which passengers, many of them in transit, find themselves, is increasing. this lack of consumer protection is truly intolerable and deserves a european response, including the evaluation of airlines, the promotion of aid to passengers who are victims of such situations and compensation for damage and loss. the commission notes that the airport services agreement concluded between ryanair and ams was signed on the same day as the airport services agreement between ryanair and ams.
in writing. − the increasing number of airline bankruptcies affecting thousands of citizens of the member states has led to the need for the eu to take action to defend them. in fact, it is important to safeguard the rights of those who use airplanes as a means of transport on a daily basis. in that sense, the express vote.
in writing. − european regions that excel as tourist destinations must ensure the highest level of service and quality to users of services in this sector. madeira is an example of this, having been ranked last week as one of the world's top tourist destinations by the world tourism organization, which gave the region the highest ranking in 13 out of 15 evaluation parameters. in order to maintain this position in a highly competitive market, the work carried out by public and private entities with environmental, economic and social sustainability criteria must be continued. this objective also includes providing the maximum guarantees to tourists visiting the island with regard to their air travel and accommodation conditions. the resolution voted today goes in that direction, aiming to protect passengers in the event of the bankruptcy of an airline by creating compulsory insurance and guarantee funds for these companies, as well as optional insurance for their customers. the commission is also invited to submit a proposal to compensate passengers of airlines that go bankrupt by ensuring their repatriation in the event of being detained at an airport.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution on origin labelling because it is based on the principle that consumer protection requires transparent and consistent trade rules, including origin labelling. in this respect, i support the commission's intervention, together with the member states, to defend the legitimate rights and expectations of consumers where there is evidence of fraudulent or misleading use of the indication of origin by non-community importers and producers.
in writing. - takes note of the need to ensure that consumers are adequately informed when making a choice about the purchase of certain products, in particular about their country of origin and the relevant safety, hygiene or environmental protection standards applicable to their production; i cannot, however, fail to mention that, when assessing the commission's proposal on origin labelling, i will pay particular attention to the appropriate framework for traditional products, so that the necessary and desirable greater protection of consumers cannot be fatal to small producers of traditional products. i will also pay particular attention to the mechanisms used to define origin, so that this does not become a competitive disadvantage for european producers vis-à-vis their competitors.
in writing. - we believe that the resolution adopted falls far short of what we believe that origin marking should be, namely, among other things, an instrument for the defence of industrial jobs in europe, particularly in small and mediumsized enterprises, but also an instrument against social and environmental dumping. that is why we abstained. moreover, this resolution omits the consequences of the liberalization of world trade for employment and the industrial fabric in the various member states. it is not concerned with the many moves to seek easy profits and their consequences, such as the deindustrialization of vast areas, the increase in unemployment and the deterioration of economic and social conditions. it is a resolution which merely calls on the commission and the council 'to take all necessary measures to ensure a level playing field'. finally, we regret that the majority of the ep has rejected the proposals we put forward, which, among other things, aimed at preserving jobs, respecting workers' and consumers' rights, combating child labour or slavery and the importation of products from occupied territories, and which insisted on the need to withdraw european aid to companies and investors who resort to the relocation of their production.
in writing. - the express vote follows from the fact that the designation 'made in' is essential for the transparency of markets and for the proper information of consumers on the origin of the products they consume. the community economy needs to be strengthened by improving the competitiveness of eu industry in the world economy. we can only have fair competition if it works with clear rules for producers and for exporters and importers, taking into account the common social and environmental conditions.
may this international day for the elimination of violence against women be the beginning of the collapse of the wall of silence and indifference that still exists in our society about this real scourge that affects millions of women in the european union and the world. violence against women is a violation of human rights and an obstacle to their participation in social and political life, public life and work, preventing them from realizing their full rights as citizens, although the various types of violence, as already mentioned, also vary according to cultures and traditions, the economic and social crises of capitalism increase the vulnerability of women, worsening their exploitation and leading to poverty and marginalization, which also feeds trafficking in women and prostitution. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a welcome step towards the creation of a european health insurance system.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would also like to thank the secretariat of the envi committee, the secretariat of my group and my assistant for the support they have given me. they were all excellent. on behalf of the committee on the environment, i would first of all like to express my regret that the swedish presidency has decided to close this dossier without waiting for the european parliament's report. this attitude represents an unacceptable disregard for the elected representatives of the european citizens. that is why i ask you on behalf of the committee on the environment: can the council confirm its intention to adopt conclusions on this issue at the council on 1 december 2009, despite parliament's timetable? why is the council rushing to adopt the recommendation without waiting for the european parliament's position? given that parliament has been consulted on the commission proposal, is the council prepared to take parliament's view into account when drawing up its conclusions? it should be clear that the environment committee supports the aims of the recommendation, as tobacco remains the main cause of death and disease in the european union. the envi committee would therefore like to see its timetable respected, so that a thorough debate on the subject and a position of parliament could be taken. exposure to environmental tobacco smoke, also known as secondhand smoke, is a significant additional source of mortality, morbidity and disability in the european union. secondhand smoke contains over 4,000 gaseous and particulate compounds, including 69 known carcinogens and many toxic agents. there is no safe level of exposure to secondhand smoke. the most conservative estimates point to thousands and thousands and thousands of people dying every year because of secondhand smoke, and this also represents significant burdens on the economy, burdens in direct medical costs and also indirect costs associated with lost productivity. considerable progress has been made in the creation of smoke-free areas in some member states in recent years. to date, more than one third of the member states have adopted comprehensive legislation banning smoking in workplaces and enclosed public spaces. however, significant differences in the level of protection against exposure to tobacco smoke remain within the eu. for example, workers in the hotel and catering sector are a particularly vulnerable occupational group due to the lack of comprehensive protection in most member states and the exceptionally high concentration of tobacco smoke in bars and restaurants. at eu level, the issue of smoke-free spaces has so far been the subject of several non-binding resolutions and recommendations, which do not provide detailed guidelines on how to create smoke-free spaces. the subject is also included in some directives adopted in the field of occupational health and safety, in some cases only indirectly and in others not ensuring a sufficient level of protection. i would remind you of article 8 of the world health organisation's framework convention on tobacco control, which has so far been ratified by 26 member states and the community, which obliges all parties to ensure effective protection against exposure to tobacco smoke in workplaces, enclosed public spaces and public transport. we believe that only a total ban on smoking in enclosed workplaces, including the catering and hotel sector, public buildings and public transport, will ensure the protection of the health of workers and non-smokers and encourage smokers to stop smoking. i would like to conclude by saying that we hope that the council will take account of this resolution, which we hope will be adopted by the european parliament tomorrow.
a writer. - madam president, we support the call on the member states to take account of the strong social arguments in favour of ratifying the conventions which the ilo has considered to be up to date. we know that since 1919 the international labour organization has maintained and developed a system of international labour standards covering a wide range of subjects including work, employment, social security, social policy and related human rights. we therefore also agreed to the first version of the joint resolution presented here. but unfortunately, under pressure from the epp, the other signatories agreed to dilute the importance of the resolution and weaken its scope by including a reference to the lisbon strategy for growth and jobs. we all know that it was in the name of the lisbon strategy that the european commission presented some of the worst proposals against labour and social rights, insisting on flexibility and deregulation of labour. who has forgotten the proposal to amend the working time directive, which aims to devalue work and make it even more precarious, to extend the working day, to call into question collective bargaining and the role of trade unions, exactly the opposite of what is advocated by the ilo conventions? it is mainly because of this regrettable reference to the so-called lisbon strategy that we have withdrawn our support for the resolution. but on behalf of the group of the european united left - nordic green left, we call on the member states to ratify the ilo conventions and we ask the european commission to take them into account in its proposals.
the figures illustrating world hunger and malnutrition, which we all know and see repeatedly, are so dramatic that it is almost obscene to mention them. the right to food is associated with the most sacred of all rights. the right to life, understood as having a minimum of dignity, and not as a mere struggle for survival. the fight against world hunger should therefore be the top priority of all political agendas and all resources should be mobilised to achieve this objective. unfortunately, we also all know that resources, including financial resources, are not always the main limitation. the problem is almost always in their management and use and in the absence of sound governance and effective coordination at global, regional and national levels. the draft resolution which is before us today - and which my group, the socialists and democrats, is one of the signatories to - contains proposals and recommendations which, if followed, can certainly make a major contribution to alleviating such a serious problem. i therefore urge the commission to give it due attention and, on the basis of it, to put forward legislative proposals and to adopt procedures to give concrete expression to them. the political time of difficulties and uncertainty we are living in is also a time of change in the policies which are the best instruments of intervention of the european union in this matter: the common agricultural policy and the common fisheries policy, whose profound reforms we are now going to start. with the new powers that the lisbon treaty gives us, this is also a great opportunity for the european parliament to move from proclamations to concrete action. the european socialists are motivated by this challenge. we hope that the new commission and the other political groups will be available to accompany us in this mission.
madam president, the final declaration adopted at the last fao summit by its 193 member countries is unfortunately a real empty dish in the fight against hunger. there was no deadline and, above all, no definition of the concrete means and conditions for combating a scourge that affects more than six billion people. according to the data available, during the scant 90 seconds that this intervention will last, 15 children will die of hunger in the world. this is the most vivid and forceful libel against an unjust, exploitative, irrational and, for that very reason, historically condemned economic system. a system based on concrete policies and guidelines, and, by the way, mr louis michel, on protagonists and liberal rhetoric, which have led to the present situation: the promotion of the agro-industrial model, in line with the defence of the interests of the large agro-food industry, and the consequent qualitative impoverishment of the world agricultural sector. years and years of underinvestment in agriculture, of encouraging the abandonment of the agricultural sector, of the liquidation of small and mediumsized farms, a sector which provides the livelihood of 70% of the world's poor. market fundamentalism, privatisation and liberalisation policies and free trade have had and have the effect of abandoning the land, concentrating ownership and domination of production by a few and food dependency by the many. experts estimate that $44 million would be needed to end the scourge of chronic malnutrition. this is a much smaller sum than the transfer made by the states to big capital in order to save it from the systemic crisis which is now under way.
madam president, there is a great irony here, isn't there? what google has done, taking intellectual property, reproducing it for its own use, and at first clearly illegal, according to the american court's own findings, is what, on a small scale, we call piracy. why should it be any different when it's done by the individual user and when it's done by a huge company like google? so this must be our first principle: there must be no privilege for the great. the second principle is that we cannot depend on the goodwill of a monopoly. like many people here, i find google an interesting company. i think their idea is innovative and it's a good idea. but what if they later raise prices, as do, for example, the publishers of academic journals, who raise prices hundreds of times? what if they restrict access to certain books? they have a 15% quota for objectionable books. we need a true world digital library, in which a global consortium has the power of initiative, with universities controlling the quality that is not being controlled, with national libraries, and in which, of course, europe has a much greater power of leadership and initiative than it has now. it started with books, then we'll move on to art in european museums, and europe cannot miss this train.
in writing. − i voted in favour of the scurria framework report on the european year of volunteering (2011) for advocating greater support from the eu institutions for this sector, which involves millions of europeans and is essential for promoting solidarity and social inclusion. i believe that an increase in the budget and coordination at community level is necessary to enable the initiatives under the european year of volunteering to be strengthened in terms of awareness campaigns and the transnational exchange of ideas and good practice.
in writing. - volunteering is, in its simplest definition, goodwill in action. it's free, it's generous, it's free and it's uncommitted. it is also a fundamental pillar of any society, with the work of thousands of volunteers, young and old, acting formally or informally in the areas of health, care, education, environment or culture, making a difference in thousands of lives every day. the initiative to establish a european year of volunteering is therefore to be welcomed, in which the right emphasis should be given to these anonymous faces, the volunteers, making us all aware of their immense work and seeking to create more favourable conditions for them to develop their activities. this is in line with the cds, the first and only portuguese political party to look at the issue of volunteering and to make concrete proposals to help volunteers, giving them the dignity and recognition they deserve.
in writing. - we vote in favour of this report, despite some contradictions and partial aspects of it which we do not follow. volunteering undoubtedly plays an important role in society, promoting the values of solidarity and mutual aid and contributing, among other things, to social integration and the dissipation of feelings of discrimination. the report addresses key issues such as the social protection of the volunteer, focusing on issues such as health, safety and training, and the distinction between paid employment and voluntary activities. we believe, however, that it is necessary to ensure that volunteering does not replace state action and that it should not be used as a means of meeting needs in the field of social services. we are calling for the promotion of the activities of non-profit organizations, including many others, such as the cooperative movement, local authorities and societies, residents' committees, sports, recreation, cultural, youth and child-oriented associations, with effective and appropriate support. it is important to stress that voluntary work also depends on workers having free time, and that it is incompatible with exploitative situations, irregular or excessive working hours, low wages and precarious employment.
in writing. - despite all the criticisms that can be made, reality clearly demonstrates the enormous desire of many states to join the european union. the brutally disturbing histories of several of them, particularly in the balkans, have served to strengthen the conviction of the candidate countries that under the protective umbrella of the european union they would be free both from the expansionist tendencies of their neighbours and from russian influence. in the face of the countries listed, it is relatively easy to see that there is no equality between the states in their desire and conditions to join the european union. of this group, iceland stands out, of course, as its democratic traditions, the high living standards of its people and respect for the acquis communautaire put it at the forefront of the accession process. i am alert to the need to comply scrupulously with the copenhagen criteria and to fulfil the commitments arising from them, and the european union should not, out of unreasonable obstinacy, evade welcoming those who are actually prepared to do so.
in writing. − this resolution is another example where, on the subject of enlargement and on a communication published by the european commission entitled enlargement strategy and key challenges for 2009-2010, the majority of the european parliament wants to move forward with the recognition of kosovo, even stating that it 'welcomes the commission's intention to strengthen relations with kosovo, including exploring the possibility of kosovo participating in community programmes'. it thus encourages the future accession of a territory which has declared itself independent in flagrant violation of international law, claiming that it is a state which is the product of an illegal war, a state which operates on the basis of an illegal status which is not recognised by the un. the question of turkey is also not being dealt with properly, as it continues to occupy a member state of the european union - the northern part of cyprus - and does not respect the rights of the kurdish people as it should. so, although we believe that the question of enlargement of the european union is first and foremost a decision of the people of each country wishing to join, we voted against the report because of the way it is drafted and because of the negative aspects of the strategy it proposes to pursue, which, however, have no legislative value.
in writing. − the eu enlargement process must always be considered and any new accession must always respect common reference factors of the eu countries. i believe that turkey's accession should warrant a preliminary debate, which is to say that there are doubts which at least warrant such a debate. the european union is not a political union, but a political union, and it is not a political union, but a political union. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. − the international day for the elimination of violence against women is an initiative of the un and the council of europe to raise awareness and raise awareness of victims of domestic violence and other forms of maltreatment. the situation in portugal is worrying in this area. the number of domestic violence crimes recorded by the apav increased by 9% compared to the same period in 2008. according to apav, physical and psychological abuse, threats and sexual crimes have increased the most since 2008. this year in portugal, 26 women have already died as victims of domestic violence. yet the vast majority of acts of violence remain unreported out of fear and shame. the eu must step up its action in this area. i agree with the need to encourage member states to develop national action plans to combat violence against women. we support all initiatives that can help to change mentalities, agreeing to the organisation of a european year to combat violence against women in order to denounce and alert the authorities and society to this worrying situation.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the elimination of violence against women, as i consider it necessary for the commission and the council to step up eu action in this area. it is urgent to equip the eu with a more comprehensive policy to combat violence against women, including through the preparation by the commission of a proposal for a directive, which will ensure a clear legal basis to combat all forms of violence against women, including trafficking. it is worth noting and welcoming the fact that the spanish presidency has made this a priority issue in its action programme.
in writing. − in a week in which the shocking numbers of cases of domestic violence in portugal have again made the news, i believe that violence against women and children is a subject that deserves serious reflection and action by governments. i condemn all violence, but especially that which is directed against those who are most vulnerable - socially, economically or emotionally - such as children and, often, women. states should therefore seek to eliminate all forms of violence against women and children, including trafficking for sexual exploitation, violations of their sexual self-determination and domestic violence. respect for human life and dignity is incompatible with the crimes of which many women and children in europe are victims, which is why strong policies to prevent violence and punish offenders are warranted.
in writing. − on the international day for the elimination of violence against women, we want to highlight this serious social, economic and political problem which challenges women's rights in a wide range of areas: at work, in the family, in society in general. violence against women is a violation of human rights and an obstacle to their participation in social and political life, public life and work, preventing them from fulfilling their role as full citizens. although the different types of violence also vary according to cultures and traditions, the economic and social crises of capitalism increase the vulnerability of women, both collectively and individually, exacerbating their exploitation and leading to poverty and marginalization, which also fuels trafficking in women and prostitution. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a welcome step towards the creation of a european health insurance system.
in writing. - when we talk about violence against women, we are faced with a reality that is very evident in all the member states, as one in four women is a victim of violence. in an eu which wants to defend the rights and freedoms of all its citizens, we must make every effort to put an end to this scourge. the adoption of this motion for a resolution is a very important step in that direction towards finding new solutions, hence the meaning of my vote.
in writing. - somalia is one of the most flagrant cases of the total disintegration of central power and its retreat into a tribal and belligerent reality, and is a hotbed of violence and instability that goes far beyond its borders. its coast has been under constant threat from armed groups who not only compete for control but also carry out intolerable acts of piracy against other vessels, including commercial, cargo and fishing vessels, humanitarian aid vessels and recreational vessels. these events, because of their gravity and frequency, call for a firm reaction on the part of the entire international community, to which the european union cannot remain indifferent. the european union must be committed to combating piracy and do everything in its power not only to analyse its causes and consequences but also to mobilise all the forces, somali and international, willing and able to deal with it. i cannot therefore fail to greet, in the person of its commander, the entire garrison of the portuguese frigate corte-real, which has distinguished itself in the fight against this scourge and has recently been honoured by the international maritime organisation.
in writing. - in all this somalia problem it is necessary to reaffirm that there can be no military solution to the crisis there and that the lack of financial resources in the country, caused by the international debt crisis, which has created a vacuum, which has been exploited for illegal fishing in its territorial waters, being one of the main reasons for somali fishermen losing their livelihoods, since the government of somalia was forced to suspend its coast guard for lack of resources. one of the key issues is therefore to ensure technical and financial support, including support for a process of conciliation and mediation between the parties to the civil war. the commission and the council should therefore review their political strategy for somalia, including operation eunavfor-atalanta, and should focus on the situation in the country in general, especially the need to address the humanitarian situation on the ground and to help to eradicate the real causes of the disastrous situation that is causing suffering to millions of somalis. finally, we reaffirm that the resources earmarked for development aid, i.e. the edf, must not be used for any military purpose.
in writing. - what is happening off the coast of somalia is reprehensible on every level and affects all countries in a transversal way. i therefore consider it very important that, as long as there is no political solution to the problem in somalia and as long as instability persists in that area, the security strategy implemented by operation atalanta should be continued or even strengthened as regards the means of action of the forces used in that operation.
in writing. - voting in line with the ppe, i cannot, however, fail to express some of the concerns which this motion for a resolution raises for me. firstly, i believe that tobacco policies should be decided by the states and that the role of the european institutions, in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, should therefore remain at the level of non-binding recommendations. on the other hand, i also believe that states should promote anti-smoking policies but should not impose any restrictions on the freedom of choice of operators, especially in the hotel sector, where owners of establishments should have the choice to be smoke-free or not. in this respect, the law recently passed in portugal has proved to be balanced. my second concern is the proposed end to direct subsidies linked to tobacco production. as portugal is also a tobacco producer, i believe that this type of policy should be examined with extreme caution. otherwise, it may cause serious damage to farmers who are unable to continue their production without being given any viable alternative. that is my view on paragraph 9 of this resolution.
in writing. - the protection of human health, the quality of life of workers in their workplaces and of the general population is at the heart of this resolution. that is why we voted in favour. it is necessary to increase the protection of non-smokers by avoiding passive exposure, but also to create the necessary conditions for monitoring and encouraging cessation of tobacco consumption. the prohibition policy should act in situations where it is indispensable. as regards direct subsidies linked to production, we support incentives for the conversion of tobacco production. we believe, however, that conditions should not be created to promote tobacco imports from outside the eu, thereby benefiting the big business of multinational tobacco companies.
in writing. i voted in favour of deleting paragraph 13 of the original text, which i transcribe as follows: 'invites the commission to submit to parliament and the council by 2011 a proposal for a legislative act on the prohibition of smoking in all indoor workplaces, including all indoor public buildings and public transport in the eu, for the protection of workers' health.' the deletion was approved in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity in this area. i believe that there is still a long way to go in each member state in creating smoke-free spaces, promoting effective awareness raising and implementing best practices for the implementation of article 14 (demand reduction measures for tobacco dependence and cessation). but i believe that these actions must be taken first of all by each state. the original text of the resolution confused tobacco production with consumption. i therefore welcome the adoption of the epp amendment which deletes part of paragraph 9 of the original text, thus eliminating the references to tobacco production. these two amendments improved the final resolution, the remaining points of which i regard as positive and therefore voted in favour.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution on the fao world summit on food security - ending hunger on earth, because i believe that urgent action is needed to put an end to this scourge, which affects one sixth of the world's population. in view of the impacts of climate change on agriculture, in particular the decrease in productivity due to water scarcity, especially in countries already facing these problems, it is important to ensure the development of agricultural policies consistent with climate protection and the fight against hunger.
in writing. - knowing that more than 40 million people die of hunger every year and that every six seconds a child dies of malnutrition, none of us can remain indifferent to this global problem. the european union is the largest donor of development aid and humanitarian aid, but only a small part of that aid is channelled into the agricultural sector, which could meet the food needs of thousands of people who continue to suffer from malnutrition. it is therefore urgent that the european union should reflect on its development support policies and give greater prominence to support for agriculture in developing countries, a sector which is the source of income for over 70% of the working population. at the internal level too, the cap must adapt to the current crisis, with rising production costs for european farmers, by refraining from dismantling market support measures and/or reducing agricultural subsidies, paying particular attention to supporting small and mediumsized farmers and their access to credit, so that they can maintain their production despite the escalation in the price of the factors of production.
in writing. - by stressing that the fight against hunger must be rooted in the recognition of the right to food sovereignty; - by recognising the right of the national population to control agricultural land and other natural resources of vital importance to their food security. the resolution also calls attention to the importance of agriculture in the fight against hunger and stresses the importance of farmers' incomes. it remains to be seen whether this parliament will be consistent in the future with what it has adopted today. or if, as often happens, it makes just considerations and then, when it is necessary to implement them, it goes back on its word and passes legislation in violation of those principles. it is important to remember that the successive reforms of the cap, which were made in response to the liberalisation of agricultural markets under the wto and which were designed to serve the interests of the large agro-food companies, have contributed to the impoverishment of the world agricultural sector. agricultural production must be used first and foremost to feed the population, not to favour export monocultures.
in writing. - the phenomenon of hunger is a scourge that affects more and more people. as a space of solidarity, the eu must be at the forefront of tackling the problem it is creating. all european and global food security policies must be stepped up, as the global food crisis is one of the major threats to peace and security in the world.
