i am not saying that the european parliament should be the only institution in the world to have a right to vote. it was urgent to discuss here the recent tragedy caused by hurricane ida as it passed through el salvador in early november. it has caused more than 200 deaths and disappearances, destroyed essential infrastructure and equipment, particularly in the areas of health, education, water and sanitation, and exacerbated poverty in the country. it was urgent to be here to discuss the provision of extraordinary funds and the reorientation of available funds from the european union to the emergency situation and to start the reconstruction and risk reduction plan and to support the population of el salvador. it was urgent that this european parliament condemn the military coup in honduras and demand the return of power to president zelaya, who was legally elected by the honduran people. it was urgent to demand respect for the fundamental rights of the people of western sahara. unfortunately, none of this has been possible because of the opposition of the epp. so, as my colleagues have already said, we will not cooperate in this false urgency, which is a disgrace to the european parliament.
on behalf of the s&d group. in china, all those who seek to exercise basic labour, social, civil and political rights continue to be repressed. the repression is most cruel and indiscriminate when it is directed against ethnic minorities such as the uighurs and tibetans, but the han are not spared either, as is the case with the sakharov prize hu jia and the lawyers and activists who are now being persecuted following president obama's visit to beijing. the administrative detention of hundreds of thousands of people, systematic torture in prisons, religious and political repression, executions - all these are methods incompatible with the obligations of the people's republic of china as a member of the united nations. and these are ineffective methods, which will not be able to silence or restrain those who fight for freedom and human rights. in china, as in the rest of the world, because the chinese are not martians, they are no different from the rest of humanity.
i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community. for the reasons we also explained during our speech, we will not be taking part in this debate... in this vote. we have participated in the debate, but we are not going to participate in this vote, which is a false urgency, as we have already explained.
mr president, i bring to the house today the question of the taxation of portuguese farmers in the form of vat. under community law there are three possible schemes: the general scheme, with the organized accounting; the option of the small and medium-sized enterprises scheme; or the flat-rate scheme, where there is no refund but there is a right to compensation. unfortunately, since portugal's accession to the community, this compensation right has been 0%. at the moment there is an infringement procedure against the portuguese state. this taxation covers 18 000 farmers, accounting for approximately 5.3% of their sales. i want to make one point very clear here so that it is possible for national legislation to be in line with the acquis communautaire.
over the years and in the successive changes to the common agricultural policy we have warned of its consequences and presented alternatives. unfortunately, they did not want to listen to us and today we are experiencing a disaster situation, as is the case in portuguese agriculture. we have warned of the consequences of liberalizing international trade in agri-food products. we have said that agriculture cannot be treated as an industrial commodity and should be excluded from the negotiations of the world trade organization in order to avoid its being subjected to stock market and financial speculation. we have always said that food sovereignty and food security should be given priority and that those who work the land should be valued, and that is why we were against the withdrawal of production aid and the end of milk quotas. we have always said that there are specific features of the agriculture of the southern countries of the european union which must be respected in order to safeguard their production of high-quality foodstuffs such as wine, olive oil, fruit and vegetables, rice, but also meat and milk production under conditions which are very different from those in other areas. we therefore insist on the revision of these policies to take account of the high prices of the factors of production of gas oil, electricity, fertiliser, feed, credit and insurance, and we are intervening in defence of farmers with policies which support those who produce and create jobs in rural areas. it is time to give priority to family farming and the increasingly deserted countryside, to regional agricultural production and to native species. farmers deserve it, because they produce what is essential for our food.
i would like to congratulate you once again, madam commissioner, on your presence in this chamber - which has been a constant throughout your entire term of office - even though the commission is in a transitional phase. but the problems of agriculture are not subject to the vagaries of the political calendar, and farmers expect answers from us to their problems, their difficulties and their aspirations. as we all know, the economic crisis has also hit agriculture, not just the milk sector - as the media might make us believe - but other sectors as well. milk, fruit and vegetables, cereals and olive oil have been hit hard in recent months, as has meat production, with the tremendous rise in the cost of production factors that has now occurred. the situation for fruit and vegetables is likely to worsen further as a result of the extension of the market opening to moroccan products under the new agreement which is about to be approved. it is true, as the commissioner has said, that in recent weeks the markets have shown positive signs, but this should not give us too much enthusiasm, because if the recovery comes, as we all hope, it will surely bring with it an increase in the cost of oil and with it the inevitable increase in the cost of production in agriculture. that is why, commissioner, the european parliament, despite the political timetables, cannot but demand answers from the commission to these questions. farmers are waiting for political signals from parliament and the commission and so it makes perfect sense that we have brought this agenda here today, hoping that the commission can give some signals, indicate the information it has and the measures it proposes in the short term to mitigate these problems.
in writing. the economic and financial crisis has also dragged us into an agricultural and social crisis, and i welcome this debate in this parliament, even though it is a bit late. producers (cereals, pork, olive oil, fruit, vegetables, etc.) are experiencing extremely difficult conditions at this time, resulting from the collapse in demand, obtaining prices for their products which are very low compared with the cost of their production. there are also difficulties in obtaining credit. these issues are affecting your income a lot. price volatility in agriculture is a very complex issue, affecting the planning and predictability needed in agriculture, and decisively affecting profitability and the level of investment. it is therefore essential to ensure that farms are sustainable and that prices are stable, so that producers can maintain their activity by improving the quality of their products, with a positive impact on the whole chain from producer to consumer.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject of the protection of the environment. although the doha round, or the doha development agenda, was not on the official agenda of this ministerial conference, the fact is that a large majority of the official delegations from the member countries of the world trade organization took the opportunity to comment on the issue and to declare their desire to conclude the doha round by the end of 2010. however, it is public knowledge that no recent progress has been made in the negotiations. i have therefore the honour, on behalf of the committee on international trade, of which i am chairman, to put the following questions to the commission: firstly, can the commission give us an assessment of the situation with regard to the main areas of negotiation on the doha round, in particular in the area of agriculture, the so-called nama, i.e. market access for non-agricultural products and services? what are the main developments recorded by the commission at the 7th ministerial conference of the world trade organization? what do you think are the issues that remain to be discussed? and what are the most difficult issues in the negotiations? secondly, what impact could the wto ministerial conference have on the ongoing doha round negotiations? how does the commission intend to ensure that the negotiations actually focus on development? will the commission also present an assessment of the likelihood of the doha development round being successfully concluded by the end of next year? third and last question: how does the commission intend to include the members of the european parliament delegation in the ongoing doha round negotiations and in the world trade organisation structure? the question is put on behalf of the committee on international trade, and i would like to conclude by emphasizing that this parliamentary committee attaches great importance - in the exercise of the new responsibilities and powers conferred on it by the treaty of lisbon - to establishing more fruitful and deeper cooperation with the commission in the field of international trade. but this presupposes, of course, that the commission is fully prepared to cooperate with the european parliament, starting with the answers to the questions we put to it.
it is time to change the priorities in international trade and to reject free trade for the negative contribution it has made to the financial, economic, food and social crisis that we are living with the increase in unemployment and poverty, serving only the interests of the richest countries and economic and financial groups. a fundamental change in the negotiations is needed to give priority to development and social progress, to creating jobs with rights, to combating hunger and poverty, which means ending tax havens, investing in food sovereignty and food security, supporting quality public services and respecting the right of governments to preserve their economy and public services, particularly in the areas of health, education, water, culture, communications and energy.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group. mr president, we will vote in support of the workers made redundant in sweden, austria and the netherlands. but each request for the mobilisation of this fund increases my perplexity as to its fairness and effectiveness. first, efficiency: in 2009, the fund supported 16,000 workers, a drop in the bucket in a sea of redundancies, and spent 53 million of the possible 500 million. then justice: why is a swedish car worker supported by 6500 euros and an austrian by 14300? how do i explain this to a portuguese textile worker whose support was 524 euros, when her catalan counterpart could receive 2000? worse still, why were the fixedterm workers excluded from any support in the dutch application when they are the most vulnerable? this fund cannot continue to serve to widen the gaps in unemployment or to accentuate the inequalities between rich and peripheral countries.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i congratulate my fellow rapporteur on her work on this report and on her speech a short while ago. this new microfinance facility will allow micro-credit to be granted to small businesses and to people who have lost their jobs and want to set up their own business and their own workplace. this is very important at a time when the economic crisis is expected to result in the loss of 3.5 million jobs in the european union alone. as a consequence of the economic slowdown, banks have stopped lending for jobs and businesses and access to credit is more difficult at a time when it should be more affordable. this new form of microfinance will counteract the current trend towards restricting access to credit by making it easier to obtain the funds needed to launch new businesses and create new jobs. the european commission's proposal is to reallocate eur 100 million from the progress programme budget to this financing instrument. we cannot agree to this proposal. the financial and economic crisis is also a social crisis. so reallocating resources from progress to the most vulnerable groups is not the most appropriate solution. the creation of a separate budget line to finance this instrument and an increase in the allocation to eur 150 million are therefore supported. we also agree with the need to clarify in the legislation itself that the target group is vulnerable groups who have difficulty entering or re-entering the labour market and who face the threat of social exclusion. therefore, the reference to specific groups should be deleted. finally, we stress that it is essential that those receiving financial support also have appropriate training.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, this budget is a response to the economic crisis, with the european citizens at its core. so this is a budget that will serve to combat unemployment and to revive the economy. we therefore give priority to the plan to revive the european economy, which has a budget of eur 2.4 billion for next year. we also support the economic recovery by increasing the payment appropriations for the main budget lines. i would also like to stress the proposal to revise the erasmus programme in the fight against unemployment and youth unemployment. we welcome the granting of additional support to the milk sector of eur 300 million, but i insist that a budget line should be created to establish a permanent fund for this sector. we also consider it of great importance that the current multiannual financial framework 2007-2013 be revised and extended for the financial years 2015-2016 as we propose. i would remind you that the fight against climate change, together with energy security, are priorities of the european union which are not sufficiently reflected in this budget. the budget for parliament and the other institutions, which is now being proposed for second reading, is the same as that which we had already adopted at first reading. the pp reaffirms the priority of rigor and transparency and therefore advocates a long-term real estate policy, the implementation of a zero-based budget at the beginning of each legislature and the cost/benefit analysis of policies such as, for example, the parliament's communication. as for the new requirements, with the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, we are in favour of legislative excellence and the need for the means necessary to achieve this objective.
i do not think we can talk about trees in this budget without mentioning the forest. and the question i ask you, quite frankly, is this: without the crisis, would this budget be very different from what it is? we all know it's not. and this is the decisive question. that is why the report we are voting on thursday itself acknowledges that the council is not prepared to increase funding to deal with the crisis, that it is reducing the appropriations for the structural and cohesion funds at a time when the crisis is present and severe, and that it does not sufficiently reflect, i repeat, the priority of climate change. the report itself makes further criticisms, such as those made by jutta haug: that there is a huge disparity between the level of authorized expenditure and payments, that it is below the limits set and, fundamentally, that we do not even know whether what is being spent is being spent well. in the face of such a severe examination, how can this house vote on such a budget? there is only one explanation: parliament is the weak link in the budgetary authority. even children know that the government is in charge and the state is in charge. ladies and gentlemen, today we are also talking about the future, because within a year europeans will be faced with the biggest budgetary adjustment programme in the history of each of our countries. it is very simple for the ordinary citizen: after the crisis, again the crisis, now in the name of the cleanup of public accounts. it is irresponsible to pursue this policy while maintaining a zero deficit in the european budget. the difficulties cannot continue to be borne by the same people as always, by the unemployed, by the precarious and the precarious, or by the reduction of pensions. the union will only be part of the problem when it starts sending invoices to the right addresses. without closing offshore, without taxing financial transactions, without issuing bonds, we will not be able to attack the crisis with what is the only answer that can end it: social justice. so they're counting on the left for a robust, radical, redistributive overhaul of the financial perspective to 2016, not counting on the left to make up a mediocre budget that doesn't respond to the social urgency or the boldness we need to combat climate change.
mr president, i regret that in the midst of the economic and social crisis, with its dramatic effects on employment and the living conditions of the population, this draft community budget for 2010 falls far short of what was needed and, for example, does not even take into account either the financial perspective which pointed to 1.1% of community gross national income or the proposal itself presented and adopted at first reading in this budget, which was 1.08% of community gross national income. this proposal, which is now before the house, continues a budget which privileges and finances the neoliberal and militarist orientations of the european union. it is the first budget of the lisbon treaty to be devoted to the detriment of the so-called economic and social cohesion, which, moreover, has its share here reduced, since in total payments the cut in cohesion expenditure is some 2.5 billion compared with what was approved at first reading. for all these reasons, we cannot agree to this budget proposal which is more than eur 11 billion below the amount provided for in the multiannual financial framework for 2010.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the creation of this microfinance instrument for employment, as it aims to give the unemployed opportunities and encourage entrepreneurship. this instrument, which is aimed at people who want to start or develop a micro-enterprise (less than 10 employees and a turnover of less than eur 2 million), will open up new horizons for many unemployed people. in the current economic environment, this is an important programme which would have been beneficial if it had been launched earlier, as the difficulties in accessing credit have long been felt. as this instrument is intended for public and private entities established at national, regional and local level in the eu member states that provide microcredit to individuals and micro-enterprises, it should be closely monitored by the competent bodies to ensure that credit is effectively getting to where it is needed most and that situations similar to those experienced during the last financial crisis are not repeated.
in writing. − as europe is going through a serious economic and financial crisis, which has led to serious social problems, with unemployment rising in all member states, it is important that the eu establishes effective mechanisms to combat the crisis and also to support those most affected, such as unemployed people and companies in difficulty. i therefore believe that it is right to focus on a microfinance instrument for employment which focuses on entrepreneurship and the possibility for each individual to create his or her own job. european microfinance will be used to help those who are denied or hindered in accessing bank credit by supporting the growth of micro-enterprises with mentoring, coaching and interest rate subsidies granted by the esf. i also think it is important to stress that this instrument should serve the sole purpose of supporting entrepreneurship as a way of creating new jobs, making sound business ideas viable, and should not be used for social support or consumption.
in writing. − in the current context of economic crisis and rising unemployment, it is important to promote the growth of self-employment and the formation and development of micro-enterprises. microcredit will help to achieve this. i therefore welcome the commission's proposal and parliament's work on this matter. this encourages entrepreneurship and increases employment and social inclusion. this microfinance facility is complementary to other programmes to support employment and inclusion. i therefore consider it important that the financing of this new instrument should not be at the expense of existing programmes or call them into question. i had already voted in favour of this instrument in the committee on budgets in the context of the 2010 budgetary procedure, which created two new budget lines, one for operational expenditure of eur 37.5 million and the other for administrative expenditure of eur 0.25 million. i hope that this programme will be put into operation quickly and that the approval process for applications will be expeditious and unbureaucratic.
in writing. - we regret that once again the majority of this parliament has 'taken the unspoken word for the unspoken' and approved a proposal to withdraw money from the progress programme, which was already manifestly low in view of its areas of intervention in the social field. regardless of the considerations regarding the objectives of the new european microfinance instrument, it is not acceptable that it be financed at the expense of the reduction of funds from existing programmes, in this case a community programme in the field of employment, social inclusion and equal rights, the progress programme. the text adopted by the committee on employment and social affairs clearly rejected the financing of this instrument from progress and proposed the creation of a new budget line with own resources, that is, with 'new' money. the amendments tabled by our group, which have now been rejected, were also in this direction. in view of the worsening social conditions in the various member states, it is unacceptable that funding from the employment and social inclusion area is being reduced to channel it to other priorities that have been defined in the meantime, even in the area of microfinance.
in writing. − in a time of crisis, when credit is scarce and access to it is difficult, this type of instrument is essential to help provide micro-credit to small businesses and people who, having lost their jobs, wish to start a new activity on their own. without such a support mechanism, it is difficult to achieve these objectives. moreover, in the current crisis, the bank is not sensitive to the credit needs of start-up companies, which in most cases makes their emergence unfeasible. with the prospect of the loss of some several million more jobs in the eu, it is important that there are incentives that these unemployed can access in an attempt to rebuild their lives, avoiding social crises and reducing state expenditure, with social support measures.
mr president, i am afraid the portuguese interpretation is not working. i have not heard one single word from the previous speaker. something must be done; otherwise, i cannot follow the debate.
on behalf of the epp group. the strengthening of the european parliament's powers was one of the main points made by various political leaders in their speeches on the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. the president of the european commission, mr barroso, for example, said the following in strasbourg on 25 november, in preparation for the european council: 'now the lisbon treaty gives us a new opportunity to move forward. we all know that freedom, security and justice will see some of the most significant changes in the treaty and in particular will increase the democratic method of these policies by the full involvement of your parliament'. i stress 'the full involvement of your parliament'. the president of the council, mr van rompuy, also said, in his first official speech, that the treaty is a powerful tool for meeting the challenges of our time. if that is the case, then it would make no sense to emphasize in the official speech the strengthening of our powers and our competences, but then to interpret the treaty of lisbon in a restrictive way, so as to deprive the european parliament of prerogatives which it previously had and which it would make no sense to lose. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. there is also a practical consequence which stems from the old maxim of who can do the most, can do the least and it has to do with this: what sense does it make for someone who has competence in criminal matters and the prevention and combating of terrorist attacks, and who is involved in a codecision procedure, to be excluded injunctionally if other measures are involved which, by affecting citizens' rights, may even be important in this context? and so, mr president, in conclusion, it is essential that the normative interpretation of the treaty of lisbon corresponds in reality to the stated strengthening of the powers and competences of the european parliament. and at least in some cases, as raised in the question, providing for the possibility of a dual legal basis where citizens' rights are at stake and counter-terrorism policies are threatened. and in other cases, such as zimbabwe and somalia, the possibility of optional consultation, as enshrined in the stuttgart declaration on european union, was also raised. that is the position i wanted to express here, mr president.
i would like to return to the subject which has been raised by several colleagues, and particularly in the recent speeches by mr papanikolaou and mr luhan. let us break it down first into parts: the proposals for zimbabwe and somalia. i recognize that we are faced with the application of sanctions imposed by the united nations as such, clearly in the context of the union's external action. in principle, article 215 seems to be the most appropriate. however, according to this article, any acts adopted under it must contain the necessary provisions on legal guarantees, which is not covered by any of the proposals. these initiatives are the sole initiative of the commission and not a joint initiative of the commission and the high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. it is therefore clear that these proposals do not fulfil the necessary conditions for adoption on the basis of article 215. as regards the third proposal, concerning persons and entities associated with osama bin laden, the al-qaida network or the taliban, as mr luhan has just said, it is not possible to support the idea that we are in the framework of the union's external action. since the prevention and combating of terrorism is one of the main priorities of the european union's internal action, this objective is explicitly contained in article 75 of the lisbon treaty. this should therefore be the most appropriate legal basis on the basis of the ordinary legislative procedure. we are now starting to apply the treaty of lisbon, minister malmström. does the council intend to show its good will to start our international relations on the right foot by the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon? or do you intend to do a restrictive analysis? i think we'd all like to get off to a good start.
the european union is a party to the treaty of rome, and it is a party to the treaty of rome. the european institutions are always asking for the confidence of their citizens. during the lisbon treaty process, for better or for worse, they asked for the citizens' trust. the financial crisis started, they asked the citizens for confidence. well, trust is a two-way street and it is not possible to be always asking for the citizens' trust and at the same time not to trust the citizens to show them all the processes, all the internal mechanics that run the commission. it is not possible for the rulers to say that the citizens do not trust the politicians and then the politicians themselves do not trust the citizens. this is a problem of democracy and, in addition to a problem of democracy, it is also a problem of waste. society is itself a vast repository of knowledge which we will be wasting if we do not involve citizens in the process of leadership, of governance of the european union. i have no doubt, therefore, that i support the efforts of the rapporteur, that he is doing a good service to democracy and that we must move not only towards transparency but above all towards ensuring that transparency is not just a pretty word, that it has a concrete meaning.
mr president, i would like to begin by congratulating the promoters of this debate because it is very important that the situation of countries which are economically weaker and of countries which are outside the euro zone is discussed. i am not saying that the european union should not be involved in the negotiations, but that it should be involved in the negotiations. and if the situation is difficult in many eastern european economies and in many central european economies, it is also beginning to be difficult in many western european economies that are within the euro area. i am speaking, for example, of my own country, portugal. the strategy has to take account of the various situations which differ between the states. there are many ways. it is very important that monetary and fiscal policy be appropriate, perhaps increasingly determined by political criteria which look at the need for liquidity for firms and small and mediumsized enterprises, which do not create and maintain obstacles which are very difficult for non-euro area states to overcome and which create pressure on national governments to make the necessary medium and long-term reforms. finally, we must move from words to deeds. cohesion has to be effective.
mr president, i would like to begin by congratulating the swedish presidency and in particular prime minister reinfeldt, both on behalf of the epp and on behalf of the portuguese epp delegation. our record of the swedish presidency is one of almost total success, essentially in four key areas. firstly, in the institutional area, the contribution of the swedish presidency to the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, to the very successful election of the president of the commission and to all matters associated with ratification was extremely professional and was at the level of what is expected of best practice in the european union. of course, for a country like portugal, which played a decisive role in the adoption of the treaty of lisbon, this contribution of the swedish presidency is invaluable. a second point is the climate agenda, where the commission's work has also been very important. i should like to say that in my view and in the view of many other members of the epp, the european union has been most successful in the area of climate change, it is on the front line of the global fight, and this is due to the efforts of both the swedish presidency and, in particular, the presidency of the commission. we also consider this to be a very positive balance. a third point is financial regulation, which, especially with this last council, has taken a step which we consider decisive and which can have a very big impact on the way out of the crisis. i would therefore like to congratulate you on the fact that an agreement has been reached in this area. finally, i would like to mention an area which is very personal to me, namely the stockholm programme and therefore the area of security, justice and freedom. i, who have followed both the tempere process and, in particular, the subsequent hague process, consider the stockholm programme absolutely essential and would like to congratulate the swedish presidency and prime minister fredrik reinfeldt on it.
in writing. the six months of the swedish presidency under prime minister reinfeldt were a great success and an excellent one. the swedish presidency played a crucial role in bringing the treaty of lisbon into force. this ended almost a decade of debate and institutional deadlock and opened the door to new opportunities for the european union. the fight against climate change has always been at the top of his agenda. the eu is a leader in this area, and has demonstrated its ambition by proposing, among other things, emission reductions of 80%, 95% by 2050. it also agreed to provide 7.2 billion euro in financing for developing countries over the next three years. the swedish presidency has faced the economic crisis and financial turbulence with solid and realistic measures. faced with the worst financial crisis since the 1930s, the eu quickly adopted extraordinary support measures. the work of 'preventing' further crises was also carried out through a new architecture for financial supervision. the swedish presidency has helped to tackle the crisis and made europe stronger, enabling it to continue on its path of peace, success and modernity.
in writing. it is true that it was during the swedish presidency that significant institutional changes took place, notably the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, after the whole process of pressure and blackmail on the irish people to change their vote in the second referendum they had to hold. but even for those who are keen to see european integration becoming increasingly neoliberal, militarist and federalist, it is unacceptable that they have not had a word to say about the seriousness of the social situation in the european union, which is clearly visible in the increase of more than five million unemployed in the space of just one year, now reaching more than 23 million people. however, it is symptomatic that the emphasis is placed on launching the debate on the european union's strategy for 2020, forgetting the evaluation of the so-called lisbon strategy, adopted ten years ago, which promised an oasis in the european union, so as not to have to refer to the causes of the greatest economic and social crisis of recent decades, to which liberalization and labour flexibility contributed, which created precarious and low-paid work and multiplied unemployment.
despite the difficulties resulting from the delay in the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, the swedish presidency achieved notable victories. these include the energy efficiency package and the telecommunications package, the creation of a prudential financial supervisory body, the agreement on the 2010 budget, notably as regards the financing of the economic recovery plan, the baltic sea strategy and the alignment for this week's copenhagen climate conference. the spanish presidency, which will start in 2010, will ensure the transition between nice and lisbon and continue the strategy of promoting employment through stimulating and boosting economies, as well as tackling other key challenges such as financial regulation and climate change. because of its geographical and historical proximity, portugal and, in particular, the outermost regions such as madeira, which are looking forward to the performance of the spanish presidency, should seek to make the most of the opportunities that will surely be generated. the pioneering eu-morocco summit, for example, will be a privileged forum for the dynamisation of the euro-african atlantic cooperation area involving madeira, the azores, the canary islands and neighbouring countries, in particular morocco, to which i will devote my full efforts and which i will follow closely.
in writing. − the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up to provide additional assistance to workers affected by the consequences of major changes in the structure of international trade. it has already been used in portugal, germany, spain, the netherlands, sweden, ireland and austria, thus demonstrating that this problem affects member states with different geographical situations and different economic models and paths. these developments, which are occurring at a worrying rate, call for a thorough reflection on the european economic and social model, its sustainability and its future. they make it crystal clear that we must promote the creation of new quality jobs, which will only be able to pay off if we know how to support and free from unjustified burdens and red tape those who, despite the adversities, are still able to take the risk of setting up new businesses and joining innovative projects. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the cases in question, which i support, concern sweden, austria and the netherlands and have a broad consensus among the parliamentary committees responsible for both the motion for a resolution and the opinion.
in writing. - while maintaining our critical stance on the european globalisation adjustment fund, because we believe that it would have been more important to have taken measures to prevent unemployment in order to avoid it, we voted in favour of mobilising the fund to provide additional assistance to workers affected by the consequences of company restructuring or the liberalisation of international trade. in this case, it is the mobilisation of some eur 16 million for sweden, austria and the netherlands to provide assistance to workers made redundant in the automotive and construction sectors respectively. this is the fifth mobilisation of the fund in 2009, with a total of eur 53 million of the eur 500 million allocated being used. it is symptomatic that, in a period of severe social crisis, only a little over 10% of the funds provided have been used, which in itself shows the need to review the rules governing the fund.
in writing. - the eu is a space of solidarity. the european globalisation adjustment fund is in that spirit. these are essential supports for helping the unemployed and the victims of displacement in a globalised context. moreover, we know that an increasing number of companies are moving to other countries, taking advantage of the low prices of labour in a number of countries, notably china and india, often at the expense of so-called social, labour and environmental dumping.
in writing. - this amending budget reflects the contradictions of the european union budgets. on the one hand, it has low overall values in relation to the needs of economic and social cohesion policies. but on the other hand, the money is not spent because the countries that needed the money the most had budgetary difficulties to co-finance the money they needed. however, they rejected the proposals we put forward to reduce the co-financing requirements, particularly in a time of crisis. it is the contradictions and irrationalities of community policy which only favour the richer and more developed countries, and which end up exacerbating social inequalities and regional asymmetries. hence our vote against. the report itself justifies our position by pointing out that 'there are different reasons for the apparent slowdown in payments compared to the expected pace, depending on the member state. firstly, the current economic situation has in some cases caused difficulties in the provision of national co-financing. secondly, the fact that rural development implementation in 2009 shows a less dynamic profile than in the relevant year of the previous programming period is explained by the late adoption of certain programmes and, in the case of romania and bulgaria, by a lack of sufficient previous experience in the implementation of rural development programmes. "
in writing. - i believe that the doha round is of fundamental importance for international trade and can go a long way towards reducing poverty in developing countries and a more equitable sharing of the benefits of globalisation. it is therefore important that the doha development agenda takes account of that effect and makes a real contribution to the achievement of the millennium development goals. it is essential that wto members continue to avoid the adoption of protectionist measures, which could have a very serious impact on the world economy. i am convinced that the failure to adopt protectionist measures has allowed a better, albeit slow, recovery from the economic crisis we are currently experiencing. it is therefore crucial that wto members combat protectionism in their bilateral and multilateral relations in future agreements.
in writing. − i advocate a leading role for the eu in the ongoing wto negotiations to conclude the doha round, bearing in mind new global challenges such as climate change, food security and food sovereignty. i hope that the result will be the creation of new market opportunities and the strengthening of multilateral trade rules to put trade at the service of sustainable development. the wto can do a better job of managing globalization. however, i recognise that in the context of the current economic crisis, the rules and commitments under the wto have largely prevented its members from resorting to trade restrictive measures and have allowed economic recovery measures to be taken. it is important that wto members remain committed to actively combating protectionism. i hope that greater cooperation will be achieved between the wto and other international organisations and bodies such as the food and agriculture organisation of the united nations (fao), the ilo, the united nations environment programme (unep), the united nations development programme (undp) and the united nations conference on trade and development (unctad). that is why i voted in favour.
in writing. - this resolution insists on the european parliament's neoliberal positions on the doha round launched in 2001, albeit with some reference to social issues and the millennium development goals. but the central line is the commitment to complete liberalisation of the world market, which has refused to recognise that it is time to change priorities in international trade and to reject free trade for the negative contribution it has made to the financial, economic, food and social crisis we are experiencing, with increasing unemployment and poverty, serving only the interests of the richest countries and economic and financial groups. by rejecting our amendments, they have said no to a fundamental change in the negotiations to give priority to development and social progress, to the creation of jobs with rights, to the fight against hunger and poverty. it is regrettable that they have not made it a priority to end tax havens, to invest in food sovereignty and security, to support quality public services and to respect the right of governments to preserve their economy and public services, particularly in the areas of health, education, water, culture, communication and energy.
in writing. the various imbalances in the international trading system perversely contribute to accentuating the asymmetries between the various continents. in this sense, everything that contributes to the correction of the present imbalances is beneficial for all and certainly contributes to a multilateral system based on fairer and more equitable rules and to finding a fair trade that serves all. that is the spirit of the doha development agenda.
in writing. − public access to documents is an essential element in ensuring democratic control of the institutions and their effective functioning, and in strengthening citizens' trust. in the context of the stockholm programme, the council recalled the importance of transparency and invited the commission to examine how best to ensure transparency in the decision-making process, access to documents and good administration in the face of the new opportunities opened up by the lisbon treaty. i have no doubt that the legal basis of the regulation on access to documents needs to be amended and the legal context in which it is to operate must also be changed, particularly as regards the relationship between the union's institutions and citizens. it is also necessary to make substantive improvements, for example in what i consider to be a fundamental area: the possibility for the ep to exercise its right of democratic control through access to sensitive documents. transparency, both vis-à-vis the public and in interinstitutional terms, is a fundamental principle of the eu. the action and decisions taken by all eu institutions, bodies, offices and agencies must be guided by the principle of openness as fully as possible.
in writing. - the question at issue concerns the interpretation of the treaty of lisbon, in particular the compatibility of articles 75 and 215 thereof with regard to parliament's competence in the procedure for adopting restrictive measures against natural and legal persons. while article 215 seems to exclude parliament from the decision-making process, article 75 provides for the ordinary legislative procedure and the consequent participation of this house in the definition and adoption of measures aimed at preventing terrorism and related activities. the restrictive measures provided for in article 215 are often aimed specifically at combating terrorism, and it is therefore important to determine whether there is a derogation from article 75 and whether it is acceptable, from a systematic point of view, for the european parliament to be completely sidelined in the process of adopting the measures. i think it is clear that the legislator intended to entrust the adoption of those measures exclusively to the council and that such a trust may be due to criteria of speed and unity in decision-making. however, whenever urgency does not prevent it, i believe that there is an advantage in hearing parliament when adopting such measures.
in writing. - again, under the pretext of combating terrorism, restrictive measures and sanctions are envisaged against governments of third countries, natural or legal persons, non-state groups or authorities, which is unacceptable, since, despite some attempts at distinction, the report itself acknowledges that it is difficult to distinguish in practice the issue of threats. we know that another framework of respect for international law is needed. we do not accept the policy of 'double standards' towards governments of third countries, natural or legal persons, groups or non-state authorities, determined by the interests of the united states or the major european powers, of which there is no lack of examples. to name just a few, there are the cases of the illegal occupation of western sahara, of aminatu haidar and other sarawi prisoners held by morocco, of the turkish intervention against the kurds and against cyprus. we therefore voted against this report, although we agree with some of the paragraphs, particularly with regard to requests for clarification from the european commission.
in writing. the rational nature of the questions raised goes beyond the mere doctrinal assessment and the coherence between the declared will and the interpreted will. there is also a practical consequence which follows from the old maxim "he who can do the most, can do the least" and it has to do with the following: what sense does it make for someone who has competence in criminal matters and the prevention and combating of terrorist attacks, and who is involved in a codecision procedure, to be excluded in the first instance if other measures are involved which, by affecting citizens' rights, may even be important in this context? it is essential that the normative interpretation of the treaty of lisbon corresponds in reality to the stated strengthening of the european parliament's powers and competences. and at least in some cases, as raised in the question, providing for the possibility of a dual legal basis where citizens' rights are at stake and counter-terrorism policies are threatened. and in other cases, such as zimbabwe and somalia, the possibility of optional consultation, as enshrined in the stuttgart declaration on european union, was also raised.
the council says that the situation in afghanistan and pakistan has a direct impact on europe. this is the main message we must convey to the european citizens with honesty and courage. in this regard, i welcome the new european union action plan for afghanistan and pakistan which provides for investing in a broad capacity building programme at all levels of the afghan administration. only the effective implementation of this action plan as an aggregator of european efforts in afghanistan can contribute to the state building needed to end the war and underdevelopment. europe cannot abandon the afghans and it is not there because the americans have decided to do so. the international military and civilian presence will continue to be needed there for many years to come. i would like to conclude by expressing my strongest condemnation of the forced return, which has been unworthily imposed by the french government on afghans fleeing the war in their country.
mr president, as we know, 15 directives on the recognition of professional qualifications have been consolidated into a single legislative act, which has given rise to a number of practical problems in the implementation of the directives in the various member states. the difficulties are well known and the commission should draw up a list of critical points and analyse the problem areas which arise in the application of this important internal market provision. protectionism at various levels is an obstacle not only to the free movement of persons but also to the internal market. we must try to identify the nebulous points so that the effectiveness of the directive is not undermined by mistrust. the union is not asked to do what the member states should do, but there is a facilitation effort to be made to help overthrow professional corporatism. improving the application of this directive should be achieved by spreading the recognition of qualifications in the solvit and eures networks, by encouraging common platforms, by implementing effective contact points and, not least, by providing an articulation of this directive with the services directive.
mr president, we are very concerned about all the developments in the budget reform, particularly in the structural and cohesion policy. in a situation of crisis and of great social inequalities and regional asymmetries, structural and cohesion policy is essential for effective economic and social cohesion, which implies keeping precise objectives in this area and not trying to nationalise the costs of community policies, whether in agriculture or fisheries, to the detriment of the least developed countries and regions and the outermost regions. but there are several indications that the concept of cohesion is being further subverted, whether by attempting to include and finance all kinds of actions and actors that have nothing to do with the objectives of cohesion, namely the european union strategy for 2020 and policies on climate change, or by changing eligibility and management criteria. so it makes perfect sense to have this debate on cohesion policy after 2013. we expect clear answers in defence of a genuine policy of economic and social cohesion.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, there is an urgent need to have a debate on the community budget and how it can contribute to its sustainability and transparency in the future. the financial framework after 2013 should be built on the foundations of solidarity and territorial cohesion, which are crucial values for regions like madeira, which are facing permanent difficulties and therefore in need of permanent support. i would like to share with you some ideas which i think should always be present when thinking about and building cohesion policy. the commission has on several occasions referred to some aspects which i consider essential, such as the need for greater flexibility, simplicity, efficiency and a greater focus on results in the definition and implementation of this policy. but there are some issues which give me some concern, particularly in view of the needs of the outermost regions. i would like to make three brief comments on this subject: first, the current approach in terms of regions must prevail over alternative approaches, such as value-added sectors. such a reorientation could jeopardise objective 2, which currently benefits about two thirds of european regions, and could be counterproductive in regions with their own distinctive economic profile and competitive advantages; secondly, it seems to me that the maintenance of the proximity criterion in cohesion policy is vital. the allocation and management of the structural funds should continue to be prioritised from a regional rather than a national or even centralised european perspective; thirdly, and last but not least, i would like to recall and emphasize article 349 of the treaty of lisbon, which provides for special treatment for the outermost regions as regards access to the structural funds, precisely because of their economic and social situation, aggravated by the unique and perennial conditions and characteristics which are manifested in these constant difficulties and which therefore require the permanent aid claimed.
cohesion policy is the european union's main instrument for ensuring that all european citizens have the same chances of success wherever they live, it makes a decisive contribution to creating a european feeling and is one of the most successful and effective european policies for development. i cannot hide my concern, commissioner, about the european commission's notorious 'non-paper' and its proposals. we do not accept the renationalisation of cohesion policy, which would in fact cease if its effectiveness depended on the wealth of each member state. i sincerely hope that the commission will abandon this course for good. as you know, mr commissioner, the new article 349 of the treaty establishes the need for european policies to be adapted to the specific features of the outermost regions, in particular to mitigate their handicaps but also to exploit their potential. what ideas can you give us about the future strategy for the ors that the commission will be releasing in the course of 2010?
just to say this: diplomatic work is very important, but aminatou haidar is on her 33rd day of hunger strike in a hospital. the worst thing we could do would be to tell a woman who is fighting for basic human rights that now, here in this european parliament, she would be forgotten.
in writing. the draft resolution under debate concerns european funding for the period 2010-13 for the decommissioning of the kozloduy nuclear power plant in bulgaria and the mobilisation of the flexibility instrument to this end, thus honouring a commitment made to bulgaria during its accession process. it also covers additional funding from the flexibility instrument for the second phase of the european economic recovery plan (eerp) in the field of energy, which has not been adequately funded. while i do not deny that nuclear energy can be part of a more balanced european energy mix and that its use is likely to reduce external dependence in this field, i believe that the european union cannot fail to share in the effort to close down the obsolete nuclear complex and thus ensure that projects to revive the economy deserve adequate funding. both the environmental safety motivated by the decommissioning of the kozloduy power station and increased investment in energy projects are thus good reasons for the mobilisation of the instrument.
in writing. − at the conciliation meeting of 18 november 2009, the flexibility instrument was mobilised to finance the decommissioning of the kozloduy nuclear power plant with an amount of eur 75 million in 2010. i regret that the commission only presented these funding needs in its letter of amendment no 2/2010 after parliament's first reading, i.e. after parliament had approved its priorities. this has created additional pressure on heading 1a, considerably affecting the dynamics of the 2010 budget negotiations and undermining parliament's political priorities. however, the commitments made are to be fulfilled, as is the case with the protocol on the conditions of bulgaria's accession to the european union, which contains the european union's commitment to finance the decommissioning of the kozloduy plant. the additional funding needs, totalling eur 225 million in 2011-13, should be addressed in the context of a mid-term review of the mff. it is noted that the continuation of the funding of kozloduy in 2011-2013 should not prejudice the funding of existing multiannual programmes and actions. that is why i voted in favour.
in writing. − the €5 billion european economic recovery plan (eerp), agreed by the european parliament in march 2009, is divided into trans-european energy projects and broadband internet development projects in rural areas. this is an important instrument for dealing with the economic crisis, reviving the economy and, consequently, creating jobs. at the conciliation meeting on 18 november, the european parliament, the council and the commission reached agreement on the financing of the second year (2010) of the european economic recovery plan (eerp), which amounts to eur 2.4 billion. this funding for projects in the field of energy and broadband internet requires the revision of the multiannual financial framework for 2007-13. the financing of the pree should not have been postponed for later years and i am therefore pleased with the agreement reached. i would also point out that the current multiannual financial framework does not correspond to the financial needs of the european union. the commission should therefore submit a proposal for its mid-term evaluation as a matter of urgency. i also support the need to extend the 2017-2013 mff for the 2015-2016 financial years. that is why i voted in favour.
, in writing. - even if there is some positive use of the money mobilised here, it is still far from meeting the needs of rural areas. agriculture in the countries of the european union has many other shortcomings and farmers who work the land need an agricultural policy that encourages production, that guarantees them an income that allows them to live with dignity and prevents the continuing desertification of the rural world. so it is not enough to say that solidarity is being promoted in the field of energy sources and the promotion of broadband in rural areas to be considered as contributing to the revival of the european economy. as we have said, a thorough review of community policies and a substantial increase in budgetary resources were needed. that is why we abstained.
in writing. - as i have said before, i consider it essential that the particular conditions of the crisis we are currently experiencing be taken into account when we discuss the allocation of funds to the different sectors of the european economy. in particular, i consider it essential that funds be allocated to the implementation of the european economic recovery plan, especially as regards the possibility of adopting measures to stimulate economic growth, competitiveness, cohesion and the protection of jobs. i would like to reiterate that the allocation of the funds needed to enable small and mediumsized enterprises, some of the main victims of the crisis, to benefit from support enabling them to survive this crisis is fundamental, as is the crucial importance of the structural and cohesion funds for national economic growth. however, i regret that only eur 300 million has been allocated, which i consider insufficient, for the creation of a fund for the dairy sector. the serious crisis currently affecting the sector would justify a greater allocation of funds to help producers overcome the difficulties they are currently facing.
in writing. - this is a budget that will help to combat unemployment and to revive the economy. the european parliament gives priority to the european economic recovery plan, which has a budget of eur 2.4 billion for next year. i welcome the granting of additional support to the milk sector, amounting to eur 300 million, but i would like to see a budget line created to establish a permanent fund for this sector. i also consider it very important that, as we propose in this budget, the current mff 2007-2013 be revised and extended for the financial years 2015-2016. i would remind you that the fight against climate change is an eu priority which is not sufficiently reflected in this budget. i would also like to remind you that energy security is crucial for the union and i therefore welcome the signing of the nabucco project. the budgets of parliament and the other institutions proposed for the second reading are the same as those which we had already adopted at first reading. in heading 5 we have a margin of eur 72 million, which will be used as a priority to finance additional expenditure directly attributable to the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon. that is why i voted in favour.
in writing. - we voted against the report which 'welcomes the overall agreement on the 2010 budget', because this is a community budget which already reflects the priorities, laid down in the lisbon treaty, of an increasingly neoliberal and militarist european union, less concerned with economic and social cohesion. it is unacceptable that, in the midst of the economic and social crisis, with its dramatic effects on employment and the living conditions of the population, the proposed community budget should be 11 billion euros short of the figure for payments in the financial perspective. we are pleased, however, that some of our proposals have been accepted, such as: - the creation of a budget line for actions in the textile and footwear sector with a view to establishing a community programme for the sector; - the creation of another line to encourage the transformation of precarious work into work with rights. these proposals are intended, on the one hand, to highlight the serious crisis in the textile sector, to which the exponential increase in imports from third countries has contributed, and, on the other hand, to make a contribution to the fight against the growing precariousness of work, unemployment and poverty.
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competition' in the context of the directive on the protection of the environment. there are also various measures to assess the situation of key sectors of the economy, such as the textile and footwear sector and the fisheries sector, in order to promote the renewal of the fishing fleet, which is very important for my country. pilot projects to combat desertification and to preserve jobs are also of great importance in this period of economic crisis. it should also be noted that this was the last eu budget in the light of the treaty of nice. the treaty of lisbon extends the powers of the ep to the whole budget.
in writing. - believes that european citizens aspire to a secure europe, welcomes the increase in appropriations for sub-heading 3a on freedom, security and justice compared to the 2009 budget; it stressed the importance of making sufficient funds available from the eu budget to manage legal immigration and the integration of third-country nationals, while at the same time fighting illegal immigration. it recalls that these policies must always be based on respect for the eu charter of fundamental rights. it also considers it important to increase funds for border protection, including the european return fund and the european refugee fund, in order to promote a policy of genuine solidarity between member states. conscious that all eu countries face increasing challenges in relation to the policies covered by this heading, it urges the member states to take advantage of the increased appropriations for freedom, security and justice in the 2009 budget to jointly address these and any new challenges that may arise by promoting a common immigration policy, which is inclusive where possible but which has at its core the absolute respect for human rights.
in writing. the vote on the budget of the european union is an annual institutional framework in which commitment to community programmes is confirmed and the role of the european parliament underlined. the completion of the second financing phase of the european economic recovery plan for 2009 and 2010 is welcome, as economic recovery and employment are major concerns of the epp in the current crisis context. the launch of the new microfinance instrument programme underlines the priority of the lisbon strategy objectives and the importance of the role of smes in job creation. i cannot but express my concern about the reduction in the budgetary appropriations for the structural and cohesion funds, which are essential for economic dynamism and territorial cohesion, especially in the outermost regions such as madeira. it seems to me essential to continue the flexibility of cohesion policy with a view to increasing the current low levels of implementation of programmes financed by community funds. on the other hand, projects with a strategic vision, added value and long-term competitiveness should continue to be promoted. i therefore voted in favour of this report, which is the culmination of a technical and negotiating process which has been painstaking and difficult.
in writing. − the openness of the european institutions and the clarity of their procedures ensure that civil society and public opinion in the member states are not only informed, but are an integral and informed part of their decision-making process. despite the relative distance between the populations, enhanced by the troubled and not always clear and uncritical process that led to the entry into force of the lisbon treaty, the european institutions must persist and make a real effort to make as many documents as possible available in a timely manner, thus helping to reduce that gap. i cannot, however, fail to regret that the parliamentary left has taken advantage of the necessary improvement in the legal framework for access to documents following the entry into force of the treaty to disregard in a populist way the real need for confidentiality in certain aspects of european action, seeking to reduce itself to the category of the sole champion of transparency. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. i regret that this was not possible.
in writing. − the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon requires improvements to be made to the legal framework for access to documents. such improvements must safeguard the rights of all citizens of the union. since the union is an area of freedom, security and justice, as enshrined in the 'preamble to the charter of fundamental rights', all efforts to increase transparency in access to documents of the eu institutions are welcome. however, it is also very important to assess to what extent full freedom of access to all documents cannot be detrimental to the proper functioning of the institutions. it is therefore very important to find a balance in this matter.
in writing. - belarus has been given and deserved the unflattering epithet of the last soviet republic in europe. despite the progress that has been made in some aspects of political, social and economic life in that country, it is clear that no reforms there justify, for the time being, the refutation of that regrettable attribute. in belarus, elections are not free, there is no freedom of expression, association or demonstration, and repressive activities by the authorities are on the increase. as it did with cuba, the european union is in danger of being inclined to pursue a conciliatory line towards this dictatorship. i think it would be a mistake to do so and not make it clear that the values of democracy and freedom are not only non-negotiable but are also irrelevant to any agreements to be concluded with belarus. the european union must therefore maintain and strengthen its contacts with the democratic opposition in belarus, in fidelity to those who have been awarded the sakharov prize by the european parliament for their fight for the democratization of that country.
in writing. - the eu cannot be a space concerned only with the problems of the countries that are part of it. it must also be the driving force behind the democratization of the countries which emerged after the break-up of the former soviet union. all measures aimed at improving the conditions in these countries are very important. this is the spirit of this motion for a resolution which calls for measures to support belarus, which must be accompanied by clear signs of democratic reforms by belarus, as well as human rights and the rule of law. the participation of belarus in the eastern partnership is an important step in this direction, but there is still much to be done to improve the freedoms and guarantees in belarus.
in writing. - since the fall of the kleptocratic regime of mobutu sese seko, the democratic republic of the congo (formerly the belgian congo and zaire) has been plunged into the most brutal unrest and violence, which has already resulted in millions of deaths. the eastern part of the country has been particularly hard hit by this scourge, which knows no borders and is more ethnic than national in character. the most impressive atrocities are happening at a rate that is impossible to keep up with, without the international community, in particular the united nations forces, having demonstrated the ability to deal with them, despite all the civil and military efforts that have been made in the meantime. the lack of governance in certain african countries and the loss of control of parts of their territories by central governments are unfortunately phenomena that have yet to be eradicated and that often coincide with the illegal exploitation and plundering of raw materials by both insurgent forces and the regular forces and their leaders. if these phenomena have their origin in european decolonization and the way in which borders have been defined, they cannot absolve african governments and decision-makers of their responsibilities and call on the civil societies of those countries to take decisive control of their destinies.
in writing. the eu cannot be indifferent to the various armed conflicts that exist somewhat all over the world. the violence in the democratic republic of congo is a conflict that has dragged on for several years and has already caused millions of deaths, displacement and displaced persons. it is therefore very important to call on all parties to the conflict to cease hostilities so that the people of these regions can return to live in peace. it is very important that we continue to support the un missions on the ground in order to alleviate the suffering of the entire population, especially the elderly, women and children.
mr president, this is a point of order, given that we had a vote this morning on the justification of the negotiations that existed between the european union and morocco. since a document from the european commission has just come out, on the same date as today, which says that negotiations between the european union and morocco are progressing in the agri-food and fisheries sectors, i wonder whether it was to these negotiations that the chairman of the socialist group in the european parliament was referring this morning when he used the argument of negotiations to remove from the agenda the subject of aminatou haidar, the sarawi fighter who has been on hunger strike for 32 days and who is at this moment in danger of her life. it is a question that needed to be properly clarified, because what happened this morning is unacceptable.
europe must intervene in this matter with all the tools at its disposal, particularly in the framework of the cotonou agreement. we must demand that this draconian project be stopped immediately, or else there will be consequences for economic and political cooperation with uganda. when we learn about the content of this future law and when we hear reports of the sad fate of homosexuals in uganda, sudan, iran and nigeria, to name just a few examples, we see how homophobia is still sadly entrenched in many cultures and the flagrant violation of the human rights obligations of these countries. the role of the american evangelical christian far right in mobilizing and funding various initiatives of this nature in africa is shocking. europe and the european institutions must do everything on the ground in the countries concerned to counteract and combat the harmful and obscurantist influence of these agents of intolerance who are instigating a new era of hate crime in africa.
